# Continuity State

## Canon status

**Volume 01, Chapters 1–50, is written, closed and audited. The audit is `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md` and is the authority on every decision a batch deferred to a planning phase.** Every fact below is either an author-level lock for drafting, or a fact established in the finished prose, and the two are marked. The first section of this file is unchanged author-level canon; the sections **Batch 0001 as written** through **Batch 0005 as written** record what the prose actually did and are binding on the volume that follows; the section **Volume 01 close accounting** is the audited account of the volume, which supersedes the batch's own account of itself; and the section **Volume 02** at the foot is the audited starting state for Chapters 51 onward.

**Every proposed-canon item that stood open while Volume 01 was being written has now been ruled or closed, dated 26 September 2026, and the rulings are in this file under *Rulings of the Volume 01 volume audit*.** Nothing in this file is waiting for a planning phase to decide.

## Fixed opening facts

- Calder Marr is thirty-two and starts as a practical **Line Diver** in Morrow Quay.
- Oren Marr is Calder's older brother. He was twenty when the *Aster Gannet* sank thirteen years ago, making him thirty-three now.
- The ferry sank during the *Vigil*'s seizure with sixty-three workers aboard. The public account blames an illegal crossing; the surviving pressure log contains a deliberate three-second gap.
- The public record is a set of separate documents that were never meant to be read together: the harbor **salvage report** Alden signed, which omitted the sealed case; the **Navy seizure report**, which denies the ferry's hold was ever occupied and denies the cutter's own case needed declaration; a **Registry custody log** recording the evidence as uncontested; and the **public pressure log and ferry survey** copied into the inquest record, which show a three-second gap and record the compartment's dimensions. Calder sees only the public version at the opening, and he has read all of it.
- **Clue availability for the compartment fact, one canonical answer:** the *Gannet* survey and its compartment figures are public from the opening, so nothing is being withheld from the reader or from Calder. What Calder cannot do at the opening is *use* them, because proving the compartment was too large to have been empty requires the sealed case's own dimensions, and those sit in Navy-held custody papers he cannot read. Chapter 9's joinery match supplies the case's type and approximate size and makes the contradiction testable without proving it. Only the Volume 1 midpoint supplies the physical measurement that proves the compartment could not have been empty. No document, character, or forum may treat the compartment as proven before that.
- **The unaccounted plate note:** the repairer's note about a plate cut from the *Gannet*'s iron plating appears in the public copy of Alden's signed harbor salvage report, which Calder has read. It concerns the ferry's hull iron, not transparent glass, and it must never be fused with the weathered transparent hull plate lifted in Chapter 4. Alden will not discuss the report, and Calder does not yet know what Alden left out of it. The note authenticates no object, does not identify the shard's origin, and cannot be used to authenticate the Chapter 4 plate as a *Gannet* artifact. Its pay-off belongs to Volume 4, where the page removed from the Gannet file accounts for the plate that was never accounted for. Alden's own partial confession is a separate, later thread: it begins in Volume 9 and is completed in Volume 15 with the original repair log, so the two are never collapsed into one scene.
- Alden is not yet ready to confess, and Calder does not yet know what Alden omitted.
- Ilya Serr was a junior Registry official during the Gannet Cut. At the opening he is a fifty-six-year-old senior Lantern Registry examiner. He becomes the active central antagonist in Volume 4, is promoted to Minister of Returning Memory at the close of Volume 8, and holds the office from Volume 9 onward. Do not call him minister in Volumes 1–8, and do not describe him as the central antagonist before Volume 4.

## Institutions and jurisdiction

- The **Morrow Quay harbor authority**, working from the reeve's office on the west basin, issues claim windows, work permits, and one-season provisional working charters, and can order a wreck held for inspection. It cannot certify a glass witness or overrule a Registry seizure.
- **Three boundaries, never blurred:** the **harbor line** runs along the mole and the inner shoals and lets harbor crews work any wreck inside it for a share; the **imperial line** is the Crown's outer-shelf boundary, beyond the harbor line, and working past it needs the full Second Bell license; the **one-season provisional working charter** is the harbor authority's wider seasonal permission and is the first instrument that lets a crew work beyond a single claim. In Volume 1, every dive stays inside the harbor line under a claim window or a **work permit** that names a site and dates. No chapter in Volume 1 may put an unlicensed Calder past the imperial line, because the one-season charter is not granted until Movement 5 and the full license is a Volume 4 question. The *Vigil*'s naval salvage claim in Movement 2 bites precisely because it sits over the same shoal and past the line the crew cannot cross.
- The **Lantern Registry** certifies provenance and custody, not truth. Mara works from the **Morrow circuit office**, whose copying room holds the public pressure log and the copies.
- The **Crown Navy** asserts salvage authority at sea. Keep the Registry, the harbor authority, and the Navy distinct in every scene, with distinct documents.
- The **Free Keel** shares tools, rescue plans, and legal risk among Morrow Quay's independent crews. It has no leader and is frequently broke.
- The **Office of Returning Memory** is the ministry; Ilya's seal represents it in Volume 1 through a seizure order, not through his presence.

## License and Volume 1 resolution

- The full **Second Bell license** remains a later legal goal. Volume 1 does not promise that Calder earns it.
- Volume 1 resolves the crew's claim to the first transparent recovery: Calder secures a one-season **provisional working charter**, retains the agreed salvage share, and preserves the provenance record through Mara.
- The Volume 4 inquiry removes Calder's eligibility for the full Second Bell examination. A provisional working charter is not a full license.

## Oren artifact custody

1. Volume 1: a small contaminated shard is recovered, a weathered transparent hull plate is lifted beside it, and both leave the yard in one witnessed surrender to a Registry seizure; a public provenance duplicate and the crew's measurements, repair-seam records, and scaled drawing survive. Both glass objects are entered in the Morrow circuit custody room. The freed shoal iron is ordinary iron and stays with the crew, so the batch loss is glass, not livelihood.
2. Volume 4: the Registry releases a **provisional public copy record** of that fragment — a certified transcript with provenance and stated limits, not the object.
3. Volume 8: Calder and Mara win supervised, limited access to the seized original.
4. Volume 9: that access produces a stable, provenance-marked **Oren route copy**, a new working panel authorized for limited route use and public display by mixed custodians. The original stays in Registry custody under contested claim.
5. Volume 13: the independent routes identify a separate **restricted remainder** in the Ninth Bell. Calder receives a recognized but unexercised exclusive family claim. A forced interface exposes only a temporary route signal without opening the original; that signal is lost when the route collapses. The remainder remains sealed.
6. Volume 15: Calder releases the family claim into Mara and Nessa's dual seal under rotating-council oversight. He never opens the remainder or obtains a private, unshared version of Oren's final voice.

## Relationship lock

- Calder and Mara are equals with separate careers and public duties. Intimacy grows through consent, accountability, and consequences, not rescue or professional subordination.
- Mara leaves the active crew in Volume 3 and the Registry in Volume 4. Her later public testimony costs her former institutional standing; it does not cost a Registry job she still holds.
- Their public commitment follows the Volume 11 cordon crossing; their small civil ceremony occurs in Volume 12.
- They remain married and professionally independent through Volumes 13–15.
- Mara's expertise must affect the plot on its own. She cannot enter an echo, certify truth, or become Calder's assistant.

## Power and interface lock

- Formal stages are Line Diver, Tide-Ear, Faultreader, Bellwright, Storm-Reader, Keelwright, Chorus Diver, and Openhand.
- The first tide-ear entry is supervised and limited to one anchored scene. It requires a named tender, an independent line, a fixed air limit, a seated anchor, a cutoff the tender can pull without permission, and a recorded reason. A tender's stop is not a request: Nessa calls the cutoff in Chapter 6 and takes the standing shutdown decision in Chapter 7, and she uses that authority again in Volumes 14 and 15.
- Costs: a limited practice trace, rope-burn reenactment, tinnitus, emotional aftertaste, no second entry that day, and no way to verify truth.
- **The first entry costs three days' worth of yard time, not one.** The Chapter 4 work dive is one paid working day. Chapter 5 is a separate **bench day** in which Nessa builds the physical anchor, the cutoff rig, and the written reason while the crew earns nothing, and the yard cannot bill it to anyone. Chapter 6's entry spends a night's air. The permit is two dates and does not renew. Do not compress these into a single afternoon and evening: `bible/power-system.md` names the bench day explicitly, and the cost only lands if the reader feels the two days going.
- Volume 14 rehearses Calder offering his body as a temporary living anchor; Nessa exercises the named-tender cutoff before synchronization. The aftermath is severe but reversible: acute aftertaste, joint pain, three days of poor sleep, and temporary hearing loss.
- Volume 15 is Calder's informed final continuous connection. It costs permanent hearing damage and the inability to enter an echo without other people.
- This novel has no game-style System, panels, stat blocks, status messages, floating text, or software metaphors. Approved warnings must be physical, bell-based, or Registry-native. A future change to that rule would be a recorded canon decision here, never an improvisation in a chapter.

## Ending lock

- The final enemy is the already-established Ilya Serr and the Serein Crown's effort to make history singular; no new final enemy is permitted.
- The Volume 15 midpoint reveals that the public evacuation bell had sounded and a second rescue line was active when Oren cut the live keel-line. His hand turns toward that line, but the record cannot establish whether he understood the cost. The resulting uncertainty prevents his choice from becoming a clean heroic command and forces the coalition to keep every route's provenance and limits visible.
- Calder rejects sole-witness authority, releases his unexercised claim to the sealed remainder, and helps braid independently governed routes.
- Ilya dies trapped by the permanent living-custodian bond he chose. His death is not absolution.
- The ending leaves a distributed Open Keel Assembly, local custody, shared maintenance, public correction, and a real right to refuse.
- The origin of tideglass, and the possibility of behavior in merged echoes beyond their witnesses, stay intentionally open.

## Volume 1 reveal lock

Volume 1 may establish the existence of the Gannet Cut, the glass fleet, the *Vigil*'s claim over the shoal, a physical alignment between the shard's provenance and the cutter's recorded mooring position, and a first hint of a route toward Orison. It must not explain the Quietening, identify the Crown Seal, name the Nine Harbors Compact, prove who altered the ferry manifest, or reveal what the sealed case contained. No forum rules on the *Vigil*'s presence or the manifest in this volume; that ruling belongs to the Volume 4 inquiry. The separate-document record is the engine of the Volume 4–6 argument, so Volume 1 must not spend it.

## Batch 0001 locks

- The shard is unclaimed Registry glass at the moment of recovery, not yet proven to come from the *Aster Gannet*.
- Calder wins a disputed claim window, not ownership.
- **Two anchors, never fused.** The Chapter 1 fragment is wedged under the shank of a **drifted** storm anchor that came in against the mole on an earlier tide; that iron is bedded too deep for the yard crane, is tagged with a lead line for a proper lift, and stays at the mole through the whole batch. The anchor Calder frees in Chapter 4 is a **second, separate** piece of ordinary iron from the same mundane shoal wreck, still bedged under the transparent keel rib. Both are ordinary iron, neither is glass, and neither is touched by the seizure. The crew keeps the freed shoal iron; the drifted mole anchor goes back to the harbor authority as municipal tackle. The two-object trap here is the same trap as the two-object glass rule below, and the same discipline applies: name which iron, every time.
- **Jurisdiction is written, not assumed.** The Chapter 1 mole work and the Chapter 3–4 shoal work run under harbor-authority instruments: the claim window, and a separate **work permit** naming Gannet Shoal's inner edge and two dates, with the harbor line as the boundary and a bond the yard barely meets. The permit does not reach the wreck's outer marks, the *Vigil*'s recorded mooring ground, or the keel line continuing beneath the shoal, all of which lie past the imperial line. Kest says so aloud in Chapter 3. This is the mechanism that makes the *Vigil*'s claim in Chapter 10 dangerous and that closes the crew out in Chapter 11; it is not optional background.
- **The batch's two working days are both paid for.** Chapter 4 spends a dive day; Chapter 5 spends a bench day in which the crew builds the anchor, cutoff rig, and written reason and earns nothing. Those two days are also the shoal work permit's two dates, so the permit runs out spent rather than used. The Chapter 6 entry is a shallow protected dive over the Free Keel platform inside the harbor and needs no shoal permit.
- The tide-ear entry is one scene from one witness's position: a sealed glass case crossing the *Gannet*'s loading space while an official voice reads an empty public manifest, and a gloved hand leaving frame before anything is cut. The hand does not turn toward the rescue line here; that reading is reserved for Volumes 13 and 15.
- The physical shard **and the weathered hull plate** leave the deck under the same seizure order and go to the Morrow circuit custody room as two separately entered items. The crew keeps the freed shoal iron, measurements, repair-seam records, a scaled drawing, names, and a public provenance duplicate, which is a record of the objects and not a copy of their memory. No glass survives in Calder's hands past Chapter 8.
- The Oren-specific mark stays private to Calder. He tells Nessa that a sound is not an ordinary fault; he does not tell Mara what it is.
- **Whose witness the first echo is:** a *Vigil* rating who helped carry the sealed case across the *Gannet*'s loading space. The corroboration therefore comes from a participant in the seizure, not a bystander, and that contestability is a later problem rather than a defect. The witness is never named, located, interviewed, or proved to be a *Gannet* casualty in this batch, and the gloved hand with the apprentice's callus is something the witness saw rather than the witness's own body. Calder may believe the hand was Oren's; he may not be able to show it. The shard's cut-edge joinery matching the *Vigil*'s case pattern is a material comparison in Chapter 9, not a proven chain of custody for the scene itself.
- **No instructor and no manual.** Calder has no teacher for the entry. The six conditions are assembled in the yard from what Tobias remembers of old salvage practice, what Nessa can engineer, and what Mara can justify on paper. No convenient master appears to explain them.
- **The *Vigil* arrives as paperwork.** A filed naval salvage claim, a Registry order, and a cutter taking a berth. No boarding, no gunfire, no physical seizure, and no face-to-face meeting with Holt in this batch.
- **The honorific *Magister* enters as a word on a document, in Chapter 8.** The seizure order's seal line reads Magister Ilya Serr, senior Registry examiner. Calder has never met him, reads the word as part of a form, and learns nothing about the man from it. The word is the Registry honorific for senior examiners and, later, for the minister; it does not name the office of Minister of Returning Memory, and Ilya is not called minister anywhere in Volumes 1–8.
- **The batch ends on Calder's own decision, spoken aloud.** He rejects a permanent gag, takes only the twelve-day protection, logs his hearing impairment where his crew can read it, and chooses to keep investigating under naval scrutiny with no glass, no permit, and no second entry. The *Vigil*'s order to stay off the wreck and the tightening of its salvage line are the price he accepts, not the closing beat. The last image is a choice with a bill attached, not a threat received.
- The route clue toward Orison is a broad physical bearing from the anchor, the provenance diagram, and the continuing keel line. It is not a map, a route, or a purpose.
- **Two glass objects, never fused:** the Chapter 1 transparent **shard** and the Chapter 4 weathered transparent **hull plate** are separate objects with separate provenances, and every document, drawing, and measurement in the story must keep them separate. The pre-Dominion **custody mark of nine joined lines** is on the plate, which is builder's evidence of old civic construction, and the **joinery match to the *Vigil*'s sealed cases** is on the shard's cut edges. The shard is never declared pre-Dominion civic glass inside Volume 1, and the plate is never declared a *Gannet* artifact. Chapter 5's discovery is about the plate, Chapter 9's discovery is about the shard, and the provenance diagram must show them as two objects with two provenances.
- The compartment measurement is not proved in this batch. The batch may make the contradiction testable, and the physical proof waits for the Volume 1 midpoint.
- The batch ends with a twelve-day disputed-claim defense, a logged hearing impairment, a naval salvage claim filed alongside the Registry's seizure order, and the *Vigil*'s arrival.

## Batch 0001 as written (Chapters 1–10) — binding on Chapters 11 onward

### Objects and custody

- **The transparent shard** and **the weathered hull plate** are in the **Morrow circuit custody room** as **two separately entered items under two numbers**, written by the Registry courier in a small square hand on the back of the second sheet. **The specific numbers are deliberately not quoted in the prose and must not be invented retroactively**; if a later volume needs them on the page, they may be given digits at that point, and until then they exist only as "two numbers."
- The plate is entered as a transparent hull plate of undetermined origin, with the seam as Mara described it and the aperture as sealed and unopened. The shard is entered as one object of undetermined origin. No office in Veyr has to care about either, because the claim window closed on both the same day.
- The **freed shoal iron** stays with the Free Keel on the yard stack, with a harbor-authority float line still on it. Its crown shackle carries a bright **saw kerf** nearly through at the point of the wear, and it was loaded again after the cut. Nobody claims it and the harbor does not fight over iron.
- The **drifted storm anchor** stays bedged in the mole's toe off the third seam under a marked lead line and a rag float, with **Kest's own mark on the float** and a clause in the surrender releasing the Free Keel's claim to it. It is municipal tackle and it wants a proper lift with a crane, a chain, a slip's crew, and an hour written against it, on Kest's authority and at the harbor's cost. It has not been touched and must not be touched.
- The **pale run on the shoal bottom**, the transparent keel rib, and its continuous keel line with eleven keys are the same object. Calder sighted it, sounded along it, measured four foot two by eleven inches on the plate, and did not let the boat over it. The second clamp went on the rib and the rib settled two or three inches, so the outer third of the rib was given up. **The permit boundary stopped them a few fathoms short of the rib's outer end**, which is past the harbor line.

### Documents that now exist in the world

- The **salvage labor log** for the two permit dates, countersigned by Kest, with *air consumed, stated by the watch* underlined in Kest's hand. Bevin Rook's reading of the air column is on it.
- The **public provenance duplicate**, made in the yard in one sitting, with four copies. It is a record of the objects and not a copy of anything inside them. It is posted on the fish market wall and stayed up after Kest withdrew the gag. It names every hand: Marr, Rook, Pike, Wren, Mott, and Venn as independent witness.
- The **anchor-bearing sheet**, which is the yard's own drawing, with the mole's seam, the shoal, the *Alder Reach*, three independent bearings with times and the man who took each, and the run of the pale line. The three bearings agree inside four degrees.
- The **scaled plate drawing** with the eleven keys marked in Calder's hand and the note *continuous west, no break, no join*.
- Calder's **unverified memory note**, on a slate, headed as the evidence heard by Calder Marr, unverified, from himself, and not corroborated by any document, witness, instrument, or second person. **It is kept physically separate from every physical finding and must stay separate in every later reference.** It contains one line that is not true, written deliberately: that the personal association was confirmed by the diver and not disclosed. He never tells Mara what the Oren mark is.
- Mara's **separate condition sheet**, not the Registry book, holding the six stated conditions, the named tender, the fixed air limit, the written reason for the entry, and the anchor described as a brass rigger's hand bell, family property, **association with the site not established**, with Calder's own statement that he does not know where the bell was at the time of the scene.
- The **crew's book entry** recording Calder's hearing impairment in the top margin, in his own hand, countersigned by Nessa Pike and read by Bevin Rook: ringing in both ears, a second sound arriving late, sounds on the quay hard to place, unable to tell reliably which sound belongs to the room.
- Kest's **fish market notice** and the *Vigil* officer's **paper** forbidding the Free Keel to approach, sound, work, or lie within **four hundred yards of the Gannet Shoal outer marks**, with the harbor authority's instruments over that ground stated to have lapsed.

### The plate's three repair histories, in order

1. A **keyed keel seam** down the long side with a good grey compound of mica, pine resin, and ash. Calder states as his own testimony that this is a **Morrow Quay yard mixture** used by his father, his grandfather, and his great-grandfather, and possible in only four harbors on this coast. It is the only fact in the examination that ties the plate to a *place* rather than a *date*.
2. A **patch in the upper quarter** in iron from a mill that closed its furnace two generations before the Serein Accord. This kills the short chain Calder wanted: the plate was already broken and being repaired long before the Gannet. Mara gave this as a **ceiling, not a date**, and that wording is in the record.
3. A **Crown-shop bolted flange** over a cut aperture in the lower corner, with stamped studs on a jig spacing, and **darker, older glass under the lid** that has not gone the way the rest of the plate has. Mara calls the aperture a **chamber** with something settled in it and refuses to let the lid be lifted in a shed.

### The nine-line mark

The **custody mark of nine joined lines** is on the **underside of the plate**, clear of fouling, standing about two inches proud, cut with a tool rather than a mould, made of harder grey glass. It predates every Registry mark. Mara's reading, which is binding: it is a **builder's mark, not a claim seal, not a cargo seal, and not a mark of consent**; it describes a thing **held together from more than one place** rather than a thing owned; the plate's own construction is **older than the Serein Accord**; she will not name a builder, and she has asked and been told no and believed them. The **Nine Harbors are not named anywhere in the batch and must not be named in Volume 1.**

### The shard's provenance, kept separate

- The **shard's ground band**, a band about a thumb wide ground flat the whole length of its lower cut face, with the worked lines running down into it and stopping dead at its edge, and curved rather than straight because the thing it was cut from was round. Mara measured its width and its curve by hand in **Chapter 2** and entered it as *ground* with *purpose not stated* under it, and it went into the Chapter 8 public duplicate in the same terms. **Chapter 9** identifies it as the flange allowance of a Crown yard case specification. It is a Crown-shop working practice on the **shard** and has nothing to do with the **plate's** bolted flange and sealed aperture; the two must never be described in one clause.
- The shard's **cut edges and flange form match a public Crown yard specification for mnemonic case plating** filed with a refit account for another vessel. This is a *compatibility, not an identification*. Any yard that had the pattern could have cut a case.
- The case's **own dimensions are not known and cannot be known from public papers.** A bellwright can give a **length and breadth as a range within a foot either way**, from the plate thickness and the flange diameter plus the two-hundred-year-old workshop rule about flange allowance. It is recorded as a range, not a number.
- The shard's **origin ground** is placed on the same bearing as the *Vigil*'s recorded mooring, by the three bearings and the public mooring line. The plate's builder's mark tells the crew nothing about the shard, and the shard's cut edges tell the crew nothing about the plate. Every document, drawing, and measurement keeps them as two objects with two provenances.
- The **ferry's compartment is not proved.** The survey's ninth page gives number four as eight feet four by six feet nine clear and eleven feet two deep; the case range is between seven and eight feet in its longest face; the manifest says the hold stood empty. The two numbers are now in the same room for the first time and the contradiction is **available to anybody who holds all four documents**, but nothing has been measured at the site and the site is past the harbor line. **The physical measurement that proves it belongs to the Volume 1 midpoint.**
- The **public pressure log's three-second gap** is now read by Calder and Mara together: a hand was elsewhere for three seconds and then returned, and the mark after the gap is heavier. It proves nothing about motive and is not a lie, because nobody composed it.

### The first echo, as written

- **Whose witness:** a *Vigil* rating who helped carry the sealed case, on the near end, because he was the smallest and could not be seen from the rail. He is **never named, never located, never interviewed, and never proved to be a *Gannet* casualty.** The gloved hand with the caulking-iron callus is something the witness *saw*, not his own body.
- **What the scene shows:** a case crossing a loading space, a man with a paper reading numbers and a phrase containing *standing empty* twice and turning the paper over while the case passes, shouted words from behind and above that come apart in the middle, a tagged keel-line at deck level, a gloved hand closing on a fitting and going out of frame before anything is shown to be cut.
- **What it does not show:** the case's mark, the case's dimensions, the space's dimensions, where the case came from or went, the far side of the deck, whether any other record corrects the spoken count, and who acted later.
- **The frame tears** into a doubled, hard-rimmed edge with a hard bright rim and something moving behind it. Calder stops pushing and does not enter it. Mara calls this a **possible seam** and writes the consequence: a seam on a loose fragment is a nuisance, a seam on a panel still joined to a keel-line is a splinter that travels to the next panel without asking it. Her handling note: held separated, no keel-line contact without a written plan, no bell entry without a conservator present and a stated reason.

### Bodily state after Chapter 6

- **Tinnitus**, continuous, with a **second sound under it that arrives late** — the recorded loading bell of the *Gannet*'s loading space. Calder's ordinary harbor bells now overlap it, and on a test he turned toward the wrong bell. He cannot reliably tell which sound belongs to the room.
- **A rope-burn reenactment** on the right forearm, four inches, fresh, in the shape of a line over a hand, beginning the moment the hatch opened rather than in order.
- **Emotional aftertaste** strong enough that he answered Nessa in **Oren's call mark** — a low short call with a lift at the end, three marks and a flick — without deciding to, on a rotten platform in the small hours of the same night as the entry, and did not know the words in it were *I'll take the near end, I'm smallest*.
- **No second entry** is possible while he carries this one, and no entry at all until **two clean daylight looks** have happened. He is off the water for **twenty-four hours minimum** on Nessa's measurement, pending the burn closing.
- He is **not a faultreader**, has never claimed to read seams, and cannot judge whether any part of what he saw is true.
- **The hands do not shake, and that is deliberate.** `bible/power-system.md` lists "Calder's hands shake for two days" among the costs of a first entry. The prose inverts it: his hand did not shake when he drank the water in Chapter 6, and his hands were "perfectly steady" in Chapter 7. The cost is not in his hands; it is in his ears, on his forearm, and in his brother's voice coming out of his mouth. **A later volume must not pay the shake as a debt that was owed** — if his hands ever do shake, it has to come from something else (cold, air, a rope, a held panel, plain fear) and be read as that. This deviation is flagged for ratification in `state/open-threads.md`, because a prose phase may not edit the bible.

### The named-tender cutoff, as a physical method

A wire runs from the diver's harness up inside the hose to the tender's hand, made fast to a cleat on the working deck with a good long turn and a half hitch. It means nothing until the tender puts her weight on it and goes over the fall; then the clip comes off the shoulder and the diver is a man on a line with a man on the surface. The tender's word is **"Enough,"** said **once** and not twice, because twice is a conversation a man can join. **Nessa Pike was the named tender, held the line, and called it**, and Calder surfaced with seventy minutes of air unspent. A slate signed at the platform in Chapter 5 and again in Chapter 7 records that the tender's decision stands without discussion while the diver is attached, and that the diver is right about the going down and the tender is right about the going up. **This is the same procedure used in Volumes 14 and 15.**

### Caleb's revealed private object

**Oren's brass rigger's hand bell**, a hand's-breadth thing with a hard brown handle gone shiny, a filed-down lip where somebody once hit it wrong, and a plain iron collar soldered into the shoulder. Calder has carried it since the day after he was told and has never told a human being in Morrow Quay that he has it. He told Mara in front of the crew, in Chapter 5, and let her write down that the association is *not established*. It is the only personal object he has shown anyone. **It is not a power, a charm, or a retrieval device, and it must never be shown to work.**

### Legal and economic state

- **The shoal work permit is spent.** Two dates: the Chapter 4 dive and the Chapter 5 bench day. The two paid working days are gone and neither is refundable. The yard earned nothing on the bench day.
- **A twelve-day disputed-claim defense**, entered by Kest in the harbor authority's hearing room, holding for twelve days, lapsing on the thirteenth unless a sitting is held.
- The **naval salvage claim** over the whole Gannet Shoal ground was lodged at ten-forty on the day of Chapter 9 and reaches past the harbor line, which a naval claim is not bounded by. A *Vigil* tender set floats on the bearing of the pale run.
- The **air pot is a third gone**, the chandler's note is eleven weeks into six, the pump housing cost is absorbed, the new clamp is lost overboard, the doubled hose is scrap, the mole anchor's float line is Kest's, and the *Kittiwake* wants a cradle before the winter gales.

### Authority and honors as written

- **Magister** enters the prose for the first time in Chapter 8, in the seal line of the Registry seizure order: *Magister Ilya Serr, senior Registry examiner*. Calder has never met him, reads the word as part of a form, and learns nothing about him. Mara says she has read his examinations for three years and that he is very careful, and explicitly declines to put even that in a record. **Ilya is not called minister anywhere in Volume 1, and the Office of Returning Memory is not named as such in Chapters 1–10.**

### The place named at the end of the batch

**Orison** is named once, by Tobias Wren, as what lies west-southwest of the bearing at **seventy fathoms** down (`bible/world.md`, `bible/terminology.md`), with the flat statement that nobody on this coast goes there and that every old fool on the quay has a story about it and not one of them is any good. There is **no map, no route, and no purpose**, and the batch establishes only a broad physical direction.

## Fixed distances and times in Batch 0001, binding on Chapters 11 onward

These are the numbers the prose asserts. A later chapter may add detail inside them and may not contradict them.

- **The drifted mole anchor** is bedged in the mole's toe off the third seam at **about eight feet** of water, a fathom and a little over, tagged with a lead line, a bone toggle, and a rag float carrying Kest's mark. It was *about eight feet* when Calder found it, *under eight feet and going to nine* as the flood took the mole, and eight feet on the day he reported it to Sera Mott. It is a two-fathom storm anchor **in size**, which is a different number from its depth and must never be written as a depth.
- **The freed shoal iron** lies with the *Alder Reach*'s frames at **eight fathoms** on the shoal's inner edge, and the hole off her quarter runs to **twenty-two fathoms** with no bottom in it that a lead will find. The wreck's shoulder behind the frames is **eleven fathoms**.
- **The Chapter 4 dive, to the minute:** the bell went over at **5:10**, slack water was at **5:20** and the set was given as **6:15** (fifty-five minutes), the first clamp went on at **5:25**, the silt curtain stood at **6:00** for four minutes, the second clamp came off at **6:05**, the current made at **6:15**, and Calder was up at **6:20** — five minutes past what Nessa had given him, which is why she says so.
- **From the mole, seaward:** the *Vigil*'s recorded mooring is three-quarters of a mile out from the shoal; the Gannet Shoal **outer marks are a mile and a quarter off the mole**; the officer's four-hundred-yard limit therefore begins about **a mile and a half** off the mole; and the pale run lies **about two and a half miles out** on the same bearing, so it runs through water the Free Keel may not enter at all.
- **The *Vigil* tender's own position** is **a quarter of a mile off the mole**, on the bearing of the pale run, which is where a line of marks has to begin if it is to run out and meet the outer marks at all (Ch10, restated in Ch16 and Ch20). She is therefore inside the water the Free Keel may work, and no chapter may put her at the mile-and-a-half figure, which is the near edge of the officer's limit and not a boat.
- **The batch's calendar:** Chapter 1 is a market day on a Tuesday, the 11th. Chapters 2–4 are Wednesday the 12th (the claim in the morning, the permit in the afternoon, the dive in the late afternoon), Chapter 5 is Thursday the 13th (bench day, rig built, permit expired at dark), Chapters 6–8 run from 11:30 p.m. Thursday to about 6:20 a.m. Friday the 14th, and Chapters 9–10 are Friday the 14th, which is the date Calder writes in the crew's book. Everything said about *last night*, *yesterday*, or *two days* in the batch resolves against that calendar, and the next Monday is the 17th.
- **Tobias Wren's account of the old Veyr bell entries is thirty-one years old** and is secondhand. There is no second date for it anywhere in the batch.

## Deliberate soft points, to be settled when the volume that needs them is outlined

These are known seams in the long plan. They are not errors, and no batch should close them by improvisation.

1. **Captain Holt after Volume 3.** She loses her ship and command in Volume 3, yet she is the officer who pressures Kest into altering the harbor authority's custody copy of the Gannet salvage report in Volume 9. Her posting after her defeat, and the channel by which a disgraced officer reaches a Morrow Quay reeve, must be fixed when Volume 9 is planned.
2. **The first echo's witness.** The rating who settled a witness into the case glass is deliberately unnamed and unlocated. Whether he can ever be found, and what finding him would cost, is an author-level decision reserved for the volume that first needs him.
3. **The abandoned Oren lead in Volume 5.** Calder saves a living Lowfen bell archive and gives up a lead on Oren to do it. The lead's specific content, and whether it ever resurfaces, is not yet fixed.
4. **Lira Venn's function.** She is defined in the bible and must be used before her later role in duplicating public records matters, but the volume that gives her that work is not chosen. She cannot be invented into a plot after the fact.

## Batch 0001 costs the volume may not waive again

These are now debts on the page, not plans. Chapters 11 onward must be able to point at them.

- **Money:** a third of the air pot spent, a cracked pump housing absorbed, a replacement clamp lost overboard, a doubled hose run once and then scrap, a forty-mark permit bond, forty marks for a certificate of a public record's figures, eleven weeks into six on the chandler's note and rising, and a *Kittiwake* cradle not yet bought.
- **Body:** a rope-burn reenactment on the right forearm, tinnitus with a second bell a third of a second late, hearing confusion, and a twenty-four-hour minimum off the water.
- **Time:** two paid working days spent, one on the water and one at a bench that earned nothing, and the permit's two dates consumed with nothing turned into salvage.
- **Standing:** both pieces of glass surrendered, the crew's wages insecure, Calder's shoal permit gone, and a naval claim sitting on the only ground where the compartment could be measured.
- **Authority:** Calder gave Nessa the standing shutdown and gave Bevin the logbook job, in front of the crew, on the record.

## Rules for recording canon changes

Any proposed change to a world rule, a character's locked motivation, the mystery chain, the ending, or the no-System rule is written into `state/open-threads.md` as an author-level thread with a flag, and is never applied silently in a chapter.

Two items were resolved silently in the drafting of Batch 0001 and are recorded here rather than treated as new canon:

1. **The anchor for the first echo entry.** `outline/batches/volume-01-batch-0001.md` requires a physical anchor and no Gannet-associated object existed in the cast. The prose supplies Oren's brass rigger's hand bell, a private keepsake from before the ferry, and the prose has Mara record the association to the site as **not established** and Calder state that he does not know where the bell was at the time of the scene. This is a personal object, not a world rule, and it is the first thing Calder has ever told anybody he owns. No rule about anchors is changed.
2. **The working-day labels.** The batch card's day labels do not fit a calendar in which Kest sends Calder to the office the following morning. The prose runs market day, then a first full working day, then a second working day, then the night, then the following day. No card is contradicted in substance.

No other rule, motivation, chain, or ending element was changed. The no-System rule held without exception.

## Batch 0002 as written (Chapters 11–20) — binding on Chapters 21 onward

### Calendar
- Sat 15th (Ch11), Sun 16th gale (Ch12), Mon 17th (Ch13), Tue 18th (Ch14), Wed 19th (Ch15), Thu 20th (Ch16), Fri 21st (Ch17), Sat 22nd (Ch18), Mon 24th sitting (Ch19), Tue 25th (Ch20). Sun 23rd unwritten rest day.
- **The harbor's lift of the drifted mole anchor is on Thursday the 20th**, as Kest orders it on Tuesday the 18th, and Chapter 16 is that afternoon from the fish dock. It is not Wednesday, and no chapter may place the lift on the 19th.

### Legal and money
- Twelve-day defense entered 14th extended 24th into **twenty days further defense** on new terms: twelve marks paid, yard gear (pump, clamp stock, cordage, drawing chest) held to a clerk's assessed **eighteen**, harbor eighth off any future raising, duplicate stays public, four-hundred-yard bar restated, echo work requires two clean daylight looks plus conservator. **The term runs to the 14th of next month** and lapses then unless a further sitting is held. The bearing sheet is admitted **as a drawing, not as a finding of where the *Vigil* lay**, and may only be spoken by the men who took each bearing.
- **The Crown's note of the yard's connection is on the sitting record**: the *Vigil*'s officer put it that the harbor authority was being asked to found an instrument on figures taken by the yard that signed the *Aster Gannet* salvage report thirteen years ago. Kest took the labor chain as proved on the strength of his own countersignature and the harbor's paid tender, did not adopt the connection, and wrote the officer's question into the record as the officer's question and not as a finding. Calder did not defend his father's report in that room. **This is the Movement 2 family beat and it is on the page; it does not repeat in a later volume.**
- Rival claim: *Low Lantern* (Sella Brigg) holds a harbor-entered third of freed shoal-iron sale via borrowed strop; iron stays unsold on stack with saw kerf intact, valued at twenty marks at a fair sale and thirteen to the crew after Sella's third (Ch19).
- Ordinary harbor work always under the Morrow Quay charter inside the line; claim window spent; work permit two dates spent; one-season charter still distant and defined in Ch14 as wider seasonal permission granted after hearing.
- **The chandler's note stands at eleven weeks into six all batch and is never re-counted.** Four marks came off it on the 17th and two more on the 20th, so it is six marks lighter than it was on the 15th and not one week shorter. Chapter 10's line that it "will be fourteen by the end of this month" still governs the rate.
- **The air pot is not rebuilt and is not partly rebuilt.** It was a third gone on the 15th and is a third gone on the 25th, less a finger's width, because a third of the pot is a hundred marks' worth (Ch3) and the whole batch bought fifteen marks of air. There is **no margin in the pot**: a third of it does one morning of somebody else's hull. The "partly rebuilt" claim that stood in this file and in `state/current.md` after the first draft of the batch is **withdrawn** as an accounting error.
- **The batch's money chain, which later chapters must not break:**
  - after Ch12: the *Merrit* job paid 11 of 15 booked, short because the patch was not driven.
  - Ch13, night of the 17th: 4 to the chandler, **7 kept, for air**. Nothing in hand after that.
  - Ch16 (20th): buoy job, 26 on the master's count, 8 out in rope and food (the tallow came off Kest's order on the reeve's account), **18 split as 4 air, 2 chandler, 12 into the tin**. Nothing in hand.
  - Ch18 (22nd): tide job on the inner mud, 24 on the board, less the harbor's charter share 3, harbor pump hire 3, rope and food 2, **16 in the bag**; 12 of it to the printer, **4 to air**, and **the tin untouched at 12**. This is the beat that makes the printing and the bond both fundable; the crew had no other money and the tin was never opened for paper.
  - Ch19 (24th): **the 12 comes out of the tin onto the table and the tin is empty from that night on.**
  - Ch20 (25th): nothing in the bag, nothing in the tin, fifteen marks of air bought across the batch, the pump and gear in pawn until the 14th, so any job that needs a pump must hire the harbor's at three a tide. Mara's forty marks stands first after food, the note second.
- Ten crew-only printed copies exist without Mara's name; bearing sheet and plate drawing withheld from print and kept in the drawing chest.
- *Kittiwake* November slip tides given to Free Keel; cradle deferred to spring; boat beached on the hard with her bilge blocked in and no cradle under her. Harbor lifted the drifted mole anchor on Thursday the 20th at its own cost with crane, chain, and slip's crew; Free Keel never touched it and Calder watched from the fish dock rail, not the mole.

### Body and power
- Calder's burn reopened (two inches, barnacle strike) and ungloved; hearing misplaces bells operationally; crew compensates by call-backs (Bevin repeats numbers and the true bearing back, and says them at the ladder whether or not they match his own count). Nessa's shutdown exercised in Ch12 surge dive with work undone; Calder obeyed. No echo entry in batch; power state unchanged (line diver, unstable tide-ear, no legal grounds).
- Calder is **off the bottom for the rest of the volume unless a sitting lifts it**; Bevin is the bottom man inside the line under Nessa, and Calder tends. Stated in Ch20 and in force from Ch20.
- Mara refused bearing-sheet certification and custody-room line and was right; refusals stand. Her Registry query costs her the standing that lets her refuse: while it stands every entry she makes goes to a circuit officer to be countersigned, so any certificate she issues to any crew takes three weeks instead of one. Alden refused work-witnessing for roof reasons, costing the scrub job. Kest in Ch14 barred mole attendance and gave pump packing on reeve's account in the same scene.

### Cast added by Batch 0002
- **Sella Brigg**, about fifty, runs the *Low Lantern*, a two-boat Free Keel crew, keeps her own slate, and holds a harbor-entered third of the freed shoal iron's sale. She presses nothing at a forced price and stands rescue cover last and loudest. She is not an antagonist and holds no institution.
- **Harl Dace** of the *Grey Widow*, broad, with a burned ear, asks the Free Keel whether crews will stand cover for a yard the Registry has touched, and is the reason the cover is scoped to inside the line. He takes Calder's slip paper into his coat at Ch15 and is entitled to inside-the-line rescue cover from that night.
- Both are Free Keel crews in an association that already existed, and neither introduces an institution, a rule, or a rival claim beyond Sella's strop share, which is on Sella's own paper and entered with the harbor clerk.

## Batch 0002 review repairs (recorded, not hidden)

The review of Batch 0002 returned FIX. Seven canon/continuity faults and eight prose or clarity faults were repaired in the chapters and the state without restarting the batch and without changing the plot. The items a later batch would otherwise have inherited wrong:

- **A fourth-wall reference in the prose.** Chapter 11 referred to "the iron nobody had wanted in Chapter 8." It now reads "since the day it came up out of the mud."
- **The mole lift's date.** Kest ordered the harbor's lift for "tomorrow" on Tuesday the 18th, which would have put it on the 19th while Chapter 16 depicts it on the 20th. It is now **Thursday's slack water**, and the 20th is locked above.
- **The bond tin did not reconcile.** The first draft paid twelve marks for the printing out of money already committed to air and the chandler, and then claimed twelve in hand against a nine-mark tin with no income on the page. The batch now earns the printing and the bond in Chapter 18, and the full chain is written out above. Nothing else in Chapters 11–20 spends a mark.
- **The chandler's note gained a week in two days.** It is **eleven weeks into six** throughout, exactly as Chapter 10 left it, with six marks paid off it across the batch. The "twelve weeks" figure that had propagated into this file, `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, and the Batch 0003 prompt is corrected in all five.
- **The *Vigil*'s tender's distance.** Chapter 20 put her a mile and a half out, which is where the officer's four-hundred-yard limit begins, and contradicted Chapters 10 and 16. She is **a quarter of a mile off the mole** on the pale bearing in all three, holding the near end of the marked line.
- **The air pot was described as partly rebuilt**, which no arithmetic in the batch supports. It is a third gone on the 15th and a third gone on the 25th, and the margin is gone rather than restored.
- **The Movement 2 family beat was in the wrong room.** The hearing in Chapter 19 now carries it: the naval officer puts the yard's name on the board, Calder refuses to defend his father's report and answers with the harbor's own countersigned log, and the officer's note goes into the record.
- **The "Free Keel pledge" was renamed.** The crews' mutual pledge is the **Free Keel charter** per `bible/terminology.md` and `bible/world.md`, and Chapters 11 and 15 now use the canonical term. No prose phase may invent a second name for a bible instrument.
- **Chapter 16's payment terms** no longer book a two-day job and pay it in full at noon on the first day; the buoy is a one-day job paid on the buoy master's count and the clerk's slip, and the cheering crew at the mole lift is the harbor's own, with Calder off the wall.
- **The batch's four thinnest chapters were given their scenes back** (16, 18, 19, 20), which raised the batch from 8,002 words to 12,864. The honest per-chapter accounting, the reason, and what was not fixed are in `state/current.md`.


## Batch 0003 as written (Chapters 21–30) — binding on Chapters 31 onward

### Calendar
- Wed 26th (Ch21), Thu 27th (Ch22), Fri 28th (Ch23), Sat 29th (Ch24), Sun 30th (Ch25), Mon 31st (Ch26), Tue the 1st (Ch27), Wed the 2nd (Ch28), Thu the 3rd (Ch29), Fri the 4th (Ch30). Ten consecutive days, no rest day.
- **The months are locked.** The 12th to the 31st are in a **31-day month, October**, and the 1st to the 7th are in **November**, which is the current month for Chapters 27–30. Proof on the page: Chapter 30 says the defense of the **twenty-fourth of last month** is still running and that the margin formula was on the fourteenth of last month, and Chapter 21 has the pump coming back "on the fourteenth of next month" when spoken in October. **Every present-tense reference to the term's end is "the 14th," never "the 14th of next month."**
- **The autumn equinox in this calendar falls on about the twentieth of October** (Chapter 14 on the 18th: the iron "may not fetch before the equinox"; Chapter 16 on the 20th: the bell buoy "before the equinox tides"). **The 3rd of November is therefore a fortnight past it**, which is what Chapter 29 says.
- **The harbor's tide rule, locked from three chapters.** At the top of the flood the water goes slack and to a sheet of glare and the bottom shows — Chapter 23 at eleven feet, Chapter 28 from the mole head, Chapter 29 at the fifth seam. **The slop is visible at the top of the tide, not on a falling one.** The harbor's tide bell goes at the top of the water, so "the bell went" and "top of the make" and "top of the flood" are the same state. No later chapter may put a look at the bottom of this harbor on a falling tide.
- **The hearing is on the seventh of this month.** Kest moved it from the tenth in Chapter 23 because the office is busy on the tenth, so **intervals spoken before the 28th count to the tenth** (Ch21 fifteen days, Ch22 fourteen) and **every interval from the 28th counts to the seventh** (Ch27 six days on the 1st, four on the 3rd, three on the 4th). The defense lapses on the **14th of this month**, which is fifteen days from the 30th and sixteen from the 29th. The reeve's six-clause instrument is dated the **8th** and is void on its face.
- The *Vigil* tender's floats went out on the 14th of last month and she has been on station **twenty days** as of the 4th, a quarter of a mile off the mole on the pale bearing. Never put her at the mile-and-a-half figure.

### The batch's money chain, which later chapters must not break
- Start on the 26th: nothing in the bag, nothing in the tin, air pot a third gone, note eleven weeks into six with six marks paid across Batch 0002 and nothing forgiven.
- **26th–27th: nothing in, nothing out.** No job was possible, because air must be bought before a job starts and there is no margin in the pot.
- **28th:** Sella Brigg advances **six marks** of air on her own slate against the crew's share; the chandler's takes the six. Harbor pays **eight** for the mole's third and fourth seams. Out: two in rope and food, six back to Brigg. Bag **0**. Air bought: 6.
- **29th:** mole's ninth seam, **26 on the board, 18 paid** because the work was not completed and the shackle was under the authority's own masonry. Out: charter's share 3, harbor's pump 3, rope and food 2, air 1. Bag **9**. Air bought: 1.
- **30th:** bonesetter **1**, surgeon's written account **1**. Bag **7**.
- **1st:** air **2**. Bag **5**.
- **4th:** five marks on the bench, every one of them spoken for. Bag **5**, tin **0**.
- **Total air bought this batch: nine marks. Across the yard's life: twenty-three.** The pot is a third gone at both ends and ten marks has not put a mark back into it. **Nothing was paid on the chandler's note in this batch. Nothing was paid on the certificate, which is forty marks and first after food.**
- The stove in the *Low Lantern's* second boat is **Sella Brigg's own cost** and is entered in the yard's book as hers, so that it cannot quietly be carried as the yard's.
- The harbor's pump is still hired at **three a tide**; the yard's own remains in pawn to the 14th with a cracked housing and no replacement bought.
- The *Kittiwake* is still on the hard, bilge blocked in, no cradle, and Tobias got two feet of sound oak baulk out of the mole's inner face on the 3rd and left the rest.

### Documents and instruments created in this batch
1. **The yard's balance sheet of the 26th** — eleven debts in one line, read by Nessa and capped by her. Recurs in fuller form on the 4th.
2. **The reeve's four lists** (Ch21) — permitted acts, permitted work, the bar, and two notices to the reeve himself, in Kest's own hand, in a hearing voice.
3. **The harbor authority's shoal book**, in two parts, read in the barge with the clerk present, **not copied**, and the only figure given to the yard is station six of the spring course. The book has a **dated gap** and two reeve's-hand lines in it.
4. **The written purpose filed on the 27th** and read into the hearing record, asserting the ground is **placed and not scattered**. Its load-bearing sentence is **the crew had no measurement of the assertion it is making**, and it is that sentence, not the last one, that the circuit office leaves out of the notice of the 31st; the purpose's closing sentence is the one stating why the file of thirteen years ago is wanted. **It stands uncorrected in the record. The yard struck the word *placed* out of its own copy on the 2nd and left the record alone.**
5. **The harbor authority's 13-year-old wreck entry for the *Aster Gannet*** — read in the barge, not copied, figures written fair by Calder and read back by Bevin; the material line is *number four, port side aft, grating in place, not cleared, contents not removed*; the entries are marked *as surveyed*; and **no figure of that space as found exists in any file in this harbor**, which Kest states from his own search on the 22nd of last month.
6. **The notice of the 31st** — the Morrow circuit office's four clauses, quoting the conservator's note of the 14th in the circuit's own type, sealed to the market wall, served by Kest at eleven on the mole with his own enforcement added. **Clause one** closes the mole's toe and inner face from the third to the eleventh seam to all persons on any work. **Clause two** keeps recovered transparent mineral out of every shed in the harbor. **Clause three** stops any person who handled it from going in the water inside the harbor on the authority's work without a plan countersigned by a circuit officer. **Clause four** is the contagion argument, and it quotes the yard's assertion out of the purpose **while leaving out the sentence in which the yard said it had no measurement of that assertion.** The note it quotes is the conservator's note of **the 14th of last month**, written on a bench about one loose fragment; it is not a note of April.
7. **The *Vigil* captain's one-page request** of the 31st, brought in by the cutter's boat at seven, asking for nothing, because she has no authority in this harbor. Captain Veyra Holt is **named on paper and nowhere else in this batch** and has no conversation with Calder.
8. **The yard's own board copy of the notice** (Ch26), in Bevin Rook's right hand, with Calder's line at the foot distinguishing a copy for working hands from a publication.
9. **The conservator's second note** (Ch27), four paragraphs, lodged for countersign and **not yet dated by the office**; the office date entered in the yard's book is **the 22nd of this month**.
10. **Kest's station note of the 28th** — *for the purposes of a bearing only, taken from the top of the mole, by any person, at any hour, off the office's own compass, the office having no objection to a station on its own ground that does not approach his own ground.* Signed *O. Kest*. **It is not a favor and Calder could not turn it into one.**
11. **Kest's six-clause instrument of the 3rd** for a supervised look at the mole's inner face, fifth seam, eleven feet, dated the 8th, held open to a later date without a new purpose and without a new bond. **Void on its face** pending a circuit officer's countersign.
12. **The reeve's notation on the harbor chart** (Ch30) — *ground claimed by the harbor authority, pending examination, no other claim may be entered while the defense runs, the claim is the authority's, it is not the Free Keel yard's, and the yard has asked for nothing, has been given nothing, and has declined to ask.* **Priced at the timber fenders, four sets of moorings and two herring berths on the third-to-eleventh seams of the inner face, all out of service from March.**
13. **The three sheets of the 2nd** and the **fifth-seam sheet of the 4th**, all on the market wall, all in the crew's own hands, without a conservator's name and without the reeve's name, **with no line drawn between any two points by the reeve's ruling**. The fifth-seam sheet states the look was made **at the top of the tide on the 3rd, in the presence of the reeve and three witnesses**, and offers no figure of any kind.
14. **Ten written terms for the sitting of the 7th**, written on the 4th, sealed against addition after the 6th, signed by Calder left-handed and by Bevin Rook right-handed and countersigned by Nessa Pike.

### The three points, as the numbers stand
- **First point.** The mole's toe, third and fourth seams, **nine feet at the south end and ten and a half at the north end**, on the 28th, the drifted mole anchor on the same ground lying in about eight feet: a bedded pale seam of material in the mole's own foundation running **north** under the rubble to two boat lengths, three keys seen, deeper at the north end, seen at the ladder by Bevin Rook and entered as the **tender's** count by Calder, with the harbor's tide-gang present and two of its men at the water's edge. **Kest entered the toe, the depth and his own lead's figure and refused to enter *pale material*, *bedded*, or *bedded in the mole's foundation*.**
- **Second point.** Gannet Shoal, inner edge, **eight fathoms**, on the permit of the 12th of last month: a transparent keel rib with a continuous keel line and eleven keys, running west-southwest on a dead straight, no break and no join. Bearing by three men agreeing **inside four degrees**, hour and man's name against each. The bearing sheet is **a drawing and is not and may not be used as a finding of where the *Vigil* lay**, and Term Three of the seventh withdraws the yard's own filed suggestion that the sheet and the mooring line be laid together.
- **Third point.** **Station six of the harbor authority's own annual course over the outer ground, taken in the spring: fifteen fathoms and two corrected, the wire having been paid out to eighteen fathoms and two**, against neighbours at **sixteen fathoms and three** and a firm-clay flat, in one figure out of **thirty-six on two miles of course**. At about **two miles and a quarter from the mole**, inside the officer's water, inside the pale run's known extent of about two and a half miles. The office's correction column is the only reason the figure has ever been legible: an uncorrected line puts the station within two fathoms of its neighbours.
- **Interval.** First to second, **a mile and two hundred yards**. Second to third, **a mile and two hundred and ten yards**. **Difference: ten yards.** The figures are a tender's count and an office's corrected count and are not the same kind of number, and the tolerance is stated by the men who took them and is **not better than ten yards**.
- **What it shows:** three places on one straight where the bottom comes up, and nowhere else on two miles of a sounded course. **What it does not show:** what is under sixteen fathoms of water at two miles and a quarter; whether the three things are one thing, three things, or anything at all; any purpose, any route, any map. No line is drawn between them by the reeve's order, so any reader must take a ruler out of their own pocket and do it themselves.

### The panel at the fifth seam
- The mole's inner face, **fifth seam, forty yards south of the third, eleven feet**. Four foot by some part of four of transparent material in plates, **bedded in the mole's own foundation stone under three feet of the mole's own stone, lying in the rubble and not dropped or slid**; three keys along the upper edge; **a Crown-shop bolted rim in the lower corner, studs in a jig spacing, darker glass inside the rim** — the same description of a rim as the plate in Registry custody, and **no chapter has said so and none may**.
- Seen on the 3rd from the steps inside the mole wall **at the top of the tide**, in the slop's glare, by **Kest and three of the yard — Marr, Pike, Rook** — with Tobias Wren above them on the steps, and the entry's witness line names those three. **Four men saw it; three are named as witnesses, and the book says three.** Not measured, not touched, not brought up. No figure is offered for it and the yard will not offer one.
- **Kest served a notice closing that face on the 31st and went down onto it on the 3rd anyway, said so out loud, entered it in his own book with the three yard names as witnesses, and stated that he knows exactly where that puts him and did it on purpose.** He drafted the six-clause instrument on the 31st, naming the fifth seam and eleven feet because that is the one piece of the face a man's hand can reach off a step, and kept it back until he had something to point it at, **because showing a yard that paper on the 31st would have sent them out at night to look for whatever was under it.** He records that the office is not required to send a thing it has merely seen to the circuit office and is not sending it today.
- **The panel is foregone and stays foregone.** The lawful route requires the plan countersigned by a circuit officer of the office that has just argued the ground is dangerous; the only diver the yard has is barred by the same clause; and Calder has entered in his own book that he thought about going alone at two in the morning with a hired punt and a line on the rim and did not, with three reasons, the second of which is that a figure he took alone in the dark makes every other figure in the record a figure somebody must take on trust from him.

### Documents and custody unchanged
- Shard and weathered hull plate remain in the Morrow circuit custody room as **two separately entered items under two numbers, digits still not quoted in prose**. **Neither returns in this batch and neither is referred to except as seized material.**
- The unverified memory note stays on its own shelf with its own heading and its one false line, **was not added to in this batch**, and is expressly kept apart from the three points because a man's own account of what he heard is a different kind of document from a figure four people can check with a lead.
- The anchor-bearing sheet, plate drawing, labor log, measurements, three repair-seam records, crew's book and public duplicate are unchanged and all still held. **The ten crew-only printed copies are one object with one count, from Chapter 18: two on the market wall beside the original, three on other crews' walls, five rolled in oilcloth in the bench chest.** The **bench chest** holding the sheet, the drawing and the five rolled copies is pledged to the reeve against the bond and comes back on the 14th.
- **The nine-line mark is not mentioned once in this batch** and remains a builder's mark of pre-Dominion construction describing a thing held together from more than one place. Not named, not interpreted, not spent.
- **The compartment is still not proved.** What the batch added is the fourth document and the **reason nobody measured**: the standing space was never cleared on any record in this harbor, its emptying was another party's operation, and the harbor authority was not present and did not ask to be.

### People
- **Bevin Rook, 22.** **Left** hand crushed in a chain bight, fourth and third, set by the fish-dock bonesetter and re-bound higher by Alden Marr with wood across the heel of the palm. **The left arm is the one in the sling and the right hand is the one that writes.** Cannot close, cannot grip, cannot hold a line or a tender's cleat, cannot hold a compass steady, cannot write with the left. Off the bottom and off the cleat. Wages nil for about six weeks, four a week, **twenty-four marks the yard does not have**. Now reads the board, writes the crew's book, reads every entry back, and signed the seventh's terms with his right hand. He has said once, and asked to stop saying it daily, that a note dated the twenty-second is for the next man, so the next six days are theirs.
- **Nessa Pike, 27.** Holds the tender's cleat, the pump, the fall, the board and the slate. Refused the mole's inner face on the 23rd on water grounds and Calder's conclusion on the 2nd on record-keeping grounds, both times practically and both times right. Made the yard worse on purpose, in writing, in front of the boy, so that nobody could say afterward they pretended. Named the four jobs inside the line and gave each of them to a person in front of the boy who had just lost one of them. **Wants Rook, who cannot close a hand, back in the water rested, and has said she will not be arguing about it in April.**
- **Ors Kest, 44.** **Thirty-one, and two years in the office, in the year after the Gannet Cut — fifteen years in it now, and forty-four years old.** Wrote the four lists, the station note, the enforcement paragraph the notice does not contain, the six-clause instrument, the chart notation, and a reeve's own chart claim that costs the town its fenders and two herring berths. **Broke his own served notice once, on his own ground, on purpose, and named the three yard witnesses in his own book**, and said out loud that the book would say so before he had seen it. Gave Calder three things the yard had not had and refused the fourth: he will **not** enter *placed* into a record, and he will **not** lift the bottom restriction without a hearing, and he has already told Calder the answer at the seventh will be yes and that he does not enjoy having thought about it.
- **Mara Venn, 30.** Quoted by her own office at length, correctly, and has said so herself first. The note it quotes is hers of **the 14th of last month**, written on a bench about one loose fragment. Spent roughly a quarter of the standing that lets her refuse; will be spent by Christmas. Lodged a second note on the 1st and will not witness position, will not write *safe*, and will not write *unsafe*. **Did not break, did not resign, did not become an advocate, and said no three times in ten days.** Her forty marks are still owed and still first after food.
- **Alden Marr, 61.** Set a hand, said the cold arithmetic about a boy and a boat — six weeks of wages, twenty-four marks, and a defense with **fifteen days** to run — noticed that his son asked him nothing and said so, and told one fragment about a man off the *Kittiwake* in 1961. **No confession, no report talk, nothing about the ferry, and the plate note untouched.** The chisel thread is closed on the page: entered as *not chased*, with the reason.
- **Sella Brigg.** Advanced six marks of air on her slate, stated all three conditions of the southerly before the crew left, withdrew her crew's rescue cover for the winter on her own statement, paid for her own stove in, and asked that the boy choosing nine with the hand already caught be written in the book before it became a story. **Not an antagonist and did not press anything.**
- **Harl Dace.** His cover stands inside the line. Not on the page this batch and nothing owed.

### The delayed note
- **Heard five times in this batch and logged three of them.** The logged occasions are the coal wharf on the 26th, the mole head on the 2nd, and the slip on the 4th, each with hour, state of the water, the harbor's own bell, and the line *knock, delayed, in the bottom off the mole, no source, not written as a voice*. **The two unlogged hearings are the mole's chain alarm on the 28th**, when both of Calder's hands were busy on the cleat and Nessa told him what the bell was instead of asking him, **and the top of the make on the 29th**, when he had no book, no pencil, no slate and nobody to read it back. The 29th is the one occasion on the page where he heard it and could have written it and did not. **It has never been resolved and Bevin's book is never going to resolve it.** On the 29th he understood that a record no other man can check is the thing the whole batch has been about.

### Costs the volume may not waive again
- **Money:** nine marks of air, a bonesetter's mark, a surgeon's mark, a harbor's pump three a tide, a harbor's share, six marks repaid to Sella Brigg, and no payment at all on the note or on the certificate. Twenty-six marks of gross work in ten days and five marks in the bag.
- **Body:** a crushed hand and six weeks of a boy's wages, twenty-four marks the yard does not have. Calder's forearm still ungloved, his ears still doubled, and now four people compensating for him instead of one.
- **Standing:** the crew's ability to earn anything inside its own harbor line is stopped by clause three of a notice it did not write and cannot lift. The mole's toe and inner face are closed to all persons for eight seams. The bottom is restored only by a hearing, and the panel is behind a countersign the yard cannot obtain.
- **Time:** one sheltered in-water look (the 28th), one bad descent (the 29th), one breach of a served notice by a reeve (the 3rd), and ten days of a month with nothing in it.
- **Authority:** Calder gave up the dive he wanted in writing, in front of the crew, with the reason, and the panel is still there.

## Proposed canon changes awaiting a planning phase — CLOSED, all items ruled 26 September 2026

**This block is closed. Every item that stood here has been ruled or ratified by the Volume 01 volume audit and the rulings are recorded under *Rulings of the Volume 01 volume audit* at the foot of this file, in `state/open-threads.md`, and in `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md` Part 2. The text of the old items is kept below so that the reasoning behind each ruling is not lost.**

0. **The harbor line, the work board, and who may be paid a countersign — RULED, a clarification lock, and it stands.** Three different instruments, a later batch must not merge them. **Carried into `outline/volume-02.md` as part of lock L1's jurisdiction section.**
1. **The first entry's hand cost — RULED: the prose stands and the deviation is accepted, not applied.** `bible/power-system.md` lists *Calder's hands shake for two days*; Chapters 6 and 7 state the opposite deliberately and the Batch 0001 review confirmed the inversion is the better beat, because the whole point of the cost is that it is not in his hands. **The bible was not edited and does not need to be.** The ruling is a decided reading: **no later volume may pay the shake as a debt that was owed. If Calder's hands shake, they shake from cold, air, a rope, a held sheet of glass, or plain fear, and it reads as that.**
2. **The mole's toe as the near end of the chain, and the fifth seam as the deeper panel — RULED, confirmed as the volume's geography.** Batch 0003 placed the near end of the transparent run **inside the harbor line, under the mole's own foundation stone**, which is new geography for the chain and not a new rule, and **the audit ratified the ground that made it lawful** (item 11 below). Movement 4 and Movement 5 did reach the deeper structure by moving seaward. **Binding:** the run is continuous from the mole's toe through the shoal's inner edge to about two miles and a quarter, and the pale run's known extent at about two and a half miles is unchanged. **Volume 2 extends the run landward instead, under the town, and that is the volume's discovery.**
3. **The Crown-shop bolted rim on the fifth-seam plate — RULED: the standing caution stands and is now a lock.** The plate in Registry custody has a bolted flange over a cut aperture in the lower corner with stamped studs on a jig spacing and darker, older glass under the lid; the panel seen at the fifth seam on the third has a rim with the same four-word description. **The two are never fused.** They are two separate sightings, one in custody and one not, and the rule that keeps the shard and the plate apart applies here too. **Carried into `outline/volume-02.md`.**
4. **A claim in the conservator's second note is not a world rule — RULED, the caution stands.** Mara's note asserts that no instrument can tell a joined line from a line lying in a row. **That is a statement about the present state of measurement, not a law about glass, and it may not be hardened into one.**
5. **Movement 3 stops at order, where the outline says *positioned* — RULED: THE CHAPTERS GOVERN. The auditor accepted *order shown, purpose withheld*.** `outline/volume-01.md` Movement 3 states the finding as *the transparent vessels are positioned, not scattered*; Chapters 28–30 do not say that, Nessa refuses the word on the second, Calder strikes *placed* out of the yard's own copy of the filed purpose and leaves it standing in the reeve's hearing record, and the sheets on the market wall say what is for is not known to this crew and no person in this harbor has been asked. **The ruling is that the chapter's reading governs, because order is what four months of labor can honestly establish and position implies an agent, and because the yard striking a word out of its own document six days before a hearing is a better beat than the yard being right.** **This changed nothing the volume paid and it strengthens the new-volume question.** **Binding for the whole series: no chapter may assert that the run was laid or that it was built by a person, and the word *placed* survives only inside the hearing record where a clerk may read it.** Carried into `outline/volume-02.md`.

## Batch 0004 as written (Chapters 31–40) — binding on Chapters 41 onward

### Calendar

- **Mon 7th (Ch31), Tue 8th (Ch32–33), Wed 9th unwritten, Thu 10th (Ch34), Fri 11th (Ch35), Sat 12th (Ch36), Sun 13th (Ch37–38), Mon 14th (Ch39–40).** Eight days, no rest day, two of them (the 13th and the 14th) carrying two chapters each.
- **The weekdays are forced by Batch 0003 and are not negotiable.** 31 October was a Monday (the notice arrived) and 1 November was a Tuesday, so **the hearing of the seventh is a Monday**, the 10th is a Thursday, the 12th a Saturday, the 13th a Sunday and the 14th a Monday. An earlier draft had the whole batch one day out and every weekday in it was wrong; this is the correction.
- **The harbor authority's clerk wipes the hearing room's slate wall every Monday at eight o'clock whether there is a sitting or not.** It therefore goes blank twice in this batch — on the morning of the seventh before the terms are read, and on the morning of the fourteenth before the resumed sitting — and Kest re-chalks the yard's three points himself on the tenth, in his own hand, and signs the corner. The clerk has done it every Monday for six years.
- The hearing **opens on the seventh at ten**, is **adjourned at four** when the *Vigil's* boat comes alongside, is **resumed on the eighth at ten**, stands **adjourned from two to half past two while the clerk looks for the harbor authority's own book**, and is **adjourned at five** to the fourteenth, and the resumed sitting is **held at eleven on the fourteenth** because a man in a coat asked to be told something by eleven. **There is one interval exchange in the batch and it is at four-forty on the eighth**: the officer puts the question to the yard, Kest rules it stands unanswered and gives the yard until the fourteenth, and the officer then states his own view of it, entered as a statement and not as an answer. Chapter 32's morning does not stage it and Chapter 33's afternoon does not stage it twice.
- The *Vigil*'s salvage claim was **lodged on the fourteenth of October at ten-forty**, **tendered on the seventh of this month at four in the afternoon**, and **entered on the eighth at ten** — twenty-four days in a drawer, and both dates entered in the harbor authority's record.
- The *Vigil's* tender: **twenty days on station as of the fourth of last month, twenty-three on the seventh, twenty-four on the eighth, thirty on the fourteenth.** A quarter of a mile off the mole on the bearing of the pale run, floats run, oars shipped, holding the near end of a marked line.
