| 12–14 Jan | 19 gross, less the eighth 2, the share 2, air 2, rope and the three days' victuals 3, and the *Grey Widow*'s diver's four = 13, so **6** to the yard; less a bonesetter's mark of 1. **The hand crane is not in this column: the harbor authority hired and paid for it** | | **13** |
| 17 Jan, Mon | — | 2 air, 1 rope, 1 the bonesetter's second mark = 4, giving 9; and the week's bread, oil and candles, **five, entered on the chandler's note and paid out of the bag the same afternoon** = 9 out of 13 | **4** |
| 18, 19 Jan | — | — | **4** |

**The bag closes Volume 02's opening movement at four marks, which is below the five it opened on and below the five it has held since the first of December. The tin is empty.** Nothing has been paid to anybody on the whole chain. **The chandler's note, mark by mark:** the last figure ever written in the chandler's own book was **six**, entered on the sixth of November; on **the fourth of January**, at the yard's asking and for the first time in nineteen years of that page, the chandler wrote **nineteen marks at twenty-two weeks**, with a line under it: *entered at the asking, by Marr, of the Free Keel yard, with a request against the figure, that it be paid as the first money that comes into the yard and not out of a bag that cannot*, and both signed. **One mark went on it on the seventh of January, the first money on that note since the eighteenth of November, and the figure became eighteen. Five marks of bread, oil and candles went on it on the seventeenth of January, which is where a yard's food goes, and the figure is thirteen.** **The five is entered on the creditor's page and paid out of the bag on the same afternoon, and the two facts are the same five marks and not two deductions: the note is the instrument and the bag is the payment, and a figure written on a note is not a figure paid until somebody puts the money on the counter.** The boy carried the five down himself. **The yard's own count of the weeks on the note is thirteen and has been since the eighteenth of November, and it is a figure in Calder Marr's head only, and that fact is entered in the crew's book and the number is not**, because a number in a book that cannot be checked becomes a lie inside a month. **The chandler said his own count was twenty-two weeks on the fourth of January — on his own count, not the yard's — and the boy contradicted him with the yard's figure in front of him.** The promise Calder made on the first of December — that he would take the note to the chandler on the second — **is broken out loud in the shop on the fourth of January and named as broken**, and is not repaired by the nineteen, and is not repaired at all.

**Debt, unchanged and unpaid.** **Forty marks to Bevin Rook** — sixteen for the reading of the book from the fourteenth of October and twenty-four for the hand from the twenty-ninth — stand whole and are not reduced and are not asked to be reduced. **Forty marks to Mara Venn**, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, and **not paid in this batch.** **A certificate of method is six marks and three weeks** (Chapter 52), which is the price a conservator with no standing charges, and nobody pays it. **Twenty marks of shoal iron** on the stack, a third of the sale entered to **Sella Brigg for February**, a saw kerf in one crown shackle, the whole stack the charter's bond at a clerk's thirteen. **The air pot is a third gone** and four marks of air bought in this batch have not put a mark back into it. **A pump with a cracked housing, not fitted before the spring, and therefore not hireable, and therefore four months of not being able to hire one out.** The *Kittiwake* on the hard with no cradle.

**Money that is entered and has not arrived: the first of February.** Kest entered in the authority's own book that the nineteenth of January is **paid as a day at eighteen marks** for a crew of three and a boat, out of the authority's own account, with the eighth and the share to come off anything raised and **nothing to be raised**, and the authority's bills in that quarter go out on the first of the month, so **the yard's fourteen marks arrive on the first of February, which is the same day the boy's first wage falls due.** Calder does not tell the boy, because a boy told his wage is coming on a date is a boy given a date, and the yard has spent four months taking dates out of it.

### 3. Every document and instrument created in this batch

- **The re-written chandler's ledger line of the fourth of January** (nineteen marks, twenty-two weeks, a request entered at the asking, two signatures). The only new line on that page in nineteen years.
- **The second request of the circuit office, dated the thirtieth of December, received by the party on the fourth of January** — four paragraphs, delivered into the custody room so that *the record of a contested material may be single*, with the **fourteen-day clause and the second seal of delay**. It is addressed to the party, not to Mara Venn, and **names her in the copy as a person who has already declined a related request**, so the yard cannot spend her twice. Read out in the copying room on the fifth.
- **The fee receipt of the fifth of January** — three marks, the request entered and dated, both dates on one line, and the office's own added line that **the fourteen days run from the date of the paper and not the date of receipt, and six of them are gone, and six remain, and the fourteenth is the thirteenth of January.**
- **The crew's book entry of the fourth of January**: five in the bag, nothing in the tin, nothing in either since the first of December, and *the fact that the yard's count of the weeks is in Calder's head and not in the book*.
- **The crew's book entry of the seventh of January**: the north wall job, twenty-four on the board, the outgoings, and a hired hand-crank at a mark a tide entered as a hire and not a gift.
- **The entry of the tenth of January, in the boy's own right hand and countersigned by Pike**: *Bevin Rook to be entered as a hand at four marks the week from the first day of February, in addition to and without reduction of the claim of forty marks, which stand whole*, with the sentence that the boy asked for a worse bargain than he could have asked for and said so out loud in his mother's front room and it is entered here because he asked for it to be entered here.
- **The crew's book entry of the eleventh of January**: the going out, the ninth logged occasion of the delayed note, Corvin Sarn's stated costs, the offer of the crew's share of the day and the refusal, and the fouling comparison.
- **The reason entered in the harbor authority's book on the twelfth of January at eight**, unedited, in Calder's own left hand, with the clerk present, **carrying a sentence nobody had put there before**: that the first clause of the notice of the thirty-first closes the mole's toe and inner face from the third seam to the eleventh to all work and all approach by all persons **including this office**, that a permit of this office does not lift a notice of the circuit office and this office has no power to lift it, and that **this office is putting a man inside that closure on the ground named and is entering the fact of it on the same sheet, unedited.** Calder Marr puts the reeve's own November ground to him in the room — *that this office will not put a person inside a closure its own notice created* — and Kest orders the clause written on the sheet and read back **and not softened by a word**, on the ground that a man in Veyr in four years will have a reeve who said no in November and yes in January with **the reason for both on one sheet in two hands, which is better than a clerk's slip in a net loft saying this office did not know what it was doing.** Also entered, **the reeve's own addition under it** — that the ground, the stone, the shackle, the mooring and the fouling are all the authority's, and that the reason the fouling is not narrowed is a notice this office did not write naming three men, and that a man of a herring boat paid a diver out of another crew's locker to shift a stone on this office's ground while three of the best men in this harbor watched from a wall. Both read back, both on one sheet.
- **The harbor authority's entry of the thirteenth of January**, read out in the barge on the seventeenth: that the authority paid **Neale's tide gang one day on the wall, three marks, and a hand crane out of the net loft for two days at two marks a day, four marks, at this office's cost and not the yard's**, seven marks for a day of work the office did not get done. **This is the same hand crane and the same two days as the yard's itemisation of the fourteenth, and the hire is the authority's: the yard's own list says so in the yard's own hand and does not carry another office's money twice.** The yard's *other* hand-crank — the north wall's, out of the net loft at a mark a tide — is the yard's own, and came out of the rope money on the sixth and seventh. **Two hires, two payers, two rates, and they are not the same hire.**
- **The ground naming of the seventeenth of January**, in Kest's own hand, read back: the nineteenth of January, a Thursday, the top of the flood, the mole's toe at the third and fourth seams, the bedded seam in the office's own foundation running north under the rubble of which the south end is the party's position since the twenty-eighth of October, the water inside the harbor line, a hand lead, a slate and the core, and **no diver unless the reason names one.** **And, said in his own voice in his own barge and not in the paper, because a clerk would not know what to do with it on a page: the first clause of that notice closes the ground he has just named, he did the same on the twelfth and entered it, this is the second time, and he is telling Calder Marr so that the yard can be holding it as well as he is — *I have been the only man in this harbor holding things since the thirty-first of October and it has made me a man who decides things alone and writes them down afterward.*** This is now **`outline/volume-02.md` lock L24** and it binds every later chapter of the volume.
- **The authority's line of the seventeenth that the day is paid as a day at eighteen marks, out of the office's own money, eighth and share off anything raised and nothing to be raised, in the holder's hands on the first of February.**
- **The eight-clause plan for one supervised look at the fifth seam**, lodged on the seventeenth, in Calder's own left hand, on the yard's own paper with no harbor authority in it, delivered by **Elias Voke**. **It is not answered in January and it will be refused again, correctly, and the refusal has to cost something.** The second of the two proper plans owed to Volumes 2 and 3.
- **The re-written count of the printed crew copies, the eighteenth of January**: ten becomes **eleven**, both numbers true of their own dates, the seventh line naming Amos Pryde, master, of no crew of this association, at his own asking, to be kept and not returned.
- **The reason for the nineteenth, two hundred and sixty-nine words** as printed in the crew's book, in the crew's book on the eighteenth and in the authority's book on the nineteenth at eight, **with no diver in it**, and the entry of **the omission** in the yard's own book, in the boy's own right hand: that the reason states no purpose, that the excepting has been gone at since the sixth of January, and that a reason with a hole in it that is not written down is a hole. **The party disclaims clause three of the notice in its own third paragraph and says nothing about clause one, and that silence is now deliberate and known: Kest answers it in a second line under the reason, in his own hand, on the nineteenth — that he named the ground on the seventeenth and knew what he was doing, that the party has not asked for the notice to be lifted, that this office has not lifted it and cannot, and that a man in Veyr in four years will find two entries in one book in which this harbor authority put itself inside a closure created by a paper it did not write.** The only sentence in the reason that is not an instruction is eleven words long: *the run of the bedded material does not stop at the mole.*
- **The entry of the nineteenth of January at twenty past eleven**, that the day was stopped by the holder of his own motion, with the reason given above and the sentence that the work will not be done again until somebody is found who can be asked.
- **Two crew copies of the reason are on the wall in seven parts** — the wall now carries seven things, the count of the documents having gone up by one with the copy given away.

### 4. The bellwright's name, and what he said

**CORVIN SARN, the harbor's own bellwright of Morrow Quay, about fifty, with a blacksmith's forearms, in that yard since the twentieth of October, NAMED IN CHAPTER 55, in Nessa Pike's mouth, and spoken again by Calder Marr when the man comes down the steps.** He is the harbor's and not the Free Keel's, and he says so. **He teaches nobody.** He states, in his own terms, that a bell is iron, and that the whole of the craft is the going out and not the making, and that fouling is not the enemy and must not be cleaned, and that water in a gudgeon writes the bell off and water in the lead clouds four pieces of glass for good with a Crown furnace two hundred miles off as the only repair. **He refuses the crew's share of the day in one sentence** — a man who sells his going out is a man whose going out belongs to somebody — and both the offer and the refusal are in the book. **He gives the mechanism, and it is a mechanism and not a mystery:** the core's seaward face carries a pale scour because thirteen years of fouling has been taking gravel off it at every flood, and the plate under the mole's root has had nothing moving over it since the year of the Cut, and *a bell only speaks while somebody keeps the weather working on it*. **The costs he states: a rope a year, a man's day every four or five years for the tongue, and a person in a boat in the weather the rest of the time.** The day's hire in the book is **eight marks and a day of a boat**, and the chapter says it will be the same again in a year and the year after.

### 5. The dated positions of the charter, the berth, the ninth seam and the wages

- **The one-season provisional working charter** is unchanged: it runs to the last day of March, it is inside the harbor line bounded by the outer marks, **it may be suspended after an inspection, for cause, by one man with a key and a book**, and the reeve said out loud on the twenty-sixth of December that he has no cause and will not, and has said nothing since and has not been asked. The suspended-instrument warning is not repeated in this batch. **The notice of the thirty-first is not lifted, narrowed, re-served or altered, and the third clause is not reached, and the yard does not ask for it to be reached.**
- **The north berth is still out of service** and is the one that takes a northeast blow. On the first of March, forty herring boats, one berth short.
- **The ninth seam's mooring: bought back, not worked.** On the seventeenth Kest spends the authority's own money on a **fourth set of moorings** and on **the herring fleet's winter warp for the season**, cut and laid by Neale's gang in the fortnight before the first of March. **This is the cost that lands on third parties:** the gang loses three men in the last week of February, the money that went on moorings and warp is money that was not going to be spent on the nineteen winters, so **the disputed and unpaid nineteen winters stay disputed and unpaid**, and Voke's claim is extinguished in practice and not on paper and he cannot show anybody. **The mooring is not yet worked**: the stone was shifted four inches on the twelfth and the shackle was not freed, and the work must be done in a hard month by a hired diver, and the diver is very likely Tam Slee again. **Kest states the price out loud before he grants the thing**, as he did in November about the charter, and says the thing the yard wants and the thing the town pays for are not the same thing. **The cost of the twelfth is the thirteenth's entry: seven marks the authority paid, and the boy's wrist, which Kest says is not his to pay and does not pretend it is.**
- **The wages.** Nothing has been paid. **A wage at four a week from the first of February is entered in the crew's book in the boy's own right hand**, in addition to the forty marks, on a day the yard can name and cannot pay until it raises something, and **the first four weeks of it fall in February**, the same month the authority's fourteen marks arrive. **The word *wages* is not put back into the book**, and the boy asks for it to be written longhand on the wall copy as well, without the word.
- **The coast office** is still unanswered. It asked for a **statement of the authority's capacity to moor the fleet**, in four lines dated the twenty-eighth of November, and the harbor authority promised an answer in a hearing room on the same day and has not written it in seven weeks. **Voke will write the statement himself for nothing**, and now Kest has also promised the coast office a statement of what the authority spent, and both will be read by the same man in Veyr.

### 6. The bodies and the crew

- **Calder Marr**, 32, a line diver with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice and not used again in this batch. **No dive of his own in this batch and no possibility of one**, because the notice's third clause names him. **His tinnitus is named once, in Chapter 57**: he loses the harbor's crane, cannot place it, asks for a sentence to be said again, and is told it is the fourth time since the fourteenth of October and there will be a fifth and there will be a twentieth, and that no paper in this harbor or in Veyr takes it out of a man's head. He loses a sound and recovers by asking, not by pretending.
- **Nessa Pike**, overrules him for the sixth time since the twenty-fifth of October and wins the sixth, and the sixth cost a boy a hand. She calls *Enough* at nineteen minutes on the twelfth, does not apologize, does not soften it and does not look away. She holds the cleat on the nineteenth with no diver to tend. She reads the money for the boy when he cannot. She signs three things away in four months and is thanked for none of them.
- **Bevin Rook**, 22, left arm in a sling, left hand crushed in October, **left wrist crushed again on the twelfth of January when a chain came tight and he put the bad arm into the fall.** The bonesetter's word in January is different from November's: *it will close, it will not be worth what it was, and I will not promise you June; what is in my book is a boat hand in June, and that is not a promise and it is not a lie.* **June is not resolved.** His mother is told the truth once in a front room and once more in a shed and is not to be told again in a room. He enters his own wage, his own count, his own omission, his own read-backs, and the sentence that the man who calls the day is the man who does not get to be the man who goes on.
- **Elias Voke**, herring skipper of the *Anna Ray*, 51, nineteen winters disputed, pays twenty-four for the warp and nineteen for the mole's toe, and is the fisher's hand on the delivery of the fifth-seam plan. His brother-in-law in the third lay is the man who will lose a boat in a northeast blow.
- **Neale**, foreman of the harbor's tide gang, names the leash and what it is for, and three of his men pay for it in the last week of February.
- **Harl Dace** of the *Grey Widow*, the only rescue cover the yard has, asks *who is it for* in a net loft and the yard has no answer.
- **Amos Pryde**, coaster's master, not a Free Keel man, owed nothing, asks for a copy to keep and is given one.
- **Tam Slee**, 41, diver out of the *Grey Widow*, hired on the twelfth, came up whole.
- **Corvin Sarn**, the bellwright, named.
- **Sella Brigg**, her cover gone since the twenty-ninth of October and **now entered as a fact in the yard's own book with a date**, and her third of the iron still to be paid in February.
- **Tobias Wren**, at the bench, one sentence about a day with a lead and a slate finding out a number, and no question asked.
- **Alden Marr**, at the far bench, silent since the thirteenth of November, **not asked for anything, and Calder does not go.** No fragment, no report talk, no second story.

### 7. New entries in the delayed note, and what they are not

**Logged in the flat form — hour, state of the water, the harbor's three strokes, the delay of a hand's width of a second, no source, not written as a voice — and read back twice.**

- **The ninth: the eleventh of January.** Top of the flood, south end, a light southerly, the harbor's three at ten minutes past ten, the answer out of the core under the third. **There is no call in it, and it is the first logged occasion without one, entered as a fact.**
- **The tenth: the nineteenth of January.** Top of the flood, a light air off the north, the harbor's three at four minutes past ten, the same second as the eleventh and the sixth and the twenty-sixth of December, the note only. **That is the second logged occasion running with no call in it — there are two of them, the eleventh and the nineteenth, and every earlier logged occasion had a call in it, the twenty-sixth of October, the second, fourth, eighth, fourteenth and twenty-third of November, the sixth and the twenty-sixth of December — and the batch enters the count of the two and nobody in the boat asks him a question about it. The count is two and may not be raised.**

**Span still begins on the twenty-sixth of October. The answer inside the note is not approached, not named and not resolved, and is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.** The two unwritten occasions of Volume 01 stand.

### 8. The delayed note's mechanism, which is craft and not mystery

**The core answers because the flood scours its seaward face and the fouling is what does the scouring; the plate under the mole's root never answered because nothing has moved over it since the year of the Cut. A thing only speaks while somebody keeps the weather working on it.** The delay is **a hand's width of a second** and it is a figure, written down, and as of Chapter 60 it is **a figure in the yard's own book as well as the harbor's**, and it has not become an instrument. The bell still reads nothing, settles nothing and says no word, is still fouled, has a tongue still half worn through, and cannot be worked in a shed because of the second clause of the notice of the thirty-first.

### 9. What Chapter 60 found, and what it may not be used for

**The run's near end is not at the mole and it is not at the shoal. It goes landward, under the mole's root and under the quay, and the figures are ordinary.** South end **nine feet**, which is the tender's count of the twenty-eighth of October off the mole's toe at the third seam and the figure on the hearing record, and **Bevin Rook's own count of the same water is seven**; **ten and a half feet forty feet north at the north end of the bedded material**, which is the tender's count and **Rook's own count of that is eleven**, given aloud on the ladder in October and entered on the fourteenth of November; **seven and three-eighths at the mole's root**; then **five and a quarter, landward, under a stone quay**, sounded four times, coming up at about a foot in thirty. **Nine and a quarter feet is not one of these figures: it is the *Aster Gannet's* ferry plate in a coaming on the mole face beyond the eleventh seam, sounded on the twenty-seventh of November, and the plate and the bedded seam are two objects at two parts of the mole and are never fused.** **The harbor authority has no chart of what is under the town because the town was built on it.** **No diver was in the water and the reason entered in the reeve's book names a hand lead and a slate.** **Calder stops the day at twenty past eleven at a figure that is not the figure the yard went out for**, and gives the reason aloud in the boat: *a route that no paper has ever been written for cannot be asked about in a hearing room, and a man who cannot ask is a man with no instrument.* **What is under the town is not explained, is not named, and is not to be pursued by a night party or a punt.** The **re-eving's ruling that no line may be drawn between the three points stands and is not lifted**, and nothing in this batch draws one. **The whole of it was worked on ground the notice of the thirty-first closes, under a claim window the harbor authority had no power to write, and Kest says so twice in the record — see lock L24.**

### 10. The costs this volume may not waive again

- **The note, re-written in a creditor's own hand at nineteen marks and now at thirteen**, and the first money on it since the eighteenth of November was a mark, and then a fortnight of food went on it and was paid out of the bag the same afternoon. **A yard's living goes onto a creditor's page in his own hand and the bag pays it inside the hour, and that is where the money goes, and no mark of it is counted twice.**
- **A promise with a date on it, broken out loud, thirty-three days late, and not repaired.** Volume 2 may not quietly make Calder a man who keeps such promises.
- **A wage named on a date the yard cannot pay, by a boy who asked for a worse bargain than he could have asked for.** It is not paid and may not be paid early out of a scene.
- **A wrist, four months after a hand, and a bonesetter who has changed her word and will not promise June.** June is a sentence in a book and not a promise and not a lie.
- **A reeve's price paid in a third party's winter** — the tide gang's three men, a brother's-in-law's boat, nineteen winters that are still unpaid because the money went on warp. **Kest's authority may be spent again and it may always cost somebody who is not the yard.**
- **Eleven copies of the public record instead of ten**, and a naval slate holding the old number, and a man in a net loft asking *who is it for* with no answer. **The consent question has begun to cost records.**
- **The first time a reason written for a municipal book was short a purpose, the omission was entered in the yard's own book, and the crew of the boat agreed not to ask the question.** That agreement is a cost on all of them and is not free.
- **Four marks in a bag, nothing in a tin, a note at thirteen, a pump that cannot be hired, a boat on the hard with no cradle, and fourteen marks and a first wage both falling due on the first of February.**

### 11. Proposed canon changes

**One, and it is a correction rather than a change: the calendar line in `outline/volume-02.md` lock L1 and in the Batch 0001 prompt, which said that the fourth, eleventh, eighteenth and twenty-fifth of January are Mondays, was arithmetically incompatible with the same lock's statement that the fourth of January is a Wednesday, and the interval of eighty-one days was wrong for nine weeks and two days, which is sixty-five.** The chapters used the corrected grid and always did. **On the Batch 0001 review both planning files were corrected in place, with the correction and its date entered in the outline itself** — `outline/volume-02.md` lock L1, Movement 1's *eight weeks off*, and `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md`'s *eight weeks off* and *six weeks less a day*. **No bible file is amended.**

**One addition, not a correction: `outline/volume-02.md` lock L24, the notice's own closure and the reeve who works inside it.** The first clause of the notice of the thirty-first closes the mole's toe and inner face from the third to the eleventh to all work and all approach by all persons including the harbor authority, and the harbor authority has no power to lift it; the batch had the permit of the eighth, the reason of the twelfth, the claim window of the seventeenth and the day of the nineteenth all inside that closure. **This was a contradiction in the batch and it is now the volume's standing cost, entered in the reeve's own hand three times and made a lock.** **No antagonist, institution or enemy is added by it and nothing about the ending moves.**

### 12. Deliberate reconciliations, flagged rather than hidden

1. **The ninth seam's mooring is bought back into service in Chapter 58**, which changes the position it stood in at the volume's opening and at Chapters 51, 53 and 57. The card for Chapter 58 requires a fourth set of moorings and the herring fleet's winter warp on the authority's own account, and the fourth set in Kest's own words of the twenty-eighth of November is the ninth seam's; the consequence is written into Chapter 58 and into Chapter 60 rather than smoothed away. **The north berth remains out of service, one berth remains short on the first of March, and the nineteen winters remain unpaid**, so the volume's clock is unchanged.
2. **The fouling on the core and on the plate is compared from the water in Chapter 55**, the plate being still on the mole's toe and untouchable, and the comparison is made by the bellwright with a thumb over the side. **Nothing on the plate is touched and no custody object moves.**
3. **The harbor authority's breach of its own notice, entered rather than hidden.** See lock L24. **The permit, the reason, the claim window and the day all sit inside a closure created by a paper the harbor authority did not write and cannot lift, and the batch's decision was to have the reeve say so out loud, write it in his own hand, and take the cost, rather than to pretend the closure does not reach a boat with a hand lead in it.** This is consistent with the volume's standing position that no notice has ever closed water and with ruling 1 above (ordinary harbor work landward of the third seam is outside the closure), and it does not narrow the notice by one word.
4. **The hand crane is paid once.** There are two hand-crank hires in the batch and they are two hires: the yard's own on the north wall at a mark a tide out of the rope money, and the authority's for the two days of the stone at two marks a day out of its own repair account. **Chapter 56 now says the second is the authority's and the yard's itemisation does not carry it**, which is what Chapter 58's entry of the thirteenth of January already said.

---

## Batch 0001 review repairs — the disposition of every finding

A reviewer pass over Chapters 51–60, the four state files, `outline/volume-02.md`, the Batch 0001 card file and the queued Batch 0002 prompt returned **FIX**: twelve required changes, eight prose and state improvements, and three items of planning-file residue. The reviewer's own log is not kept in this repository, and `state/current.md` § *Batch 0001 review repairs* holds the short form. **No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut for length, and no planned plot was altered** — Movement 1, the leash, the fourth of January, the nineteenth, the body, the going out, the reeve's price and the ending are all as the volume outline has them, and `outline/ending.md` was not touched.

**What was wrong and is now right on the page or in the file it was in:**

1. **The first of February was a Tuesday in four chapters and in a state file.** On the locked grid **1 February is a Wednesday** (1 March is a Wednesday and a February is four weeks). Corrected in Chapters 54 (twice), 57, 58 and 59, and in this file. **Chapter 54's own derivation was also wrong** — *a month of that length takes four days off a week backwards* — and is now *a February is four weeks and four weeks takes nothing off a week at all*, with the boy's second check off the Mondays added. The state file's *the 10th of February is a Tuesday, which the boy works out for himself* was wrong in the date, wrong in the weekday, and it presented the chapter's derivation as sound; **all three are corrected.**
2. **Five marks were deducted twice in Chapter 57.** The week's bread, oil and candles were said to go *onto the note and not out of the bag* while the bag fell by nine. **The five is now entered on the creditor's page and paid out of the bag on the same afternoon, and the chapter says both and says why: the note is the instrument and the bag is the payment, and a figure written on a note is not a figure paid.** The chain is 13 − 4 = 9, less the five the boy carried down, = 4, and the note goes 18 − 5 = 13. The money table in this file, `state/current.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md` and the Batch 0002 prompt all carry that version. **A second contradiction the same repair found in the same chapter is fixed with it:** the bench-reassignment entry of the Monday night said the note stood at eighteen with nothing on it since the seventh, four hours after the chapter had read out that it stood at thirteen.
3. **The hand crane was paid twice, by two parties, at two rates.** **The second of the two hires is the harbor authority's** — out of its own repair account, four marks for two days at two marks a day, entered in its book on the thirteenth of January and read out in the barge on the seventeenth — and the yard's itemisation of the fourteenth does not carry it. The yard's *own* hand-crank, the north wall's at a mark a tide, comes out of the rope money and is named as such. The money chain is unchanged at thirteen in the bag and the chapter is unchanged in what it costs.
4. **Chapter 52's office arithmetic was wrong and a day-count had been hardened into a date.** The paper is dated the thirtieth of December and the entry is made on the fifth of January, so **six** of the fourteen days are gone, not eight, and the office's added line now says so and gives the fourteenth as the thirteenth of January. **The clerk's *fifteenth day* and *twenty-ninth day* are day-counts, and the chapter now gives both as dates — the thirteenth and the twenty-seventh of January — so that no later chapter can read the twenty-ninth as the twenty-ninth of January.** `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and the Batch 0002 prompt are corrected to the same.
5. **The delayed note's no-call count was contradicted five ways.** **The log supports two:** the eleventh and the nineteenth of January, against eight earlier logged occasions that all had a call in it. Chapter 55 now says the eleventh is the first, Chapter 60 says the nineteenth is the second time running, and this file, `state/chapter-summaries.md` and hard rule 11 of the Batch 0002 prompt all say two and may not raise it.
6. **Chapter 60 fused two objects and gave the wrong depth.** *Nine and a quarter, the figure of the twenty-seventh of November, off the coaming* is the ***Aster Gannet's* ferry plate** in a coaming on the mole face **beyond the eleventh seam**. The bedded seam at the mole's toe at the third and fourth seams is **nine feet at the south end on the tender's count, with Rook's own count of seven**, and **ten and a half at the north end, with his own count of eleven** — and the second of those is now on the page in the boy's own mouth with the rule that goes with it. The two objects are two objects again and the figure list in this file, in `state/chapter-summaries.md` and in hard rule 20 of the prompt carries the corrected run.
7. **Work was done inside the notice's closure under harbor-authority instruments and nobody noticed.** **Now Kest notices, three times, in his own hand, and it is a lock.** The reason of the twelfth carries a sentence nobody had put there before — that the office is putting a man inside a closure its own notice created, that a permit of this office cannot lift a notice of the circuit office, and that the office is entering the fact of it — and Calder Marr puts the reeve's own November ground to him in the room and Kest orders it written and read back unsoftened. The claim window of the seventeenth is named on ground the same notice closes and Kest says so in his barge and says he wants the yard holding it as well as he is. The line he writes under the reason on the nineteenth says it a third time. **`outline/volume-02.md` lock L24** and hard rules 2 and 3 of the Batch 0002 prompt now carry it as binding.
8. **Chapter 60 gave the boy a sentence he never said.** *A boy who had said in a front room on the ninth of January that he would be a boat hand in June* is the **bonesetter's** sentence out of her own book, and in the front room the boy said the opposite — that he would rather have a wage with a start and no money behind it than forty marks with a date on it. Chapter 60 now says whose sentence it is and that it was not his to say out loud.
9. **Chapter 55 contained an impossible sum.** *Fourteen less three this morning was two* is now **five less three on Thursday was two**, which is the copying room of the fifth, and the rest of the sentence — fifteen in on the Saturday, one of it on the note, eight out on the Sunday, eight in the bag — checks as printed.
10. **Chapter 53 put a cost in the twenty-four that the itemisation did not contain.** The hired hand-crank now comes out of the rope money, and the itemisation reads *rope and the two days' victuals, three, and the hired hand-crank a mark of it* — still nine out of twenty-four, still fifteen to the yard, and now nothing in the prose is outside the sum.
11. **The batch's length record was wrong in two files.** The per-chapter figures in `state/current.md` were exact and the batch total of 30,632 was exact; **the reviewer's own corrected figure was also wrong, because it dropped Chapter 51 at 3,645 and so reported three chapters past the guideline when four were.** Measured before the repairs: **six inside, four past (Ch51, Ch56, Ch57, Ch58), the largest Chapter 51 at about fourteen per cent over.** Measured after: **31,769 words, five inside and five past, the largest Chapter 51 at fourteen per cent over.** The summary sentence in `state/current.md` and in the Batch 0002 prompt is now the measured one and names the largest chapter.
12. **Planning-file residue.** `outline/volume-02.md` Movement 1's *eleven weeks off* and lock L1's *the fourth, eleventh, eighteenth and twenty-fifth of January are Mondays* and *(eighty-one days)*, and `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md`'s *eleven weeks and a half off*, are all corrected in place with the correction and its date entered in the outline. **The batch had recorded two of the three and missed the third; all three are now fixed and none of them is a proposed canon change.**

**Prose and state improvements, applied in place.** Chapter 51's slate wall was said to have been blank *since eight that morning* on a Wednesday when the wipe is every Monday — **it was blank since eight on Monday the second**, which is what makes Bevin's *five days* true. The chandler's *twenty-two weeks on your own count* now reads **on my own count**, which is the whole of the exchange in that scene. Chapter 60's tide gang no longer claims to have stood on the mole on the eighteenth of January, which no chapter supports. Chapter 58's *a brother of Tam Slee, who dived out of the Grey Widow* no longer attaches the diving clause to the brother, and **the two brothers are now a fact Kest entered on the fifteenth: one on this office's gang and one diving for Harl Dace.** The wrist in Chapter 60 is **seven days** old on the nineteenth and not eight. Chapter 57's closing line no longer says a wage that *started* on a Tuesday in three weeks: it starts, on a Wednesday, in a fortnight. Three two-word dialogue lines that were exact duplicates across chapters are now three different lines, the money-beat sentence that appeared near-identically at the end of Chapters 53 and 55 is now two sentences, and **the closing beat that ran three times — *the book went on the bench and the pencil went on top of it* — now appears in that form once, in Chapter 54, where Alden Marr is in the room.** The Batch 0002 prompt's stray bullet about a note the *Vigil* put in against the crew's records is gone, and *the twenty chapters that matter most* is now *the ten chapters that matter most*.

**Three calendar errors the review did not flag were found while verifying its grid and are fixed on the same pass:** Chapter 53's *a line in his own book on Tuesday* and *the nineteen in the book on Tuesday* are **Wednesday**, the fourth being a Wednesday; Chapter 57's *the eighth of March is a Friday* is **a Wednesday**, with the Friday named as the tenth; and Chapter 57's *six weeks and two days* to the first of March, spoken on the Monday the seventeenth, is **six weeks and a day**.

**One checkable number the review did not flag was also wrong and is now true: the reason for the nineteenth is two hundred and sixty-nine words as printed, not two hundred and eleven**, and the boy who finds the sentence in it is told it is eleven words long, which is the length of the clause he puts his finger on. Both are corrected in Chapters 59 and 60 and in this file, in `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/current.md` and `state/open-threads.md`.

## Batch 0002 as written (Chapters 61–70), Volume 02 — binding on Chapters 71 onward

**Movement 2, weather and the capacity of a harbor. It opens the leash, prices the weather, and does not resolve either.** Everything below is what the ten chapters actually say and every figure in it can be checked on the page. The chapters are the authority on what happened.

### 1. The calendar the batch actually ran on

**`outline/volume-02.md` lock L1's grid, unchanged, and the chapters ran on it without an exception.** 20 January a Friday, 21 a Saturday, 22 a Sunday, 23 a Monday, 24 a Tuesday, 25 a Wednesday, 26 a Thursday, 27 a Friday, 28 a Saturday, 29 a Sunday, 30 a Monday, 31 a Tuesday; 1 February a Wednesday, 2 a Thursday, 3 a Friday, 4 a Saturday, 5 a Sunday, 6 a Monday, 7 a Tuesday, 8 a Wednesday, 13 a Monday, 14 a Tuesday, 15 a Wednesday, 17 a Friday, 20 a Monday, 21 a Tuesday, 22 a Wednesday, 23 a Thursday, 24 a Friday, 25 a Saturday, 26 a Sunday, 27 a Monday, 28 a Tuesday; 1 March a Wednesday. **The Mondays in February are the 6th, 13th, 20th and 27th, and the Mondays in March are the 6th, 13th, 20th and 27th.** The hearing room's slate wall is wiped every Monday at eight, and the last wipe before the batch's one sitting was **Monday the twentieth of February**; the wall was blank from **eight on Thursday the twenty-third of February**, when a cloth came off it, and it goes again on **Monday the sixth of March at eight**, whether there is a sitting or not. The first of March is a Wednesday and the 1st of February is a Wednesday, the fourth week of a boy's four-a-week wage fell due on **Tuesday the twenty-first of February** and was not paid until seven o'clock on Wednesday the first of March, and **Friday the twenty-eighth of February was the last day of the warp week and the Saturday before the first of March, not a Saturday in itself** — Voke wrote his statement on the Saturday night, which is the night of the twenty-fifth, and the chapters leave it undated.

**The two dates the prompt's day-counts produce, on the page:** the second seal came on **Friday the twenty-seventh of January**, which is the twenty-ninth day from the thirtieth of December; and the request stood unanswered on the fifteenth day, which is the thirteenth of January. **The twenty-ninth is a day and is nowhere written as the twenty-ninth of January, and Chapter 70 has the reeve order the day-count and not the date into the ledger precisely so that no later volume can harden it.**

### 2. The money chain, mark by mark

**Four marks in a bag and nothing in the tin on the twentieth of January. The tin is empty and has been since the eighteenth of November, and two crowns went into it on the first of February and were not spent.**

| Date | In | Out | Bag | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20–25 Jan | — | — | **4** | 13 |
| Thu 26 Jan | 26 gross, the *Sea Wren*'s claim day at the twelfth seam | the eighth 3, the share 3, air 2, rope 1, the *Sea Wren's* pump a mark = 10 | **20** | 13 |
| Sat 28 Jan | — | air 2, rope 1 = 3 | **17** | 13 |
| Tue 31 Jan | — | **5**, a fortnight of bread, oil and candles, entered on the creditor's page and paid out of the bag the same afternoon | **12** | **8** |
| Wed 1 Feb | **14**, the harbor authority's own account, for the day of the nineteenth of January | the boy's first week at 4 | **22** | 8 |
| Mon 6 Feb | — | **6**, being the round figure of a true third of twenty marks at **6 marks 8s 10d**, and the **8s 10d** is entered in Sella Brigg's name on the same line and is not paid | **16** | 8 |
| Tue 7 Feb | — | 4, the boy's second week | **12** | 8 |
| Tue 14 Feb | — | 4, the boy's third week; **4**, a fortnight of bread, oil and candles, on the note | **4** | **4** |
| Fri 17 Feb | — | air 2, rope 1 = 3 | **1** | 4 |
| Tue 21 Feb | — | 4, the boy's fourth week, **not paid**, entered as unpaid in the boy's own right hand | **1** | 4 |
| Mon 27 Feb | **26 gross**, ninety fathoms of the authority's warp on the authority's wall | the eighth 3, the share 3, air 2, rope 2, a hand at 2 a day for 2 days = 4, and 26 − 14 = the yard's 12. **The going out's 8 marks is entered on the same line under a rule and is NOT paid** | **13** | 4 |
| Tue 28 Feb | 14 gross, a herring skipper's ring and warp off the north wall | the eighth 2, the share 2, air 2, rope 1, the hand's second day 2 = 9; and **3**, a fortnight of bread, oil and candles, on the note | **15** | **1** |
| Wed 1 Mar | — | 4, the boy's fourth week, **paid at seven in the morning** | **11** | **1** |

**The bag closes Movement 2 at eleven marks, the note at one, and the tin with two crowns in it, on Wednesday the first of March.** Nothing has been paid to anybody else all winter. **The note is the instrument and the bag is the payment, and a figure written on a note is not a figure paid** — the same sentence as Chapter 57, applied three times, and on the twenty-eighth of February a **third of a crate of fish from the herring fleet's mutual account** goes into three men's mouths and is entered as a thing and not as a figure, and is the reason the fortnight's eating falls from five to four to three.

**The two hand cranes are still two hires and this batch adds a third and it is the authority's.** The authority's hand crane came out of the net loft on the seventeenth of February at two marks a day out of its own repair account, and the yard's book does not carry it. The yard's own hired hand-crank of the sixth and seventh of January was out of the rope money at a mark a tide and was not used again. **The yard's pump is not fitted before the eighth of March and is not hireable and the batch says so twice.**

**Debt, unchanged and unpaid: forty marks to Bevin Rook** (sixteen for the reading from the fourteenth of October, twenty-four for the hand from the twenty-ninth), standing whole, not reduced, not asked to be reduced, and named in a herring skipper's statement of the capacity of a harbor in a hearing record. **Forty marks to Mara Venn, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, not paid in this batch, and she asks for six of them in daylight on the seventh of February and is not paid and does not ask twice.** **A certificate of method is six marks and three weeks and no one in this harbor can pay it or hurry it.** **Two debts are opened by this batch and are entered owing on the day they are entered, and neither is reduced in any later volume without a scene that pays it: eight marks and a day of a boat to the harbor's bellwright, entered under a rule on Monday the twenty-seventh of February and not paid, being the first going out this yard has paid for out of its own pocket; and eight shillings and tenpence of Sella Brigg's third of the iron, entered in her name on the sixth of February beside the six marks that were paid, being the difference between a round figure and a true one and not swallowed.**

### 3. Every document and instrument created in this batch

- **The inventory of the twentieth and twenty-first of January**, nine heads, no figures, in the boy's own right hand, because a list with figures in it is a list of what a man is worth. The bench reassignment of the fifteenth of January stands.
- **The second request of the circuit office, second seal affixed Friday the twenty-seventh of January**, the same four paragraphs of the thirtieth of December with **nineteen words in the office's own type: *That no ground having been received, the said request stands unanswered in this office, and this office awaits the same.*** Entered, dated and read back; **not answered, not refused, and a ground not stated.** The fee receipt of the fifth of January, which said the fourteen days run from the date of the paper, is the yard's own authority for the arithmetic.
- **Mara Venn's price of it, in her own words, in the yard's book, on the twenty-seventh**: three marks to have anything entered at the counter, a day's walk each way for two people, a third mark for a man's time in a queue, so six marks and two days; **not to answer costs nothing in marks and costs fourteen days a month in the shape of the file**; and it costs her nothing in standing, because there is none of it left to cost.
- **The three facts of the count, entered on the twenty-seventh with no opinion in them**: the office's paper of the thirtieth of December says ten copies; the count is eleven; the second seal was affixed today and carries the count in the office's own type; **and nobody in the shed knows whether the office has not been told, has been told and has not corrected it, or has been told and has left it standing.**
- **The crew's-book entry of the seventh of February**, in advance: that a hand hired for two days is not paid on the day if the yard has nothing on the day and is paid on the next with the reason in the book. The first promise the yard's book has made to somebody outside the yard about money that has not arrived.
- **The boy's entry of the first of February**, in his own right hand: that the four marks of his first week came out of the harbor authority's account and not out of the yard's, out of an account drawing on four sets of moorings, a season of warp and a day of a tide gang standing at a wall watching three men it cannot use — **asked for by him and suggested by nobody.** A wage with a named source is a wage a man can say out loud in a market.
- **The reason for the twenty-sixth of January**, Calder Marr's own left hand at eight with the clerk present, naming the twelfth seam, the two men, the length, the tide, and **that the ground is not inside the closure of the first clause** — the first reason since the twelfth of January with no hole in it, written for another man's account. **Kest read it and did not write under it, and said why out loud in a room**, and then wrote one sentence in his own book anyway: the yard has been sent to this office for work because its own ground is shut.
- **The reason for the seventeenth of February**, **Voke's reason written by Calder Marr in his own left hand**, Voke being named as the party and signing for it, with a purpose in it in full in the fourth paragraph, and **the fouling on the ground line between the third and the ninth seams entered in the same paragraph as a fact of the office's own account and not of the office's work.** Under it, the **fourth acknowledgement of lock L24** in Kest's own hand: this office names ground inside the closure, engages a diver inside it, has no power to lift the notice, is not lifting it, and the party is not on the face and was told so and did not argue.
- **The line entered in the harbor authority's own ledger on the seventeenth of February in Bevin Rook's own right hand**, in two columns, the clerk initialling the foot of it and not amending one word, **with the entry stating in the boy's own hand that he was not in the water, was not on the face, was not allowed to be, and that every figure in the first column is a figure he took off a man's slate and not one he saw.** The clerk's part: *I do not fill it in. I fill in a box and I say what a box does.* The first thing entered in that office's book in four months on which the harbor authority did not have to write underneath itself — which was true of the twenty-sixth of January and is not true of the seventeenth of February, and the chapter says so.
- **The two-column convention, extended and named.** A diver's own figure goes in the second column when no man on a mole can check it, with the reason against it. It is used for Tarner's twenty-one minutes against the boat's nineteen, for a sounding that came up heavy, for a ring seen in an eddy, and for forty feet of the harbor authority's own marline in the sea, with the name of the man who lost it.
- **The harbor authority's statement of what it has spent since the twenty-eighth of November**, read into the hearing record of the first of March in the reeve's own hand, and **Elias Voke's statement of the capacity of this harbor to moor the fleet**, written at his own table on the Saturday night before the sitting — **the night of Saturday the twenty-fifth of February**, which is what the chapter says and is left undated on the page — for nothing, read out by him, sent to the coast office. **Both will be read by the same man in Veyr.**
- **The boy's answer in the hearing record of the first of March**, entered in the ledger's margin in the clerk's own hand and initialled, unsummarized: that the fourth week fell due on the twenty-first and was not paid, that he did not ask, and that it was paid at seven o'clock that morning out of a column he worked out himself on a slate on Saturday night.
- **The second seal read into the hearing record of the first of March on the reeve's own motion**, with the day-count and not the date, so that no man in Veyr can read the twenty-ninth as the twenty-ninth of January.

### 4. The three storm-exposed recoveries, and how each was funded before it was attempted

1. **The ninth seam's mooring, Friday the seventeenth of February.** Funded by the harbor authority out of its own repair account, on Elias Voke's own account so that the notice's third clause does not reach it, with Tam Slee hired out of the *Grey Widow* for the day and Neale as the named tender. **Funded and named a week earlier; the clerk's slip says six in the morning to six in the evening and it is done at four o'clock.** Storm-exposed: a south-easter, rain, and a hard swell on the face. **It took two goes on the same day because the shackle was cast in a sleeve of the mole's own foundation stone.** The mooring is in service from four o'clock. **The yard was paid nothing and lost a day it could not sell.**
2. **The herring fleet's winter warp along the north wall, Monday the twenty-seventh of February.** **Ninety fathoms of the harbor authority's own tarred marline, bought on the seventeenth of January with the fourth set of moorings.** Funded when Kest said in his own barge on the Thursday the twenty-third that the authority had no money for the labor and named its rate — twenty-six gross, a bad rate, refused as a figure and taken as a fact. The gang could not lay it: **three men off from the twenty-fourth of February until the fourth of March, being Rowlandike, Petch and Wil Slee, whose brother Tam went down twice on the seventeenth.** Storm-exposed: a hard north-easter. **Forty feet of the ninety went over the wall head and out into the harbor at twenty past twelve, on a shackle of the harbor authority's own moorings gear, and the crew is paid in a wall with fifty fathoms on it where ninety belonged.**
3. **A herring skipper's ring and warp off the north wall, Tuesday the twenty-eighth of February.** Edric Pringle's, of the *Anna Beth* — the woman who was difficult about nine marks on the twenty-sixth of January and paid them. **Funded before anybody touched a boat: fourteen gross, a crate of fish from the mutual account, and the cause stated out loud by the yard before anybody said it.** Storm-exposed: the same wind. **No permit, no diver, no reason in any office's book — because the work is a herring skipper's own ground tackle on his own account at the north wall, landward of the third seam, and no notice has ever closed water — and the yard keeps the practice in its own book anyway and will not have the two confused.** The ring came up with nine feet of warp still bent through the shackle and the splice was good and was cut anyway, because there is no time on the first of March.

**Every one of the three spent a day the crew cannot replace, and the batch's own accounting of it is a labor ledger in which the yard appears once, as a record, and twice, as a bad rate.**

### 5. The dated positions of the charter, the berths, the ninth seam and the wages

- **The one-season provisional working charter** is unchanged: to the last day of March, inside the harbor line bounded by the outer marks, **suspendable after an inspection for cause by one man with a key and a book.** Kest has said in the hearing record of the first of March that **whatever comes back from Veyr decides whether there is a second season, that there may not be one, and that the yard's part in that is nothing.** The suspended-instrument warning is not repeated in this batch.
- **The ninth seam's mooring is WORKED and IN SERVICE from four o'clock on Friday the seventeenth of February**, on the ring of the fourth set bought on the seventeenth of January, and **a mooring is a place to tie a boat and a berth is a place to lie in a wind, and Elias Voke will not let the two be the same thing in a piece of paper.** **The fouling on the ground line between the third and the ninth seams is untouched and is now a fact in a reason in an office's own book.**
- **The north berth is still out of service** and is still the one that takes a north-easter, and on the last three it took eleven boats' ground tackle off men who could not pay for it and one boat off a man of sixty-two who has just paid nine winters and cannot pay the tenth. **There are forty-one boats in the basin on the first of March where the harbor authority's clerk said forty on Thursday the twenty-sixth, and the difference of one boat between two figures in the same harbor in the same week is the whole of the difference between a berth short and a harbor that is short.**
- **Nineteen winters of dues are disputed and unpaid and were not touched in February**, because the money went on four sets of moorings and a season of warp, and the reeve read that out in a room on the first of March and said he would have said it first.
- **The wages.** All four of the first four weeks of a wage at four marks a week are paid, the last of them on Wednesday the first of March at seven in the morning, **a week after it fell due and out of the fourth week's own arithmetic, and the boy has not asked for it and has been entered in a hearing record saying so.** The five weeks and the sixth fall in March and are not paid and are not discussed.
- **The hard at Ashtide** was applied for by Voke on the ninth of November and is unanswered in eleven weeks, and it is in his statement of capacity, and the *Kittiwake* is on the hard in Morrow Quay with her bilge blocked in and no cradle and both facts are in the same document.
- **The coast office's word is answered.** It asked on the twenty-eighth of November, in four lines, for a statement of the capacity of this harbor to moor the fleet, and **the answer is a fisher's document and not the harbor authority's and not the yard's, and neither the yard nor the office can fix it.**

### 6. The bodies and the crew

- **Calder Marr**, 32, a line diver with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice in Volume 01 and not used again. **No dive of his own in this batch, and he states on the mole on the seventeenth of February that he was the diver and that the thing that stopped him is a piece of paper, and that he does not know whether that is justice or a waste, and asks that it be entered in a municipal book that he had no right to an opinion in front of a clerk.** **His tinnitus is named again, in Chapter 67, for the fifth time since the fourteenth of October**, and Nessa Pike tells him what it is in a year: a hole where a bell used to be that fills in when somebody says the sentence out loud.
- **Nessa Pike** overrules him and wins again — she will not have a boy of twenty-two take a heaving line in a northeast, and there is no rule and she is not making one — and prices things in the order she is asked, and enters a boy's refusal to work as a fact in his own words, and pays a hired hand for a day he told her he could not do and gives him the fish, and states that a man who can do his own arithmetic is a man nobody has to lie to about money.
- **Bevin Rook**, 22, left arm in a sling, hand crushed in October, **wrist crushed again on the twelfth of January and five weeks and two days old on the seventeenth of February. June is still the bonesetter's sentence and not his and not a promise and not a lie.** He refuses figures in the yard's own book, refuses a heaving line, refuses to write another crew's reason for them, asks for the harbor authority's ledger to write the record of a dive he was not in, and **comes within four inches of being asked what came out of a man's mouth on the eighteenth of January and is not asked and enters that neither of them asked.**
- **Elias Voke**, herring skipper of the *Anna Ray*, 51, nineteen winters, **writes the statement of capacity himself, for nothing, and puts the north berth, the ninth seam and the boy's name in it, and says in a room that a skipper who has never asked to see a book in his life saw the yard's book in January and that is the only recommendation he has ever had for a man he did not know.** **Neave Tarrant**, of the third lay, nine winters of his own, is out in the blow on the twenty-eighth of February and is not back till Wednesday.
- **Edric Pringle**, of the *Anna Beth*, fifty-four, difficult about nine marks in January, and the yard that freed her warp on the twenty-sixth of January lost her ring on the twenty-eighth of February, and she asked for the cause to be said by the yard first.
- **Neale**, foreman of the harbor's tide gang, who says the price of the fourth set of moorings out loud in his own mouth a month after the reeve said it, and who is out three men, **one of them Wil Slee, whose brother Tam went down twice on the seventeenth under his eye and has said nothing about it.**
- **Noll Tarner**, 34, of the *Sea Wren*, with **Wend Crale** and **Piet Marrow, fifteen, who cannot read.** A rival crew, not a villain: he took a job at Ashtide in November without telling his own crew first and says so in two sentences and would do it again. He asks *whose book is the day in*, and he says the sentence nobody in the Free Keel says — *if a man in Veyr ever comes up that road, the first thing he will learn about this harbor is that a yard put its own errors on a public sheet and asked a fleet to check them, and I have never in my life seen a fleet check anything, and I do not think they are going to start with us.*
- **Wend Crale** has a broken finger on the twenty-eighth of February, says so at eight in the morning, is not asked to be fit, is paid for two days anyway and is given a third of a crate of fish.
- **Corvin Sarn**, the harbor's bellwright, is the harbor's own and not the Free Keel's, and **hears the answer nine times in five months and has never heard a voice in it, and on the twenty-seventh of February there is one, and he is a man who works with bells and does not know what it is, and it goes in the book in his own words.** He teaches nobody.
- **Sella Brigg**, whose cover went off the slate on the twenty-ninth of October and whose entered third of the iron — a true figure of six marks, eight shillings and tenpence, there being no coin for the two thirds of a penny missing off a third of twenty marks — was paid at the round figure of **six marks at the counter on the sixth of February**, with the **eight shillings and tenpence entered in her name on the same line, undiminished and unpaid**, and she said out loud that she is not going to be handed a tidy sum and be told the rest of it is understood, and she named the two men a yard can hire off the wall in a blow.
- **Mara Venn**, thirty, no standing, a trade, **who says *do not make me useful*, prices a refusal in the order she is asked, asks for six marks in daylight and does not ask twice, and says in a hearing room before she is asked that the room will not get a conservator's opinion out of her. She does not break with the Registry in this batch or in this volume, and nothing here is a step toward it that she has not chosen.**
- **Harl Dace** of the *Grey Widow*, whose cover is still the only cover in the harbor and still has not been asked about.
- **Alden Marr**, at the far bench, silent since the thirteenth of November, not asked for anything, and Calder Marr does not go. No fragment, no report talk, no second story.

### 7. New entries in the delayed note, and what they are not

**The eleventh logged occasion is Monday the twenty-seventh of February**, the going out, at the top of the flood off the south end, the harbor's three at nine minutes past ten, the answer a hand's width of a second under the third, the same second as the nineteenth of January and the eleventh and the sixth of December. **There is a call in it, and it is longer than the one of the twenty-sixth of December and further off, and it is a grown man's, and it is not written as a voice, and there is no source.**

**The count of occasions logged with no call in them remains two, the eleventh and the nineteenth of January, and the boy enters that count as a fact and not a question, and enters that this is not one of them, and nobody in the boat asks him a question about it and he has not got one.** **Corvin Sarn enters separately that he heard it and cannot place it.** **Nobody may name it, nobody may treat two empty answers as an answer, and the answer inside the note remains reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.**

**Two occasions were heard and not entered and neither of the two is an occasion:** on the mole steps on the evening of **Thursday the twenty-third of February** the core answered the harbor's tide bell a hand's width of a second late and neither Calder Marr nor the boy wrote it down, because an occasion is a thing a man goes out in a boat to find out; and the answer on the morning of the first of March, heard in a hearing room with a municipal ledger open on the table, which the boy would not put a line of his own into and which is **the twelfth occasion since the twenty-sixth of October and is not entered anywhere at all**. **The count of logged occasions is eleven and the span still begins on the twenty-sixth of October. There is no thirteenth, and there was never an occasion on the eleventh of February: two unentered hearings stand, the twenty-third of February and the first of March, and nothing else.**

### 8. What the batch may not be used for

**The run under the town is not touched in this batch.** Four hundred feet of ordinary figures stand as they stood on the nineteenth of January — nine at the south end with the diver's own seven, ten and a half at the north end with his own eleven, seven and three-eighths at the mole's root, five and a quarter sounding landward under a stone quay — and **nothing in these ten chapters explains it, names it, or sends anybody after it at night.** The twelfth seam, the ninth seam, the north wall and the first and second seams are ordinary ground and ordinary work, and the two objects are still two objects: **nine and a quarter feet is the *Aster Gannet's* ferry plate beyond the eleventh seam and is not one of the run's figures.** The reeve's ruling that no line may be drawn between the three points **stands and is not lifted, and nothing in this batch draws one.** The compartment is not referred to and **no chapter states or implies that the sealed case was in standing space number four.** The dressed aperture and the fouling that gives no hour are not referred to. **The nine-line mark is not explained and the Nine Harbors, the Quietening and the Crown Seal are not named.** Ilya Serr, Halven Rook and Captain Veyra Holt do not appear anywhere in the batch, on paper or otherwise. **No custody object is asked for and none returns, and the yard does not ask for the custody room.** **The municipal claim on the third item stands and is not withdrawn.** The hearing room is used once, in Chapter 70, and is the volume's own machinery.

### 9. The costs this volume may not waive again

- **Eleven marks in a bag, one mark on the note, two crowns in a tin, a fourth week's wage paid a week late out of a column the boy worked out himself, and forty marks to the boy and forty marks to Mara Venn still standing whole and still unpaid.**
- **A harbor authority's own shackle put through a stone post that never had a shackle in it, and a herring skipper's ring and forty-one feet of warp in the sea because the yard accepted a bad iron and worked on it anyway.** A poor crew does not get rescued: the skipper paid fourteen and took the loss and asked for the cause to be said first, and Nessa Pike's line about a poor crew is not waived by a crate of fish.
- **Forty feet of the harbor authority's winter marline gone out of ninety, and a wall with fifty fathoms on it where ninety belonged, on the first of March, in a north-easter, with forty-one boats in the basin.**
- **Three tide-gang men stood off from the twenty-fourth of February to the fourth of March, and one of them is the brother of the man who went down twice on the seventeenth.**
- **A plate in a mole that is now in a reason in an office's own book, written by a man who has no standing to write one, saying that the fouling on the third-to-ninth line is a fact of the office's account and not of the office's work.** The mechanism for ever reaching it is a change of politics in an office and is not a better argument from a shed, and the batch says so again.
- **A record in a man's keeping.** Eleven copies on the yard's own count, a count in a naval slate that is out of date, a count in the circuit office's own type that is out of date on a paper that was re-sealed today, and **the Free Keel yard's book is now the book of another crew's work.** Nobody has asked who the record is for and the answer is still not in the yard.
- **The concealment's second part, extended and not broken.** The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. The false line on the unverified slate is still false. **The boy entered on the twenty-third of February that neither he nor Calder Marr has asked the other, and that two of them keeping a thing out of a book is twice as safe and half as honest, and that he would rather it was half as honest than twice as safe.** The woman with no standing left still knows what is in the anchor and has still not been asked, and in Chapter 70 she said in a room, in advance, that she is a door that has been tried and that she will not answer for it.
- **A coast office's word answered by a fisher's document the yard did not write and cannot fix, and a second season that may not exist, and the yard's part in it is nothing.**
- **A supply problem the yard now understands to the last mark and has not solved:** the pump cannot be hired and will not be fitted before the eighth of March, the air pot is a third gone and a quarter of a mark worse than in January, the *Kittiwake* is on the hard with no cradle, and a crew of three men and a book's worth of hands, which is what a notice naming all three of them is worth on the market for hiring a crew in this harbor.

### 10. Proposed canon changes

**One, and it is an addition and not a correction, and it is a date-count discipline rather than a rule.** The circuit office's second seal is owed on the twenty-ninth day from the thirtieth of December, which is the twenty-seventh of January. **Chapter 70 has the reeve order the reeve's own book to carry the day-count and not the date**, so that no later volume can read *the twenty-ninth* as the twenty-ninth of January, and the same instruction is now in `outline/volume-02.md` lock L24's file through this entry. **No bible file is amended and no rule changes.**

**One reconciliation, flagged rather than hidden.** **The ninth seam's mooring is worked and returned to service in Chapter 66**, which changes the position it stood in at the volume's opening, at Chapter 51, at 53, at 57, at 58 and at 60, where it was bought back and not worked. The consequence is written into the chapters and not smoothed: **a mooring is not a berth, the north berth is still out, one berth is still short on the first of March, and the fouling on the third-to-ninth line is untouched and is now on the page in a reason in an office's own book.** The volume's clock is unchanged.

**No new antagonist, institution, enemy, stage or named route appears in this batch.** The only new named people are ordinary working people: Noll Tarner, Wend Crale, Piet Marrow, Edric Pringle, Neave Tarrant, and the hands of the *Anna Beth*. **Sera Mott is not asked in this batch and may still be asked once in this volume, in Movement 3 or 4.**
