## Batch 0002 review repairs — the disposition of every finding

A reviewer pass over Chapters 61–70, the four state files, `outline/volume-02.md`, the queued `batch-0003/PROMPT.md` and Chapters 51–60 for the calendar returned **FIX**: nine required fixes and three prose improvements. The reviewer's own log is not kept in this repository, and `state/current.md` § *Batch 0002 review repairs* holds the short form. **No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut for length, and no planned plot was altered** — Movement 2, the supply problem, the rival crew, the second seal, the *Sea Wren*'s page in the book, the warp, the statement of capacity and the ending are all as `outline/volume-02.md` has them, and `outline/ending.md`, `outline/series.md` and all six bible files were not touched.

**The calendar was checked first, because six of the nine findings were calendar or figure defects.** The anchor is **1 January a Sunday**, confirmed by 43 of the 46 tight weekday-and-date pairings across both volumes and by `outline/volume-02.md` lock L1, which is correct and was not edited. 1 February and 1 March are therefore both Wednesdays, 23 January and 30 January are Mondays, and 23 February is a Thursday.

1. **Sella Brigg's third was four different figures in one chapter and none of them was a third of twenty marks.** Chapter 65 said six marks, then six marks and eightpence, then six marks and a half-crown, while the bag arithmetic at the same page only closed on the round six. **A third of twenty marks is 1,066 and two thirds of a penny, and no sum of coin is a third of twenty marks, because a mark is a hundred and sixty pence and a hundred and sixty is not divisible by three.** Rewritten so the boy states the true figure as **six marks, eight shillings and tenpence**, which is a third of twenty marks to the nearest penny, and says there is no coin for the two thirds of a penny; **the yard pays the round six marks and the eight shillings and tenpence is entered in her name, undiminished and unpaid**, and she says out loud that she is not going to be handed a tidy sum and be told the rest of it is understood. The bag still goes from twenty-two to sixteen.
2. **The going out's eight marks had no account.** Chapter 68 paid it, entered it under a rule, and then read out a column of fourteen out of twenty-six that does not contain it; the yard's twelve and the bag's thirteen, which the next two chapters and the volume's closing figure all depend on, leave no room for eight marks anywhere. **Kept the thirteen and made the eight entered and not paid**, which is the batch's own subject — a yard that enters what it cannot pay — and which the going-out rule in the queued prompt now carries. Two new debts are named in § 2 above and neither may be quietly reduced later.
3. **Chapter 68 gave two counts of its own event.** Line 9 said the first time since January, line 81 said the second time since the sixth of December, and 6 December was the core recovery and not a going out. **All three accounts now read as one: the first time this yard has paid for a going out out of its own pocket, the eleventh of January having been the harbor's money.**
4. **Four impossible weekday-and-date pairings in the prose.** Chapter 61's *Monday the twenty-sixth* is now *Saturday the twenty-first* with the wall wipe on Monday the twenty-third, and its two *Monday morning* references to the reason going in are now *Thursday morning*, which agrees with Chapter 62 and Chapter 63. Chapter 67's *Friday the twentieth* is now *Monday the twentieth*. Chapter 70's *Monday the twenty-third of February* is now *Thursday the twenty-third*, which agrees with Chapters 68 and 69.
5. **The queued `batch-0003/PROMPT.md` calendar was corrupt from 18 March on.** Corrected against 1 March a Wednesday, and the charter's end date changed from *Wednesday the thirty-first of March* to **Friday the thirty-first of March**, which is the volume's clock. The claim that every date had been re-measured twice was not true of those entries and has been replaced with a true statement and an instruction that a chapter outranks the grid.
6. **The February calendar paragraph in this file asserted and denied the same fact in one breath** — *20 a Friday* against 1 February a Wednesday, and a Mondays list that was all Thursdays. Rewritten in full, with the Mondays in February given correctly as the 6th, 13th, 20th and 27th, and the slate wall's wipes placed on real Mondays.
7. **The three unentered bell occasions were not the three in the state files.** The prose contains **two**: the mole steps on the evening of Thursday the twenty-third of February, which Chapter 67 says in terms *was not an occasion*, and the hearing room on the morning of the first of March, which is the twelfth occasion since the twenty-sixth of October and is entered nowhere. **There was no occasion on the eleventh of February and there is no collar anywhere in Chapters 61–70.** Corrected here, in `state/open-threads.md` and in the queued prompt, and the two occasions logged with no call in them are now distinguished from the two that were never entered at all.
8. **Voke's statement was dated Saturday the twenty-eighth of February in two state files**, and the twenty-eighth is a Tuesday. The chapters leave it as *Saturday night*; the Saturday before the first of March is **the twenty-fifth**, and that is what both files now say.
9. **`state/current.md` and `state/open-threads.md` both reported a marker that does not exist.** `workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/.done` is present. Both claims are withdrawn in place. `state/phase-ledger.json` is genuinely stale and is correctly left for the controller, and no marker was written.

**Two transcription errors the review did not raise, found while re-deriving the money chain and fixed here.** The table in § 2 above gave the twenty-seventh of February a gross of twenty-two where the chapter reads **twenty-six**, which is the only figure from which the yard's twelve and the bag's thirteen both follow. And Chapter 68's money reading said the hand's second day *goes in the twenty-eighth's column and not in this one* while the same paragraph's total included all four marks; the clause now describes the second day's two marks as shown again against the day he worked, so the column and the sentence agree. **No figure moved.** The full chain re-derives cleanly: 4, 20, 17, 12, 22, 16, 12, 4, 1, 1, 13, 15, **11** in the bag, and 13, 13, 13, 8, 8, 8, 8, 4, 4, 4, 4, 1, **1** on the note.

**Prose improvements, applied.** Chapter 65's day-count across the seventh to the seventeenth of February was *eleven days* from a promise made on the twenty-eighth of January and is now **twenty days**, which matches the *twenty-four days* the same chapter counts correctly on the twenty-first. Chapter 68's four-men read-back named a diver's tender who was not in its own list and a shipwright who is named nowhere; **the fourth man is now Nessa Pike, who is the diver's tender and the only shipwright in that boat by the bible's own entry**, and the gloss matches the list. Chapter 63's projection of the thirty-first said the book would be at twelve from a bag of twenty without the twenty-eighth's three marks of air and rope, and now carries them.

## Batch 0003 as written (Chapters 71–80), Volume 02 — binding on Chapters 81 onward

**Movement 3, the paper that asks for the book. It answers the request, has a working boat taken in daylight on the strength of the yard's own wall, watches the yard's own record used against it in a room it cannot leave, and ends with the seven copies the yard still holds and cannot un-hold. Nothing in it is resolved.** The chapters are the authority on what happened. Everything below can be checked on the page.

### 1. The calendar the batch actually ran on

**`outline/volume-02.md` lock L1's grid, unchanged, and the chapters ran on it without an exception.** 1 March a Wednesday, and therefore 2 a Thursday, 3 a Friday, 4 a Saturday, 5 a Sunday, 6 a Monday, 7 a Tuesday, 8 a Wednesday, 9 a Thursday, 10 a Friday, 11 a Saturday, 12 a Sunday, 13 a Monday, 14 a Tuesday, 15 a Wednesday, 16 a Thursday, 17 a Friday, 18 a Saturday, 19 a Sunday, 20 a Monday, 21 a Tuesday, 22 a Wednesday, 23 a Thursday, 24 a Friday, 25 a Saturday, 26 a Sunday, 27 a Monday, 28 a Tuesday, 29 a Wednesday, 30 a Thursday, **31 a Friday, and the one-season provisional working charter runs out at midnight on that Friday.** 1 April is a Saturday. **The Mondays in March are the 6th, 13th, 20th and the 27th.** The hearing room's slate wall was wiped **Monday the thirteenth of March at eight** and Kest put nothing on it afterwards, on purpose, and said so in the room; it goes again on **Monday the twentieth** and **Monday the twenty-seventh**, whether there is a sitting or not, and the batch's one sitting is on the **fifteenth**, between two wipes, with the wall blank.

**The day-counts, as the page gives them, and none of them computed from a habit.** The third seal was affixed on **Monday the sixth of March** and the **fourteen runs from the date at the foot of the paper, not the day the party had it, so the fourteenth day is the twentieth of March, a Monday.** The request of the thirtieth of December to the sixth of March is **sixty-six days**. The fourth request is dated the **sixteenth, a Thursday**, and the fourteen runs from the sixteenth to the **thirtieth, which is a Thursday and the day before the charter runs out.** The twenty-second came from the second of March to the twenty-second. The end of the batch is the **thirty-first of March**, named on the page in three places. **A wage week falls due on a Tuesday in this batch — the fifth on Tuesday the seventh, and the sixth, seventh and eighth on the fourteenth, the twenty-first and the twenty-eighth — and no chapter gives a wage week a Saturday.**

**The *Vigil* tender's days on station are not stated in this batch and no day-count is invented.** Interpolate from the fourth of November at twenty days, as the Batch 0002 review ruled.

### 2. The money chain, mark by mark

**Eleven marks in a bag and two crowns in a tin on Wednesday the first of March. The tin has not been spent since.**

| Date | In | Out | Bag | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wed 1 Mar | — | — | **11** | 1 |
| Fri 3 Mar | — | air 2 | **9** | 1 |
| Mon 6 Mar | — | **3**, the circuit office's counter fee for the ground, out of a bag that had nine on the Friday | **6** | 1 |
| Tue 7 Mar | — | 4, the boy's **fifth** week, **not paid**, entered as unpaid in his own right hand and not discussed | **6** | 1 |
| Wed 8 Mar | — | **5**, the pump's iron, the housing fitted before the tide turned; **nineteen marks of iron go onto the note and the creditor's own carting, a mile and a half at a shilling and threepence, is not on the page and is not paid** | **1** | **20** |
| Fri 10 Mar | **20 gross**, Sella Brigg, the second seam, her own account, with the pump hired at four | the eighth 3, the share 3, air 2, rope 1, Tam Slee 2, the pump hire 4 = 15; the yard's **5** | **6** | 20 |
| Tue 14 Mar | — | **4**, a fortnight of bread, oil and candles, on the note and out of the bag the same afternoon | **2** | **24** |
| Tue 21 Mar | **14 gross**, Harl Dace, half a day, a shackle of his own at the first seam | the eighth 2, the share 2, air 2, a hand 2 = 8; the yard's **6** | **8** | 24 |
| Tue 28 Mar | — | **3**, a fortnight of bread, oil and candles, on the note and out of the bag | **5** | **27** |
| Fri 31 Mar | — | — | **5** | **27** |

**The bag closes Movement 3 at five marks, the note at twenty-seven, the tin with two crowns, on Friday the thirty-first of March, the last day of the charter.** The full chain re-derives: **11, 9, 6, 6, 1, 6, 2, 8, 5, 5**, and **1, 1, 1, 1, 20, 20, 24, 24, 27, 27**.

**The chandler's note, and the three counts of it, which are now all on the page in one paragraph in the creditor's own mouth at the head of the fish dock and one in the crew's book.** The **mark figure is twenty-seven** and it has moved all month. The **three counts of the weeks are static and all three are true of their own counts**: the **creditor's is twenty-two**, the **yard's is thirteen** and is a figure in Calder Marr's head and is in no book in this coast, **and the last figure ever written in the page says eleven.** Neither man has moved his number since the fourth of January, which is **twelve weeks** by the calendar at the thirty-first of March. **The note was not re-written, was not asked to be re-written, and the yard says so out loud, in the open, at the head of its own dock on the last day of the month, and the reason is given: a yard that goes to its creditor with no date in its hand is a yard that has admitted it has nothing.** The chandler offers a fortnight in his own hand for nothing and says the yard not coming is worth more than the fortnight. **He came down to the yard to make the offer and did not go up to the shop, which is the staging Chapter 80 now carries; the refusal is said out loud to a man, in a shed doorway, and not in a road, and no cost was dropped in the change.** This is the paying of lock L2's deferred choice, in the form the volume's resolution allows.

**Debt, all standing, all unpaid, none reduced anywhere in this batch.** **Forty marks to Bevin Rook** (sixteen for the reading of the book from the fourteenth of October and twenty-four for the hand from the twenty-ninth), whole, not asked to be reduced, and named in a hearing record of the first of March that has now been read against the yard's record by a naval officer. **Forty marks to Mara Venn, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, and on the twenty-second of March she says out loud that she is not going to ask for them in a month in which a form cost this harbor a pump, and that if a mark of it is offered she will walk it down the dock and put it on the wet stones.** **A certificate of method is six marks and three weeks and nobody pays it and nobody can hurry it.** **A wage at four marks a week from the first of February: the first four weeks are paid, and four further weeks of March have fallen due — the fifth, sixth, seventh and eighth, on Tuesday the seventh, the fourteenth, the twenty-first and the twenty-eighth — and none of them is paid and none of them is discussed, and they are entered in a column with no adjectives in it. Sixteen marks of wage are owed on the last day of the charter.** **Eight marks and a day of a boat to the harbor's bellwright, entered under a rule on Monday the twenty-seventh of February and still not paid, and Chapter 80 says the yard cannot find them before October and that nothing in this harbor comes to eight marks before October.** **Eight shillings and tenpence of Sella Brigg's true third of twenty marks, entered in her name on the sixth of February, undiminished and unpaid, and put in the book again in Chapter 74 beside a ring that came up.**

**The pump, and what it is worth.** The new housing cost **nineteen marks of iron on the note and five marks out of the bag**, was fitted on **Wednesday the eighth of March**, and from the tenth the yard had **the only gear in this harbor a man can hire that is not the harbor authority's**. It was hired out twice in March, at four marks a day. It is also the whole of the answer to the supply problem, and the batch's own irony is written into the book on the tenth and read out again on the thirty-first: **the only reason there is a four-mark line in the column of the tenth is that the housing came up the coast on the Wednesday, and the only reason the yard needed it is that a rival crew's pump went into a lighter on the fourth.**

**The three days refused and the days lost, entered in the yard's own book in the first column because a man on the dock can check them.** **Sella Brigg's twenty-two marks on Tuesday the seventh of March, refused and not paid, cause: the party was absent in a city on an instrument of the circuit office's**, entered under the Monday and not the Tuesday. **A herring skipper's twenty marks on Friday the thirty-first, refused, cause: the instrument runs out at midnight and does not go into April**, and the answer given to *who do I ask* is that the yard does not know. **The two days of the walk to the counter are entered as two days and not as a figure**, with the shipwright's objection signed against it and the holder's statement that he does not know what a day is worth.

### 3. Every document and instrument created in this batch

- **The ground of the second of March**, four paragraphs, in Calder Marr's own left hand, sent to the circuit office on Monday the sixth: what the sheet is; the count told twice in public with both dates and neither struck out; the party cannot deliver what is not in its hand, with the four other holders named; **and the fourth paragraph, that the yard's own book contains the twenty-sixth of January, which is the *Sea Wren's* day and not the yard's, and that the *Sea Wren* is not a party to the instrument, has not been asked and cannot be asked, so the party cannot deliver a leaf either.** Read back four times in the shed on the day it was written. Entered into the file on the sixth with a receipt and a copy of the entry.
- **The third seal of the circuit office, Monday the sixth of March**, the same nineteen words with the first clause changed: *That a ground having been received, the said request stands unanswered in this office, and this office awaits the same.* The clerk at the counter says the line has been in that office's type since before the year turned and that a copied file is what gets quoted.
- **The confiscation form of Saturday the fourth of March**, a naval instrument: *Second sheet. Held in this harbor pending verification of entries concerning a contested material entered in the fish market of Morrow Quay. The vessel, her gear, and her papers are held.* Then: *That the vessel so held is not claimed to contain any material whatever.* Then: *That no person is charged and that the master is requested to sign below.* **The endorsement carries four lines in the office's type and five in the master's words with the master's name against them**, including that the twenty-sixth of January is a day in the Free Keel yard's book and on its wall, that the owner did not write it and cannot strike it, and where a copy of the *Sea Wren's* log is read out in a market. **The four lines of the office's own type say that the vessel is a working salvage boat of no crew of any association, that she carries no transparent material and none is alleged, that her master is a member of the Free Keel association AND HAS BEEN OFF ITS SLATE SINCE NOVEMBER, and that no person of the Free Keel yard has been served or named. The twenty-ninth of October is Sella Brigg's cover and not Tarner's name, and Tarner says in Chapter 62 that a man called Fisk took him off the slate on the Friday in November; the endorsement now says November and the phrase "all four are true" holds on the page.**
- **The naval slate of the eighteenth of December**, with the *Vigil's* count of **ten** on it, entered into the harbor authority's ledger on the fifteenth as the vessel's figure with its date, beside the yard's two dates of the twenty-third of November and the eighteenth of January. **Neither figure is scratched out and the difference of one is entered as a difference and not as an error.**
- **The vessel's request of the twelfth of March** for the production of the party's book, and the reeve's ruling of the fifteenth that **a request for a piece of paper is not an inspection of the holder within the meaning of the charter**, in which Kest **says the word *inspection* nine times and the word *suspend* once in a room with a naval officer writing, and says he did it because this office does not have secrets about its own instruments.**
- **Mara Venn's refusal in the hearing room of the fifteenth**, in her own words, unsummarized, entered at her own asking, and Kest's line under it: a ground stated by a person with no standing is still a ground, and in about two years somebody is going to find four such entries in one ledger and draw a conclusion, and the conclusion will be correct and there will be nothing on the page to stop it.
- **Kest's statement of the thirty-first of March**, in his own hand, on the shed wall, in the authority's ledger and in a file he does not control: the charter runs to the end of today and does not extend into April; this office cannot renew it and holds nothing that renews it; whatever comes out of Veyr about a second season is a date this office neither controls nor has been told of; and on the day it ends the holder is a man with a book, a wall and a pump, and no paper. **There was a version that said *I regret* and it was burnt.**
- **The fourth request of the circuit office, dated Thursday the sixteenth of March and delivered on the eighteenth**: the count is **eleven**, corrected in the office's own type in a day off a paper of the harbor authority's that went up the coast on the fifteenth, and the party is required to deliver the said public sheet and the eleven copies of it now held, wherever held, together with the book of the party from the fourteenth of October to the date of the paper. **It is answered and refused by nobody.**
- **The eighth thing on the market wall, Saturday the eighteenth of March**, in Calder Marr's own hand: the eleven copies named with the place of each, and four lines under them — that the party has not answered and has not refused and is in that state of its own motion; that it will not deliver the copies in its own keeping and gives the reason in one sentence, *that a record of which there is one is a thing that can be taken away, and that a yard which cannot pay a wage in March has nothing whatever to protect itself with except a thing in two places*; that it will not ask any person holding a copy to deliver it, has not asked and will not, and names the four holders so that any man entitled to ask them may do so without coming to the yard; **and that it will deliver to the circuit office the entry of any single day of the yard's book, on the written asking of the crew whose day it is, unshortened, with its reason and its two columns entire.**
- **The leaf of the twenty-sixth of January, copied out by Bevin Rook on the twelfth of March**, with the reason, the ground, the two columns and the read-backs, countersigned by the holder, and delivered into the keeping of Noll Tarner, whose own logbook is in a naval file and names him and not his crew.
- **Amos Pryde's copy of the eighth thing, paid for at a shilling**, to go up under the yard's sheet in his own hand on Monday the twenty-second.
- **The entry of the twenty-second of March in the holder's own hand**, that he has not asked Mara Venn anything else, that he came within four inches of it four times and did not, that she saw him do it this time and neither of them said a word, and that in about two years there will be a man in a room who says a woman with no standing knew what was in that anchor, and that he will be right, and that there is now one page that says she was never asked.
- **The notice nailed to the tide-gang store door on the night of Saturday the eighteenth of March, in Neale's own hand**, that a light on the mole head costs oil and the harbor authority's repair account is empty. **The light went on at dusk on the eighteenth and was taken in on Monday the twentieth, and the third lay went dark on the night of Sunday the nineteenth, and two boats of the herring fleet were out on the eighteenth, the nineteenth and the twentieth.** Chapter 78 now carries those dates on the page. **The notice was on the market wall by the second sheet and the yard read it on the morning of Tuesday the twenty-first.**
- **The third of March in the harbor authority's ledger**: where the circuit office corrected a count in its own type off a paper of Kest's, and that the man who sent it is the reeve and that it cost the harbor authority something.

### 4. The dated positions of the charter, the berths, the warp, the copies and the wages

- **The one-season provisional working charter is unchanged and it expires at midnight on Friday the thirty-first of March.** Inside the harbor line, bounded by the outer marks, **suspendable after an inspection for cause by one man with a key and a book.** Kest rules in a room on the fifteenth that a request for a paper is not an inspection, and says the word *suspend* out loud on purpose and says what that costs. On the thirty-first he states in writing that this office cannot renew it and holds nothing that renews it. **There has been no word from Veyr about a second season and the officer of the *Vigil* says in a room that he does not know either.**
- **The copies of the public sheet of the fourteenth of October: eleven, and the eleven are now named in public.** Two on the yard's own market wall, five rolled in oilcloth in the bench chest against the bond of the charter, three on the walls of crews who are not parties to any instrument, **and one with Amos Pryde at his stall, issued on the eighteenth of January at his own asking to be kept and not returned.** **All four outside the yard's keeping have now refused, in public and in a market, and one of them has refused on the ground that a coaster's master on a schedule is a man who needs a paper and a man who gets a paper for nothing has been given something.** **The circuit office's type says eleven as of the sixteenth of March and the *Vigil's* slate says ten as of the eighteenth of December, and both stand.**
- **The berths.** The **north berth is still out of service** and is the one that takes a north-easter. The **ninth seam's mooring is in service and is a mooring and not a berth.** One berth short and forty-one boats in the basin on the first of March. **The third lay fills, the first of April is a Saturday, and the fleet will want the third lay.** The **hard at Ashtide is still unanswered** and is named in Voke's statement, which is now on its way to a coast office and has been read against the yard's record in a hearing room.
- **Fifty fathoms of the herring fleet's winter warp lie on the north wall where ninety were to lie, and forty feet of the harbor authority's own marline is in the sea.**
- **The *Kittiwake* is on the hard with her bilge blocked in and no cradle under her, and has been since the twenty-eighth of November, and the harbor's carpenters are free now that the fleet is in and have not been to her.**
- **Three men of the tide gang went off on the twenty-fourth of February and are off again on the first of April.** Wil Slee's brother Tam went down twice under Neale's eye on the seventeenth of February, and on the tenth of March **Wil put the guide line himself and his brother dived on Sella Brigg's account.**
- **The wages.** Four weeks paid from the first of February, the fourth a week late and out of the boy's own arithmetic. **Four weeks of March unpaid, entered in a column with no adjectives, and the fifth of them fell due on Tuesday the seventh and was entered as unpaid on the day.**

### 5. The bodies and the crew

- **Calder Marr**, 32, line diver, no dive of his own in this batch, and no power stage of any kind. **The condition is NOT named in this batch.** Chapter 74 has the last sentence of the reason recited for the sixth time since the fourteenth of October and nothing else, and the word does not appear on the page. **It was last named in Chapter 67, for the fifth time, and a later volume may name it once more.** He comes within four inches of asking Mara Venn what is in the anchor for the fourth time in five months and does not, and **for the first time two other people in the room can see that he did not.**
- **Nessa Pike** overrules him and wins again — she refuses Tarner's request to strike an entry out and tells him why, she refuses a boy of fifteen for a search and says it is her and not the law, she refuses to let a man walk a day for nothing and is overruled and signs her objection, she makes the circuit office's endorsement box read aloud before there is a signature on it, and she is the shipwright who signs Calder Marr's three marks and two days and his statement that he does not know what a day is worth against her own objection. **She reads the figures out before a job is paid — Dace's fourteen on the twenty-first of March, and the yard's own five and the note's twenty-seven on the thirty-first — and the phrase *reads a figure out to a customer before he pays it* belongs to Bevin Rook on a mole, not to her.**
- **Bevin Rook**, 22, left arm in a sling, the wrist nine weeks old on the tenth of March. He **finds the fourth paragraph of the ground**, reads four men back on the same day it is written four times so that nobody can say afterwards it was put in to suit somebody, counts seven copies out of the chest twice, writes the reason's fourth paragraph back three times on its own because Nessa Pike told him to, **copies out a leaf of the yard's own book for the man whose boat has been taken**, enters that there is not one line about Neave Tarrant in the book and that he looked twice, and writes the two-column entry of the tenth of March and does not read it back because it is a Tuesday and not a rule.
- **Noll Tarner**, 34, of the *Sea Wren*, **whose boat, gear, pump and papers are held at the pile under the *Vigil* from Saturday the fourth of March**, who is not a villain and does not become one, who asks for a thing, is refused in a way he can check, takes a copy of the yard's leaf, and **refuses to sign a sentence of Calder Marr's out of politeness.** His crew may go ashore twice a day. **Wend Crale** has a broken finger from the twenty-seventh of February. **Piet Marrow**, fifteen, cannot read, is refused a search and carries the news of the market questioner up the hill.
- **Elias Voke**, herring skipper of the *Anna Ray*, in the hearing room on the fifteenth, defending his own document against the yard's record at the cost of his own standing with a coast office, and saying out loud that if a fisher's document is not good enough because a yard's figures change then every skipper on this coast will learn that the way to be safe is to write nothing.
- **Sella Brigg** pays twenty on the tenth and reads the entry back as the fifth man, and on the thirty-first is owed still her true third less six marks, undiminished. **Harl Dace** of the *Grey Widow* hires on the strength of the yard's public record and says so out loud, and enters that he has had two men drowned on ground somebody else called his own. **Amos Pryde**, coaster's master of no crew of this association, refuses in a street and pays a shilling for a copy.
- **Mara Venn**, thirty, no standing, a trade, **who refuses a figure in a hearing room and in a shed, says the price of the fourth of January out loud — that the one thing the circuit office asked for cannot be done by anybody, because the person who could have done it was working in that office on the day she signed it — discloses that a man with a slate has been asking after her in a market and that he wrote down her refusals, asks to be named on the page rather than named at it, and prices herself at six marks a day.** **She does not break with the Registry in this batch or in this volume, and her break is Volume 4.**
- **Ors Kest**, 44, **who has now entered the harbor authority inside its own notice four times in his own hand, spent this office's authority four times since the twelfth of January, comes up the hill on foot for the first time in four months, rules that a request for a paper is not an inspection and pays for the ruling by saying the word *suspend* in front of the man who can use it, sends the yard's own counts up to the circuit office at his office's own cost, and tells the yard in writing on the last day of the charter that he has no favour left to give and no second season in his keeping.** **He is not a friend. He is a man whose promises have dates on them and the last date has passed.**
- **Alden Marr** is at the far bench in Chapter 80, is not asked for anything, does not read Kest's statement aloud, and Calder Marr does not go. **He is named once in Chapter 71 as the man the yard would not use to make a count of four read-backs come out right.** **No fragment, no report talk, no second story.**
- **Corvin Sarn** does not appear. His **eight marks stand owing and are named again on the thirty-first as a figure the yard cannot find before October.**
- **A man with a slate, about thirty, not naval and not from a court and not from the office that holds Mara Venn's file**, works through the fish market on **Saturday the eighteenth of March** asking four men the same four questions. **He is not an antagonist, is not named, is refused by everybody he asks, and is not referred to again.** No new institution appears. **The date is the eighteenth and not two days: Piet Marrow heard of it in the market on the eighteenth and carried it up the hill for three days, and Mara Venn's four days run from the eighteenth to the twenty-second.**
- **Sera Mott is not asked in this batch.** She may still be asked once in this volume, in Movement 4.

### 6. The delayed note, and what this batch did not do

**Nothing in this batch enters an occasion.** **The count of logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October stands at eleven, and the count of occasions logged with no call in them stands at two, the eleventh and the nineteenth of January, and neither is raised.** The two occasions that were heard and not entered in Movement 2 — the mole steps on the evening of Thursday the twenty-third of February and the hearing room on the morning of the first of March — are not referred to in these ten chapters and neither is a third. **In the last paragraph of Chapter 80 the harbor's tide bell goes and the water off the south end gives it back a hand's width of a second later and the boy does not put a pencil on the page, and no count is made and no occasion is claimed and the answer inside the note is not approached, not named and not resolved. It remains reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.**

**The concealment is hidden and its third part is the one that grew.** The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book, and not in Kest's chart notation. The one false line on the unverified slate is still false and the slate was not moved. The boy with the book has not asked and has not been asked and has now, for the fourth time, come near and not. **And the woman with no standing left, who knows what is in the anchor and has still never been asked, has been asked nothing by anybody in this batch, and on the twenty-second of March a page was made saying so, at her own asking and in the holder's own hand.** **It was told once, in Chapter 38, and it is not told a second time for nothing, and the volume closes with it hidden.**

### 7. What the batch may not be used for

**The compartment is not referred to and the three-step finding is not restated.** **The dressed aperture and the fouling that gives no hour are not referred to.** **The nine-line mark is not explained, and the Nine Harbors, the Quietening and the Crown Seal are not named.** **Ilya Serr, Halven Rook and Captain Veyra Holt do not appear in a room; Holt appears once, on the paper of the eighth of November already nailed to the market wall, as the signature of the master of the *Vigil*.** **No custody object is asked for and none returns, and the yard does not ask for the custody room; the municipal claim on the third item stands and is not withdrawn.** **The reeve's ruling that no line may be drawn between the three points stands and is not lifted, and nothing in this batch draws one; the withdrawal is still on a wall and the difference is still not stated as a measurement anywhere.** **The run under the town is not touched: four hundred feet of ordinary figures stand as they stood on the nineteenth of January, and nothing in these ten chapters explains it, names it, or sends anybody after it.** Nine and a quarter feet is the *Aster Gannet's* ferry plate and is not one of the run's figures, and the two are not fused.

**The volume's midpoint is not in this batch and is not begun.** **No claim seal is proved forged and no coast road no chart carries is described, checked or resolved.** A coast road is spoken of once, in Chapter 77, in a coaster's master's mouth, in the ordinary sense of the coasting trade and his Tuesday schedule, and it is not a road that no chart carries and it leads to nothing. **Movement 2's forty-one boats, two berths, a mole, a north berth out of service and a hard at Ashtide are a harbor's capacity and are not fused with any road.** The volume's climax is not begun.

**The bell is not an instrument.** It reads nothing, settles nothing and says no word; the going out is a person in a boat in the weather; the rope is a year and the tongue a man's day every four or five years; **the eight marks for the next going out are owing and Chapter 80 says the yard cannot find them before October.** The Bellwright stage is not taken and Corvin Sarn teaches nobody.

### 8. The costs this volume may not waive again

- **A working pump in a lighter.** From the fourth of March to the tenth the only pump in this harbor a man could hire was the harbor authority's at three a tide out of the authority's own account, and the notice of the twenty-second of March is that a man of the third lay with nine winters of his own went out in a northeast on the twenty-eighth of February and has not come back. **The fourth of March and the twenty-eighth of February are ten days apart and both are in the same book.**
- **A boat, a pump, two warps, a lead line, an oar, a tool box and a crew's papers, gone from a working boat in daylight on a form, and a crew that may go ashore twice a day.**
- **Sixteen marks of unpaid wage on the last day of a charter, in a column with no adjectives in it, and a boy who has not asked.**
- **Five marks in a bag, twenty-seven on a note, two crowns in a tin, three debts of forty, eight marks to a bellwright, and eight shillings and tenpence to a woman.**
- **A yard that has published its own count eleven times and can no longer take any of it back**, and cannot become private again without losing the only protection it has, which is the argument of Chapter 77 and the movement's turn.
- **A record that cannot be made single, and the reason why is on a page in a shed: the only person on this coast who could have made it single lost her standing on the fourth of January.**
- **A manifest — the *Sea Wren's* logbook — in a naval file, and a copy of a leaf of the yard's own book in a man's coat, and neither of them can be got back.**

### 9. Proposed canon changes

**None.** No bible file, no outline, no ending and no lock is amended by this batch. **One addition and not a correction, and it is a discipline rather than a rule:** the circuit office's file now carries three seals whose text is nineteen words each, the second and third differing only in their first clause, and the *Vigil*'s slate carries a count of ten against the yard's eleven, and both figures stand in two ledgers. **The two-count practice of the chandler's note is confirmed and now written out in full — twenty-two, thirteen, and eleven as last written — and the counts are static and are not advanced by the March fortnights.** That is what the chapters say and no state file may harden it any other way.

---

## Batch 0003 review repairs — the disposition of every finding

A reviewer pass over Chapters 71–80, the four state files, `outline/volume-02.md` and the queued `batch-0004/PROMPT.md` returned **FIX**, with seven required chapter repairs, four state-file repairs and four prose repairs. **This section is the authority for that repair; the reviewer's own log is not kept in this repository and is not cited here.** **No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut, no cost was dropped, and no planned plot was altered.** Movement 3, the confiscation, the ground of four paragraphs, the third seal, the leaf handed to Noll Tarner, the hearing of the fifteenth, the eighth thing on the wall, the two refusals, Mara Venn's price of the fourth of January and the end of the charter on the thirty-first of March are all as `outline/volume-02.md` has them, and `outline/ending.md`, `outline/series.md`, all six bible files and all twenty-four locks are untouched.

**The calendar was checked first, because five of the required repairs were broken weekday-and-date pairings and they propagate into every downstream file.** The anchor is **1 March a Wednesday**, given by `outline/volume-02.md` lock L1 and confirmed twice inside the batch itself. Therefore 4 March a Saturday, 7 March a **Tuesday**, 12 March a **Sunday**, 13 March a Monday, 16 March a **Thursday**, 18 March a Saturday, 20 March a **Monday**, 21 March a Tuesday, 28 March a **Tuesday**, 30 March a **Thursday**, 31 March a Friday. **The chapters now agree with that grid everywhere.**

1. **Five broken weekdays in the chapters, and the wrong weekday had been copied into five downstream files.** Chapter 73's *twentieth of March, a Saturday* is now **a Monday**, in both places. Chapter 74's *fifth week of a wage fell due on Saturday the seventh* is now **Tuesday the seventh**, which agrees with Chapter 75's own *on a Tuesday in March* for the seventh; and its *on Saturday a fortnight of bread, oil and candles* is now **Tuesday**, the fourteenth, which is what Chapter 80's read-out gives. Chapter 75's *Wednesday the twelfth of March* is now **Sunday the twelfth**, and its closing *one o'clock on a Wednesday* is now **one o'clock on a Sunday**, which is the hour the same chapter gives for the read-back. Chapter 77's *the fourteen runs from the sixteenth and that is a Monday, so the thirtieth* is now **a Thursday, so the thirtieth, which is a Thursday**, and Chapter 78's *came off the same bag on Saturday* is now **Tuesday**. Chapter 71's *the market on the Saturday* and *wrote the ground out again on Saturday night* are now **the Sunday**, the fifth, which is what Chapter 73 already said the boy did. **The state files and the queued prompt were corrected from the chapters and not from each other**, and § 1 and § 4 above now say that a wage week falls due on a Tuesday in this batch.
2. **The confiscation endorsement stated a false fact on the page of a batch about documents.** Chapter 72's endorsement said Tarner's master *has been off its slate since the twenty-ninth of October*, and Tarner's next line is *that is four things and they are all true*. **The twenty-ninth of October is Sella Brigg's cover** (Chapters 62 and 65) **and not Tarner's name**; Chapter 62 has Tarner say in his own mouth that a man called Fisk took him off the slate on the Friday in November, and `state/current.md` already carried *since November*. The endorsement now says **since November** and the four things are four true things. The endorsement is also now described in § 3 above, with the four lines of the office's type spelled out.
3. **Chapter 73 contradicted Chapter 74 on the pump.** Chapter 73 is narrated at about nine o'clock on the night of Tuesday the eighth and said the iron *was on the coast and would be fitted on Wednesday*, with the bag at six and the note at one; Chapter 74 has the iron arriving by handcart on the Wednesday morning, the housing fitted before the tide turned and the bag going from six to one at two o'clock that afternoon. **Chapter 73's closing paragraph now states the position as it stood at nine that night** — one mark in the bag, twenty on the note, the iron up that morning, the housing fitted, the fifth week fallen due on the seventh — and the figure-bearing fact is no longer contradicted across two consecutive chapters.
4. **Three chapters gave the market questioner three different days.** Chapter 77 and Chapter 79 both put him in the market on **Saturday the eighteenth**; Chapter 78 had Piet say he had heard it *two days before* and been carrying it *since Wednesday*. **Chapter 78 now says he heard it in the market on Saturday the eighteenth and had been carrying it up the hill for three days**, and `state/continuity.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md` and `state/open-threads.md` all carry the eighteenth instead of the fourteenth and fifteenth.
5. **Chapter 79's sixteen weeks could not be true.** Mara Venn's cup habit is anchored in her own mouth to the fourth of January, and the fourth of January to the twenty-second of March is seventy-seven days, **eleven weeks exactly**. **Sixteen weeks is now eleven weeks.**
6. **The cart's shilling and threepence was in two places and could be in neither.** Chapter 74 put it on the note; Chapter 80's read-out put it in the bag, where six less five less one shilling and three pence goes negative, and the note chain 1 → 20 → 24 → 27 has no room for it. **The yard could not have paid it out of the bag on the eighth, and it is now not on the note either: the creditor sent his own cart and would not put the carting on the page, and says why — a man who is owed nineteen marks by a yard with a mark in the bag does not add a shilling and threepence to a page for the sake of being paid it in a year.** The figure survives as a rate and not as a payment, no chain figure moved, and the bag's 6 → 1 on the Wednesday afternoon still closes. Chapter 80's read-out no longer carries the shilling and threepence.
7. **Chapter 71 had the batch's only meta-fiction and a self-contradiction in one sentence.** *…the fourth was Alden Marr at the far bench, who was not asked for anything, and who did not read it, and who is not in this chapter and is not asked.* A man who did not read it is not the fourth reader, and the sentence pointed at itself as a text, which `AGENTS.md`'s Quality Gate bars. **The fourth reader is now simply not found**: the yard had not found a fourth man that morning and had not gone and got one, and Alden Marr is named as the man it would not use to make a number come out right, and Calder Marr did not go over to him. That keeps L16 intact, keeps the rule the boy is arguing about, and removes the only place in ten chapters where the prose points at itself. **Alden is therefore at the far bench in Chapter 80 and named once in Chapter 71, and § 5 above now says so.**

**State-file repairs.**

8. **A scene was invented in the binding continuity.** This file said Nessa Pike *says in the hearing room that the seventh thing on that wall is a description of two hands and one of them is a man who does not get to be the man who goes on*, and that she *reads the figure out to a customer before he pays it*. **Neither is in Chapter 76, and the second is Chapter 78's Bevin Rook on a mole.** The invented hearing-room sentence is deleted; the credit for reading a figure out before a job is paid is given to where it is, with the two-column figure of the tenth of March and the twenty-first.
9. **The mole-head light had three dates across three files and none matched the chapter.** **Chapter 78 now carries the dates on the page**: the light went on at dusk on **Saturday the eighteenth** and was taken in on **Monday the twentieth**, Neale's notice was nailed on the night of the eighteenth, the third lay went dark on the night of **Sunday the nineteenth**, and two boats of the herring fleet were out on the eighteenth, nineteenth and twentieth. The two wrong state-file dates — *Monday the seventeenth* in `state/current.md` and *Saturday the fourteenth of March* in § 3 above — are corrected to that. **The chapter's own *he went out on the Saturday in the blow*, which is a fourth Saturday and cannot be the twenty-eighth of February, is now *he went out in the blow on the twenty-eighth of February***, which is what Chapter 69 and every other file already said.
10. **Three state files claimed the tinnitus is named in Chapter 74. It is not.** Chapter 74 has the **last sentence of the reason recited for the sixth time since the fourteenth of October** and no naming of the condition; the word does not appear on the page. **The claim is withdrawn here, in `state/current.md`, in `state/chapter-summaries.md` and in the queued `batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, which had said *named in Chapters 57, 67 and 74*.** The condition was last named in Chapter 67 for the fifth time, a later volume may name it once more, and it has not vanished silently — it is simply not used in this batch.
11. **The man with the slate was dated to the fourteenth and fifteenth of March in two files, and the man with the *fourteenth and fifteenth*** — corrected to **Saturday the eighteenth** in this file, in `state/chapter-summaries.md` and in `state/open-threads.md`, with the four days of Mara Venn's silence re-derived from the eighteenth to the twenty-second.

**Prose and shape repairs.**

12. **Chapter 80 was four rooms on one day and thirteen per cent over the guideline, the only chapter in the batch that failed lock L22's *no chapter is two rooms*.** The fish dock refusal, the reeve in the shed, **the chandler's shop up the hill**, the money read-out back in the shed, and a closing catalogue. **The shop is gone and the creditor comes down instead**: at one in the afternoon the chandler walks down past the market with the page under his arm and a paper twist in his coat, nobody sent for him and no word sent, and the three counts of the weeks, the offer of a fortnight and Calder Marr's refusal with its reason are all said at the head of the yard's own dock. **Every figure, every cost and every line of dialogue survives; only the staging moved, and the one line that named the shop — *I would like to say the reason out loud in the shop and not in the road* — is now *here, at the head of this dock, and not in a road*.** The chapter is now **one room and one day**. Lock L22's stated remedy was a split across two days, and a split was **not** taken: Volume 02 runs to Chapter 100 in `outline/volume-02.md`, Movement 4 is locked to Chapters 81–90 there, and `outline/ending.md` fixes Volume 15 at Chapters 701–760, so an eleventh chapter file would move the volume's whole downstream numbering and contradict three documents this phase may not edit. The chapter was therefore compressed instead, from 3,621 to **3,378 words — five and a half per cent over, in the same class as Chapter 76's three per cent** — by removing a duplicated recital of the note's three counts from the read-out, by not repeating a list of standing costs in Kest's mouth and the closing catalogue both, and by tightening connective tissue. **No cost, figure, document or beat was dropped to do it.** The remaining excess is the structural property of a day that ends a charter, and the Volume 01 close review's own judgment covers it: *a chapter long because it is one complete room is a different fault from a chapter long because it is two.* `state/current.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` no longer defend the old length and carry the new figures.
13. **Four British spellings**, a class Volume 01 ruled on volume-wide and fixed: *a defence* in Chapter 75 to *a defense*; *a licence* in Chapter 76 to *a license*; *a favour* in Chapter 79 to *a favor*; *a favour* in Chapter 80 to *a favor*. **A scan of Chapters 71–80 for the whole class now returns nothing.**
14. **A duplicated section break** — `---`, blank line, `---` — in Chapter 71 between the entry of the two days and the opening of the bench chest. Removed; no other chapter has one.
15. **Two figures that could not be true.** Chapter 80's *neither of us has moved his number in eleven weeks* is now **twelve weeks**, the fourth of January to the thirty-first of March being eighty-six days. **The circuit office's count was *corrected in three days*** in Chapter 77 and Chapter 80, which cannot fit a paper the reeve sent on the fifteenth and a request dated the sixteenth; it is now **corrected in a day**, in both chapters and in § 3 above.

**One unflagged defect of the same family was found while applying the rest and is repaired here.** Chapter 79 has Mara Venn say she was *up the hill at ten this morning* in a scene that opens at half past eight; it is now **ten yesterday morning**, which is consistent with Calder Marr telling her at nine the night before and with her having been in the shed at four the previous afternoon.

**Verified in the repair and not changed.** The money chain reconciles mark by mark (bag **11, 9, 6, 6, 1, 6, 2, 8, 5, 5**; note **1, 1, 1, 1, 20, 20, 24, 24, 27, 27**; the tin two crowns untouched since the first of February). The eleven logged occasions and the two empty answers are untouched. The false line on the unverified slate is still false and the slate was not moved. The reeve's ruling that no line may be drawn between the three points stands and is not lifted. Alden Marr confesses nothing. No custody object returns. The nine-line mark is not explained and the Nine Harbors, the Quietening and the Crown Seal are not named. Ilya Serr and Halven Rook appear nowhere and Holt only as a signature on a paper already on a wall. The volume's midpoint is not begun and the climax is not begun. No interface vocabulary and no second person. No duplicate paragraph and no exact repeated sentence anywhere in the batch.
