## Volume 02 close — the chapters the audit must read

**The volume's own resolution, in one place, for the audit.** A Free Keel yard with no instrument, no claim and no paper of any kind, and three marks in a bag and thirty on a creditor's page on the seventeenth of April, spent a fortnight being the most reliable crew in Morrow Quay and closed the month with **a provisional writ granted in the harbor authority's hearing room on Monday the first of May, with a Crown reporting clause attached to it and four names on it, of which the fourth is a hired diver who was asked in person and said yes.** The act that earned it was a rescue: a man of thirty-one working without a permit on a mooring inside a notice nobody could lift, a half sea on a top of tide, a boy of twenty-two with a bad hand and a knife on a shackle, a reeve's barge committed into a half sea, five men off that mole of whom not one was on the authority's work, a tender's cutoff pulled at eleven minutes, a man of thirty-one out of five fathoms, and the cost of a compressor gland, ten feet of hose, a hand lead's line, a claim window of one date, two days' work, a three-week-old pot of air and every mark of it. **The seal is false and the witness is real and both are on a market wall. The glass is in a master's keeping and was given back in a market. The boy is paid sixteen marks out of four men's pockets and the yard's own debt to the conservator is not paid and the writ cannot reach her. The bellwright's eight marks are paid out of the market and the chandler's note is not re-written and that is said out loud on a wall.**

**The volume's opened question, in one place.** *Who edits the sea charts, and why do all the missing roads converge on a place seventy fathoms down?* It is asked on a market wall on the third of May in the form of a question about a man with a pen, and it is not answered, and the yard will not put a name on the road, and the seventy fathoms and the eleven feet are in one street on the same afternoon and are not in the same sentence.

---

## Rulings of the Volume 02 volume audit — 26 September 2026

**Volume 02, Chapters 51–100, is closed and audited. The audit is `reviews/volume-02/volume-02-close.md` and it is the home of every ruling below, which supersedes the five batch accounts of themselves above wherever they differ.** Every author-level decision Volume 02 deferred is now **ruled**, not deferred again, and every load-bearing figure has been **re-derived from the chapters**.

### The ten rulings

1. **The Edric Pringle pronoun — RULED: SHE IS A WOMAN, AND CHAPTERS 62 AND 69 ARE REPAIRED IN PLACE.** The state files recorded **seven** wrong pronouns in three locations. The true count in the chapters is **ten, in two chapters, in four places**: Ch 62 line 71 (*knows exactly what he is doing*), Ch 69 line 19 (*In his words, in his own hand*), Ch 69 line 23 (*we are two men*), Ch 69 line 29 and line 71 (*on his own ring and his own warp and his own account*, three each). **All ten are corrected on the page.** Chapter 63 has no wrong pronoun and never had. **A pronoun that contradicts the volume's own canon in three later chapters is a mechanical error and this phase may correct it; leaving it would put a defect on the page in order to preserve a record of a defect.** Nothing else in either chapter was touched.
2. **The lead plug with a number in it — RULED, and given an owner and a closing volume.** **It is not a custody object, it is not one of the three items, and it is not connected to the ferry plate dug on the mole's toe in February, to the Oren mark, to the worked panel, or to the coast road, and the two may never be fused.** Owner: **Corvin Sarn**, who is not of the party, took it out of the bell on the twenty-seventh of February, carried it four months, and put it on a bench on purpose. **Who may open the chest: the yard's own key, and the boy has written on the lid that there is nothing in it that anybody in this harbor can check.** **The number is not to be written in any office and not printed.** It is a thread across Volumes 3 to 6. **His offer to say it in a room if somebody asks him in a room, and not before, is a Volume 03 thread and is not a teaching scene.**
3. **The glass storm as a standing hazard — RULED, and the ban on naming a cause is now absolute.** It is a physical and psychological hazard and not a monster; it came through the water and not off it; it is in all of it; **no cause may be named for it in any later volume**; **the delay on the call a diver heard in five fathoms is not given and the yard does not intend to invent one**; and **it may never become a voice that tells anybody anything.** It may be used again and it goes in a second column with a reason against it.
4. **The yard's third column — RULED: a method and not a rule, and it may not be applied retroactively.** Created on the second of May for what the boy said when he was not in the boat, carrying the time it was read on the steps and the time it was written in the book. **It is of the yard's book and of no office's** — the harbor authority's clerk entered it as being of the yard's book, not his office's — **and it may not be applied to any date before the second of May 2026.** It is not a defense against the writ.
5. **The writ's four-day reporting clock — RULED, in the terms a chapter may and may not use it.** **A chapter may** have a return fall due and be made, be late, be seated by the harbor authority between two disputants, or report the yard's own findings against its own interest. **A chapter may not** treat a return as a confession, treat the clause as reaching Mara Venn, treat the clause as reaching the coast road, the marks, the bar or any use of them, let any office widen the clause, or honor it through a person who is not one of the four names. **Only the harbor authority may issue or suspend the writ and no office other than the harbor authority may renew it, and the harbor authority has said in its own instrument that it is not the office that can renew it.**
6. **The man of fifty-one with a bad wheel — RULED A THREAD AND ASSIGNED TO VOLUME 03.** He is of the *Grey Widow*, unnamed on the page, forty-nine years in the harbor, cannot place a sound, is better on a lead than the boy and is not put on a lead, does not know why, and has asked not to be told. **His bad wheel is his own and it is not Coll Ferrow's — the *Kestrel's Sister's* bad wheel is that boat's, and the two may never be fused. He is not to be interviewed by Calder Marr. The glass storm's two flat instructions to a man with his hands on a wheel are in a yard's second column and the yard has not asked him about them and is not going to.**
7. **The deliberate soft points — each ruled or assigned to a named volume, which is a decision and not a deferral.** **Captain Holt's posting after her defeat and the channel by which a disgraced officer reaches a Morrow Quay reeve are Volume 03's decision and Volume 03 may now take it; no other volume may invent either.** **The first echo's witness stays where he is** — he was not mentioned once in Volume 02 and Volume 03 is not the volume that needs him. **The abandoned Oren lead is Volume 05's, and Volume 02 planted no Oren lead, which is exactly what it was required not to do.** **Lira Venn's volume is not assignable from a volume audit and remains a standing instruction: until a series phase allocates her, no chapter in any volume may use her as a device; Volume 02 did not use her.** **The teaching question is ruled to the volumes that own the stages — 5, 9 and 11 — and the rule binding all three was worked twice in this volume: no convenient master, no teacher who arrives and explains the craft, a team craft and not a gift.**
8. **The three bare items with three counts — RULED, and the live instrument is re-stated as live.** Three items, three numbers, **digits not quoted in any of the fifty chapters and not spoken in a hearing room**, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third cannot be withdrawn without a request and the yard will not make one. **The custody-log re-entry of the twentieth of December gives no reason, and no paper states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material — a live instrument, and it was not answered by a clerk in this volume and may not be answered by a clerk in any volume.** A chapter may name the fact that there are three and may not name a digit.
9. **The plate dug on the mole's toe in February, and the loose iron — RULED, and the answer is that they are not in this volume.** **A search of all fifty chapters returns nothing for either.** They are new appearances in Volume 03, on the same ground as the lead plug, and **not connected to it, and the two may never be fused.** The saw kerf in the shoal iron's crown shackle is a third thing, unresolved, and on the second of May a man of the south lay offered a shilling for the shackle on its own and the yard would not take it.
10. **What a batch flagged and did not repair — ruled on each, and inherited forward.** **The six chapters of Batch 0005 past the guideline are not repaired and are not a defect to repair**; the remedy is the forward instruction now in `outline/volume-03.md` lock L23. **The "no chapter is two rooms" rule is WITHDRAWN** — three were (Ch 92, 98, 100) and a rule a volume's own best chapters break three times in a fortnight is not a rule; **the rule that replaces it is that no cost is paid twice and every date in a chapter belongs to one day**, and the hearing room and the market wall are never the same day, which is verified in the chapters. **The two outline clauses discharged in another form are ruled in the audit and are not unspent debts.** **The Batch 0005 review repair is inherited whole and was not re-opened**, and the corrected word count of that batch is **33,327**. **The log cited for that review does not exist; see the corrections below.**

### Seven figures corrected by this audit, against the chapters — six on the first pass and one on the second

**The seventh, found on the second pass, is this one:** `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0005 as written* said **the slate wall is wiped Monday the twenty-fourth of April at eight and Monday the first of May at eight, and that Kest wrote on it on the twenty-fourth.** **The only wipe named in Batch 0005 is Monday the first of May; there is none on the twenty-fourth; and Chapter 97 says the wall was bare at eight that Monday and that the chalk figure went up on it, the wall having stood bare for six weeks, which is the fifteenth of March.** The next wipe is **Monday the eighth of May**, which follows from the rule on the page and is not a quotation. That line has been corrected in place and carries the correction on its face.

| The state files said | The chapters say |
|---|---|
| Batch 0002 is **30,995 words**, with Chapters 61, 63, 65, 68 at 3,025 / 3,172 / 3,356 / 2,648 | **31,300 words**, and 3,024 / 3,195 / 3,498 / 2,790. The stated total was measured before the Batch 0002 review repair and was never recomputed |
| The bag stood at nothing for **seventeen days**, from the twentieth of April to the thirtieth | **Five days, from the afternoon of the twentieth to the evening of the twenty-fifth.** Twenty, twenty-first, twenty-second, twenty-third, twenty-four, twenty-five |
| The claim window's day rate **never reached the bag** | **Six marks came in on the twenty-eighth**, out of the clerk's coat, on the step, in front of three people, with the hour written and read back twice, **and the tin was emptied the same day** |
| **A tongue half worn through that is still not dealt with** | **The tongue is not on the frame. The tongue is two parts of a chain in a lead plug, and the frame is cut through the casting on one side** (Ch 98). **The phrase is withdrawn and may not be revived** |
| The Batch 0005 reviewer's log is at **`logs/batch-0005.review.log`** | **That file is not in the repository.** `logs/` holds only `close.log` and `models.log`. The repairs are real and **§ *Batch 0005 review repairs* in this file is the authority for every one of them, and no file was invented to satisfy a citation** |

**The volume's own length record, re-measured:** **31,769 / 31,300 / 26,358 / 25,683 / 33,327, and 148,437 words in Volume 02 across fifty chapters.** Four of the five batch totals were already exact.

### The reviewer log family — the sixth figure, and a correction to the Batch 0003 ruling

**The prompt this phase was handed cited `logs/batch-0005.review.log` and this audit found that file absent. The finding is larger than the prompt stated, and the Batch 0003 ruling at item 14 above corrected one citation and then described the directory wrongly.**

**`logs/` holds `close.log` and `models.log` and nothing else. No `logs/batch-000N.review.log` has ever existed in this repository for any batch in either volume, and none was ever committed — `git log --all -- 'logs/batch-*.review.log'` returns nothing.** Fifteen such citations stood in the four state files when this audit opened, naming Batches 0001, 0002, 0004 and 0005; the Batch 0003 pair had already been withdrawn at the time of that batch's review. **All fifteen are now withdrawn, and no file was invented to satisfy one.**

**What actually exists, and what is the authority:**

| Batch | A review file kept in `reviews/` | The authority for every finding |
|---|---|---|
| Volume 01, Batch 0001 | **`reviews/volume-01/batch-0001.md`** | that file, and this file's Volume 01 sections |
| Volume 01, Batch 0002 | **none** | the writer's own repair sections, and `state/open-threads.md` |
| Volume 01, Batch 0003 | **`reviews/volume-01/batch-0003.md`** | that file, and this file's Volume 01 sections |
| Volume 02, Batches 0001–0005 | **none** | **`state/continuity.md` § *Batch 000N review repairs*, with the short form in `state/current.md`** |

**The repairs are real and none of them is in question.** Every Volume 02 review repair is set out in this file in full, finding by finding, and the two batches whose reviews survive as files in `reviews/` are Volume 01's. **What was false was never a repair; it was a provenance claim, and it is withdrawn.**

**Two references are deliberately left standing, and neither is a file this phase may edit:** `reviews/volume-01/batch-0003.md` cites the same false path for the Volume 01 batch, and the Volume 02 close prompt cites it as the input this phase was handed. **A prose or planning phase does not get to rewrite a file it was given, and a volume's own review artifact is that volume's record.** The controller owns the remedy: if `.opencode/agent/novel-reviewer.md` reports only to `logs/`, then nothing persists the log, and `AGENTS.md`'s Quality Gate line "a reviewer has checked the result" has no file to point at. That thread is carried in `state/open-threads.md` and is not repaired here.

### The audit's own first pass — twenty repairs in five chapters, and three of its own figures corrected

**THE REPAIRS ARE TWENTY, NOT TEN, AND THEY ARE IN FIVE CHAPTERS, NOT TWO.** The first ten are the Edric Pringle pronoun in Chapters 62 and 69, ruled at item 1 above. **The second ten are a British spelling of *harbor* and *favor* in a book that writes the American forms 512 and 8 times, and they are repaired in place in Chapters 91, 93 and 97**, listed word for word in the audit at § 21: **four *harbour* in Chapter 91** (line 21 in Neale's speech, which writes *this harbor* three clauses later in the same sentence, and line 71 twice in a woman's speech); **five *harbour* in Chapter 93** (two in the opening narration of the storm — *whether the water had come up the harbour or whether the harbour had come up him* — one in the boy's speech, one in the first column, one in the narrative); **one *favour* in Chapter 97**, in Kest's own mouth in the hearing room. **Word counts are unchanged: Chapters 91, 93 and 97 are 2,791, 2,972 and 3,978 before and after, and Batch 0005 is still 33,327 and the volume is still 148,437.** The only remaining hits in Volume 02 for the British-spelling class are *circulation*, correct in both, and *Grey*, which is the boat's name.

**THREE FIGURES IN THE AUDIT'S OWN FIRST PASS WERE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED. THE RULING IS THE SAME IN ALL THREE: THE CHAPTERS DECIDE A DATE AND THE PLANNING FILES ARE CORRECTED TO THEM.**

1. **A slate-wall wipe on a Wednesday.** The first pass placed a wipe on *Monday the first of March*. **The first of March 2026 is a Wednesday and was the sitting day; Chapter 70 says the wall had been blank since the cloth came off it at eight on Thursday the twenty-third of February**, which is the one wipe in the volume that is not on a Monday and is on the page. **The wipes named in the volume are the second, ninth, twenty-third and thirtieth of January, the twenty-third of February, the thirteenth of March, the seventeenth of April, and the first of May.** There is **no** wipe on the twenty-fourth of April, and the chalk figure was not put up on the twenty-fourth: **Chapter 97 says the wall was bare at eight on Monday the first of May and that Kest put one figure on it, and that the wall had been bare for six weeks, which is the fifteenth of March.** The rule *a figure on that board is a figure for a week and a figure in this book is a figure for good* is on the page at Ch 62 and Ch 97, and **the next wipe after the first of May is the eighth of May, which follows from that rule and is not a quotation.** No file outside the audit was affected by this one.
2. **Three Mondays in May 2026 that are Thursdays, propagated into the three Volume 03 files.** The first pass of `outline/volume-03.md` lock L1, of `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0001.md` hard rule 2, and of `workspace/volume-03/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` each gave **the Mondays in May as the 4th, the 11th, the 15th, the 18th, the 22nd and the 29th**. On the grid that holds everywhere else in this book, **the fourth, eleventh and eighteenth of May are Thursdays**, and the day-by-day list in the same lock and the fact that the boy goes off the stone on Monday the fifteenth of May are both right. **The correct Mondays in May are the 1st, 8th, 15th, 22nd and 29th, and the first of May is the FIRST Monday of May and not the fifth.** **All three files are corrected and the correction is binding on the writer who meets them.** A Volume 03 card had also put a wipe on **Thursday the fourth of May**; that is removed and the card now says the wall was wiped on Monday the first and carries the one chalk figure the reeve put up that morning.
3. **A scan that reported a clean volume and was not clean** — the British spellings above. The scan claim is rewritten.

**Also corrected, in the same pass: two chapters each entered "the seventh" of the reeve's acknowledgements**, which would have given a ledger that holds six two sevenths. **Chapter 102 is the seventh, at the yard's asking; Chapter 107 is the eighth, on the reeve's own motion and not asked for by anybody, which is the first one in this harbor entered that way; and there is no ninth**, and Chapter 109's reference to the age of the acknowledgement is corrected from four days to **three, from the seventeenth to the twentieth of May.** `outline/volume-03.md` lock L6 now says a seventh and an eighth may be entered in the volume and no ninth.

## Volume 03 planning-phase review repairs (recorded, not hidden)

A reviewer pass over `outline/volume-03.md`, `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0001.md`, `workspace/volume-03/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, the Volume 02 audit that rules all three, and the state-file handoff returned **FIX**: ten required changes and nine suggested. **No chapter was restarted, no card was cut for length, and no planned plot was altered.** `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md`, the six bible files, `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.opencode/agent/` and `state/phase-ledger.json` were not touched. The full disposition is below and every correction is now also made in the three planning files and, where the sentence was false in an audit or a state file, in that file in place.

**Two figures in the audit and in the state files were wrong on the page and are corrected here, in the audit, and in the locks:**

1. **"The only money that has gone out of that yard's book to anybody since the first of February is the bellwright's eight marks." FALSE.** Wages went out of that bag on the seventh and fourteenth of February and the fourth week fell due on the twenty-first with one mark in the bag and three marks short of it, bread, oil, candles, air and rope went onto the note and out of the bag, and sixteen marks of a boy's wage went out of four other men's pockets in a market on the twenty-ninth of April (Ch 65, 90–92; Ch 96:51; Ch 100:73). **The sentence is now scoped — "other than wages, and other than bread, oil, candles, air, rope, and what has gone onto the note" — in `outline/volume-03.md` lock L2, in the batch file, in the writer prompt, in this section, and in `reviews/volume-02/volume-02-close.md` § 13.1, where the correction is stated in place. The meaning is unchanged: the bellwright is the only man in this harbor the yard has paid a price of its own to since February.** Ch 103 of the batch pays a wage week out of the yard's own book, which the unscoped lock would have forbidden.
2. **"The yard has not argued clause two in eight months." FALSE, and it is a CHAPTER 100 ERROR, not a planning-file error.** The notice is of the thirty-first of October (`chapter-0060.md:83`, `chapter-0084.md:57`) and Chapter 100's read-back on Wednesday the third of May says eight months. **Three May minus thirty-one October is six months and two days, and the figure for the whole of Volume 03 is six months.** The figure appeared in the Volume 02 audit at § 3.11 and in the Chapter 102 card, and **both were propagated from the chapter, which is the error.** The audit's sentence is corrected in place and the card is corrected; **Chapter 100's line is a figure in a boy's mouth and is NOT altered — the ruling of this section is that the chapters decide what happened and the planning files are corrected to them, and a figure spoken by a character is that character's figure.** It is recorded here so that no later file propagates it, and `outline/volume-03.md` lock L1 now says the grid and the chapters are the authority over any almanac and over any later audit that "corrects" this volume to one.

**The rest, and where each fix landed:**

3. **Chapter 110 staged a second going out that was already bought, already paid for and already done, three weeks later, and against a public sentence.** The going out was paid in a market on the twenty-ninth of April for the second of May (Ch 96:51, Ch 100:73), and **it was done at about ten past three on the second of May in a flat calm** with Sarn, the holder, Pike and Dace in the boat and the boy on the mole's steps (Ch 99:27), and the two-column and third-column entries for it are in Ch 98 and Ch 99. The standing sentence said in a street on the third of May is that the next one is in about four years (Ch 100:101). **Chapter 110 is re-cast, not re-funded.** It is now **"The Boat Off the South End"** (retitled in the card file; the chapter's dates, POV, pressure type and handoff are unchanged): a boat is hired for the day out of the pump's eight marks, **the difference between a bought going out and a boat is put on the page in the yard's own words before anybody gets wet**, the bellwright comes because the ground and gear are the harbor's **and is not paid to come and is not paid at all**, an eight marks and a day of a boat is entered as owing him a second time, **the going out of the second of May is stated as done and spent and is not repeated, no second eight marks is paid, and no acknowledgement is entered.** The card's information-revealed beat that duplicated Chapter 99's two-column entry is now restricted to **the part of the bellwright's account that is new**, and the hand-lead's-line beat is now a **payoff** — the line made badly on the second of May is right on the twenty-third, said in about nine words by the woman who said it would be right in a fortnight or not at all (Ch 99:71) — instead of a second making. **L8 now carries the rule that no volume may buy a second going out inside four years or enter a second eight marks as paid.**
4. **The four-day clock's trigger was stated three incompatible ways and the batch's engine depended on it.** Lock L5 now carries, once and binding: **the second limb is a CLAIM, not a closure** — a ground closed by a notice of a third office is not a claimed ground, the *Vigil*'s claim since the fourteenth of October lies beyond the outer marks and does not touch the mole's face, and the card's substitution of "ground the first clause of the notice closes" is deleted; **and the counting rule: the day of the finding is not counted and the return falls due on the fourth day after it,** which reproduces the page's own dates (a finding of Tuesday the twenty-eighth of April is due Saturday the second of May) and makes the rest of May and June computable. **The returns are enumerated in the batch file and the prompt and there are two:** **Return 1, Thursday the fourth of May, two days late**, about the raising of the twenty-eighth, carrying the iron, the gun-port and a lump of green glass the yard has not looked at; **Return 2, Wednesday the tenth of May, on the day**, about the look on the sixth, saying the lump is worth nothing; **and no third, with a dated line in the yard's own book on Tuesday the thirteenth saying no finding was made and nothing was raised and no return goes up, which is a fact about the yard's book and is not sent to anybody.** Chapter 109's flat contradiction — a clerk asking whether the yard is going to look at a lump Chapter 102 had spent a day looking at — is resolved: **the clerk's question is now "did you look at it", the answer is yes, on the sixth, here is Return 2, and it is worth nothing.** Chapter 109's reference to "a copy of the fourth return" is replaced by **the two returns, both produced unshortened, with the yard saying out loud that it has made two and that if a third is asked for the yard will say it has made two**, and the drafting artifact in that card is resolved to the single action of giving the first return whole the first time it is asked.
5. **Chapter 109 also put a man inside a closure with no acknowledgement, which breaks rule 5's budget of two and rule 5's "no ninth."** The production is now made **at the top of the mole's steps, above the third seam, and not one foot below them**, the yard asks what a ninth would cost and is told the cost of it, and no ninth is entered. **Chapter 103's day is moved to the second seam for the same reason, and the card also says why: the seventh acknowledgement is spent.**
6. **The bellwright was to be asked one question "in a shed" in one file and "not in a room" in another, against lock L9 and the volume's own handoff, which both say a room.** All four files now agree and the agreement is stronger than any of them: **the batch does not ask him at all.** Chapter 110 has the question ready and does not ask it and says why out loud, **and the reason is not that he is difficult: it is that the boy who could have read the answer back went off the stone on the fifteenth, and a number said in a room is a number with no hand to enter it and no second column to put a reason against.** His own words stand and the question is carried whole into Movement 2, and the volume's handoff to Chapter 111 now says "who has not been asked in this batch."
7. **The pump's hire history was wrong in four places on a figure the boy reads out loud.** The page: hired and a half day by Harl Dace on Tuesday the twenty-first of March (Ch 78:5); **the true inherited figure, read back on the thirty-first of March, is "not out of this shed since the twenty-first of March because a man has not wanted it" (Ch 81:29) — which was true on the thirty-first of March and stopped being true on the fifth of April**; hired for the day at four marks by Iven Toft on Wednesday the fifth of April (Ch 82:5, 47, 69); **and a hire asked for on Saturday the twenty-second of April and missed, because the pump was on the mole and the yard was in a coal store, which the yard entered against itself (Ch 92:47, 51).** All four wrong figures — "since the housing came up on the eighth of March", "has not been out of the shed once", "it has not been out of the shed since", and "hired out for the first time since March" — are replaced with **not out of the shed since the fifth of April, and the missed hire of the twenty-second**, and the missed hire is kept because it is the better beat. **Chapter 104 now has the man from the twenty-second of April come back and get the pump: two days at four marks a day, eight marks in, two days lost, and he says he would have paid four and a quarter and should not have been.**
8. **Chapter 108 spent Movement 3's reserved beat.** `outline/volume-03.md` Movement 3 reserves for Chapters 121–130 "a man in a city reads a writ with a reporting clause on it and writes back a question about a bar of gravel," and the Chapter 108 card had exactly that document arriving from a city as somebody else's question. **The city document is removed from Chapter 108 and Movement 3 now carries an explicit reservation paragraph.** The chapter's turn stands and is now local: **a man who trades up and down this coast and is nobody's clerk wants to buy a copy of the sheet with the bar on it, and the yard refuses, and the refusal is recorded in his own hand on his own slate in this harbor's market, and that record is now in a market and is not the yard's to correct.** The circuit office's fourth request of the sixteenth of March is a different paper and remains referable at any time.
9. **One wage week was unaccounted for.** The thirteenth week of four marks fell due on **Tuesday the second of May**, on the locked grid, and appears in no column: Chapter 92 makes the twelfth the twenty-fifth of April at eight lines and thirty-two marks, the twenty-ninth of April pays sixteen marks and leaves four lines and sixteen marks, and Chapter 100's read-back on the third says "four lines and sixteen marks in the wage column." **The gap is a Volume 02 omission and is not a new fact.** It is closed in Chapter 101's card: **the boy enters the thirteenth week himself on the fourth of May, in his own right hand, four marks, the second of May, with the reason that it went in on the fourth, and the column becomes five lines and twenty marks with the fifth of March still the oldest line.** The audit's Movement 5 chain, which read "1, 2, 3 May, nothing," is corrected in place.
10. **The batch file contradicted itself on the bellwright, and the two other raw drafting artifacts are resolved.** "Corvin Sarn is asked one question and answers it in a shed" (`:89`) is replaced per item 6. The Chapter 109 card's "— no. It gives it, unshortened…" is resolved to the single action. **Chapter 102's "is not going to start on a Monday" is removed, because the chapter sits on a Saturday and a Sunday**, and its "eight months" is corrected per item 2.
11. **"Nineteen marks of iron's cousin" is dropped.** It was a new unchained figure in the prompt's chapter list, it contradicted Chapter 104's deliberate refusal to price the gland, and it collided with the nineteen marks refused for the iron stack on the second of May and with the nineteen marks of the pump's housing on the eighth of March. **The phrase is gone from the prompt and the batch card now say "a gland on a page at twenty-three" and, in the card's continuity line, that the March iron was the housing and is a different sum for a different casting and the two are never put in the same sentence.**
12. **The prompt's cast list omitted three working people who are on the page.** **Tam Slee** — the fourth name on the writ, asked in person, who read it and put it down (Ch 97:23, Ch 98); **Wil Slee** — of this harbor's own tide gang, brother of Neale's wife, the second man in the water on the twenty-eighth of April (Ch 94:39), whose boat the card had wrongly merged with the *Grey Widow*'s; and **Ewan Tristram**, wharfinger of the second lay, whose cistern thread closes inside Volume 03. All three are now in the cast list, **and the card's line about "two hired men of the *Grey Widow*" is corrected: there is one, and the other is of the tide gang.**
13. **Four hundred and forty pounds of old iron and a bell-metal gun-port had no closing position in Volume 02 and none in the batch.** They are on the page (Ch 94:67) and nowhere else. **They are now given a closing position in `outline/volume-03.md`'s starting state, in the batch file's starting condition, in the prompt's starting state, and in Chapter 110's continuity line: unsold, on the yard's own ground behind the shed, in the same heap as the twenty marks of the yard's own iron, in a heap the yard may not sell because it has declared a thing about its own gear in a book.** The gun-port is the one piece of the three that is not iron, **and there is one man in this harbor who would know bell metal at a glance and is not of this yard and is not of the Free Keel and teaches nobody — and he is not asked about it, because the number in the lead plug is the only question this volume has for him.**
14. **Chapter 101's location spent the one market-wall use the batch allows.** "The head of the market wall" is removed from the Chapter 101 card and from the prompt, **and the prompt's shape section now says in terms that the one market-wall use in this batch is Chapter 108 and that Chapter 101 is at the fish dock and the mole's steps.**
15. **Two sourcing slips.** The Chapter 104 card had Calder Marr saying the "no date in its hand" sentence "in a market on the thirty-first of March"; **Chapter 80 has it at the head of the yard's own dock and Chapter 92 repeats "not in a road," and the card now says so and names the man at the sign of the Drowned Bell as having heard it.**
16. **The replacement shape rule did not test what it replaced.** "Every date in a chapter belongs to one day" permits everything the withdrawn "no chapter is two rooms" rule was withdrawn for, **because the three withdrawn chapters — Chapter 92, the shed and the market; Chapter 98, the net loft, the mole's steps and the shed; Chapter 100, the boards and the dock — are each one day in two or three rooms.** Lock L23, the batch file's shape section and the prompt's shape section now all say in terms that **a single-day multi-room chapter is allowed and is not a fault, that those three are the precedent, and that a later reviewer may not re-raise the withdrawn rule against them.** The new negative test is added: **a man may be in three rooms in one day; a man may not be paid for two days in one chapter; no day may be in a chapter twice.**
17. **The prompt and the batch file disagreed about whether Movement 1 may name the condition.** Lock L25 now settles it and the two files are aligned to it: **Volume 03 names the condition once and no more, and Movement 1 is the movement that may name it. If a Movement 1 chapter earns the naming, no chapter in Movements 2, 3, 4 or 5 may name it again. If Movement 1 does not, the one naming is available to one later movement and to no more than one, and Movement 4 is where the volume's structure wants it.**
18. **Nothing existed to draft Batch 0002 from.** The writer prompt both required `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` to be built from "the next set of cards" and forbade inventing a batch outline, and no cards for Chapters 111–120 existed. **`outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0002.md` is now written** — ten cards for Movement 2, twenty-five hard rules, a pressure distribution, a midpoint at Chapter 115 and a climax at Chapter 120 — **and `outline/volume-03.md` now carries a "Handoff to Chapter 121" section** that the cards land on. The writer prompt's closing instruction now names that file and tells the writer to read it and not to reconstruct it. Batch 0001's header no longer claims to be the only Volume 3 batch file.

**Nothing else was changed and nothing was withdrawn from the plot.** Movement 1 still runs the clock, loses the boy, refuses the man who was not on the writ, watches a correct refusal land on the man who carried the paper, produces the writ on a mole and goes out to a bell; the batch still ends in craft and a cost that recurs; the volume's climax, resolution, new question, power stage and planned ending are untouched; and `outline/ending.md` was not opened.

**A new risk this batch creates and the next reviewer should watch: a chapter may now pay a return "out of a market's money two months ago." Nothing may.** The eight marks of the twenty-ninth of April were spent that afternoon — the bellwright, the boy and the note, thirty out of thirty in — and the second of May's going out was paid for and done. **Every mark that moves in Volume 03 from this point has a named source on a named day, and the going out recurs in about four years.**

## Volume 03 — the audited starting state for Chapters 101 onward

**These are the locks a Volume 03 writer inherits. `outline/volume-03.md` is the governing document and carries all twenty-eight of them as locks; this is the quick reference, and every figure has been verified in Chapters 51–100 by the audit.**

1. **Calendar.** **The third of May 2026 is a Wednesday and the volume closed on it.** 4 May a Thursday, 8 a Monday, 9 a Tuesday, **15 a Monday**, 16 a Tuesday, 22 a Monday, 23 a Tuesday, 24 a Wednesday; 1 June a Thursday, 1 July a Saturday. **The Mondays in May are the 1st, 8th, 15th, 22nd and 29th; in June the 5th, 12th, 19th and 26th — and the fourth, eleventh and eighteenth of May are Thursdays.** **A wage week falls on a Tuesday: the 2nd, 9th, 16th, 23rd and 30th of May.** **The hearing room's slate wall is wiped every Monday at eight** — the last wipe was Monday the first of May, so in Volume 03 it comes down on the **8th, the 15th, the 22nd and the 29th of May** and on no other day. **The boy is off the stone on Monday the fifteenth of May and no chapter may move it.**
2. **Money.** **Thirteen marks in a bag, twenty-three on the note, nothing in a tin, on the third of May.** The bag stood at nothing for **five days** from the afternoon of the twentieth of April. **The only money this harbor has paid that yard since the first of February is the six marks of the claim window's day rate of the twenty-eighth of April. Other than wages, and other than bread, oil, candles, air, rope, and what has gone onto the note, the only money out of that yard's book to anybody since the first of February is the bellwright's eight marks, out of four hands in a market on the twenty-ninth of April.** **The scoping is binding and was added by a planning-phase review: the unscoped sentence was false on the page, because a wage of four marks a week has gone out of that bag every week since the first of February and sixteen marks of a boy's wage went out of four other men's pockets on the twenty-ninth of April, and `reviews/volume-02/volume-02-close.md` carried the same false sentence and is corrected in place. The point of the sentence is that the bellwright is the only man in this harbor the yard has paid a price of its own to since February.** **A figure written on a note is not a figure paid until somebody puts the money on the counter.** The eighth and the share come off a day rate as well as a raising, and the yard has never argued either.
3. **The chandler's note.** Mark figure **twenty-three**. **Three static counts of its weeks: the creditor's twenty-two, the yard's thirteen, and eleven as the last figure ever written in that page. None has moved since the fourth of January and none may be advanced by arithmetic.** The note is not re-written and the refusal of the thirty-first of March stands unreversed, and the yard said so out loud in a market on the third of May. **A later volume may re-write it or keep it un-written and either is a scene.**
4. **The writ.** A provisional writ of salvage working, granted Monday the first of May, running to the first of March next, **not the Second Bell license, not a charter, renewing nothing, and its ground may not be widened past what it names** — the mole from the first seam to the twelfth, the dump, the north wall, the first and second seams, all ground landward of the third seam, and private ground within the line. **Four names: Calder Marr, Nessa Pike, Bevin Rook, and Tam Slee of the *Grey Widow*, hired and not of this association.** **Only the harbor authority may issue, suspend or renew it, and its own last line says the second season is not its to give.**
5. **The reporting clause.** Four days; every finding of worked glass and every raising of material from **ground which any other party claims — a claim, and a ground closed by a third office's notice is not a claimed ground, and the *Vigil*'s claim lies beyond the outer marks**; place, hour, keeper, limits; a copy to the harbor authority; **the harbor authority may seat one of two disputants and take the finding as given**; production on demand. **Three names on paper and no face in the room.** **It does not reach Mara Venn and she is not of the yard.** **A finding is not a conclusion, and the yard's written objection is in the record.** **The counting rule, binding on every chapter of the volume and stated once in lock L5: the day of a finding is not counted and the return is due on the fourth day after it — a finding of Tuesday the twenty-eighth of April is due Saturday the second of May, which is what the page says. THE RETURNS OF BATCH 0001 ARE ENUMERATED AND ARE TWO: Return 1, Thursday the fourth of May, two days late, about the raising of the twenty-eighth — the iron, the gun-port, and a lump of green glass the yard has not looked at and will not describe; and Return 2, Wednesday the tenth of May, on the day, about the look of the sixth, saying the lump is worth nothing. There is no third, and Tuesday the thirteenth of May carries a dated line in the yard's own book saying no finding was made, nothing was raised, and no return goes up, and that line is not sent to anybody. A day on a mole's crown with the writ in a man's hand and nothing going into the water raises nothing and produces no return. Movement 2 raises worked glass on Saturday the tenth of June, so a THIRD return of the volume falls due on SATURDAY THE SEVENTEENTH OF JUNE, outside Batch 0002.**
6. **The notice of the thirty-first.** Four clauses, not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered, **and the harbor authority has no power to lift it.** Clause three names three people in the yard who have not been on the authority's work since the day it was served. **It is of the thirty-first of October, and the figure for the whole of Volume 03 is SIX MONTHS — Chapter 100's "eight months" is an error on that page, is not altered, and must not be propagated into any file.** Six acknowledgements in the harbor authority's ledger at the volume's opening, the sixth the second a party asked for; Volume 03's Batch 0001 enters a seventh at the yard's asking in Chapter 102 and an eighth on the reeve's own motion and unasked in Chapter 107, **and there is no ninth: Chapter 109 puts one within reach, asks what it would cost, is told the cost of it, and produces the writ at the top of the mole's steps and not one foot below them, and Chapter 103's day is at the second seam because the seventh is spent. BOTH ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS ARE SPENT AT THE END OF BATCH 0001 AND MOVEMENT 2 ENTERS NONE.** The cost is Kest's and is never free. **A man was inside that closure on the twenty-eighth of April and nearly drowned, and the yard will not say the notice saved him and will not say it was not worth the paper it is on, and both are in one page.**
7. **The plan for the fifth seam.** **Lodged on the seventeenth of January in the yard's own left hand with a fisher's hand on the delivery, and NEVER ANSWERED IN VOLUME 02.** Kest said in his own mouth what would happen to it. **Volume 03 must answer it; the refusal has to cost somebody who is not the yard.** The plate is in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched.
8. **The bell.** In eight and a half feet off the south end, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was**, a new rope bought on the eleventh of January. **Eight marks and a day of a boat, paid on the twenty-ninth of April, and THE GOING OUT THEY PAID FOR WAS DONE at about ten past three on the second of May in a flat calm, in a boat with Sarn, the holder, Pike and Dace, with the boy on the mole's steps (Ch 99:27), and it is spent.** **The going out recurs in about four years, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed: no volume may buy a second going out inside four years, pay a second eight marks, or enter a second going out as done.** It is not an instrument. **The bellwright has been in a boat off the south end for nothing and is owed eight marks and a day of a boat for it, and he is NOT asked his one question in Batch 0001, in a shed, in a boat, in a market or in a room, and the question is carried whole into Movement 2.**
9. **The lead plug.** In a chest in a shed, four descriptions that do not agree, a number in no book. **Not a custody object, not one of the three items, not connected to the ferry plate dug in February, to the Oren mark, to the panel or to the road. The number is written nowhere and the bellwright may be asked in a room and not in a shed — AND BATCH 0001 DOES NOT ASK HIM AT ALL, because the boy who could have read the answer back went off the stone on the fifteenth.**
10. **The glass storm.** Came through the water and not off it, in a second column with a reason, **no cause named in any volume, and it may never become a voice.**
11. **The coast road.** Published in six boats, doubted, half-believed, **not resolved; not explained; not named a network; not connected to anything under the town; the word used once by a rival and refused.** **The nine final routes are not named in the prose. Orison is a place seventy fathoms down, and the yard cannot put that and eleven feet in the same sentence.**
12. **The run under the town.** Four hundred feet of ordinary figures and no chart. **Nine and a quarter feet is the ferry plate in a coaming beyond the eleventh seam and is not one of these figures. Nothing under the town is explained, named, or pursued at night.**
13. **The compartment.** Spent; **not referred to in any chapter of Volume 02**, so the three-step form has not been restated since Chapter 47. **No chapter may say the sealed case was in standing space number four.**
14. **Custody.** Three items, three numbers, digits not quoted, none returns, the municipal claim on the third cannot be withdrawn without a request. **No paper states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material, and that is a live instrument.**
15. **The concealment.** Hidden, told once in Chapter 38, **and not told a second time for nothing.** The false line is still false, the boy has not asked and has not been asked and has entered two omissions in the same breath, and **the woman with no standing has still never been asked to say what is in the anchor, and has said in a room, unprompted, that the writ cannot reach her.**
16. **The delayed note.** **Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, two of them with no call in them, and neither count is advanced by arithmetic or by a measurement.** The delay is a hand's width of a second and has not moved. **The answer inside it is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.**
17. **Who may appear.** Ilya Serr on a seal line and never in a room; Halven Rook on paper and not in a room; Captain Holt named, signing, sending a paper, described from a distance, and **not in a room in this volume**; **her posting after her defeat in Volume 03 and the channel by which a disgraced officer reaches a Morrow Quay reeve are Volume 03's own decision.** No new antagonist, no new institution, no new final enemy.
18. **Labor.** The forty marks twice; the wage with the fifth of March the oldest line; the eight marks to the bellwright **paid**; the eight shillings and tenpence and four hundred feet of warp on the bottom; the note at twenty-three and not re-written; no gland, no hose, no air; the *Kittiwake* on the hard; the harbor's gear on its own account; the north berth out; the ninth seam out with no end of it entered; the tide gang four short; the cistern on a rota; the dead hose; the light not on; **the harbor authority with no boat of its own**; the *Sea Wren* at the pile; the hard at Ashtide; nineteen winters; **the eleven copies and the four unreached holders not asked**; and **Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found.**
19. **Power.** **No stage is taken in Volume 02. The Faultreader stage belongs to Volume 03, late and badly, and a seam identifies difference and not truth.** The condition was named once in Volume 02, in Chapter 88, and not used again in it. **Volume 03 may name it once and no more, and lock L25 settles which movement spends it: Movement 1 is the movement that may name it; if it does, no chapter in Movements 2 to 5 may name it again; if it does not, the one naming is available to one later movement and to no more than one, and Movement 4 is where the volume's structure wants it.**
20. **The shape rule.** **"No chapter is two rooms" is WITHDRAWN. No cost is paid twice, and every date in a chapter belongs to one day.** A rescue and a hearing are split across two days, a hearing room and a market are split across two days, **and the hearing room and the market wall are never the same day.** Ten speeches are not ten chapters. **A single-day multi-room chapter is ALLOWED and is not a fault, and Chapters 92, 98 and 100 are the precedent and may not be re-raised against: each is one day in two or three rooms. A man may be in three rooms in one day; a man may not be paid for two days in one chapter; no day may be in a chapter twice.**
21. **The pump.** Housing fitted on the eighth of March. Hired and a half day by Harl Dace on Tuesday the twenty-first of March (Ch 78). **THE INHERITED FIGURE IS NOT OUT OF THE SHED SINCE WEDNESDAY THE FIFTH OF APRIL, when Iven Toft hired it for the day at four marks (Ch 82) — not "since the housing came up" and not "since March".** A hire was asked for on Saturday the twenty-second of April by a man with money and missed, the pump being on the mole and the yard in a coal store, **and the yard entered that refusal against itself with no reason against it (Ch 92).** Chapter 100's read-back of "not out of this shed since the twenty-first of March" was on the thirty-first of March and was true that day. **Chapter 104 has the same man back and gets the pump: two days at four marks a day, eight marks in, two days lost, and he says he would have paid four and a quarter.**
22. **The wage column.** Four lines and sixteen marks with the fifth of March the oldest, as read out on Wednesday the third of May. **THE THIRTEENTH WEEK, four marks, FELL DUE ON TUESDAY THE SECOND OF MAY AND IS IN NO COLUMN — a Volume 02 omission, closed in Chapter 101, where the boy enters it himself on the fourth with the reason that it went in on the fourth, and the column becomes five lines and twenty marks.** A wage week falls on a Tuesday and is paid or entered unpaid on that Tuesday.
23. **The iron of the twenty-eighth of April.** **Four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in three pieces, one of them a bell-metal gun-port off something with a gun in it, raised off the mole's own face between the third and fourth seams under a claim window that has been spent and logged, none of it glass and all of it heavy (Ch 94:67). IT HAD NO CLOSING POSITION IN VOLUME 02 AND IS NOW GIVEN ONE: unsold, on the yard's own ground behind the shed, in the same heap as the twenty marks of the yard's own iron and a saw kerf in a crown shackle, in a heap the yard may not sell because it has declared a thing about its own gear in a book and a buyer must read the declaration first.** The gun-port is the one piece of the three that is not iron, **and the only man in this harbor who would know it at a glance is the harbor's bellwright, who is not of this yard, is not of the Free Keel, teaches nobody, and is not asked about it — the number in the lead plug is the only question this volume has for him.** The nineteen marks of iron on the page in March was the pump's housing and is a different sum for a different casting and the two are never put in the same sentence.

---

## Batch 0001 as written (Chapters 101–110), Volume 03 — binding on Chapter 111 onward

**Movement 1, the first return and the first bill. THE FOUR-DAY CLOCK RUNS TWICE, THE BOY GOES OFF THE STONE, THE RIVAL IS REFUSED A DAY, THE PLAN OF THE SEVENTEENTH OF JANUARY IS REFUSED, THE WRIT IS PRODUCED ON A MOLE, AND ONE BOAT GOES OFF THE SOUTH END AND IS NOT A GOING OUT.** The chapters are the authority on what happened. Everything in this section is taken from Chapters 101–110 and from nothing else, and **the whole batch is 29,905 words after the Batch 0001 review repairs and was 26,767 before them** (Ch101 2,638 · Ch102 2,742 · Ch103 2,803 · Ch104 3,011 · Ch105 2,535 · Ch106 3,104 · Ch107 3,361 · Ch108 2,785 · Ch109 3,373 · Ch110 3,560). **Every chapter is inside the 2,200 floor; three are past the 3,200 ceiling — Ch107 by 161, Ch109 by 173 and Ch110 by 360 — and all three grew because the review required material the batch did not have: a delivered cost beat for Nessa Pike in Ch107, Calder Marr's interior life in Ch109 and Ch110, and the move of Chapter 110's boat trip to the top of the flood in Ch110 so that the tide bell rings while the bellwright is on the ladder. No cost was dropped to bring them back, which the batch's own length rule prefers.**

### 1. The calendar the batch actually ran on

**`outline/volume-03.md` lock L1's grid, unchanged, and the chapters ran on it without an exception.** The third of May a Wednesday, and therefore 4 a **Thursday**, 5 a Friday, 6 a **Saturday**, 7 a Sunday, 8 a **Monday**, 9 a **Tuesday**, 10 a **Wednesday**, 11 a Thursday, 12 a **Friday**, 13 a **Saturday**, 14 a Sunday, **15 a Monday**, 16 a Tuesday, 17 a **Wednesday**, 18 a **Thursday**, 19 a Friday, 20 a **Saturday**, 21 a Sunday, 22 a **Monday**, 23 a Tuesday, 24 a **Wednesday**. **The Mondays in May are the 1st, 8th, 15th, 22nd and 29th, and the first of May is the first Monday of May; the fourth, eleventh and eighteenth of May are Thursdays.** **The wage Tuesdays in this batch are the ninth and the sixteenth, and the sixteenth is the first wage week in this column's history that has gone by without a line in it, and the twenty-third is the second.**

**The ten days of story are the fourth to the twenty-fourth of May, and every chapter carries its own dates:** Ch101 the 4th and 5th, Ch102 the 6th and 7th, Ch103 the 8th and 9th, Ch104 the 10th and 11th, Ch105 the 12th and 13th, Ch106 the 14th and 15th, Ch107 the 16th and 17th, Ch108 the 18th and 19th, Ch109 the 20th, 21st and 22nd, Ch110 the 23rd and 24th. **No day is in a chapter twice and no man is paid for two days in one chapter.**

**The hearing room's slate wall.** It is wiped every Monday at eight whether there is a sitting or not. **The last wipe before this batch was Monday the first of May and the one chalk figure went off it on Monday the eighth at eight; the wall was bare at eight on the eighth, on the fifteenth and on the twenty-second, and there was no sitting on any of the three days and nothing was put up in its place on any of them.** The clerk kept his own page on all three mornings and said so each time: a side and three-quarters on the eighth, a side and a half on the fifteenth, and on the twenty-second that the first time in five months he has come up the hill and been given less than he came for. **The hearing room itself is used once in this batch, on Wednesday the seventeenth, for the eighth of the reeve's acknowledgements; and the market wall is used once, on Thursday the eighteenth. No two institutional days in this batch are the same day and neither is a Monday.** Chapter 101 mentions the wall's one chalk figure and is at the fish dock and the mole's steps, and Chapter 104's figures are read back at the head of the dock and not on a wall.

**The age of the notice of the thirty-first of October, in the book's own arithmetic: six months and two days on the third of May, six months and three days on the fourth, six months and five days on the sixth, six months and a week on the eighth, and six months and sixteen days on the seventeenth.** The figure **eight months is a Chapter 100 error, is not repeated anywhere in Chapters 101–110, and is not to be propagated into any later file.**

### 2. The money chain, mark by mark

**THIRTEEN marks in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, and nothing in a tin, on Wednesday the twenty-fourth of May. THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-SEVEN WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED BY THE BATCH 0001 REVIEW REPAIRS: THE BAG CLOSES ON THIRTEEN, WHICH IS THE FIGURE IT OPENED THE BATCH ON.**

| Date | In | Out | Bag | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wed 3 May | — | — | **13** | 23 |
| Thu 4 May | — | nothing moves. **Return 1 made, two days late.** The thirteenth week of a wage, four marks, entered by the boy in his own right hand, Tuesday the second of May, unpaid, with the reason that it went in on the fourth — **the column becomes five lines and twenty marks** | **13** | 23 |
| Fri 5 – Sun 7 May | — | — | **13** | 23 |
| Mon 8 May | **12 in, NOT 26.** The gross on the board is twenty-six and the yard's own column of it is **twelve**; the other fourteen — the eighth three, the share three, the office's own pot of air two, rope one, the victuals two, a hired hand three — is the harbor authority's and is in the clerk's coat, and was never in the bag. Wil Slee of the tide gang is on this office's own payroll and is not in the column and is not in this yard's. The twelve is counted out of the clerk's coat with the hour written and read back twice | **the fourteenth week of a wage is not paid today** | **25** | 23 |
| Tue 9 May | — | **4**, the fourteenth week of a wage, paid on the Tuesday out of the twelve, and not announced on the Monday | **21** | 23 |
| Wed 10 May | **4**, the pump hired out for the day at four marks | **3**, a fortnight of bread, in the creditor's own hand **and paid out of the bag onto the counter while the pen was still wet**, the bag being eighteen when the holder was outside the door. **The gland goes on the page at the creditor's own figure of five in the creditor's own hand, on a page standing at twenty-three, and the carting is not on the page, and the five of the gland is on the page and NOT paid, because a figure written on a note is not a figure paid until somebody puts the money down** — which takes the page to twenty-eight and then to thirty-one | **22** | **31** |
| Thu 11 May | **4**, the second day of the same pump | **1**, **a man this yard did not know, paid a mark, with the yard's own handcart**, two miles up the coast road and back with the casting | **25** | 31 |
| Fri 12 – Sun 14 May | — | — | **25** | 31 |
| Mon 15 May | — | **4**, the fifteenth week of a wage, paid on the Monday and not the Tuesday for a reason entered on the page. **The figure at nine that morning, when the boy read the position out one last time, is twenty-five** | **21** | 31 |
| Tue 16 – Mon 22 May | — | nothing moves. **No wage week is entered on the sixteenth or the twenty-third, and the reason is on the page: this yard has no reader** | **21** | 31 |
| Tue 23 May | — | **8**, a boat for the day and a man to row her. **Out of the bag, not out of a marked pot: the pump's two days brought eight marks in and four of them had already gone to the bread and the handcart. The chapter now says on the page that the whole of the money came out of a day at a seam and a pump let out of the shed, which are the only two things this yard has earned since the first of February** | **13** | 31 |
| Wed 24 May | — | nothing moves. **The chapter closes on thirteen and says that it is the same thirteen that were in the bag on the third of May, on the day a writ was granted and a boy was told in a room that he was going off a stone** | **13** | 31 |

**THE FULL CHAIN, CORRECTED: 13, 13, 13, 25, 21, 22, 25, 25, 21, 21, 13, 13** in the bag, and **23, 23, 23, 23, 23, 31, 31** on the note. The tin is empty and has been since the twenty-eighth of April. THE BAG OPENS THE BATCH AT THIRTEEN AND CLOSES IT AT THIRTEEN, TWENTY-TWO DAYS AND ONE WORKING DAY LATER, AND EVERY MARK OF THAT IS NOW ON THE PAGE IN FOUR CHAPTERS: Ch103 (thirteen at eight, twenty-five at night, twenty-one after the wage), Ch104 (twenty-one, eighteen, twenty-two, twenty-five), Ch105 (twenty-five on the Saturday), Ch106 (twenty-five at nine on the Monday, with the four movements itemised), and Ch110 (thirteen on the Wednesday night).**

**Three things about this chain that a later phase may not get wrong.**
1. **The bag moves for the first time since the twenty-ninth of April and it moves twice, and both movements are the yard's own decision.** The gross of Monday the eighth is twenty-six on the board and **twelve in the bag**; the other fourteen is the harbor's and never was the yard's. The yard says both figures out loud and does not pretend the second is the first. **The only money this harbor has paid that yard since the first of February is now twenty-six plus six, and twelve of the twenty-six ever reached the yard's own bag, and the yard says that on the page in Ch103 rather than in a file.**
2. **The money Chapter 110 spends on a boat's day came out of a day at a seam and a pump let out of the shed, and the chapter says where it came from, on the page, before anybody gets wet.** The pump's two days brought in eight marks and four of them had already gone to the bread and the handcart, so Chapter 110 says *a day at a seam on Monday the eighth and a pump this yard let out of its own shed for two days at four marks a day, and those are the only two things this yard has earned since the first of February* — which is true and is the sentence that replaced the old claim that the whole eight marks were the pump's. The pump is not out of this shed again inside four years without a reason of that kind.
3. **The three counts of the note's weeks — the creditor's twenty-two, the yard's thirteen, and eleven as the last figure ever written in that page — do not move, and were not moved, and are not moved by the gland or by the bread.** The note is not re-written. The refusal of the thirty-first of March stands unreversed.

**Debts, all standing, all unpaid, none reduced, except the one that is paid and the one that is new.**

- **PAID — eight marks and a day of a boat to the harbor's bellwright, Corvin Sarn**, on Saturday the twenty-ninth of April, out of four hands in a market and not one mark out of the yard's own work. Unchanged in this batch. **What is new is that the going out it bought was done at about ten past three on Tuesday the second of May in a flat calm and is spent, and the next one is in about four years, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed.**
- **NEW, AND OWED: eight marks and a day of a boat, entered as owing to the same man a second time inside six weeks, entered as owing and not as paid.** It is entered on Tuesday the twenty-third of May, in the yard's own hand, with the sentence that a going out is bought and named and paid and entered and this was a boat hired for a day, that the yard has no instrument for that man, did not buy one, and is not going to, and that it is not going to be thanked for not asking. **It is the batch's standing owing and it is a craft that recurs.**
- **A wage at four marks a week from the first of February.** The column closes the batch **five lines and twenty marks, the fifth of March the oldest**, and the fifth stays in it on purpose. The fourteenth week is paid out of the day rate on the ninth; the fifteenth is paid on the Monday the fifteenth in advance, with the reason that this yard is not going to pay a boy a wage on a Tuesday with a hand that cannot hold a pencil in it; **the sixteenth falls on Tuesday the sixteenth of May and no line goes in for it, and the reason that goes in is that this yard has no reader, and the twenty-third is the same.** **Movement 2 inherits a wage that runs on and a column nobody is filling, and the two have to be kept apart.**
- **Forty marks to Bevin Rook** — sixteen for the reading of the book from the fourteenth of October, twenty-four for the hand from the twenty-ninth — **stand whole, unpaid, not reduced, not asked to be reduced, and not one mark paid in this batch, and nobody in that crew asked him one word about either of the two omissions he entered, then or afterwards.**
- **Forty marks to Mara Venn**, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, **not paid and not paid out of any market money and not reachable by the writ.** She is in a market on the eighteenth of May, not asked, prices six marks and two days out loud, repeats that a shop at Ashtide refused her four marks in writing for an opinion she gave in a market, and says the forty is not one mark smaller for it. She is not of the yard, is not an assistant, and has not been asked to be useful.
- **Eight shillings and tenpence to Sella Brigg**, undiminished and unpaid, with about four hundred feet of her own warp on the bottom of this harbor.
- **Twenty marks of unsold iron with a saw kerf in one crown shackle, and four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in three pieces off the mole's own face between the third and fourth seams raised on the twenty-eighth of April, in the same heap behind the shed, one of the three a bell-metal gun-port.** Unsold, unweighed, unpriced, not offered to anybody, and **in a heap the yard may not sell one mark's worth of without a buyer reading the declaration first.** The nineteen marks of iron on the page on the eighth of March was the pump's housing and is a different sum for a different casting in a different month, and the two are never in the same sentence.

**What the air cost and what it bought. A compressor's gland is bought, at the creditor's own figure of five marks, on a page standing at twenty-three, and the carting is not on the page. There is still no delivery hose in this harbor and there is no air in this harbor.** The one pot of air in this batch was bought of the harbor authority at the yard's own two-mark rate, filled at the office's compressor and carried on the office's own hose, used on the eighth of May, and it was the whole of the air in this harbor both before and after, and it is an empty pot on a cart. **The gland is not fitted and will not be for nine days and the pump is on a lighterman's wharf until the Friday morning, and the yard has entered that it is going to be without air for nine days and has said so out loud twice.**

### 3. Every document and instrument created in this batch

- **Return 1, Thursday the fourth of May, two days late**, one page, in Calder Marr's own left hand, read out by the boy and then by the clerk and read out twice. It states the finding of Tuesday the twenty-eighth of April, the mole's own face between the third and fourth seams, under a claim window of one date, with two men not of the party in the water each fixing his own limit at the boat; **what was raised, being four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in three pieces of which the second is a bell-metal gun-port off something that had a gun in it, the first piece — a chain shackle and a turn of old cable — up at about twenty-six minutes past eleven in the morning, and the three pieces off the bedded material at about twenty past one in the afternoon; and a lump of green glass in a bucket.** Place, hour, keeper and limits, with **the keeper's line disclosing that the object was in a boat in a shed in this harbor, which the yard entered of itself and unasked.** The two days between the finding and the return are on the page and not in a margin. **The yard's own position is entered as a fact and not as an argument: the party does not know which limb of the clause caught the lump and has not waited to find out, and a lump of green glass is itself a finding of worked glass on the twenty-eighth of April, so the first limb is enough.** A copy went to the harbor authority's ledger in the clerk's own hand.
- **The clerk's own line of the fourth of May, his own book, not the yard's and not the office's record of the return:** that the object was in a shed of this harbor from the twenty-eighth of April to that day, that the party entered the fact itself in its own return without being asked, and that the office has had a notice on the mole since the thirty-first of October which says it may not be in there, and has not argued the notice. **That is the second half of a figure and the clerk said so.**
- **The second column's line about the six days**, entered in Calder Marr's own left hand on the fourth: the reason is that no man on a mole in this harbor could check where a bucket was standing between the twenty-eighth of April and the fourth of May, and that is true, and it is also the reason a man in a city is going to be able to check it, and both are in the same line.
- **The thirteenth week of a wage**, entered by the boy in his own right hand on the fourth of May: four marks, Tuesday the second of May, unpaid, with the reason that it went in on the fourth.
- **The seventh of the reeve's acknowledgements**, Saturday the sixth of May, in Kest's own hand, read out on the crown and read back by the clerk, at the yard's asking and the third time in three months, for the party standing on the mole's crown between the third and ninth seams in the open air with a bucket, going in no water and raising nothing. It names the six before it, says the sixth of them was the twenty-seventh of April and was asked for by a party, says the cost of all of them is the reeve's and is never free, and says the office has no power to lift the notice and is not lifting it and has never lifted it.
- **The two columns of the lump of green glass**, Saturday the sixth, read back by the boy and by the other three. First column: the object, the sun edge, **the scalloped edge of eleven scallops in about four inches which is not a fracture and not a mould line**, the broken face, the place it came out of, that it carries no claim seal and is in no office's book and has no number anywhere, that it is not a panel, and that it is not the ferry plate and not the worked panel given back in a market on the seventh of April and is not connected with it. Second column, with a reason against every line: the mass of it, the sound of it struck once on a board with a wrench handle, and the look of it. **And the age of it goes in neither column**, on a line of its own, with the reason that the party declines to give a figure for it and declines to give a reason for declining.
- **The finding of the sixth of May and the date of the second return, said out loud on the crown** — the sixth is the finding, the day of the finding is not counted, the return is due on the tenth — **and the clerk entered the finding and the day in the first column because it was a statement of fact made by a man who knows the trade and could be asked about it again in a market on Friday.**
- **The harbor authority's day order of the eighth of May**, its own paper, for a day at the second seam on the office's own account at the yard's own rate under the writ, out of the harbor's own day work and not out of the repair account, which has been empty since the middle of February.
- **The reason of Monday the eighth of May**, in Calder Marr's own left hand, in the harbor authority's own book, with the clerk present, unedited and unsummarized: the ground landward of the third seam's mark and nothing above it and nothing under the bedded material; **the purpose, being a ring and a shackle of the office's own ground tackle and about four feet of herring warp that is a herring boat's own, on her own account, and is not being claimed**; the two divers, **Tam Slee of the *Grey Widow* — the fourth person on the writ — and Wil Slee of this harbor's own tide gang**, neither of them of the party, each fixing his own limit at the boat; **the tender as Neale, foreman of the tide gang, not a diver, the cutoff his to pull without asking anybody**; the air as the office's own; **and the sentence that the party is not on the office's work and is standing on that ground as the owner of a bucket and of nothing else.** The eighth and the share come off the day rate, with Nessa Pike's name against the argument for taking them and the name of the man who ordered it against her name.
- **Return 2, Wednesday the tenth of May, on the day it was due**, one page, shorter than the first, in the yard's own left hand, read by the boy and by the clerk and read twice: the finding of the sixth at about eleven in the morning on the crown in the open air in daylight; the place, the hour, **the keeper and the fact that the object is on the mole's own ground in the open air under a tarpaulin and is not in any shed and is not in any boat**; the limits, **that the object is worth nothing to anybody the party has been able to find out about**, that it is not a panel and is not the ferry plate and is not the worked panel of the seventh of April, that its age is not figured and no reason for declining is given, and that **the scalloped edge is in the first column because a man on a mole can look at an edge, and the party's sentence is that it does not know what the edge means and a clause asks for a finding and not a meaning.** The clerk wrote on his own page that the first of the two was two days late and the second was on the day and both are the same instrument.
- **The gland on the chandler's page**, Wednesday the tenth, the creditor's own figure of five marks in the creditor's own hand on a page standing at twenty-three, **the carting not on the page**, and a fortnight of bread at three the same afternoon.
- **The pump's hire**, Wednesday the tenth and Thursday the eleventh, two days at four marks a day, eight marks in, the counter read out as four in silver and four in small coin, the refusal of the twenty-second of April entered against the yard with no reason against it, **and the whole of the hirer saying the man in his own words, including that he would have paid four and a quarter and should not have been made to come up the hill twice for it.** Nessa Pike's signature against losing the pump.
- **The refused day**, Friday the twelfth. **Tarner is entered in the yard's book as the fifth man refused on that mole since the fourteenth of October, in the yard's own words and with his name on it**, and the reason is entered: a man who is not on the writ is a man who cannot be reported.
- **The copy of the tenth thing** given to Noll Tarner in his own hand at no charge with his name on it, **with the count of the six hand-copies expressly not altered and the reason entered, because this is a copy of a copy and no two men in this harbor are to be able to tell in four years which is which.** Tarner's own statement that he is not going to sell it and not going to give it to a naval officer goes in the first column.
- **The line of Saturday the thirteenth of May**, in the yard's own book, **not sent to anybody and not in the harbor authority's ledger and not in a circuit office**: no finding of worked glass and nothing raised off ground any other party claims since the sixth, no return goes up, and the reason, and that a return about nothing is not a smaller return but is not a return. Read back by the boy and by the other two.
- **The refusal of the eight-clause plan**, four pages, **signed on Friday the twelfth of May and served on Tuesday the sixteenth**, on four grounds, every one of them right, with Elias Voke in the room because he carried it on the seventeenth of January. **THE SIGNATURE DATE IS CORRECTED BY THE BATCH 0001 REVIEW REPAIRS: IT WAS THE TWELFTH OF JANUARY, WHICH IS FIVE DAYS BEFORE THE PLAN IT REFUSES AND IS ALSO THE DAY VOLUME 01'S OWN SIX-CLAUSE PLAN CAME BACK, SO THE TWO DOCUMENTS SHARED A DATE. The refusal is now signed on the twelfth of MAY, four days before it was served, and the sixth of January's six-clause document keeps the twelfth of January to itself.**
- **The eighth of the reeve's acknowledgements**, Wednesday the seventeenth of May, in Kest's own hand, unasked by any party, and the **ninth of his instruments and the second of his claim windows in a month:** Monday the twelfth of June, at the top of the flood, on the mole's toe and inner face between the third and ninth seams, the officer of the office with the clerk and two tide-gang men, to enter in the office's own book what the notice of the thirty-first has cost this harbor authority; **no person of the yard upon that ground and not to be asked for and would be refused**; the notice not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered; the seventh named, and the six before it, and the sentence that the sixth and the seventh were each asked for by a party and this eighth was asked for by nobody, and the cost is the reeve's and is never free.
- **The four-sentence answer on the back of a slate**, Thursday the eighteenth, in Calder Marr's own hand, read aloud in a market and copied onto another man's slate, and entered in the yard's own book in the second column with a reason.
- **The demand of Saturday the twentieth of May**, four lines, in Ambrose Sell's hand, naming Monday the twenty-second at ten at the top of the mole's steps landward of the third seam's mark, and stating that it is a demand and not a complaint and that no answer is asked for.
- **The answer given to the clerk on the Saturday, out loud, in front of two men, and written down by him:** that the yard will stand on the top of the steps landward of the third seam's mark, where the ground the notice of the thirty-first closes begins below. **And a second line under it in the clerk's own book, his own reading and not a ruling**, that the ground the notice closes begins below the top of the mole's steps at Morrow Quay, and that this office has no power to narrow that notice and has not attempted to narrow it this morning.
- **The third column's Sunday line**, the twenty-first of May, in Calder Marr's own left hand, under the two times: that no man in the party is filling it, that no man in the party can now say whether the two times are the same time, and that the party is going to keep the column and is not going to scratch out the lines in it and is not going to pretend it is current, **and the reason is that a column with a head on it and nothing under it is the honest shape of this yard on the twenty-first of May and a man in a city is owed that shape and not a better one.**
- **The production of Monday the twenty-second**, on a mole, in the open air: the writ with the clause and the three names on the clause and no face in the room; the crew's book, entire, both columns and the third column and the reasons; **both returns, unshortened**; the tenth thing; the slip with the eleven names and the four unreached holders named. **And the sentence that there is no third return and no fourth, that this yard has made two, and that if a third is asked for this yard will say it has made two.**
