- **The difference between a going out and a boat**, entered in the yard's own left hand on the morning of the twenty-third, before anybody got wet, and the standing sentence of the third of May set under it unreversed.
- **The eight marks and a day of a boat entered as owing to the harbor's bellwright a second time**, on the twenty-third, in the yard's own left hand, and not as paid.
- **The two columns of the twenty-third**, carrying only the part of the bellwright's account that is new, and the same three lines of the second column with the same reasons, because a man is not to be asked to say the same thing twice.

**There is no ninth acknowledgement, there is no fifth seam access, and there is no day in this batch on which a person, a boat or a lead of this yard went inside the first clause of the notice of the thirty-first of October except the sixth of May, and that was a person with a bucket in the open air on a crown and no water and no raising, entered as the seventh.**

### 4. The bodies and the crew

- **Calder Marr**, 32, line diver with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice and not used since the eighth of December, and the lift at the end of a childhood call gone and not restorable. **He goes in no water in this batch, enters nothing, and takes no stage of any kind.** **THE CONDITION IS NOT NAMED IN THIS BATCH.** **IT IS ON THE PAGE AS THE BARE FACT ONCE, IN THE BOAT OFF THE SOUTH END ON THE TWENTY-THIRD, where the tide bell goes at ten past three, the water gives it back a hand's width of a second later, there is no boy on the steps with a slate and a stopwatch, and he cannot say in that boat which of the two sounds is in the boat — and it goes into the second column with a reason against it and nobody in that crew asks him about it. THE CLAIM THAT IT APPEARED TWICE, ONE OF THEM ON THE MOLE'S STEPS ON THE FOURTH, WAS A STATE-FILE ERROR AND IS CORRECTED BY THE BATCH 0001 REVIEW REPAIRS: NO CHAPTER OF THE BATCH PLACED A SOUND ON THE FOURTH, AND THE CHAPTER DOES NOT NOW. What is on the page on the fourth is the condition's COST and not its symptom — Calder Marr alone in the shed, on the steps, remembering the eighth of December and the second thing at the end of a childhood call that is not there and cannot be put back, and the eleven times since December that he has gone down those steps to look at a tide and come back up without looking at the water.** **On the twenty-third a man of fifty gives him a sentence for it and Nessa Pike puts it in his own words and says nobody is going to ask him about it and that there is nobody left in this yard who would think of it. The naming is NOT spent by this batch, and Movement 4 of this volume is where the volume's own structure wants it spent.**
- **Nessa Pike**, 27. **She is right in this batch five times and every one of them costs something, and the fifth is now delivered on the page rather than declared in a closing sentence: in Ch107 she makes Calder Marr enter in the office's own book, in front of the clerk, that this yard is not arguing one of the four grounds, and it costs the yard the whole of Tuesday the sixteenth — a pump stripped late and a ring and a shackle and four feet of a herring boat's own warp laid out late.** She insists the lump be entered as worked glass in the first column and the yard's "it is nothing" in the first column and not the second; she signs her own name against taking the eighth and the share on the authority's account and puts the name of the man who ordered it against hers; she signs against losing the pump for two days; she fixes the air paragraph; and on the mole's steps on the twenty-second she tells a clerk of a circuit office in advance that she will be four feet from the holder of the writ for the whole of it. **Nobody thanks her for any of it and she does not want to be thanked.**
- **Bevin Rook**, 22, **off the stone on Monday the fifteenth of May and not replaced.** His hand had been going since the fourth of April and the yard said so in a room on the first of May. He is the reader for two hundred days; he reads the position out one last time and reads it twice, the second time in eleven minutes against seven; he says the thing nobody asked for twice more, in the middle of a list and not at the end of it, on the eighth and the tenth; he is paid four marks for the week of the ninth to the fifteenth, on the Monday; and **he leaves the book on the bench with the pencil on top of it and says the sentence that is the batch's centre.** Nobody in that crew asks him one word about either of the two omissions he entered, then or afterwards. The forty marks of claim stand whole.
- **Corvin Sarn**, 50, the harbor's own bellwright, **paid**, teaching nobody, of no crew of this association. **He is NOT asked the one question in this batch — not in a shed, not in a boat, not in a market, not on a mole, not in a room — and the yard has the question ready and says the reason out loud on the water, and the reason is the boy's hand and not the man's manner.** He comes out in a boat he is not paid to be in, gives the part of his account that is new and only that, does not look away, is not thanked, says the delay has never moved in his time in the harbor and will not stand in a boat and say why, puts a hand on a line and says one word, and says the thing he said in a shed doorway in April and is told it went on a wall and the light is not on.
- **Noll Tarner**, 34, of the *Sea Wren*, at the pile since the fourth of March. He asks for a day, **is refused in a form he can check, is given a free copy of the tenth thing with his name on it, and does not become a villain and does not ask twice.** He supplies the turn — *a road that no chart carries is a thing a man can sell, and the first question is not what is under it, it is who pays for the map* — and **names Lowfen**, about nine miles off, where the channels are kept by hand by men who do not want a diver, which goes in the second column with a reason because he has not been there. **He is never thanked.**
- **Elias Voke**, 51, of the *Anna Ray*, nineteen winters disputed and unpaid. **The price of a correct refusal lands on him, in a room, in front of the man who wrote it**, and he does not shout. He asks for his name and his boat and the nineteen winters in his own hand in the account the harbor authority is sending up the coast, and says one sentence that is his and not the reeve's.
- **Mara Venn**, 30, no office, a trade, **expensive, right, and not broken.** She is in a market on the eighteenth of May, not asked, **prices six marks and two days out loud**, says this yard has had two of her findings for nothing and has not been told what they cost, repeats the refused four marks in writing from a shop at Ashtide, and says the forty marks is not one mark smaller for it. **She is not asked to be useful, she is not of the yard, she is not an assistant, and the writ cannot reach her. Her break is Volume 4 and nothing in this batch is a step toward it.**
- **Harl Dace**, 48 — the herring warp of the eighth of May goes back onto the third lay's wharf in his own hands and is not claimed by anybody. **Tarner's man of fifty-one with a bad wheel is not in this batch, is not on any lead, and is not asked anything.**
- **Wil Slee and Tam Slee**, the two men in the water on the eighth of May. **There is one man of the *Grey Widow* and he is Tam Slee, who is the fourth name on the writ; the other is Wil Slee of this harbor's own tide gang; and Neale, Wil Slee's brother-in-law, is the tender. There are not two men of the *Grey Widow* in this batch and a chapter that says so has stopped reading.**
- **Neale**, foreman of the harbor's tide gang, is the tender on the eighth of May with the cutoff his, and holds the lead line for twenty-two minutes. He is a harbor-authority man and is not a diver and is not of the party, and the reason says why he has to be.
- **The boatman of the second lay** who hires the boat and rows it on the twenty-third, **and who is the same man who hired the yard's pump for two days on the tenth and the eleventh**: unnamed, of the second lay — **THE SOUTH LAY IS CORRECTED BY THE BATCH 0001 REVIEW REPAIRS; the south lay is a distinct place with its own man of fifty-eight in it and the pump hirer is of the SECOND lay in Ch104** — nineteen years in and out of this harbor on his own account, who has charged eleven a day since he was twenty-four, who says the eight is below the going rate out loud so that the yard knows what it has got, **and who says he has not had a week's work since the twenty-first of March — WHICH IS THE SAME MAN WHO SAYS IN CH104 THAT HE HAS HIRED THAT PUMP FOUR TIMES SINCE THE TWENTY-FIRST OF MARCH, and who does NOT say he has had nothing since the first of March.** He walked down the hill on the Friday the twelfth after the pump came back, was told there might be a boat, and came on the Tuesday. He is in the first column because a man in a shed can be asked.
- **The man with a slate**, Thursday the eighteenth: unnamed, trades up and down this coast, is nobody's clerk, was in the street at the head of this yard's own dock on the third of May with the same slate, has been asking the same four questions since the middle of April, and has written down every answer he has been given including the noes. **He is the first customer the coast road has had and he is not the Crown and not an office, and the yard refuses to sell him a copy, and his four pages are in a market in this harbor and are not the yard's to correct.**
- **The chandler at the sign of the Drowned Bell**, unnamed as on the page, who entered five marks in his own hand and would not put the carting on it and said why without being asked.
- **The harbor authority's clerk and Ambrose Sell, a clerk of the circuit office**, are clerks, and say what a box can hold, and **neither is an antagonist and neither may become one.** Sell writes down where the yard will stand and why, and writes a second line under it in his own hand that he may be wrong about.
- **Alden Marr** is at the far bench in **Chapters 101, 106 and 110** — **THE CHAPTER NUMBERS ARE CORRECTED BY THE BATCH 0001 REVIEW REPAIRS; there is no Alden Marr in Chapter 104 and there never was, and Chapter 101 and Chapter 106 now render the same refusal in two different sentences, which is the only relationship in this book that should not sound the same twice** — is not asked for anything, and **Calder Marr does not go over to him. No fragment, no report talk, no second story, no confession.**
- **The woman in the net loft** who said on the second of May that the hand lead's line would be right in a fortnight or not at all says on the twenty-fourth, in about nine words, that it is right, and that she took eleven days instead of seven because a boy went off a stone that week.

### 5. The dated positions at the end of Chapter 110

- **The writ.** A provisional writ of salvage working granted Monday the first of May, running to the first of March next, **not the Second Bell license, not a charter, renewing nothing, and its ground not widened**: the mole from the first seam to the twelfth, the dump, the north wall, the first and second seams, all ground landward of the third seam, and private ground within the line. **The four names are unchanged and no fifth name has been added anywhere**: Calder Marr, Nessa Pike, Bevin Rook, and Tam Slee of the *Grey Widow*, hired and not of this association. **It was produced on demand on Monday the twenty-second of May at the top of the mole's steps, in the open air, above the third seam's mark, with the clause, the book, both returns and two public sheets, and nothing else.** The second season is a named absence and no chapter has manufactured a renewal.
- **The reporting clause.** **Two returns have been made and there is no third and no fourth.** Return 1 on the fourth of May, two days late. Return 2 on the tenth of May, on the day. A dated line in the yard's own book on the thirteenth of May says no return goes up and why, and that line has not been sent to anybody. **A third return of the volume falls due on Saturday the seventeenth of June, out of a shard of worked glass raised on Saturday the tenth of June on ground landward of the third seam, in a boat on a mole because the second clause keeps it out of every shed — and it is Movement 3 that makes it, and Movement 2 does not.** **The second limb is a claim and not a closure, and a ground the notice closes is not a claimed ground, and the *Vigil's* claim lies beyond the outer marks and does not touch the mole's face: the yard relies on the first limb in Return 1 on the ground that a lump of green glass is itself a finding of worked glass, and says it does not know which limb caught it and has not waited to find out.**
- **The notice of the thirty-first of October.** Four clauses, not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered, **and the harbor authority has no power to lift it and has said so in its own ledger for the eighth time and did not lift it on the seventeenth of May either.** Clause two is on the page three times as a reason and is not argued by anybody: the lump was not looked at in a shed, was looked at on a crown in daylight, and is now on a crown under a tarpaulin and two stones. Clause three names Calder Marr, Nessa Pike and Bevin Rook and none of the three has been on the authority's work, which is why the tender on the eighth of May had to be Neale. **There are eight acknowledgements in the harbor authority's ledger at the end of this batch and both this batch's are spent, so MOVEMENT 2 ENTERS NONE AND PUTS NO PERSON, BOAT OR LEAD OF THIS YARD INSIDE THE FIRST CLAUSE.** The cost of all eight is the reeve's and is never free.
- **The eighth-clause plan for the fifth seam is REFUSED and the refusal is in a record with a date on it.** Four grounds, all right. The plate is still in eleven feet in the mole's own foundation stone, **seen and not measured and not touched, and nobody in this harbor has said one word about it since the seventeenth of May.** What would move it is a change of politics in an office on a schedule nobody can name, and the first act of that change is a claim window for Monday the twelfth of June, in the office's own account, to go out and write down what the notice has cost — **and it is not for this yard, this yard is not to be on that ground, and no person of this yard is to be asked to be.**
- **The bell.** Eight and a half feet off the south end, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame at all and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was**, a new rope bought on the eleventh of January. **The going out of the second of May is done and is spent, and it may not be done again inside four years, and no second eight marks is paid, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed.** **What this batch adds is a boat that is not a going out, on the yard's own account, out of the pump's eight marks, with the harbor's own bellwright in it for nothing, and eight marks and a day of a boat entered as owing to him a second time in six weeks.** The delay is a hand's width of a second and has not moved, and it is not entered as an occasion, and the count of logged occasions stands at eleven and the two empty answers at two.
- **The lead plug.** Untouched. In a chest in a shed, four descriptions that do not agree, a number in no book, the boy having written on the lid that there is nothing in it that anybody in this harbor can check. **The bellwright was not asked the one question, and the yard says on the water that the reason is the boy's hand and not the man's manner.**
- **The glass storm.** Untouched and not used. It is in the second column with a reason against it and **no cause has been named for it and none may be, in this or any later volume, and it may never become a voice.**
- **The coast road.** Published in six boats in six hands, **and it has its first customer and he is nobody's clerk, and the yard refuses to sell him a copy, and the reason is given on the boards and copied into his own hand.** The word is not on the wall. **No document has come back out of a city in this batch.** The road is not explained, not named, not connected to anything under the town, and the two are never fused. The nine final routes are not named in the prose. Orison is a place seventy fathoms down and the yard cannot put that and eleven feet in the same sentence.
- **Custody.** Three items, three numbers, digits not quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request. **No paper states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material, and no clerk answered it in this batch and none may in any volume.**
- **The concealment.** Hidden. The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. The false line on the unverified slate is still false. **Nobody in this batch asked the boy, and nobody asked the conservator, and the boy was asked one word about neither of his two omissions.** It was told once, in Chapter 38, and it is not told again.
- **The three custody numbers, the compartment, the ten yards, the run under the town, the first echo's witness, the nine-line mark, the dressed aperture, the plate dug on the mole's toe in February, and the ferry plate.** All untouched by this batch. No chapter of Chapters 101–110 refers to the compartment, and the three-step finding is therefore not restated. No line is drawn between the three points and the reeve's ruling stands. **Nine and a quarter feet is not mentioned anywhere in this batch.**
- **Labor.** No air and no hose; a gland bought and not fitted; a pot spent on the eighth and no air in the harbor since; **a hand lead's line the woman in the net loft says is right on the twenty-fourth, in about nine words, and no knot in it has been taken out**; the ninth seam's mooring out of service with no end of it entered; the north berth out; the third lay full; the tide gang four men short; the cistern charged on a rota that a man does not walk; the fire party's hose dead for the season; the mole-head light not on and the repair account empty since the middle of February; the harbor authority with no boat of its own; the *Kittiwake* on the hard with no cradle; the hard at Ashtide unanswered since the ninth of November; nineteen winters disputed and unpaid, and going up a coast in a file with a harbor authority's own account attached; the *Sea Wren* at the pile; the eleven copies and the four unreached holders not asked; **the twenty marks of iron and the four hundred and forty pounds and the bell-metal gun-port in one heap behind the shed that may not be sold one mark's worth of without a buyer reading the declaration first**; and **Neave Tarrant, nine winters, out since the twenty-eighth of February, not found, one line in the book, and the yard has not gone and has asked nobody anything.**

### 6. What this batch may not be used for

1. **It may not be used to advance the delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, two of them with no call in them, and neither figure has moved and neither is moved by the figure Calder Marr took himself in a boat on the twenty-third. **A figure taken on purpose in a flat calm is a figure and not a note heard out of the weather, and no occasion is entered in this batch, and a man with a slate has four pages in a market and no record of anything said to him in a boat, because a man in a boat is not a man in a market and this yard keeps the two apart.** The answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.
2. **It may not be used to spend the naming of the condition.** It is not named in this batch, twice, in the bare form Volume 02 left it in, with a reason against it and nobody asking. **The one naming is therefore still available and is owed to Movement 4, and no Movement 2 or 3 chapter may take it, and no chapter in this batch may be read as having taken it.**
3. **It may not be used to put a fifth name on the writ, or to widen the writ's ground, or to treat the writ as a charter or a renewal, or to manufacture a second season.** The yard refused a man a day in a form he can check **because** four names is a leash and a crew at the same time.
4. **It may not be used to make a return out of a day with nothing in it, to produce a third return, or to widen the reporting clause.** The yard said out loud on a mole on the twenty-second that it has made two and that if a third is asked for it will say it has made two.
5. **It may not be used to lift, narrow, re-serve or alter the notice of the thirty-first, to argue clause two in a shed, to reach the plate at the fifth seam, or to put a ninth acknowledgement in the ledger.** **Both this batch's acknowledgements are spent and Movement 2 enters none.**
6. **It may not be used to name the Nine Harbors, the Quietening or the Crown Seal, to explain the nine-line mark, to explain or name the coast road, to connect it to anything under the town, to name the compartment or to state that the sealed case was in standing space number four, to quote a custody number's digits, to put a senior examiner in a room, to name an office of returning memory, to have Captain Holt appear in a room or at a mole, or to introduce a new antagonist, institution or final enemy.**
7. **It may not be used to open the lead plug's number, to ask the bellwright his one question, to revive the phrase *a tongue half worn through*, to make the bell an instrument, to buy a second going out, to pay a second eight marks, to enter a second going out as done, or to say the going out is anything other than the harbor's bellwright on the harbor's ground on the harbor's account.**
8. **It may not be used to name a cause for the glass storm, to let it speak, to make the man of fifty-one with a bad wheel be interviewed or be put on a lead, to tell the concealment a second time, to ask Sera Mott, to pay Mara Venn, to pay the eight shillings and tenpence, to reduce the forty marks twice, to sell one mark's worth of the heap behind the shed, to weigh, price or offer the bell-metal gun-port, or to treat a man with a bad wheel in a boat as the man of fifty-one of the *Grey Widow*.**
9. **It may not be used to bring back a custody object, to re-write the chandler's note, to move any of its three counts of weeks, to move the count of eleven copies, or to send a document out of a city and get an answer back.** The circuit office's fourth request of the sixteenth of March is unanswered and unrefused and is a different paper.
10. **It may not be used to put a diver in the water who is not a hired man with his own limit fixed at the boat, under a named tender who is not a diver, with a cutoff the tender's to pull, with a reason in the office's book before anybody is wet, in Calder Marr's own left hand with the clerk present and unedited.** One day of this batch had a person in the water — the eighth of May — and the conditions are all on the page.

### 7. The costs this batch closes with

1. **A return two days late, about the yard's own bucket.** And in its keeper's line, unasked, the disclosure that the object was in a shed for six days inside the second clause of a notice this yard has not argued and will not.
2. **A compressor's gland bought on credit and no delivery hose bought at all**, and nine days without air, and the only pot in the harbor spent on a Tuesday.
3. **Two days of the yard's own pump, the first hire out of that shed since the fifth of April, and a day lost for every day, and the yard has declared that it may not have the pump back in June.**
4. **A boy off the stone on Monday the fifteenth of May**, at the rate, for the week, with no speech and no thanks, and **a third column with a head on it and nothing under it and a line under the head saying so.**
5. **A wage week with no line in it on Tuesday the sixteenth of May, and another on the twenty-third**, and a column standing at five lines and twenty marks with the fifth of March in it.
6. **A day of work refused to a man who had better gear than this yard and no instrument**, and a copy given away for nothing with his name on it.
7. **A correct refusal of a named plan, and a claim window spent on a purpose that is not this yard's, and nineteen winters of a herring fleet's dues going up a coast in a file that will make them harder to dispute.**
8. **A boat's day at eight marks, all of it the pump's money, and a man of fifty in it who was owed eight marks and a day of a boat in April and is owed it again in May and is not paid and is not asked to be.**
9. **A sale refused to the first customer the coast road has ever had, and a record of the refusal in his own hand in a market that is not the yard's to correct.**

### 8. Proposed canon changes

**None applied. One flagged rather than applied, and one discipline rather than a rule.**

1. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the bucket was in a shed for six days, and the yard has disclosed it to a circuit office in its own return.** Chapters 91–100 are silent about where the bucket was after the twenty-eighth of April, and Chapter 100's read-out does not mention it. **Chapter 101 places it in a boat in a shed from the twenty-eighth of April to the fourth of May, which is a continuation of Volume 02's own sentence — the second clause keeps recovered transparent mineral out of every shed and the yard put the bucket in a boat and did not look at it — and it is a self-reported breach of clause two that no earlier chapter entered.** It is on the page, it is in Return 1 unasked, and the harbor authority's clerk has the other half of the figure in his own book. **It is not a canon change to the notice, which is not lifted, is not argued and is not narrowed; it is an addition to the batch's own standing exposure, and a later phase may not soften it, may not have it resolved by an argument, and may not have the yard lifted the notice as a consequence.** A Volume 03 audit may rule on it.
2. **A discipline, not a canon change: the harbor authority has a compressor, a pot and about four fathoms of hose of its own, and the yard has none of the three.** This is on the page twice — the air of the eighth of May and the pot bought on the seventh of April and filled on the twenty-eighth — and it means **a yard with a writ can work a day on the authority's ground in this harbor, and a yard without air can buy air, and what it cannot buy in this harbor is a gland's hose, because there is no hose in this harbor longer than about nine fathoms.** It does not change any institution and it may be used by any later chapter without further ruling.
3. **The eighth of the reeve's acknowledgements is entered on his own motion and is not asked for by anybody, and its price is a claim window and a file.** This is on the page and it is the volume's mechanism for the fifth seam working in the direction lock L7 asks for — **a change of politics in an office, and not a better letter from a shed** — and it is the shape the plate's mechanism takes for the rest of the volume. It is not an amendment of the notice and it reaches nothing.
4. **The counting rule and the two returns are on the page in Calder Marr's own mouth and in the harbor authority's clerk's own mouth** — the day of the finding is not counted, the fourth day after it is the day it falls due, a return entered late is a return entered late and is not a different instrument and is not a smaller one — **and the counting rule is now spoken, not only observed.**
5. **Two things this batch does not do that a later phase must not assume it did: it does not name the condition, and it does not ask the bellwright.** Both are on the page in the negative and both are owed forward.

---

## Batch 0001 review repairs — the disposition of every finding

A reviewer pass over Chapters 101–110, the four state files and the queued `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` returned **FIX**, with **fifteen required repairs** and a list of prose and craft items. **The repairs were applied in place. No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut, no cost was dropped and no planned plot was altered. The batch is 29,905 words where it was 26,767, and the whole of the increase is material the review required and did not have.** This section is the authority for every repair below, in the same way § *Batch 0001 review repairs* is not cited anywhere else in the repository: the reviewer's log is `logs/batch-0001.review.log` and it is the only review log in this repository that is actually kept.

### The fifteen required repairs

1. **THE MONEY CHAIN COUNTED GROSS AS CASH. THIS WAS THE BATCH'S LARGEST ERROR AND IT IS CORRECTED MARK BY MARK.** Chapter 103 states the column on the page: the gross on the board is **twenty-six** and **the yard's own column of it is twelve**, counted out of the clerk's coat, and the other fourteen — the eighth, the share, the office's own pot of air, the rope, the victuals and a hired hand — is the harbor authority's and was never in the bag. **Every running figure in the batch, in the three state files and in the batch outline's own read-backs derived from twenty-six, and the batch closed on twenty-seven instead of thirteen.** Four things were wrong at once: the eighth, the ninth, the tenth and the eleventh, and the twenty-third.
   - **The 8 May row is +12, not +26.** The chapter now says it in words: the bag was thirteen at eight that morning, the twelve is in it, and the fourteen that came off the top of the twenty-six is in the clerk's coat and is not this yard's.
   - **The pump yields eight, not twelve** (see repair 3), so the tenth and the eleventh are +4 and +4, and the **bread goes onto the note in the creditor's own hand and comes out of the bag the same afternoon**, which is this book's own practice and is on the page in Chapters 63, 68, 74, 90 and 91 of Volume 02. **That is the missing out in the tenth** and it is the only reason the chain closes.
   - **The handcart is a man paid a mark** (see repair 4), so the eleventh is +4 and −1.
   - **THE CORRECTED CHAIN IS 13, 13, 13, 25, 21, 22, 25, 25, 21, 21, 13, 13**, and **the bag closes on THIRTEEN — the same thirteen it held on the third of May, on the day a writ was granted and a boy was told in a room that he was going off a stone.** Chapter 110 says that in its last paragraph and the arithmetic is now checkable in four chapters without leaving the prose.
2. **Chapter 104's read-back was garbled and not merely wrong.** It itemised *+12 in Monday the eighth and four straight out Tuesday the ninth* and then concluded in two figures, neither of them 21, and printed *Thirteen marks in a bag on Tuesday the ninth of May* on the same page as the +12. **The read-back is rewritten whole and now carries the entire chain of the week**: twenty-one after the wage on the Tuesday, eighteen after the bread on the Wednesday morning, twenty-two at eleven o'clock after the pump's first day, and twenty-five at six on the Thursday evening.
3. **The pump yielded twelve on the page and eight everywhere else.** Chapter 104 said *Eight marks in on the Wednesday and four more on the Thursday* for a two-day hire priced at eight. **It is now Four in on the Wednesday and four more on the Thursday, and that is the whole of what a pump is worth for two days, and it is in the coin and not on a page.** Chapters 106, 110, the batch outline card and § 2 above were already right.
4. **The handcart was the yard's own man in one chapter and a man paid a mark in two others and in this file.** **Chapters 104, 106 and this section now agree: a man this yard did not know, paid a mark, with the yard's own handcart, two miles up the coast road and back with the casting.** The batch outline's card is silent on whose cart it is and does not need to change.
5. **Four load-bearing dates carried weekdays from a different grid, and one chapter contradicted itself on the second of May.** The locked grid in `outline/volume-03.md` L1 gives **28 April a Friday, 2 May a Tuesday, 12 January a Monday, 14 May a Sunday**, and the chapters now run on it. **Chapter 101** had the finding of 28 April called a Tuesday and the same 2nd of May called a Saturday in one breath and a Tuesday in the next — the return's due date is now **Tuesday the second of May** and the finding is **Friday the twenty-eighth of April**. **Chapter 102**'s first column is the same Friday. **Chapter 110**'s going-out paragraph said the going out was bought on *Saturday the second of May*; it was bought in a market on **Saturday the twenty-ninth of April** and done on **Tuesday the second of May at ten past three**, which is what the Volume 02 audit had already fixed and what the chapter now says. **Chapter 107**'s refusal was *signed on Tuesday the twelfth of January*, five days before the plan of the seventeenth of January that it refuses, and the twelfth of January is a Monday; it is now **signed on Friday the twelfth of May and served on Tuesday the sixteenth**, four days, and Volume 01's own six-clause plan keeps the twelfth of January to itself. **Chapter 110**'s *the fourteenth of May was a Thursday and it is the twenty-fourth* — the fourteenth is a Sunday and the sentence did not parse — is gone, and the net-loft scene now carries a checkable count instead (see repair 9).
6. **Chapter 101's *I put the bucket in the boat on the Wednesday morning* only worked under the wrong weekday.** The bucket went in on the day it came up, which is **Friday the twenty-eighth of April**, and the chapter now says that. **The six-day disclosure is unaffected and is not softened: the object was in a boat in a shed from the twenty-eighth of April to the fourth of May, and that is still in Return 1's keeper's line unasked and still in the clerk's own book.**
7. **The tide bell rang at two o'clock inside a boat trip that had ended at half past nine.** The canon and the card both place the trip **at the top of the flood**, where the harbor's bell goes and where the boy took the figure on the second of May. **Chapter 110's whole day is re-staged and it is the one repair in this batch that changes a scene's shape:** the boat is hired for the day, goes off at ten past eight with the flood, **lies to off the south end for six hours because the top of the flood is the only hour a man can hear anything in this harbor**, the bellwright goes down the ladder at about a quarter to three, **the bell goes at ten past three**, and they are on the mole's steps at half past three. **The hand's-width-of-a-second measurement and the second bare statement of the unnamed condition are now staged at the hour they belong to, with the bellwright on the ladder.**
8. **Chapter 110 said the two times at the head of the third column were both entered on the Sunday.** They were entered by the boy on or before the second of May — Chapter 106 has him saying so in his own words. **What the Sunday of the twenty-first added was a line under them, in Calder Marr's own hand.** The Wednesday read-back now says that, and adds that the yard has worked out since, out of the hour in the first column, that the two of them are about two hours apart and that nobody in this crew can now say which of the two was the right one.
9. **Chapter 105 narrated a Monday in the past tense two days before it happened.** *The boy was paid to the end of that week on the Monday* was spoken on Saturday the thirteenth about Monday the fifteenth. **It is now a statement of what this yard is going to do on the fifteenth, with the reason, and the Tuesday after is named as the first Tuesday with nobody in the yard to enter it.** The batch outline's card said *that is a Friday's problem* and the sixteenth is a Tuesday; **the card is corrected to a Tuesday's problem.**
10. **Chapter 107's document did not close against itself.** Four pages, four grounds, and a claim that the second document had eight clauses to the first one's six — with the two documents sharing a date, and the first of them a Volume 01 plan that Chapters 36–37 had returned the same afternoon it was lodged. **The signature date is moved to the twelfth of May (repair 5) and the sentence now says that the plan IT ANSWERS has eight clauses where the first one had six**, so the reader knows which document is which.
11. **Chapter 107 declared a cost it never showed.** *Nessa Pike was right about one thing in that room and it cost this yard a day of work that nobody had asked for* was asserted in the closing sentence; she spoke one line in the chapter, and the batch outline's Required character changes promise exactly this beat. **THE BEAT IS NOW DELIVERED, at the head of the dock on the Tuesday morning and again in the room.** She makes Calder Marr enter in the office's own book, in front of the clerk, in his own words, that this party is not arguing one of the four grounds; and the day is put on the page as a day — a pump stripped late, a ring and a shackle and four feet of a herring boat's own warp laid out late — with the reason against it. **The closing sentence is broken into short sentences and the cost is stated, not announced.**
12. **Chapter 103's *Wil Slee … is on this office's own hand* is not English** and this file quoted it. **It is now *is on this office's own payroll*, and § 2 above is corrected to the chapter's wording.**
13. **Chapter 110's boatman did not hold together.** He was *a boatman of the south lay* who had been in the market on the tenth buying a pump — Chapter 104 puts the pump buyer **of the second lay**, and the south lay is a distinct place with its own man of fifty-eight in Chapter 108. He said he had *had nothing since the first of March*, against his own *I have hired that pump four times since the twenty-first of March* and the two days he had just paid for. And *he had been told on the Wednesday afternoon* preceded his own Thursday's walk down the hill. **He is now of the SECOND lay, he says he has not had a week's work since the twenty-first of March, and he walked down the hill on the Friday the twelfth, after the pump came back.** He is the pump hirer and the rower, and § 4 below is corrected to say so.
14. **Three chapters carried the workflow's words inside the book.** Chapter 105's *said the thing **the batch is for***, Chapter 106's *and the sentence is the chapter*, and Chapter 108's *and the refusal is the chapter* are all gone. **The nearest reader-facing sentence in each place is now what the scene actually does** — the copy that is given away, the sentence said once and not again, and a refusal that costs more than the four marks.
15. **THE MONEY FIGURE OF TWENTY-SEVEN IS ALSO STALE IN THE QUEUED NEXT PHASE, AND THAT FILE WAS NOT TOUCHED EITHER.** `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` opens with a one-paragraph précis of the position and says *twenty-seven marks in a bag and thirty-one on a note*. **It is thirteen and thirty-one.** A queued prompt is a dispatch artifact and is not a fiction, bible, outline, chapter, summary, continuity, character or open-thread file, so **it was not edited by this phase**; the correction is recorded here, here, and in `state/current.md`, and **the prompt's own reading list puts `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0001 as written* first, so the state file governs and the précis does.** The same précis's *thirteen marks in a bag, twenty-three on the note* figure in § 33 of that prompt about the pre-batch starting condition is correct and is not the figure in question. **A later phase that is permitted to edit prompt prose should change that one word pair in the précis and nothing else in the file.**
15. **`state/phase-ledger.json` IS ONE HUNDRED AND TEN CHAPTERS OUT OF DATE, AND IT IS THE CONTROLLER'S FILE AND WAS NOT TOUCHED.** It reads `"currentPhase": "phase-000-bootstrap"`, `"status": "running"`, and holds two phases, the second of them `volume-01-batch-0001`. One hundred and ten chapters exist. `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` states that the selector *chooses one explicit next batch by reading `state/phase-ledger.json`* and that it takes *the lowest incomplete batch*, so a controller reading this file would select `volume-01-batch-0001` and rewrite ten completed chapters. **`state/phase-ledger.json` is owned by the workflow and is not a file this phase may edit, so it is FLAGGED HERE AND NOT REPAIRED. It is recorded again in `state/current.md`. No writer phase may edit it, and no volume or batch file was created or removed to compensate for it.**

### The prose and craft repairs

- **Calder Marr had no interior life in twenty-six thousand seven hundred and sixty-seven words: no private moment, no fear, no memory, no indecision, and the review called that the batch's largest gap and a missing beat rather than a style preference.** **He now has four, one in each of the four chapters that needed one, and none of them is a reveal.** On the fourth of May, alone in the shed with a boy's question nobody answered, he stands on the mole's steps in his own memory of the eighth of December and the second thing at the end of a childhood call that is not there and cannot be put back, and eleven times since December he has gone down those steps to look at a tide and come back up without looking at the water. On the sixth, on the crown in daylight, he turns a board of green glass he cannot name and is afraid of how much work it would take to make up a name for it. On the eighth, he stands at the top of the steps and counts another man's minutes and finds that he wants to go down. On the eighteenth, at a market, he has expected the shape of a folded ledger on a bench and finds he has caught himself two days late. On the twenty-first, he admits to himself that he does not want to hand the book to a man in a coat. On the twenty-third, lying to off the south end with nothing to do for six hours, he reads a mole the way a man reads a place he may have to go back to in the dark. **The condition is NOT named in any of them.**
- **Chapter 106 did not land its own chapter, and that is now fixed.** The card asks for a chapter that does not console anybody and the chapter delivered one that did not grieve. **There is now a beat after the boy goes down the hill at four o'clock and nobody follows him.** Nessa Pike refuses to let Calder Marr read the empty third column aloud to nobody in a shed and sends him up the mole instead; he sits on the top of the steps for an hour and does the arithmetic of the difference between the third of May and the fifteenth, and the four marks were paid and the hand is not going to be paid by anybody, ever, **and a hand is not a debt and cannot be put on a page.** He comes down and does the shed's work anyway, which is what a yard does on the evening a man leaves it.
- **The ledger was doing the work the scene should do.** The transcription share was worst in the shortest chapters. **Chapter 103's reason in the harbor authority's book and its column read-back, Chapter 106's whole read-out and Chapter 102's two columns are all cut by roughly a quarter to a third, with every load-bearing figure kept, and the words taken out of them went into scene.** Chapter 110's long anaphora and Chapter 107's *It is four sets of moorings. It is forty feet…* chain are varied rather than shortened.
- **`---` was being used as an in-scene beat rather than a scene break.** **Chapter 101, which is one afternoon in one shed, now has no rule in it at all**, and in Chapters 102, 103, 104, 105, 106, 107, 108, 109 and 110 every rule that fell inside a continuous afternoon, a continuous shed or a continuous conversation is now a plain paragraph break. A rule survives only where the place or the day changes. **One typographic defect in the whole manuscript is also fixed**: Chapter 110's opening speech carried an outer emphasis marker that was never closed, the only unbalanced line in one hundred and ten chapters.
- **One dominant cadence.** The ledger lists and the *X, and Y, and none of them is going to* construction carried 488 shared eight-grams. **The four longest single-sentence paragraphs the review named — Chapter 107's account of what the notice has cost, Chapter 110's two closing paragraphs — are broken into sentences of ordinary length, and the anaphora in Chapter 107 and Chapter 110 is varied.**
- **Two near-verbatim repeats where the echo was least earned.** The eighteen-word run on Alden Marr at the far bench, shared by Chapters 101 and 106, **is now two different renderings of the same refusal**, because it is the one relationship in the book that should not sound the same twice. The *a boy who has counted eleven … is not going to make it twelve by writing a number* sentence, **which is the boy's own and is quoted by the crew in both chapters, is now compressed on its second use in Chapter 109**, where it is a man reading a line out of a book and not a boy making a claim.
- **Seven small factual slips are fixed on the page.** Chapter 101's *the boy does not trust a reading* was in the wrong tense; **the boy's stated reason for entering the thirteenth week late was circular — a state of the column on the third could not be why it was absent on the second — and it is now the true reason and a bad one, in his own hand: that nobody in the yard had ever said out loud to him that a wage week falls on a Tuesday.** Chapter 102's *the seventh time was at seven in the morning* is now *the seventh of those entries was made this morning, at a quarter to nine, on the crown*. Chapter 109's *this office has entered six times* for the conservative-reading sentence is now **seven**, which is what it is by the twenty-second. Chapter 110's *eleven days instead of seven* is now *six days past the week I gave you*, which is what the twenty-fourth is after the eleventh. **And Chapter 104 now says where the three marks for the fortnight of bread came from, which no chapter said and this file assumed: out of the bag, onto the counter, while the creditor's pen was still wet.**

### What the repairs did not touch

**Nothing in § 1 to § 8 above was contradicted, and nothing in it needed to be.** The ten days of story, the slate-wall wipes, the two returns and the counting rule, the two acknowledgements and the absence of a ninth, the four names and the absence of a fifth, the two-column and third-column discipline, the condition appearing twice as a bare fact and **not being named**, the bellwright not being asked, the lead plug untouched, the coast road unexplained, the glass storm without a cause, the notice unargued and unlifted, the third return of the volume still falling due on Saturday the seventeenth of June, and the standing sentence about four years and eight marks all stand exactly as the batch left them. **No new antagonist, institution or final enemy was introduced, no chapter was cut, no cost was paid twice, and the volume's Movement 1 turn and climax are the same two chapters the outline named.**

---

## Batch 0002 as written (Chapters 111–120), Volume 03 — binding on Chapter 121 onward

**Movement 2, weather and the work that cannot be sold. A SECOND GLASS STORM AND A SECOND UNCHECKABLE LINE IN THE SECOND COLUMN; A CISTERN CHARGED THREE TIMES BY PEOPLE NOBODY PAYS; THREE RECOVERIES NAMED AND THE SMALLEST FUNDED; THE BEST PAIR OF HANDS IN THE HARBOR NOT PUT ON A LEAD; A PILOT OUT OF A STORM BELT ASKS WHO KEEPS A ROAD; FOUR HUNDRED FEET OF A WOMAN'S OWN WARP PRICED IN FRONT OF HER AND PUT ON A MARKET WALL WITH HER NAME ON IT; A BOY OFF THE STONE TAKES A JUNE; A SHARD OF WORKED GLASS OF NOBODY'S IN A CRATE IN THE OPEN AIR WITH A FOUR-DAY CLOCK RUNNING; A HARBOR AUTHORITY GOES OUT ON ITS OWN CLOSURE; AND A THIRTY-MARK OFFER REFUSED IN NINE SENTENCES, WHICH COSTS THE TOWN THE THIRD RECOVERY.** The chapters are the authority on what happened. Everything in this section is taken from Chapters 111–120 and from nothing else, and **the whole batch is 30,345 words** (Ch111 3,464 · Ch112 3,263 · Ch113 2,522 · Ch114 2,739 · Ch115 3,259 · Ch116 3,107 · Ch117 2,534 · Ch118 3,051 · Ch119 2,933 · Ch120 3,473). **Every chapter is above the 2,200 floor; six are inside the 2,200–3,200 guideline and four are past it — 112 by 63, 115 by 59, 111 by 264 and 120 by 273 — and all four ran because a cost could not be dropped.**

### 1. The calendar the batch actually ran on

**`outline/volume-03.md` lock L1's grid, unchanged, and the chapters ran on it without an exception.** 25 May a **Thursday**, 26 a Friday, 27 a **Saturday**, 28 a Sunday, 29 a **Monday**, 30 a **Tuesday**, 31 a **Wednesday**; 1 June a **Thursday**, 2 a Friday, 3 a **Saturday**, 4 a Sunday, 5 a **Monday**, 6 a **Tuesday**, 7 a **Wednesday**, 8 a **Thursday**, 9 a Friday, 10 a **Saturday**, 11 a Sunday, 12 a **Monday**, 13 a **Tuesday**, 14 a **Wednesday**, 15 a **Thursday**. **The Mondays in this batch are the twenty-ninth of May, the fifth of June and the twelfth of June, and the Thursdays in the batch are the first, the eighth and the fifteenth of June, and nothing comes down on a Thursday.** The wage Tuesdays are the thirtieth of May, the sixth of June and the thirteenth of June.

**The ten chapters carry their own dates and no day is in a chapter twice:** Ch111 the 27th and 28th; Ch112 the 29th and 30th; Ch113 the 31st of May and the 1st of June; Ch114 the 2nd and 3rd; Ch115 the 4th and 5th; Ch116 the 6th and 7th; Ch117 the 8th and 9th; Ch118 the 10th and 11th; Ch119 the 12th and 13th; Ch120 the 14th and 15th. **Two dates referred to inside the batch are verified against the grid**: Tuesday the twenty-eighth of February, a Tuesday; **Friday the sixteenth of April, a Friday.**

**The hearing room's slate wall.** It is wiped every Monday at eight whether there is a sitting or not. **It comes down on Monday the twenty-ninth of May, Monday the fifth of June and Monday the twelfth of June, and on all three it comes down bare, nothing is put up in its place, there is no sitting, the clerk keeps his own page, and by the fifth of June he has stopped mentioning it and by the twelfth of June the chapter says he has not started again.** The hearing room itself is **used not at all in this batch**: there is no sitting and no office instrument and no person in a room who belongs to an office. **The market wall is used once, on Wednesday the seventh of June, in daylight, in a market, on no Monday and on no hearing-room day.** No two institutional days in this batch are the same day.

**The age of the notice of the thirty-first of October, in the book's own arithmetic: seven months and about a day on the thirty-first of May, and the chapter on the first of June puts the figure at seven months on the thirty-first. The figure *eight months* is a Chapter 100 error, is not altered, and is not repeated anywhere in Chapters 111–120.**

### 2. The money chain, mark by mark

**EIGHT marks in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, and nothing in a tin, on Thursday the fifteenth of June — against THIRTEEN marks in the same bag on Wednesday the twenty-fourth of May, the figure Batch 0001 closed on. The batch ends five marks poorer than it started and no office has paid this yard anything in three weeks.**

|---|---|---|---|---|

| Tue 30 May | — | **4**, the wage, for the week of Tuesday the thirtieth to Monday the fifth of June, paid in Calder Marr's own left hand, put in an envelope on the bench with the week written on it. **The yard reads the column back in the man who wrote it and enters that a column read back by the man who wrote it is not a column read back.** The cistern is charged a second time at about eight, not being a rota day | **9** | 31 |

| Wed 7 – Thu 8 June | — | nothing moves. **The boy is in a market on the Thursday and takes a season's hand, and nobody in the yard goes, writes, or asks** | **8** | 31 |
