**THE FULL CHAIN IN THE BAG: 13, 13, 9, 9, 12, 12, 8, 8, 5, 12, 12, 8, 8, 8.** The note is **thirty-one throughout and is not re-written, and the three counts of its weeks — the creditor's twenty-two, the yard's thirteen, and eleven as the last figure ever written in that page — do not move and are not moved by anything a yard does with a pencil.** The tin is empty and has been since the twenty-eighth of April.

**The wage column, and it is the spine of the batch: five lines and twenty marks at the end of Batch 0001, and EIGHT lines and THIRTY-TWO marks at the end of this one, the fifth of March the oldest line in it and staying where it is.** The three weeks of the thirtieth of May, the sixth and the thirteenth of June are four marks apiece. **Each is paid in Calder Marr's own left hand because there is nobody else in that shed who writes; each goes into an envelope on the bench with the week written on the outside of it in the same hand; the envelopes are in the bench chest and not among the five rolled in oilcloth; and none of them is sent anywhere that week and none of them is going to be, and the reason is entered, and the reason is that a wage is not a reason to go to a door.**

**Debts, all standing, all unpaid, none reduced.**

- **A compressor's gland** is bought, at the creditor's own figure of five marks, in the creditor's own hand, on a page standing at twenty-three, and the five of it **is still on that page and is not paid and the page has not been re-written.** **It was fitted to the yard's own pump on Monday the nineteenth of May** — a labour, not a payment — **and it has made no difference whatever, because the thing after the gland is the hose and there is not one in this harbor longer than about nine fathoms.** THE POT OF AIR BOUGHT ON THE NINTH OF JUNE IS THE YARD'S THIRD POT AND IT IS THE WHOLE OF THE AIR IN THIS HARBOR ON THE MORNING OF THE TENTH.
- **Eight marks and a day of a boat to the harbor's bellwright, paid on the twenty-ninth of April, and eight marks and a day of a boat owing to the same man since the twenty-third of May** — entered as owing and not as paid, unpaid throughout this batch, and **not argued away and not paid and not asked to be paid.**
- **Forty marks to Bevin Rook** stand whole, unpaid, not reduced, not asked to be reduced, and no mark of it moved. **A wage of four marks a week goes on running and the boy is not on the stone.**
- **Forty marks to Mara Venn**, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, not reachable by the writ, and **not one mark of it paid out of any money that has come into this yard since the eighth of May.** She is not in a room in this batch and is not asked to be useful.
- **Eight shillings and tenpence to Sella Brigg**, undiminished and unpaid, **with her name, a figure and a date on a market wall since Wednesday the seventh of June, and about four hundred feet of her own warp still on the bottom of this harbor, and she has not been told about the thirty marks and is not going to be told this week.**
- **Thirty marks** offered on the fourteenth of June and refused, entered as a refusal and as a cost.
- **Nineteen winters of dues** on Elias Voke, disputed and unpaid, and going up a coast in a file with the harbor authority's own account of what the notice has cost it attached.
- **Twenty marks of unsold iron and four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in three pieces with a bell-metal gun-port among them**, in the same heap behind the shed, in a heap the yard may not sell one mark's worth of without a buyer reading the declaration first. **The gun-port was not looked at in this batch, was not weighed, was not priced and was not offered to anybody, and the man who would know it at a glance was not asked about it and the yard did not have him in a room.**

### 3. Every document and instrument created in this batch

- **The boat's own line about the man of fifty-one, entered Saturday the twenty-seventh of May in the yard's own book, in the first column, with a date on it and read back in a shed by two men.** *That on Monday the twenty-second instant, at the top of this mole's steps, at about ten in the morning, at the production of the writ, in the hearing of three men, a man of the *Grey Widow* of Morrow Quay, of no crew of this party and not of the Free Keel and not named on this page, said that on the twenty-sixth of April he had rung the handbell off this mole head at four o'clock in the afternoon in a half sea, that he had said in this harbor that he would be the man who rang it, and that he would be on this mole on Saturday the twenty-seventh; and that this party entered the whole of that in the hearing of three men and has not asked him one word about the twenty-sixth of April and is not going to.* **THE PLACE OF THAT STATEMENT IS AN AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION. THE CHAPTER 111 CARD SAID HE SAID IT *IN A MARKET ON THE FIFTEENTH*, AND THERE IS NO MARKET ON MONDAY THE FIFTEENTH OF MAY IN CHAPTERS 101–110 AND NO LINE IN THE YARD'S BOOK IN WHICH THAT MAN SAID HE WOULD. IT IS PLACED ON MONDAY THE TWENTY-SECOND, AT THE TOP OF THE MOLE'S STEPS, AT THE PRODUCTION OF THE WRIT, IN THE HEARING OF THREE MEN, AND IT IS ENTERED AS A FACT THE YARD LEARNED ON A DAY THE YARD NAMES.**
- **The reason for hauling the boat up on the crown, in the yard's own book, on the Saturday the twenty-seventh**: a man's own account landward of the third seam's mark where no instrument of any office is required, and that the yard keeps the practice itself and will not have the two confused. **The three money entries of the night: two men hired for the night at no figure, in victuals at a shilling and sixpence out of the same tin the victuals come out of, and neither of the two told, and the reason entered.**
- **The two second-column storm lines, written in two different hands, on two pages that are not on top of each other.** First column: that a man of fifty-one of the *Grey Widow* said a thing twice in a westerly, flat, on the crown above the third seam's mark, at about seven and again at about nine. Second column, in Calder Marr's own hand, at about ten past two on Sunday morning, with **no cause, no figure of any kind in either line, and no statement in either that anything knows anything**, and with the reason against the separation that this yard does not know whether they are two things.
- **The third-column entry of Sunday the twenty-eighth**, read on the steps at about one in the morning and written at about eleven the same morning, the two times entered and the difference of about ten hours entered, and the party entering that it is not going to pretend the two times are the same time and is not renaming the column or scratching it out or stopping it.
- **The wetting entry of Sunday the twenty-eighth**, first column: the object found in water in its bucket to a depth of about a finger, the water having come over the crown in the night, the party has not dried it and has not wiped it and has not put a cloth into it and is not going to, and the reason is the second clause of the notice of the thirty-first.
- **The three money entries of the thirtieth and of the sixth and thirteenth of June**, and **the entry that the column was read back in the yard's own hand by the man who wrote it, and that a column read back by the man who wrote it is not a column read back, and the reason is that the hand that used to do it is off the stone and is not coming back on a Tuesday.**
- **The three-recovery page of Wednesday the thirty-first**, the three costs itemised in a column a man can check, the two refusals and the funding in the yard's own words, and the sentence that the cost of refusing the third is nothing today and everything in a year with both of those in one line.
- **Sella Brigg's own words**, entered in the first column on Thursday the first of June with her name against it, that she refused at twelve and would have refused at four, and that she can see them not using her eight shillings and tenpence and wanted that entered. **She declined to read it back and the yard honoured that on the first occasion it had to.**
- **The practice read out in a shed on Friday the second of June**, the six conditions of a first entry read in order with the shipwright, with the entry that there is no reason in the harbor authority's book because nobody is going to be wet and that the party did the other five for nothing because the shipwright said do it.
- **The two columns of Friday the second of June**: four casts of the hand lead, being eleven and a half three times on the mark and eleven and three-eighths at forty feet south of it; the man of fifty-one's sentence about a lead, in the first column, in his own words; the statement that the holder on a boat at about one o'clock could not say in which of two sounds the answer to that handbell was in the boat, entered as a fact and not as a name for it, with the form of that line being the form it has had since the twenty-sixth of December. **And the ship's sentence with her name against it: a method that is only honest while one particular man is holding it up is not a method.**
- **The entry of the third column on Thursday the eighth of June**, read at about seven and written at about eight, the difference of about an hour entered against the boy's two hours, and **no reason entered for the change because the party does not have one.**
- **The three nots of Thursday the eighth**, in the first column, with one reason against all three in the second column: the yard does not write to Bevin Rook, does not go to see him, and does not ask any person who has seen him what he said to them.
- **The reason of Saturday the tenth of June**, in Calder Marr's own left hand, in the yard's own book, **with the clerk not present and the absence entered rather than filled in**, the ground being a man's own account landward of the third seam's mark where no office instrument is required. It names the diver, Rolf Cardew, twenty-nine, of the second lay, hired for the day at three marks, not one of the four names upon the writ; the tender as Nessa Pike, a shipwright who has never been in the water, the cutoff hers to pull without asking anybody, the word *Enough*; the air as a pot of the harbor authority's own; and the gland on the yard's own pump as fitted since the nineteenth of May, not paid for, and not used, because the thing after the gland is the hose.
- **The first-column finding of Saturday the tenth of June**: the object, about a foot and a half, thick at one end and thin at the other, green through the body and cloudy, with the second seam's own stone dust in one face and a broken face at the thin end; **a scalloped edge of about nine in about three inches, which is not a fracture and is not a mould line, and which this yard has seen once before on the sixth of May on a different object out of different ground and can account for neither time; and a mark on one face which is not a seal and is not a letter and is not a figure, and which no person in that boat identified and the party is not going to identify.** And the line of what it is not, in one line, ending with **and it is not connected to a lead plug in a chest in this shed, or to a mark on a stone, or to a ferry that went down thirteen years ago, or to anything under this town, and nobody is to write any two of those together.**
- **The keeping entry of Saturday the tenth**: the day of a finding is not counted, the fourth day after it is the day it falls due, a return in respect of it falls due on Saturday the seventeenth of June, and the reason the object is on the mole's crown in a crate under a tarpaulin and not in any shed and not in any boat is the second clause of the notice of the thirty-first, and **the party is now the keeper of a second thing it cannot sell, cannot give away, cannot put indoors, and must describe to two offices inside four days, and the reason it took that clause is that a yard with no paper cannot work, and the party has no boy.**
- **The office's own page of Tuesday the thirteenth**, in Ewan Tristram's own hand, at no figure, against the office's own interest: that the cistern was charged on Monday the twelfth by three persons of the yard of Calder Marr, of this harbor and not of this office, on their own account and for nothing, and that the office has not paid any part of it and would have been glad to and could not. Read back once.
- **The mooring's gear line on the office's page at no figure**: a spare fender and about forty feet of the harbor authority's own mooring rope, on the office's own account, on the two tarpaulins on the mole's crown above the third seam's mark.
- **The market-wall sheet of Wednesday the seventh of June**, under the naval paper of the eighteenth of March: the whole of the cost of raising about four hundred feet of a woman's own warp, the eighth, the share, the pot of air, the hired hand, the victuals and the rope, twelve out of twenty-six and the yard's column of one day twelve, the job not being a one-day job and no figure for a second day being given; that the party did not do the work and is not going to this year; **that the reason is money and is not the notice of the thirty-first of October and is not any closure and is not a court and not a clerk;** the hundred and twenty-one days the warp has been at the bottom; **the property of Sella Brigg of the third lay and of nobody else;** that the party is owed that woman eight shillings and tenpence undiminished and unpaid since the sixth of February and did not use that debt against her and entered that she asked them to enter that she could see them not using it; that she was told the whole of the cost in this yard's own words in about thirty people's hearing and refused it, and said she would say no at four marks as well as at twelve, and that no rule of any office was the reason and none was offered to her as the reason; **and that nothing on the sheet is a claim, binds nobody, is a seal or is a survey, and that it is the yard's own gear declaration applied to one refusal.** Read out by Sella Brigg, once, in the market, at about half past eight.
- **The refusal of the fourteenth of June, in Calder Marr's own left hand, on the back of the man's own paper, in nine sentences, and the paper given back.** The entry of what it cost: that the thirty marks would have paid for about four hundred feet of a woman's own warp, that the party entered that it had been offered money for the first time since the first of February, **that it is not able to replace that money, and that a yard that cannot replace an offer is not a yard that gets to turn one down out of a principle, and that this yard has turned one down out of a principle and knows exactly what that is worth.** Read back to the man by the shipwright and read back again by the holder.
- **The page of the two books' disagreement of Monday the twelfth**: the office's page says the date and the hour it was written, the yard's book says ten in the morning, the two are different hours, and the yard has not corrected the office's page and is not going to ask the office to.

**There is no ninth acknowledgement in this batch, no fifth name on the writ, and no return made. The only days on which a person of this yard was in the water in this batch are the second of June, when nobody was, and the tenth of June, when a hired man of the second lay was on a man's own account landward of the third seam's mark with all six conditions read out at the boat.**

### 4. The bodies and the crew

- **Calder Marr**, 32, line diver with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice and not used since the eighth of December, and the lift at the end of a childhood call gone and not restorable. **He goes in no water in this batch, enters nothing, and takes no stage of any kind. THE CONDITION IS NOT NAMED IN THIS BATCH.** **It is on the page as the bare fact, twice, and never named.** In Chapter 111, in a westerly on a mole's crown, he is certain for about a second and a half that a sound came off the water and then certain for about a second and a half that it came out of the man's own mouth, and the two certainties have no way of being in the book at the same time; and the sentence that **there is no instrument in this harbor that will tell you which of the two you have got** is quoted from a man of fifty's own words of the twenty-third of May and is attributed to him. In Chapter 114, on a boat at the second seam, the mole head's handbell goes and he cannot say which of the two sounds he has been given the answer which of the two was in the boat, **and he does not write it down.** **THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 03 IS ALLOWED IS THEREFORE NOT SPENT AND IS OWED TO MOVEMENT 4, AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH MAY BE READ AS HAVING SPENT IT. No chapter of this batch lets it vanish silently.**
- **Nessa Pike**, 27. **She is right in this batch and she is right about something that costs the yard the best pair of hands in the harbor.** In Chapter 114 she makes the rule that the chapter's real argument is made of — a column is not a place for what a yard would have liked to do — and she puts her own name to it and takes the refusal to let Calder write a line saying they wanted him on the lead. In Chapter 112 she re-makes the argument she made in February with the new numbers, because the bellwright's eight is paid and the boy is gone, and says the other half of it. In Chapter 116 she refuses to read back to Sella Brigg a thing another person wrote about her because the woman said so forty-one years ago. In Chapter 117 she says the kindest sentence in the chapter and it is about money: an envelope is not a knock and a wage is not a visit. In Chapter 118 she pulls the cutoff herself and says a tender's stop is not a request on a boat. In Chapter 120 she insists the entry say that the yard knows what a principle is worth. **Nobody thanks her for any of it and she does not want to be thanked.**
- **Bevin Rook**, 22, **off the stone since Monday the fifteenth of May and not replaced.** **He is in a market in Chapter 117 on Thursday the eighth of June for the first time since the fifteenth, listens to an offer for a quarter of an hour, and on Friday the ninth at about one o'clock he shakes hands with a man of the third lay in front of about nine people, and the holder is thirty feet away with a coil of hemp he has already paid for and does not go over.** **THE BATCH DOES NOT RESOLVE IT, DOES NOT PAY IT OFF, DOES NOT REPAIR IT, AND MAY NOT.** The three nots are entered in the first column on the Thursday. **Nobody in that crew writes to him, nobody goes to see him, and nobody asks anybody who has seen him what he said.** **A wage of four marks a week goes on running into an envelope on a bench and the forty marks of claim stand whole and are not reduced and are not asked to be.**
- **Corvin Sarn**, 50, the harbor's own bellwright, **paid**, teaching nobody, not of this party. **He is not asked the one question in this batch — not in a shed, not in a boat, not in a market, not on a mole, not in a room — and his offer stands, and he is owed eight marks and a day of a boat a second time and is not paid and is not argued away.** His sentence of the twenty-third of May is quoted in Chapter 111 with attribution and is not improved on. He is not on the mole in this batch.
- **Ewan Tristram**, the wharfinger of the second lay, **about sixty, who has been in this harbor fifty-one years, takes the dues on three wharves, keeps the keys, and is Neale's brother-in-law.** **He is the volume's municipal poverty said out loud, and he says it in his own words on the boards on Tuesday the thirteenth of June to nine people, and the yard stands at the back of that crowd and does not come forward.** He gives the yard's refusal of the twenty-ninth its reason — I have a wharf, the town has a cistern — and he says that a cistern is not a debt but a hole with a lid on it. He puts the yard's free walk on the office's own page in his own hand at no figure and against the office's own interest, and he lets the mooring's gear out at nothing, and he says the sentence the harbor is built out of: **a town is not a thing that is owed things, it pays in five kinds of coin and has run out of four of them.** He says he would rather have the rota walked than the money and is not sure why and does not resolve it. **He is not thanked and says he has not asked the rota man for anything and that it was arithmetic and not kindness.** His thread closes inside Volume 03 as the volume requires, materially improved and nothing fixed for nothing.
- **Rowan Trewin**, 54, a pilot out of a storm belt, **new in this batch, an ordinary working man, and not an institution, a faction, an antagonist or a rescuer.** He keeps a channel nine miles off the point on the other side of that headland, and has kept it nineteen years, and there are two other men there who would say the same. **He has one of the six hand-copied sheets in his own boat, which came out of this harbor in a boat of the third lay on the sixth of May, and the count of the six does not move because a count of hands is not a count of whereabouts.** He makes the bar two and a half on the flood off a spring at twenty past two and two on the ebb against the sheet's two and three-quarters and two and a quarter, **says in front of nine people that he will not be told which of the two is right, and asks for that to be on the page.** He says what a channel is and what it costs, he says the other thing about a secret being a thing one person loses and **will not name the place and asks not to be asked and not to be asked in a way that lets a man guess, and Calder Marr does not ask him.** **He offers to come back in the autumn, late, in his own boat and on his own money, to look at five miles of that coast and to say out loud on the mole's steps which of the two figures is right; and the yard says yes, and he asks to be able to say in October that nobody asked him a question.** He is the batch's midpoint and he supplies the turn the batch ends on. **He is not thanked and the yard does not expect to be.**
- **Elias Voke**, 51, of the *Anna Ray*, nineteen winters disputed and unpaid. **He asks that the February warp and the Friday warp not be put in the same place, in his own words, in Ch 116, and the yard's answer — that both are in the same place and the answer is true and is about a yard and not about a warp — is entered on the page as his request and not as the yard's.** He offers a season's hand in a market in Ch 117, gives the share figures before he is asked, and says a man who is offered a thing in a shed is a man who is told he is not ready for it. **His own day's work is the funded day of Ch 118, on his boat, on her own account, and the ring and the shackle and the swivel and four feet of warp go onto his own deck in his own hands.**
- **Sella Brigg**, of the third lay. **She is told the whole of the cost of raising about four hundred feet of her own warp in her own hearing in a market on the sixth of June and refuses it in her own voice, and says she would have said no at four marks as well as at twelve, and says she can see the yard not using her eight shillings and tenpence and wants that entered.** **On the seventh of June her name, a figure and a date go on a market wall, and she reads it out herself and does not read it twice and declines to read back a thing another person wrote about her.** **She is owed eight shillings and tenpence and about four hundred feet of her own warp and is not helped out of either for free, and on the fourteenth of June thirty marks that would have paid for the warp are refused and she is not told.**
- **Elias Voke's *Anna Ray*** — see above. **The *Kestrel's Sister*** — Coll Ferrow, thirty-one, with a bad wheel in that boat, out of the harbor since about the seventeenth of May, **says on the mole's steps on the thirty-first of May that the yard has taken the cheapest of the three, and the yard does not deny it.** **The two bad wheels in this harbor are two men in two boats and the two are not merged anywhere in this batch and may never be.**
- **Rolf Cardew**, 29, of the second lay, **new in this batch, a hired diver and not one of the four names on the writ.** His own limit of twenty-two, set by himself at the boat, **was twenty-nine minutes out when the tender called the cutoff and pulled it.** He was not hurt and was not in difficulty, says he did not like it, says he will not say he did not like it, says no man in that crew said one word to him about the twenty-nine minutes, and takes his three marks.
- **Torren Skell**, 59, a coaster with two boats and a following, **new in this batch, an ordinary working man.** He offers thirty marks, a mooring above the north wall, his own ground warps, a capstan bar, a hand lead and forty fathoms of line at no figure, and a line in an engagement book that is not the yard's, in exchange for the right to use the road. **He is not a villain, he is right about the arithmetic, he is wrong about the road, he says the refusal is a thing a man says when he has nothing, he does not threaten anybody, does not come back, does not write to any office, and says he will not put a mark against the yard's name — and the yard enters that no mark is put against his, and reads the whole of it back to him, and he says he would like to be in this harbor twice.** He may reappear in this volume as an ordinary man with a grievance and may not be given an office, a ship, a uniform or a title.
- **The man of fifty-one with a bad wheel** is of the *Grey Widow*, is unnamed on the page, is not put on a lead, is not in the water, is not hired, is not paid, is not offered anything, and **is not asked one word in this batch about what he hears, in a shed, a boat, a market, a room or anywhere.** **He is heard twice, flat, in a westerly on a mole's crown, saying two words about his wheel, and those are the only two things he said in the batch, and the yard enters that he said a thing twice and enters no cause for it.** He says one thing unasked, in about fifteen words, about what a lead is for.
- **The man with a slate** is in a market in Ch 116, reads the sheet off the wall, and does not offer to buy a copy and is not asked to and does not ask to be told anything.
- **Ewan Tristram's rota man** is on the harbor authority's own tide gang, is one of the four it is short, **has not been paid for five pay periods and was last paid at the end of January**, and **was not paid on the twelfth of June and was paid on the twelfth of June for the first time in five pay periods by a party that is not the harbor authority and by nothing at all.**
- **The harbor authority's clerk** comes up the hill once, on Monday the twenty-ninth, with no errand, and says the wall is a convenience and that he has stopped mentioning it.
- **Alden Marr** is at the far bench in Chapter 115 and is not asked for anything, and neither brother went over to him, **and he is not in any other chapter of this batch. No fragment, no report talk, no second story, no confession.**
- **Mara Venn** is in no room in this batch. Her forty marks stand and are not paid and the writ cannot reach her, and she is not asked to be useful and is not an assistant.

### 5. The dated positions at the end of Chapter 120

- **The writ.** A provisional writ granted Monday the first of May, running to the first of March next, **not the Second Bell license, not a charter, renewing nothing, ground not widened.** The four names are unchanged and no fifth has been added anywhere. It was not called for again in this batch and no chapter of it touches the fourth name. The second season is a named absence.
- **The reporting clause.** **Two returns have been made under it and no third, and a third falls due on SATURDAY THE SEVENTEENTH OF JUNE** out of a shard of worked glass raised on **Saturday the tenth of June** on ground landward of the third seam's mark, on a herring boat's own account, in a boat on a mole because the second clause keeps it out of every shed in this harbor. **The return is NOT MADE IN THIS BATCH and no card or chapter of Movement 3 may move the finding or bring the return inside.** The counting rule was spoken aloud once in this batch, in Chapter 118, in Calder Marr's own mouth and in the entry.
- **The notice of the thirty-first of October.** Four clauses, not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered, and the harbor authority has no power to lift it. **Clause two is on the page four times as a live reason and is argued by nobody: the lump is not dried in a shed, the shard is not put in a shed or a boat in the harbor, and both are on the mole's crown above the third seam's mark in the open air.** **There are eight acknowledgements in the harbor authority's ledger at the end of this batch, both of this batch's predecessors are spent, and MOVEMENT 2 ENTERED NONE, and no person, boat or lead of this yard went inside the first clause on any day of this batch.**
- **The eighth-clause plan for the fifth seam** is refused and is in a record with a date on it and is not reopened in this batch. **The eighth of the reeve's acknowledgements was WORKED on Monday the twelfth of June, at the top of the flood, on the mole's toe and inner face between the third and the ninth seams, by the officer of the harbor authority with its clerk and two men of its tide gang, to enter in the office's own book what the notice has cost it, and no person of this yard was on that ground, was not asked to be, and would have been refused.** The plate is still in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched.
- **The bell.** Eight and a half feet off the south end, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was**, a new rope bought on the eleventh of January. **The going out of the second of May is done and is spent and is not repeated and no second eight marks is paid anywhere in this batch and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed.** Eight marks and a day of a boat has been owing to that man since the twenty-third of May, unpaid and unargued. **The bell reads nothing, settles nothing and says no word, and it is not in a boat in this batch.**
- **The lead plug.** Untouched. In a chest in a shed, four descriptions that do not agree, a number in no book, **and the one question is STILL UNASKED in this batch, in a shed, a boat, a market, a room or anywhere, and the yard has not opened the chest.**
- **The glass storm.** **Used a second time, on Saturday the twenty-seventh and Sunday the twenty-eighth of May, in a westerly of about eleven hours, which was not the weather of the twenty-sixth of April.** The yard's second column now carries **two** lines about it, in **two different hands, on two pages that are not on top of each other**, and **no cause is named for it in either and none may be in any volume, and nothing in either line knows anything.** The delay on the call in the water is still not given.
- **The coast road.** Published in six boats in six hands, and **one of the six is in a boat out of a storm belt with a man's name on it and the count of the six does not move because a count of hands is not a count of whereabouts.** A man out of a storm belt has put a figure of his own beside the yard's and will not be told which is right, and has said that a road kept by three and a half people is a secret and a secret is a thing one person loses. **The road is not resolved, not explained, not named a network, and not connected to anything under this town, and the word *network* is not in the yard's hand anywhere in this batch.** The nine final routes are not named in the prose. Orison is a place seventy fathoms down.
- **Custody.** Three items, three numbers, digits not quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request. **No paper states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material and no clerk answered it in this batch.**
- **The concealment.** Hidden. The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. **Nobody in this batch asked the boy and nobody asked the conservator, and nobody in that crew asked the boy one word about either of his two omissions.**
- **The delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, two of them with no call in them, and **neither figure is advanced in this batch by arithmetic or by a measurement.** A man rang a handbell and a bell went off the mole head and a boat's crew heard an answer come back and **the hand's width of a second did not move and was not taken and was not written down.** No occasion entered. The answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.
- **Labor.** No air bought except a pot on the ninth of June; no delivery hose; a gland fitted and not paid for; a hand lead's line that is right and was used; the ninth seam out with no end of it entered; the north berth out; the third lay full; the tide gang four men short; **a cistern charged three times in a month by people nobody pays, on the twenty-ninth of May, the fourth of June and the twelfth of June, and not on the nineteenth of June because there is nothing in the bag to walk it with;** the fire party's hose dead; the light not on; **the repair account empty since the middle of February and not going to be filled**; no boat of this office's own; the *Kittiwake* on the hard; the hard at Ashtide; nineteen winters; the *Sea Wren* at the pile; eleven copies and four unreached holders not asked; the heap behind the shed that may not be sold; and **Neave Tarrant, nine winters, out since the twenty-eighth of February, not found, one line in the book, and the yard has not gone and has asked nobody anything.**

### 6. What this batch may not be used for

1. **It may not be used to make the third return.** A third return **falls due on Saturday the seventeenth of June** and is **Movement 3's** and is not made here. The finding is **Saturday the tenth of June** and may not be moved.
2. **It may not be used to spend the naming of the condition.** It is not named in this batch, twice, in the bare form Volume 02 left it in, with a reason against it and nobody asking. **The one naming is therefore still available and is owed to Movement 4, and no Movement 3 chapter may take it and no chapter of this batch may be read as having taken it.**
3. **It may not be used to ask the bellwright his one question, to open the lead plug's chest, to print any digit of anything in it, to revive *a tongue half worn through*, to make the bell an instrument, to buy a second going out, to pay a second eight marks, to enter a second going out as done, or to put a boat off the south end inside four years.** **The bellwright is NOT ASKED IN THIS BATCH.**
4. **It may not be used to enter a ninth acknowledgement, to lift, narrow, re-serve or alter the notice of the thirty-first, to argue clause two in a shed, to reach the plate at the fifth seam, to put a person, boat or lead of this yard inside the first clause, to widen the writ's ground, to put a fifth name on the writ, or to manufacture a second season.**
5. **It may not be used to name a cause for the glass storm, to let it speak, to name the Nine Harbors, the Quietening or the Crown Seal, to explain the nine-line mark or to identify the marking on the shard of the tenth of June, to name the compartment or to state that the sealed case was in standing space number four, to quote a custody number's digits, to put a senior examiner in a room, to name an office of returning memory, to have Captain Holt appear in a room or at a mole, to ask Sera Mott, or to introduce a new antagonist, institution or final enemy.**
6. **It may not be used to resolve the coast road, to name it a network, to connect it to anything under this town, to name the nine final routes in the prose, to produce a paper out of a city, or to say what is under this town.** The question written back out of a city about a bar of gravel is reserved to Chapters 121–130. **The circuit office's fourth request of the sixteenth of March is a different paper and may be referred to at any time.**
7. **It may not be used to resolve, repair or pay off Bevin Rook's June, to have the yard write to him, to go and see him, to ask anybody who has seen him what he said, to reduce the forty marks, to stop the wage, or to treat the wage as a claim on his June.**
8. **It may not be used to pay Mara Venn's forty marks, to pay Sella Brigg's eight shillings and tenpence, to raise or sell the warp, to weigh, price or offer the bell-metal gun-port, to sell one mark's worth of the heap behind the shed, to re-write the chandler's note, to move any of its three counts of weeks, or to move the count of eleven copies.**
9. **It may not be used to bring back a custody object, to tell the concealment a second time, to make Torren Skell a villain or to give him an office, a ship, a uniform or a title, to make Ewan Tristram's municipal poverty fixed for anything, or to make the cistern thread anything but what it is.**
10. **It may not be used to stage a dive of Calder Marr's own, to take any power stage, or to put a diver in the water who is not a hired man with his own limit fixed at the boat, under a named tender who is not a diver, with a cutoff the tender's to pull, with the six conditions read out in order and the stated reason entered before anybody is wet.**

### 7. The costs this batch closes with

1. **A night of weather, two men hired at no figure, a boat hauled up on a capstan at midnight, and a lump of green glass that is wetter than it was and is not dried.**
2. **The best pair of hands in this harbor not put on a lead, and a day's reading with a figure forty feet south of the mark that nobody can say what it would have read at ten o'clock.**
3. **Three recoveries named, one funded and worked, and two refused — one of them refused by the woman it belongs to, and one of them refused because there is no ninth acknowledgement and this yard is not going to ask for one for nine feet of chain, which costs nothing today and everything in a year.**
4. **Four hundred feet of a woman's own warp at the bottom of this harbor, priced to her in a market, refused, and her name and a figure and a date put on a market wall, which is a creditor of the yard's work and worse than a creditor of its money, and she is owed eight shillings and tenpence undiminished and has not asked for either.**
5. **A wage of four marks a week running for a man who is not on the stone, into an envelope on a bench, three envelopes, and the reason they are not carried is on the page.**
6. **A cistern charged three times in a month by people nobody pays, and a wharfinger who would rather have the rota walked than the money and does not resolve it, and a man who has not been paid for five pay periods and was paid on the twelfth of June by nothing at all.**
7. **A boy of twenty-two who took a season's hand in a market, and three nots entered in the first column with one reason against all three, and nobody who went after him.**
8. **A day of work lost and a hired man in the water at twenty-nine minutes of a limit of twenty-two, cut off by a tender who is not a diver, who is thanked by nobody and complains about nothing.**
9. **A shard of worked glass of nobody's in a crate under a tarpaulin on a mole's crown in the open air, worth nothing to anybody the party has been able to find out about, with a marking on it that nobody identified, and a four-day clock running that is due on Saturday the seventeenth of June with no boy to read the return back.**
10. **Thirty marks, a mooring, a man's gear and a line in a book that is not the yard's, refused in nine sentences on a principle the yard cannot fund and cannot prove, and the refusal cost a woman her warp and the yard five marks of poverty it did not choose and cannot replace.**

### 8. Proposed canon changes

**None applied. Three flagged rather than applied, and three author-level decisions taken on the page.**

1. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the wage continues to a boy who is off the stone, and the four marks a week go into an envelope on a bench.** Chapters 101–110 record the fifteenth week paid in advance on the Monday the fifteenth and the sixteenth and the twenty-third of May going by with no line in the column. **Chapters 112, 116, 119 and 120 pay three further weeks on the Tuesdays, in Calder Marr's own left hand, and put each in an envelope with the week written on the outside of it, in the bench chest and not among the five rolled in oilcloth, and enter that no envelope is going anywhere that week and that a wage is not a reason to go to a door.** **This is an addition to the yard's own practice and it is consistent with the advance payment of the fifteenth and with the reason Batch 0001 gave for that advance payment, and it is on the page in three chapters. It is not a canon change to the writ, the clause, the notice or the boy's forty marks, and the forty marks are not reduced and the wage is not offered as a reduction. A Volume 03 audit may rule on it.**
2. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the yard's own boat, hauled up on the mole's crown above the third seam's mark, and a crate and a bucket living on that crown for five weeks.** Chapter 101 established that a bucket stood in a boat in a shed; **Chapters 111, 118 and 119 establish that the yard's own boat is hauled up above the third seam's mark and that two things of nobody's are living on that crown in the open air under two tarpaulins.** **This is an application of the second clause, not an amendment of it, and the crown is landward of the third seam's mark and above the ground the first clause closes, and the yard's own reason is on the page. It is not a ninth acknowledgement and it does not need one.**
3. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — a mooring gear line on the harbor authority's own page at no figure, and a cistern walk on that page at no figure, in a wharfinger's own hand.** **The office has now recorded, in its own hand and at no figure and against its own interest, two things it did not pay for, and one man has been unpaid for five pay periods. It is a fact about an office's bookkeeping and it does not fix the harbor's poverty for anything, and the light is still not on and the repair account is still empty and the ninth seam is still out of service with no end of it entered.**
4. **DECISION TAKEN ON THE PAGE — where the man of fifty-one said he would be on the mole.** See § 3. **The Chapter 111 card's *in a market on the fifteenth* is not on any page and is not written as though a page said it.**
5. **DECISION TAKEN ON THE PAGE — the "February warp" and the "Friday warp" of Elias Voke's request are dated and grounded on the page: Tuesday the twenty-eighth of February off the mole's own face between the first and second seams on a third lay boat's own account, and four feet of warp on a ring at the second seam on Friday the sixteenth of April belonging to a different boat of a different lay.** **The first is the herring skipper's ring and warp of the twenty-eighth of February already in the money chain; the second is new and is dated to a Friday in April inside the grid. Neither is added to the other and neither is added to Sella Brigg's warp.**
6. **DECISION TAKEN ON THE PAGE — the two figures for the bar off a storm-belt pilot: two and a half on the flood off a spring at twenty past two and two on the ebb, against the sheet's two and three-quarters and two and a quarter, and the sheet's own four figures are unchanged and unresolved.** **No later chapter may resolve which is right except by the man coming back in the autumn, and the man has said he will say it out loud on the mole's steps when he does.**
7. **DECISION TAKEN ON THE PAGE — a compressor's gland was fitted to the yard's own pump on Monday the nineteenth of May and is not paid for, and it made no difference because there is no delivery hose in this harbor.** **The five marks of it are still on the chandler's page at thirty-one and the page is not re-written, and no later chapter may treat the yard as having air because a gland is on the pump.**

## Batch 0003 as written (Chapters 121–130), Volume 03 — binding on Chapter 131 onward

**Movement 3, the chart and the two men who want it. THE THIRD RETURN MADE ON THE DAY; A CHART BOUGHT OUT OF A REEVE'S HAND FOR NINETEEN WINTERS OF SOMEBODY ELSE'S DUES; A RIVAL CREW'S PRINTED SHEET THAT ANSWERS STRANGERS AND IS BETTER THAN THE YARD'S BECAUSE IT CAN BE BOUGHT; THE YARD'S OWN SLIP OF ELEVEN NAMES THE REASON A STRANGER COULD PRINT IT; A COAST OFFICE THAT ANSWERS AT LAST AND USES THIS HARBOR'S OWN POVERTY; A QUESTION WRITTEN BACK OUT OF A CITY ABOUT A BAR OF GRAVEL THAT THE YARD CANNOT ANSWER AND WILL NOT MAKE ITSELF ABLE TO; THE BELLWRIGHT'S ONE QUESTION ASKED IN A ROOM AND ANSWERED ONCE AND NOT WRITTEN DOWN; A YARD THAT DECLINES TO PUBLISH; AND FOUR MARKS IN A BAG.** The chapters are the authority on what happened. Everything in this section is taken from Chapters 121–130 and from nothing else, and **the whole batch is 33,612 words** (Ch121 3,945 · Ch122 3,178 · Ch123 3,866 · Ch124 3,448 · Ch125 3,199 · Ch126 2,970 · Ch127 3,299 · Ch128 3,135 · Ch129 3,081 · Ch130 3,491). **Every chapter is above the 2,200 floor; four are inside the 2,200–3,200 guideline and six are past it — 124 by 248, 130 by 290, 123 by 666 and 121 by 745 — and all four ran because a cost could not be dropped.**

### 1. The calendar the batch actually ran on

**`outline/volume-03.md` lock L1's grid, unchanged, and the chapters ran on it without an exception.** 17 June a **Saturday**, 18 a Sunday, 19 a **Monday**, 20 a **Tuesday** and a wage week, 21 a **Wednesday**, 22 a **Thursday**, 23 a **Friday**, 24 a **Saturday**, 25 a Sunday, 26 a **Monday**, 27 a **Tuesday** and a wage week, 28 a **Wednesday**, 29 a **Thursday**, 30 a **Friday**; 1 July a **Saturday**, 2 a Sunday, 3 a **Monday**, 4 a **Tuesday** and a wage week, 5 a **Wednesday**, 6 a **Thursday**. **The Mondays in this batch are the nineteenth of June, the twenty-sixth of June and the third of July. The Thursdays are the twenty-second, the twenty-ninth of June and the sixth of July. The wage Tuesdays are the twentieth and twenty-seventh of June and the fourth of July. NO WEEKDAY WAS CORRECTED AND NO CHAPTER STATES A YEAR.**

**The ten chapters carry their own dates and no day is in a chapter twice, and no man is paid for two days in one chapter:** Ch121 the 17th and 18th; Ch122 the 19th and 20th; Ch123 the 21st and 22nd; Ch124 the 23rd and 24th; Ch125 the 25th and 26th; Ch126 the 27th and 28th; Ch127 the 29th and 30th; Ch128 the 1st and 2nd of July; Ch129 the 3rd and 4th; Ch130 the 5th and 6th. **Three dates referred to inside the batch are verified against the grid**: Tuesday the seventeenth of June, the day a return falls due; **Monday the third of July**, the last day of the week of Tuesday the twenty-seventh of June, entered as such; and **the wage week of Tuesday the fourth of July to Monday the tenth inclusive.**

**The hearing room's slate wall.** It is wiped every Monday at eight whether there is a sitting or not. **It comes down on Monday the nineteenth of June, Monday the twenty-sixth of June and Monday the third of July, and on all three it comes down bare, nothing is put up in its place, there is no sitting, and the clerk keeps his own page and does not mention it.**

**The hearing room is used ONCE in this batch, on WEDNESDAY THE TWENTY-ONE OF JUNE, in a room over the tide-gang store, with the clerk present, six people in the room, and no sitting called.** The market wall is used **once, on SATURDAY THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF JUNE, in daylight, in a market**, on no Monday and on no hearing-room day, **and the sheet that goes up on it is a rival crew's, not the yard's.** No two institutional days in this batch are the same day.

**The harbor authority's cistern rota falls on a Monday and a Thursday.** The rota days inside the window are **Monday the nineteenth, Thursday the twenty-second, Monday the twenty-sixth, Thursday the twenty-ninth, Monday the third of July and Thursday the sixth of July. THE YARD CHARGED IT ON THE NINETEENTH, THE TWENTY-SIXTH, THE TWENTY-NINTH, THE THIRD AND THE SIXTH, AND DELIBERATELY DID NOT WALK THE TWENTY-SECOND.** **The only reason the batch gives for skipping a rota day is the one on the page on Saturday the seventeenth of June, and it is not the two-walks-in-a-week rule: it is that the gate takes four hours with three men and one of the three is a man who has not been paid for five pay periods. The yard has since walked both a Monday and a Thursday in the same week twice, on the twenty-sixth and twenty-ninth of June and on the third and sixth of July, at four hours a walk, and has said nothing about a rule against it. THERE IS NO SUCH RULE AND NO CHAPTER MAY STATE ONE.** That is **five walks in this batch and eight on the office's own page at no figure since the end of May**, and the cistern has not gone dry since the nineteenth of June.

### 2. The money chain, mark by mark

**FOUR marks in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, and nothing in a tin, on Thursday the sixth of July — against EIGHT marks in the same bag on Thursday the fifteenth of June, the figure Batch 0002 closed on. No office in this harbor has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May and none is going to, and the yard says so out loud on the page on the fourth of July.**

| Date | In | Out | Bag | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thu 15 Jun | — | — | **8** | 31 |
| Sat 17 – Sun 18 Jun | — | nothing moves. **The third return is made on the day and goes up the coast in a sealed jacket at about eight on the Saturday morning with a copy for the harbor authority's ledger. The tarpaulins go back on the crown at no figure. The condition is on the page for the third time as the bare fact. The third column is filled with an eight-hour lateness. Alden Marr is at the far bench on both days and is asked nothing and asks nothing** | **8** | 31 |
| Fri 19 Jun | — | **2**, a pot of air bought of this harbor authority at this yard's own two-mark rate, filled at the office's compressor and carried on the office's own hose, and it is the whole of the air in this harbor. **The cistern is charged on the rota, the fourth time in three weeks, at no figure** | **6** | 31 |
| Sat 20 – Sun 21 Jun | — | **4**, the wage, for the week of Tuesday the twentieth to Monday the twenty-sixth, in Calder Marr's own left hand, into an envelope on the bench with the week written on the outside of it, in the bench chest and not among the five rolled in oilcloth, not going anywhere that week | **2** | 31 |
| Mon 22 – Tue 23 Jun | — | **NOTHING MOVES IN OR OUT. THE REEVE SELLS THE CHART.** In the hearing room on Wednesday the twenty-first the writ, the book and all three returns are produced whole and unshortened; the price is named in front of the room and paid in Elias Voke's nineteen winters; the chart goes into the officer's hand at ten on the Thursday; a letter of four lines goes up the coast in a herring skipper's coat | **2** | 31 |
| Fri 24 – Sat 25 Jun | **7**, a day at the second seam on the *Anna Beth's* own account at this yard's own rate: gross sixteen, the eighth two and the share two off the top, the victuals two and a hired hand three paid at the mole by the woman who hired the day, that being nine out of sixteen and the yard's being seven; **and the seven is three more than the four this yard costed the same day of work to on the mole's steps on the thirty-first of May, when the pot of air and about a hundred fathoms of rope were both charged to the job — the pot having gone out of the bag on the Friday and the ring, the shackle, the swivel and the four feet of warp all having come on the ring, so that nothing else came off the day. THE FIGURE THE YARD WROTE UP ON THE STEPS ON THE THIRTY-FIRST OF MAY WAS FOUR AND IT STANDS** **A rival crew's printed sheet goes up on the market wall and a shilling changes hands at the foot of the dock, and the shilling is not the yard's income and is not in the bag and will not be** | | **9** | 31 |

The yard's own boat comes off the haul-off and goes back into the water at the north end, at no figure, a day gone and no money either way** | **9** | 31 |

A shipwright buys a rival crew's printed sheet for four marks OUT OF HER OWN HAND — not the yard's money, not in any column, not to be paid back, not to be thanked** | **5** | 31 |
