**THE FULL CHAIN IN THE BAG: 8, 6, 2, 9, 5, 8, 4.** The note is **thirty-one throughout and is not re-written, and the three counts of its weeks — the creditor's twenty-two, the yard's thirteen, and eleven as the last figure ever written in that page — do not move and are not moved by anything a yard does with a pencil.** The tin is empty and has been since the twenty-eighth of April.

**THE WHOLE OF THE MONEY THAT CAME INTO THIS YARD IN TWENTY-ONE DAYS IS TEN MARKS, OF WHICH TWELVE WENT OUT AS THREE WEEKS OF A WAGE AND TWO WENT OUT FOR A POT OF AIR, AND THE FOUR ARE IN THE BAG, AND THAT FIGURE IS ON THE PAGE IN THE YARD'S OWN HAND.**

**The wage column, and it is the spine of the batch: eight lines and thirty-two marks at the end of Batch 0002, and ELEVEN LINES AND FORTY-FOUR MARKS at the end of this one, the fifth of March the oldest line in it and staying where it is.** The three weeks of the twentieth and twenty-seventh of June and the fourth of July are four marks apiece. **Each is paid in Calder Marr's own left hand because there is nobody else in that shed who writes; each goes into an envelope on the bench with the week written on the outside of it in the same hand; the envelopes are in the bench chest and not among the five rolled in oilcloth; six envelopes now; and none of them is sent anywhere that week and none of them is going to be, and the reason is entered, and the reason is that a wage is not a reason to go to a door.** None of the six has been anywhere in six weeks.

**Debts, all standing, all unpaid, none reduced, and none paid in this batch.**

- **A compressor's gland**, at the creditor's own figure of five marks, fitted to the yard's own pump since Monday the nineteenth of May, **is still on the page at thirty-one and is not paid and the page has not been re-written, and the yard says out loud in Chapter 130 that the gland has made no difference whatever because the thing after the gland is the hose, and there is not one in this harbor longer than about nine fathoms.** A pot of air was bought on the nineteenth of June and it is the whole of the air in this harbor.
- **Eight marks and a day of a boat to the harbor's bellwright, paid on the twenty-ninth of April, and eight marks and a day of a boat owing to the same man since the twenty-third of May** — entered as owing and not as paid, unpaid throughout, not argued away, and **NOT PAID IN THIS BATCH**, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed.
- **Forty marks to Bevin Rook** stand whole, unpaid, not reduced, not asked to be reduced, and no mark of it moved. A wage of four a week goes on running into an envelope and the boy is not on the stone.
- **Forty marks to Mara Venn**, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, not reachable by the writ, and **not one mark of it paid out of any money that has come into this yard since the eighth of May.** She is in no room in this batch and is not asked to be useful and is not an assistant.
- **Eight shillings and tenpence to each of two women — Sella Brigg and Edric Pringle — undiminished and unpaid and not paid in this batch.** About four hundred feet of Sella Brigg's own warp is on the bottom of this harbor, a hundred and thirty-nine days at the twenty-fifth of June and a hundred and forty-eight days at the fourth of July. **Edric Pringle is a creditor of this yard and a hirer of this yard's day at the second seam and is not given a warp figure anywhere in this batch, and the two debts are kept apart and the two women are not merged in any sentence.**
- **Thirty marks** offered on Wednesday the fourteenth of June and refused, and **on Sunday the twenty-fifth of June the woman it was refused for said in a market that she had worked out about the money herself, in eleven days, from a coat, and the yard neither confirmed nor denied it and entered that she worked it out and is not going to pretend she did not.**
- **Nineteen winters of dues** on Elias Voke, disputed and unpaid, **and now standing in a naval file since Thursday the twenty-second of June, and in four lines of a letter in that man's own coat, and in a list being made up in a city in the first week of July on the same page as three lines out of this harbor's own account of what it cannot moor.**
- **Twenty marks of unsold iron and four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in three pieces with a bell-metal gun-port among them**, in the same heap behind the shed, unsold, unweighed, unpriced, not offered to anybody, **not looked at in this batch**, and the man who would know it at a glance is of another boat and is not asked and is not in a room.

### 3. Every document and instrument created in this batch

- **THE THIRD RETURN, made Saturday the seventeenth of June, on the day, out of a finding of Saturday the tenth.** One sheet in the harbor authority's own paper in Calder Marr's own left hand, unshortened, with place, hour, keeper and limits against the head of it, and a copy for the harbor authority's ledger in the clerk's own hand, and the clerk's own page with the hour it was written and not the hour it was done, because that is a clerk's practice and has been one since April. **The reason for the day went in at ten past six in the yard's own book with the clerk not present and the absence entered rather than filled in.** Contents: the object, about a foot and a half, green through the body and cloudy, two and a half inches at the thick end and a quarter of an inch at the thin, with the second seam's own stone dust ground into one face and a broken face at the thin end that is dull and gritty and is not a face anybody worked; **a scalloped edge of about nine in about three inches, not a fracture and not a mould line, all the same hand, seen once before on the sixth of May on a different object out of different ground and accounted for neither time; and a mark on one face which is not a seal and is not a letter and is not a figure, which no person in that boat identified and which the party is not going to identify, and if it means a thing to somebody then that somebody was not in that boat.** Second column: the mass, with the reason; **the age, which goes nowhere at all, on a line of its own, with the reason that the party declines to give a figure for it and declines to give a reason for declining, and the circuit office may write for a reason and the party will answer on the day it asks**; and the three sound entries. And the line of what it is not, in full, **including the eight disavowals and the instruction that nobody is to write any two of them together.** And the reason it is on the crown in a crate and not in a shed and not in a boat, being the second clause of the notice of the thirty-first. **Nessa Pike read the whole of it back in the shed at about eight and Calder Marr read it back after her and there was nobody else in that shed, and the yard entered that a column read back by the man who wrote it is not a column read back.**
- **The finding of the tenth of June is NOT MOVED, the counting rule is NOT MOVED, and there is no fourth return and no chapter of this batch produced one out of a day that produced nothing.** A dated line in the yard's own book says that on Tuesday the thirteenth of May no finding was made, nothing was raised and no return went up, and that line is still not in any office's book and is still not sent to anybody, and it was produced in the hearing room on the twenty-first.
- **The second request for the harbor authority's chart, and the rider.** One sheet, signed at the foot by **Captain Veyra Holt**, described from a distance and not present: the vessel asks for a copy, extract or tracing of the chart of this harbor, of the mole, of the harbor line, of the outer marks and of the bar lying four hundred yards off those marks, shown as this office holds them; **and states that the vessel will not contest a claim window of this office, upon any ground whatever within the harbor line, for the remainder of the term of the writ of the first of May, that is, until the first of March next; and asks for the whole of it by the first of July.** And a rider in the officer's own hand asking for the place of every copy of the public sheet of the fourteenth of October. **THE RIDER WAS ANSWERED ON THE MOLE ON THE NINETEENTH: THE ELEVEN NAMES WERE READ OUT IN FULL, THE FOUR NOT REACHED NAMED AS NOT REACHED AND NOT ASKED, AND THE YARD SAID OUT LOUD THAT IT IS NOT GOING TO ASK THEM, AND ENTERED THAT IT DID NOT BELIEVE THE OFFICER'S PROMISE NOT TO PASS THE LIST ON.**
- **The ledger entry of Wednesday the twenty-first of June, read back by the clerk and read again by the reeve after his own clerk.** That the chart of this harbor, being the basin, the inner ground, the mole from the first seam to the twelfth, the harbor line, the outer marks, the bar of four hundred yards off those outer marks, **and the notation in this officer's own hand of the twenty-fifth of December last upon the ground gear and mooring of the bell at the south end**, is to be delivered into the keeping of that vessel on Thursday the twenty-second; that in exchange the vessel has withdrawn its objection to the exercise by this office of claim windows within the harbor line for the remainder of the term of the writ of the first of May; **that the price is nineteen winters of disputed and unpaid moorage dues upon the *Anna Ray* of Elias Voke, which are not this office's money and are not the yard's money and are one man's, and that the owner of that boat was not in this room and was one day out; that there is no claim window in this transaction and there is no acknowledgement in it, and that both of the two acknowledgements of this year are spent and that no instrument of this office has put a person, a boat or a lead inside the closure of the notice of the thirty-first of October since the eighth of June and is not going to; and that the cost of all of it is this officer's and is not the yard's and is not nobody's.**
- **The letter of four lines in Ors Kest's own hand to Elias Voke, carried up the coast on Friday the twenty-third by a boy of the third lay who was going anyway and asked for nothing and was paid nothing and was not told what he was carrying.** The reeve read it back to himself once, which is not a read-back, and entered that he had done it and why: **a reeve who has just sold a man's nineteen winters owes that man the price in that man's own hand and not in an office's.**
- **The two first-column lines of Calder Marr in the hearing room**, one that the party was in the room at the officer's request, produced the writ, the book and three returns whole and unshortened, was asked for nothing, offered nothing, entered nothing, and did not know what was on the second sheet of the paper; and one, in the second column, **by Nessa Pike, that eight times this reeve has spent himself and every one of the eight times the price landed on somebody who was not in the room, and that this is the first time he has spent himself on something that was his to sell, and the price still landed on somebody who was not in the room.** The clerk heard the second at the door, wrote it on his own page, and gave it to the holder.
- **The sheet of Sunday the twenty-fifth of June in the first column**: that Sella Brigg said in a market in about forty people's hearing that she was going to ask a man of another port about about four hundred feet of her own warp; that the party said she had a right to and would not stand in the way; that **this party has no standing with that woman and has not asked for any and is not going to**; and that the party said nothing to her about the thirty marks and did not deny them either, and the reason the party said nothing is that a yard which has been refused by a woman it owes money to does not get to also be the thing that makes her hear about an offer in a street, and **the cost of that silence is that she worked it out herself in eleven days, and this party is entered that she worked it out herself and is not going to pretend she did not.**
- **The refusal to sell a copy, spoken in a shed on Friday the twenty-third**, and **the refusal to ask for the slip to be taken down**, and the reason: a slip that is not on a wall is a list a man in this harbor can read out of a window, and the only thing worse than a published list is a secret one. **And the entry that the yard did not ask which four of the eleven a man of Ashtide had found, and that he did not say, with the reason the yard has given since the third of May.**
- **The printed sheet of Perry Ollett of the *Sallow Reach*, which went up on the market wall on Saturday the twenty-fourth of June, in daylight, in a market, over the yard's own and under the naval paper of the eighteenth of March; a shilling at the foot of the dock; and a copy of it bought for four marks out of a shipwright's own hand on Friday the thirtieth and put on the yard's bench on purpose.** Its content is the yard's own public figures with the ground, the hour, the number of times and the hand against each, and **the eleven places the copies of the public sheet of the fourteenth of October are held, printed from the yard's own slip.**
- **The two first-column sound entries of the batch**, both about a person of this party not being able to say in which of two directions a sound had come, both with a reason against the line, neither with a cause: **Sunday the eighteenth of June, in the holder's own hand; and Monday the third of July, in the shipwright's own hand, after the holder said it out loud to her for the first time in five months, and with no name against the line by her own decision and his.** The shipwright's stated reason for putting no name is entered and is that the day a man of this yard writes that he could not tell which of two sounds it was, with his name on it, in about two years there is a page in that book that says what a man of this yard can and cannot hear, and that page will be read by somebody looking for exactly that. **The count of four is to go into the first column in her hand on Saturday the eleventh of July, and has not gone in yet.**
- **The third column has FIVE ENTRIES IN ALL AND 3 OF THEM ARE IN THIS BATCH** — the twenty-eighth of May, the eighth of June, the eighteenth of June, the twenty-sixth of June and the fourth of July, **each with two times at the head and a lateness against it: about ten hours, about an hour, about eight hours, about two hours and about a day and a half. The head of the column was written on the twenty-first of May and the boy went off the stone on the fifteenth, so there is no entry before him and there is none to be dated the twenty-second of May. On the twenty-first of June, when the writ was produced, the clerk could see three entries under the head and that is what Chapter 123 says.** The last is the longest since the column was created and the party has entered that it is the longest and is not going to make a practice of it. **A line at the head of the third column, in Calder Marr's own left hand, with nothing under it, saying that the plug of Sunday the second of July is not where this goes and is not going to go in a second column either.**
- **The first-column entry of Sunday the second of July**: that a man of fifty was asked in a room what is the number in a piece of lead, and that he said it once, and that this party has entered that he said it and has not entered what he said, and that this party has undertaken in this book not to ask him about it again, and that he did not ask for money and was not offered any and would not have taken any. **AND THE SECOND COLUMN: the number itself, which is not in this book and is not in any book in this harbor and is not going to be written in any office and is not going to be printed; and the reason it is in neither column, which is not a rule of this yard but a decision of it, and the reason is that a figure this party cannot check is a figure a man in a city can be lied to in without anybody lying.**
- **The letter from Veyr of Thursday the twenty-ninth of June, and the answer of Friday the thirtieth.** The letter is one sheet, four questions, no seal, and the answer went in the ordinary post at no cost. **THE ANSWER'S REASON IS IN ITS FIRST LINE AND NOT ITS LAST.** The three answered questions are answered out of the yard's own book in the yard's own left hand. **THE FOURTH IS NOT ANSWERED, AND THE REASON STANDS: a figure a yard has asked a man to stand behind is a figure the yard has published, and this yard is not able to publish a figure it cannot check, and on the third of May this yard put a sheet on a wall with a line on it saying that nobody knows who takes a road off a chart, and if we go and get a man to stand behind our own number then we have made ourselves the man who takes it off the chart, in our own hand, on our own wall.** The best sentence in the batch is in it: **a question about a bar of gravel cannot be answered by the people who printed the bar, because the people who printed the bar did not cast the lead.**
- **The page of Wednesday the fifth of July, four things the party will not permit and one thing it has decided.** It is in the yard's own book, on the yard's own account, without being asked by anybody, and it is the volume's answer to the two things the Volume 02 audit left to this volume. **Its third item is the channel, decided on the page with a date: no posting, appointment, commission, transport or instruction out of any city in the past month has put anybody past this harbor line; whatever any office in any city does with any man in any coat, this party has not asked and will find out when it finds out; and if a paper comes up the hill about the second season of the writ of the first of May it will be read, it will be answered on the day it comes, and the man who brings it will not be treated as a friend or as an enemy, because the writ's own last line says the second season is not this office's to give and will arrive out of a city from a man who has never been inside this harbor and the holder will be told when it arrives and not before. THE CHANNEL IS THE WRIT. IT HAS BEEN THE CHANNEL SINCE THE FIRST OF MAY AND THE YARD DID NOT HAVE TO INVENT ONE AND IS NOT GOING TO.** Its fourth item fixes that the outer marks and the bar of four hundred yards off them stand where the writ of the first of May says they are and are not nearer, and that this party is not going past them **this season** and has not tried and is not going to. **Its one decision is that the yard is not going to be a party to the naming of a road, is not going to buy a licence to one, is not going to sell a copy of anything again, and is not going to put a name on anything it cannot check, and that this decision is the yard's own and is not the office's and is not anybody in a city.**
- **The two lines about the four casts of Saturday the first of July**, and the four casts of the second of June beside them, and the entry that the two sets agree and that **the agreement is a fact and not a proof, because two sets of four casts is eight casts, and a man keeps a bar for nineteen years on four times a year, and eight is not four times, and a yard that begins to treat eight casts as a figure is a yard that is about to put a number on a wall.**
- **The entry of the wall of Wednesday the fifth**, in the first column, **that the party did not put a sheet on the market wall, and the reason, and that it is the first entry in the yard's book in four months that is about a thing the party did not do.**

**There is no ninth acknowledgement in this batch, no fifth name on the writ, and no return made other than the third. The only day in this batch on which a person of this party was in the water is Saturday the twenty-fourth of June, when a hired man of the second lay was on a woman's own account landward of the third seam's mark with all six conditions read out at the boat in order and the stated reason entered before anybody was wet. On the first of July nobody was in the water at all.**

### 4. The bodies and the crew

- **Calder Marr**, 32, line diver with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice and not used since the eighth of December, and the lift at the end of a childhood call gone and not restorable. **He goes in no water in this batch, enters nothing, and takes no stage of any kind. THE CONDITION IS NOT NAMED IN THIS BATCH.** **It is on the page as the bare fact TWICE and both times in the second column with a reason against the line, and it is the bare fact twice with a stated count of four.** On Sunday the eighteenth of June, on the crown, in a north-easterly, he could not say in which of two directions a voice had come from, and wrote it in his own hand. **On Monday the third of July, on the crown, at the top of the flood, the mole head's handbell went and he could not say which of the two, and he did not write it down, and he told a shipwright about it at about two in the afternoon, and that is the first time in five months he has said it out loud to anybody.** She wrote it in her own hand and he did not tell her what to write, and she put no name against the line and said why. **The chapter also names the previous three: the twenty-seventh of May in a westerly, the second of June on a boat, and the fifteenth of June at the head of the dock where it was not taken and was not written down.** **THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 03 IS ALLOWED IS THEREFORE NOT SPENT AND IS OWED TO MOVEMENT 4. NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH MAY BE READ AS HAVING SPENT IT, AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH LETS IT VANISH SILENTLY: IT IS ON THE PAGE FOUR TIMES, IT HAS A COUNT AGAINST IT, AND IT HAS A NAMED CONSEQUENCE FOR THE FIRST TIME, WHICH IS THAT THE LINE IS NOW IN ANOTHER PERSON'S HAND.**
- **Nessa Pike**, 27. **She is right in this batch four times and nobody thanks her for any of them.** In Chapter 122 she makes the officer read a date as a date rather than as a courtesy, and she stands four feet away for the whole of it. In Chapter 124 she reads the eleven names off a stranger's printed sheet and puts her thumb on them. In Chapter 125 she refuses to say a third time that a reader must be hired and says that a thing said three times in a shed is a complaint and she does not make complaints, she makes entries. In Chapter 127 **she buys the rival's printed sheet for four marks out of her own hand, and will not have it repaid and will not be thanked, and says that she said out loud that the rival is wrong about one thing and will not say which and will not let either of them write it down, because writing a man's name next to a doubt is asking a man with a bad wheel and a slate about himself.** In Chapter 128 **she is the reason there is a reader in a boat's cabin**, and she is the one who wrote the second sound entry in her own hand and refused to put a name against it. In Chapter 129 and Chapter 130 her sentences are the turn and the close.
- **Bevin Rook**, 22, **off the stone since Monday the fifteenth of May and not replaced. HE IS NOT IN THIS BATCH. He is not written to, not visited, and nobody is asked who has seen him what he said, and the three nots of the eighth of June stand and were recited on the fourth of July. A wage of four marks a week goes on running into an envelope and the forty marks of claim stand whole and are not reduced and are not asked to be. THE BATCH DOES NOT RESOLVE IT, DOES NOT REPAIR IT, DOES NOT PAY IT OFF, AND MAY NOT.** **His sentence about the lid of the chest is quoted on the page on the second of July as the reason the yard does not write the number down, and that is the only use made of him in the batch, and he was not asked and is not to be.**
- **Corvin Sarn**, 50, the harbor's own bellwright, paid, teaching nobody, not of this party, not of the Free Keel. **HE IS ASKED HIS ONE QUESTION IN THIS BATCH, IN A ROOM, AND HE ANSWERS IT ONCE.** The asking is put to him in a shed doorway on Saturday the first of July and is not asked in the doorway; **the question is asked on Sunday the second of July at about ten in the morning in the cabin of the *Anna Ray*, with the door shut and the hatch open, and there were four men in that cabin, and no office and no seal and no ledger in it and the chest was not brought down the hill and was not opened.** What he said, in his own words, once: **it is not a bell number and not a boat number and not a date and not anything this harbor has ever cut and not anything he cut in two yards in Ternwatch, and he is saying that as a man who could be wrong and not as a man who is right; that there are more marks in it than there are figures, that two of the strokes are joined at one end and one is not joined to anything, that there is a two and there is a two and a bit of another one and that there is a fourth that is not a two and that the rest of it is tooling and there is more tooling on that face than number; and that he has had it four months and cannot make it be one thing and is not going to pretend to a cabin that he can.** **NO DIGIT IS PRINTED IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND NO FIGURE GOES INTO ANY COLUMN. His three conditions, all agreed in the room: the number is not written down, nobody comes about it again, and the chest stays shut — and he did not ask for money, would not have taken any, and said so, and that he did not ask is entered.** **THE UNDERTAKING IS ENTERED IN THE YARD'S OWN BOOK AND BINDS THIS PARTY ONLY, AND NOT THE OFFICE AND NOT THE MARKET AND NOT THE BOY, WHO WAS NOT ASKED AND IS NOT TO BE. NO LATER CHAPTER IN THIS VOLUME MAY ASK HIM AGAIN.** He is still owed eight marks and a day of a boat since the twenty-third of May and it is not paid.
- **Elias Voke**, 51, of the *Anna Ray*, nineteen winters disputed and unpaid. **He sailed up the coast on Tuesday the twentieth of June, one day before the hearing room in which his nineteen winters were sold, and he was at sea on the twenty-first and did not know.** He came back into the roads on Wednesday the twenty-eighth after eight days and **said, in his own mouth on the boards and not out of a file, what a coast office told him in a room**, and the coast office's answer is on the page for the first time in the volume and is an answer and not a document. He is owed nineteen winters, is owed them in a naval file and in four lines of a letter in his own coat, **and says out loud in a market that the letter is the best thing that office has done for him in nine years and wants it written down, and that he is not angry and has said so out loud so that nobody can say he is swallowing it.** He is not carrying a paper about the hard at Ashtide a third time. **He offered his own cabin on the Saturday as the room for a question, said what it was worth in about nine words, and was in it, and said afterwards that he did not have to have a cabin in it and did.**
- **Perry Ollett**, 39, master of the *Sallow Reach*, a crew of Ashtide, **new in this batch and an ordinary working man.** Six men, a compressor of her own, forty fathoms of her own hose brought up the coast in a lighter, lying at the outer end of the second lay because the north berth is out and the third lay is full. He has a printed, scaled, dated, priced coasting sheet and he puts it on the market wall over the yard's own on Saturday the twenty-fourth. **He is not a villain, does not threaten anybody, is given no office, no ship, no uniform and no title, does not use the word the yard refused in public, does not say what a road is, does not explain anything and connects nothing to anything.** He says the yard's sheet is right about the road and is refused for a reason he thinks is right, and he says the eleven is the best thing the yard did this year and he has printed it. **He has found four of the eleven up and down the coast in five weeks; the yard did not ask which four and he did not say.**
- **Edric Pringle**, 54, of the *Anna Beth*, second lay, **new in this batch.** She says the cause out loud before the figure, which is how she has always done it. **She hires a day at the second seam on her own account on Saturday the twenty-fourth, sixteen gross, and pays the victuals and the hired hand at the mole, and the ring, the shackle, the swivel and about four feet of her own warp go onto her own deck in her own hands.** She is owed eight shillings and tenpence, undiminished and unpaid and not paid in this batch, and **no warp figure is given for her anywhere in this batch, and her debt and Sella Brigg's are kept in separate sentences.**
- **Sella Brigg**, of the third lay. **On Sunday the twenty-fifth of June, in a market, in about forty people's hearing, she asks for the price of her own warp, is given the whole of the money of this yard since the eighth of May, and says she is going to ask a man of Ashtide, and the yard says she has a right to and does not stand in the way. She then says out loud that she worked out about the thirty marks herself, in eleven days, from a coat. She is owed eight shillings and tenpence, is owed it undiminished, is not paid in this batch, and about four hundred feet of her own warp is on the bottom of this harbor for a hundred and forty-eight days at the fourth of July. NOTHING IS RAISED AND NOTHING IS SOLD.**
- **Harl Dace**, 48, of the *Grey Widow*, **is not in this batch and is not asked.** His figure is the fourth question a man in a city asked and the yard refused to ask him about, **and the shipwright said out loud in a shed on Friday the thirtieth that the rival is wrong about one thing and refused to say which, and that when the man in Veyr prints it in six weeks it will be a stranger's number against a stranger's number and the man with the slate will be right and will not know that he was right.**
- **Rowan Trewin**, 54, a pilot out of a storm belt, **is not in this batch and is not turned into a transaction and is not used.** His two figures against the sheet's two stand unresolved, and the yard's closing page says out loud that it is waiting in the autumn for a man of fifty-four to say out loud which of two figures is right, with no instrument in this harbor that can call him and none that can stop him.
- **The man of fifty-one with a bad wheel of the *Grey Widow*** is unnamed on the page, is not in this batch, is not put on a lead, is not in the water, is not hired, is not asked one word, and **is not merged with Coll Ferrow's bad wheel, which is of the *Kestrel's Sister* and is that boat's.**
- **The officer of a vessel holding a claim in this harbor** is a fourth man and is not to be confused with any of the three above: **he is the same man who was on the mole on the twenty-second of May, he did not give a name and was not asked for one, the yard entered that he is the same man, and he holds no wheel of any kind in this harbor and is not Harl Dace, who is forty-eight, and not the man of fifty-one, and not Coll Ferrow. The man who holds the figure on the bar of gravel is Harl Dace's figure, and Dace is the master of the *Grey Widow* and has a slate and no bad wheel of his own in this harbor.**
- **The harbor authority's clerk** comes up the hill on Saturday the seventeenth with the sealed jacket and does not go to the shed, and says for the sixth time that he cannot enter what a foot and a half of glass is, and writes on his own page that a party with no boy in it has put three returns up the coast in six weeks and every one has been on the day except the first. He does not mention the wall.
- **Ewan Tristram**, the wharfinger of the second lay, **is named once and used once, and his thread CLOSES INSIDE VOLUME 03.** **On Tuesday the fourth of July he says in about nine people on the boards that a cistern holding for three weeks is not a repair account, and on Thursday the sixth of July he comes down to the sluice and says the end of it out loud: eight walks on the office's own page at no figure, the cistern holding since the nineteenth of June, an end of September on the ninth seam, and a light not on, a north berth out, a third lay full, forty feet of the office's own marline in the sea, dead hose, the *Kittiwake* on the hard, the hard at Ashtide unanswered, nineteen winters in a naval file and in four lines of a letter — and that a cistern that holds is a hole with a lid on it and not a town, said in that order, in a market, and not taken back, and not asking anybody for anything. HE IS NOT USED AGAIN AS A PRESSURE THAT REOPENS ANYTHING AND THE HARBOR'S POVERTY IS NOT FIXED FOR ANYTHING.**
- **Torren Skell**, 59, **is not in this batch.** His thirty marks are not in it, his mooring is not in it, and the fact that his money was refused and what it cost stands where Chapter 120 left it.
- **Alden Marr** is at the far bench in Chapter 121, on both days, **is not asked for anything, asks nothing, and neither brother goes over to him; and he is at the far bench again in Chapter 130 on Wednesday the fifth and is not asked for anything there either. NO FRAGMENT, NO REPORT TALK, NO SECOND STORY, NO CONFESSION.** The single first-column line recording the not-asking is in both chapters and can be checked by either brother by asking the other.
- **Mara Venn** is in no room in this batch. Her forty marks stand and are not paid and the writ cannot reach her and she is not asked to be useful and is not an assistant.
- **Captain Veyra Holt** is named twice, on two papers, and described from a distance. **She is not in this harbor, did not come up the hill, is not going to, and no face-to-face meeting occurs.**
- **Magister Ilya Serr** appears once, on a seal line, in the reading of the writ in the hearing room, and never in a room as a person.

### 5. The dated positions at the end of Chapter 130

- **The writ.** A provisional writ granted Monday the first of May, running to the first of March next, **not the Second Bell license, not a charter, renewing nothing, ground not widened.** The four names are unchanged and no fifth has been added. It was produced a second time, in a room, on Wednesday the twenty-first, **whole, with the crew's book and all three returns, unshortened, and the yard said out loud that the book goes with the writ because the clause's own construction carries it and not because anybody asked for it.** The second season is a named absence and Chapter 130 puts the channel on the page in the yard's own hand.
- **The reporting clause. THREE RETURNS HAVE NOW BEEN MADE UNDER IT AND THERE IS NO FOURTH: the fourth of May, two days late; the tenth of May, on the day; and the seventeenth of June, ON THE DAY, out of a finding of Saturday the tenth of June.** The counting rule is on the page twice. The clause was not widened, narrowed, softened or argued by anybody.
- **The notice of the thirty-first of October.** Four clauses, not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered. **Clause two is on the page as a live reason six times in this batch and is argued by nobody: the two objects are still on the mole's crown in the open air, the tarpaulins were put back on on the seventeenth, and the yard's own boat went back into the water on Monday the twenty-sixth with both of them left on the crown, and the reason is that a boat in this harbor with a thing like that in her is a boat in a shed with a lid on.** **There are eight acknowledgements in the harbor authority's ledger and MOVEMENT 3 ENTERED NONE, both of this year's are spent, and no person, boat or lead of this yard went inside the first clause on any day of this batch, and Chapter 130 puts that on the page as the first of four things the party will not permit.**
- **The eighth-clause plan for the fifth seam** is refused and is in a record with a date on it. **IT IS NOT REOPENED IN THIS BATCH. It is referred to exactly once, on Wednesday the twenty-first, in nine words in a room, by a man who has just been told what a thing costs in this harbor: *I am not going to say one word about the plate at the fifth seam. I have been told what a thing costs in this harbor this morning. I intend to find out what the price of that is before I offer anything again.* No person in this batch has said a word about the plate besides that sentence, and nobody in this harbor has said a word about it since the seventeenth of May, which is now fifty days.** The plate is still in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched. **The mechanism is unchanged and it is now stated as politics and not as an argument: an office whose instruments are uncontested is an office that can do things it has not been able to do, and the first thing it did was enter an end of the first of September on the ninth seam's mooring on Monday the third of July.**
- **The bell.** Eight and a half feet off the south end, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was**, a new rope bought on the eleventh of January. **THE GOING OUT OF THE SECOND OF MAY IS DONE AND IS SPENT AND IS NOT REPEATED AND NO SECOND EIGHT MARKS IS PAID AND NO BOAT GOES OFF THE SOUTH END IN THIS BATCH.** The standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed. **THE BELL'S GROUND GEAR AND MOORING CARRY, IN A REEVE'S OWN HAND OF THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF DECEMBER, A NOTATION THAT ANY PERSON PROPOSING TO WORK, RECOVER, LIFT OR MOVE THE BELL IS TO BE GIVEN THE ACCOUNT OF THE HERRING FLEET'S DUES BEFORE HE IS GIVEN A LINE AND A DATE, AND THAT NOTATION WENT OUT OF THIS HARBOR ON THURSDAY THE TWENTY-SECOND OF JUNE ON THE CHART, AND THERE IS NO SECOND COPY OF IT BECAUSE THE NOTATION IS ON THE CHART AND THE CHART IS THE PAPER.** **THE BELL READS NOTHING, SETTLES NOTHING AND SAYS NO WORD, AND IT IS NOT IN A BOAT IN THIS BATCH, AND IT IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT IN THIS BATCH.** Eight marks and a day of a boat stands owing since the twenty-third of May, unpaid and unargued.
- **The lead plug.** In a chest in a shed with a lock the yard owns, with the boy's writing on the lid. **ITS NUMBER WAS SAID OUT LOUD ONCE, IN A ROOM, ON SUNDAY THE SECOND OF JULY, AND IS NOT WRITTEN IN ANY BOOK IN THIS HARBOR AND IS NOT GOING TO BE. THE YARD HAS UNDERTAKEN IN ITS OWN BOOK NOT TO ASK ABOUT IT AGAIN, AND THAT UNDERTAKING BINDS THE YARD AND NOT THE OFFICE AND NOT THE MARKET AND NOT THE BOY. The chest was not opened in this batch and was not brought down the hill.**
- **The glass storm.** Not used. The two written occurrences stand, the two second-column lines stand in two hands on two pages that are not on top of each other, and **no cause is named for it in this batch or in any volume and nothing in either line knows anything. The delay on the call in the water is still not given.**
- **The coast road.** Not resolved, not explained, not named a network, not connected to anything under this town, and **the word *network* is not in the yard's hand anywhere in this batch and is not put in a rival's either.** Its figures are now printed in a market in this harbor and are going to press in a city in July, and the man of fifty-four who keeps a different figure of his own is still coming in the autumn and the yard has entered that it is waiting for him.
- **Custody.** Three items, three numbers, digits not quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request. **No paper states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material and no clerk answered it in this batch.**
- **The concealment.** Hidden. The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. **Nobody in this batch asked the boy and nobody asked the conservator, and nobody in that crew asked the boy one word about either of his two omissions.** The yard is asked to read a return and refuses to make itself able to answer one, and that is on the page, and it is not a reference to the mark.
- **The delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, two of them with no call in them, and **neither figure is advanced in this batch by arithmetic or by a measurement.** The mole head's handbell went on Monday the third of July at about eleven and a person of this party could not say in which of two directions its answer came, and **no occasion was entered, the delay was not taken, and the delay is a hand's width of a second and has not moved.** No occasion is entered in this batch. The answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.
- **Labor.** A pot of air bought on the nineteenth and no other; no delivery hose and the longest thing of hose in the harbor about nine fathoms, **with a crew of Ashtide lying at the outer end of the second lay with a compressor of her own and forty fathoms of her own hose, and neither fact being anybody's fault and both being the reason her sheet is better than the yard's**; a gland fitted and unpaid; a hand lead's line that is right and was used four times since the second of June — the second, the tenth, the twenty-fourth and the first of July — of which three are in this batch, **and nothing was cast with it on the third of July, the day of the cistern, the crown and the handbell**; the ninth seam out with **an end of the first of September entered on Monday the third of July**; the north berth out; the third lay full; the tide gang four men short; **a cistern charged eight times in three weeks at no figure and holding since the nineteenth of June, which a wharfinger has said out loud in a market is a hole with a lid on it and not a town**; the fire party's hose dead; the light not on; the repair account empty since the middle of February; no boat of this office's own since the evening of the twenty-eighth of April; the *Kittiwake* on the hard; the hard at Ashtide; nineteen winters; the *Sea Wren* at the pile; **eleven copies, a list of the eleven on a wall since the third of May, a printed sheet in a market with all eleven places on it, four of the eleven answered a stranger and two of those stopped writing, and this yard did not ask which four**; a man of the third lay, nine winters, not found, and this yard has not gone.

### 6. What this batch may not be used for

1. **It may not be used to make a fourth return.** **Three returns have been made and no fourth exists, and none may be produced out of a day that produced nothing.**
2. **It may not be used to spend the naming of the condition.** It is not named in this batch, twice in the second column with a reason and with a count of four against the fourth, and once now in another person's hand. **The one naming is therefore still available and is owed to Movement 4, and no Movement 4 chapter may treat it as spent, and no Movement 4 chapter may let it vanish silently: it is on the page four times, and the fourth of those was written by somebody else.**
3. **It may not be used to ask the bellwright again.** **He was asked, in a room, once, and the yard has undertaken in its own book not to ask again, and that undertaking binds the yard. NO LATER CHAPTER IN THIS VOLUME MAY ASK HIM AGAIN, AND NO CHAPTER MAY PRINT A DIGIT OF WHAT HE SAID, AND NO CHAPTER MAY PUT WHAT HE SAID IN THE THIRD COLUMN.** It may not be used to open the chest, to make the plug a custody object, or to connect it to the plate, the Oren mark, the coast road, the worked panel or anything under this town.
4. **It may not be used to enter a ninth acknowledgement, to lift, narrow, re-serve or alter the notice, to argue clause two, to reach the plate at the fifth seam, to put a person, boat or lead of this yard inside the first clause, to widen the writ's ground, to add a fifth name, or to manufacture a second season.** It may not be used to have a ninth be asked for.
5. **It may not be used to name a cause for the glass storm, to let it speak, to name the Nine Harbors, the Quietening or the Crown Seal, to explain the nine-line mark, to identify the marking on the shard, to name the compartment or to state that the sealed case was in standing space number four, to quote a custody number's digits, to put a senior examiner in a room, to name an office of returning memory, to have Captain Holt in a room or on a mole, to ask Sera Mott, to introduce a new antagonist, institution or final enemy, or to give Torren Skell an office, a ship, a uniform or a title.**
6. **It may not be used to resolve the coast road, to name it a network, to connect it to anything under this town, to name the nine final routes, or to say who edits the charts.** The man in Veyr did not ask that question and the yard did not answer it and neither of them put it on the page.
7. **It may not be used to resolve, repair or pay off Bevin Rook's June, to write to him, to go and see him, to ask anybody who has seen him what he said, to reduce the forty marks, to stop the wage, or to treat the wage as a claim on his June.**
8. **It may not be used to pay Mara Venn's forty marks, to pay either woman's eight shillings and tenpence, to raise or sell the warp, to weigh, price or offer the bell-metal gun-port, to sell one mark's worth of the heap behind the shed, to re-write the chandler's note, to move any of its three counts of weeks, or to move the count of eleven copies.**
9. **It may not be used to make Perry Ollett a villain, to give him an office or a following, to make him the coast office or the circuit office, to have him find out what the yard has not asked, or to have him and the man of Veyr meet or correspond.** **It may not be used to have the coast office fixed, softened, defeated or forgiven, and it may not be used to have the harbor's poverty fixed for anything: the cistern holds, the light is not on, the north berth is out, the third lay is full and the hard at Ashtide is unanswered, and that is what the batch ends on.**
10. **It may not be used to stage a dive of Calder Marr's own, to take any power stage, or to put a diver in the water who is not a hired man with his own limit fixed at the boat, under a named tender who is not a diver, with a cutoff the tender's to pull, with the six conditions read out in order and the stated reason entered before anybody is wet.**
11. **It may not be used to name Captain Holt's posting after her defeat.** **THE POSTING IS NOT DECIDED AND IS EXPLICITLY STILL OPEN; the channel by which a disgraced officer reaches a Morrow Quay reeve IS decided, on the page, on Wednesday the fifth of July, and it is the writ.** See § 8, item 2.

### 7. The costs this batch closes with

1. **The yard's own slip of eleven names, nailed to a market wall since the third of May for a shilling, which a stranger used to print the place of every copy of a public record, which is the reason a rival crew's map looks better than the yard's, and which the yard could not take down and would not have taken down.**
2. **About four hundred feet of a woman's own warp, and a woman saying in a market that she is going to take it to a crew of Ashtide, and the yard having no standing and not trying, and the yard saying out loud that the refusal of the seventh of June is the reason that crew can do the job and the yard cannot.**
3. **A reeve who sold a man's nineteen winters for a chart, in a room with a clerk in it, on his own motion, having named the price before paying it, and the man it was paid out of was one day at sea and found out on a Friday and did not shout.**
4. **Nineteen winters in a naval file, and the harbor's own north berth, ninth seam and third lay in a list in a city being made up in the first week of July, all of it out of documents this harbor wrote and published for nothing.**
5. **A question about a bar of gravel that the yard cannot answer and has made a rule it will not break, and a printed sheet that answers it anyway, and a woman who said out loud that the sheet is wrong about one thing and refused to say which.**
6. **The number in the lead plug, said once in a room, not written down, and an undertaking not to ask again that binds this yard for the rest of the volume.**
7. **A cistern charged five times in twenty-one days by people nobody pays, and eight times at no figure on the office's own page since the end of May, holding since the nineteenth of June, which a wharfinger has said out loud in a market is a hole with a lid on it and not a town.**
8. **Four marks in a bag, thirty-one on a note, eleven lines and forty-four marks in a wage column, six envelopes on a bench and not one of them anywhere, and ten marks in and fourteen out in twenty-one days, and no office in this harbor that has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May and none that is going to.**
9. **A yard that declined to put a sheet on a wall it could have put a sheet on, and entered that the only thing that makes a wall worth reading is somebody else being able to be asked a question about it, and that there is nothing in this harbor that could answer.**
10. **A condition on the page four times, with a count, and for the first time in another person's hand, and unnamed.**

### 8. Proposed canon changes

**None applied. Two flagged rather than applied, and one author-level decision taken on the page.**

1. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the first request for the harbor authority's chart is referred to with NO DATE.** Chapter 122 says the first request was made in a room in this harbor in December last and was refused in about eleven seconds, and that this party was not in that room, did not hear it, and has it from a man who was, **and that the yard has been able to establish no date for the first request and has not invented one.** Chapters 49 and 50 of Volume 01 record the request and the refusal and give no date. **This is a deliberate refusal to date a thing the chapters do not date, and a Volume 03 audit may rule that a later chapter may supply a date if one can be derived from a chapter rather than from a file.**
2. **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — the channel by which a disgraced officer reaches a Morrow Quay reeve.** **The Volume 02 audit's ruling 7 left this to Volume 03 and forbade any other volume to invent it, and it is DECIDED HERE, ON THE PAGE, ON WEDNESDAY THE FIFTH OF JULY, IN CALDER MARR'S OWN LEFT HAND, IN THE YARD'S OWN BOOK.** **The channel is the writ.** The writ's own last line says the second season is not the harbor authority's to give and that whatever comes will arrive out of a city from a man who has never been inside this harbor, and that the holder will be told about it when it arrives and not before. **A man in a coat, of any rank, of any office, disgraced or not, reaches a Morrow Quay reeve as a stranger with a paper about the second season, and the yard's page says it will read it, will answer it on the day it comes, and will not treat him as a friend or an enemy.** The channel had been in a sheet on a bench since the first of May and the yard did not have to invent one and is not going to. **No new person, office or institution was created to carry it.**
3. **STILL OPEN, EXPLICITLY, AND NOT DECIDED IN THIS BATCH — CAPTAIN HOLT'S POSTING AFTER HER DEFEAT.** **Her defeat is in Movement 5 and has not happened; a posting cannot be decided before the thing it follows, and no chapter of this volume may name a posting before it exists.** What Chapter 130 forecloses on the page, in the yard's own hand, is what a posting may not be: **no posting, appointment, commission, transport or instruction out of any city in the past month has put anybody past this harbor line, and the outer marks and the bar of four hundred yards off them stand where the writ says they are and are not nearer, and this party is not going past them **this season** and has not tried and is not going to.** **A Volume 03 Movement 5 writer must decide the posting on the page with a date, and must not have it be a command in this harbor, an inspecting commission over a harbor authority, or anything that puts a person, a boat or a lead inside the closure of the notice; and may not invent a second channel, because the channel is the writ.**
4. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — two creditor figures held apart.** **The prompt's cast list gives Edric Pringle and Sella Brigg each eight shillings and tenpence, and each about four hundred feet of her own warp on the bottom of this harbor.** Chapter 116 and the whole of Batch 0002 establish Sella Brigg's four hundred feet on the page with a date, a length of time and a market wall. **Chapters 124 and 130 give the eight shillings and tenpence to BOTH women and give a warp figure to ONE of them, and the two debts are never in the same sentence with the same warp, and the two women are never merged.** **A Volume 03 audit should rule whether the February warp event was one warp with two owners or two warps, and if two, where the second four hundred feet is stated, because the chapters currently state one.**
5. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — an end entered on the ninth seam's mooring, the first of September.** Chapter 129 records it in another office's book on Monday the third of July, with the reeve's own reason, that this office's instruments are not being contested. **It is a gain and it is entered at no cost to this harbor, which is unusual in this volume, and it is recorded here so that a later volume does not mistake it for the notice having been lifted. THE NOTICE OF THE THIRTY-FIRST OF OCTOBER IS NOT LIFTED, NOT NARROWED AND NOT ALTERED, AND THE NINTH SEAM'S MOORING IS A MUNICIPAL MOORING AND NOT A PERSON, A BOAT OR A LEAD, AND NO ACKNOWLEDGEMENT WAS ENTERED FOR IT.**

## Batch 0003 review repairs — the disposition of every finding

A reviewer pass over Chapters 121–130, the four state files and the queued `workspace/volume-03/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` returned **FIX**, with **sixteen required repairs** and a list of prose and craft items. **The repairs were applied in place. No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut, no cost was dropped, no planned plot was altered and no lock was softened.** The batch is **33,731 words where it was 33,612.** The reviewer's own log is `logs/batch-0003.review.log`.

**The reviewer also verified and recorded as clean, and those hold after the repairs:** no condition named; no power stage taken; no fourth return; no ninth acknowledgement; no person, boat or lead inside the first clause; the hearing room used once (Ch123, Wednesday the twenty-first) and the market wall once (Ch124, Saturday the twenty-fourth), never on the same day; the three reserved beats each paid once; Alden neither confesses nor is approached; no interface, no second person, no invented office, no invented antagonist; Movement 3's turn lands and Chapter 130's close earns its pull without a cut-away; no duplicated paragraph verbatim.

### The sixteen required repairs

1. **THE WEEKDAY OF THE NINETEENTH OF JUNE WAS WRONG IN FOUR PLACES AND CONTRADICTED THE BATCH'S OWN CALENDAR.** `outline/volume-03.md` L1 and this file's § 1 both put **19 June a Monday**, and Chapter 122 opens on it: "Monday the nineteenth of June". Four places then said Saturday. **All four are corrected at source**: Chapter 122's two first-column entries and Nessa Pike's second-column line now read **Monday** the nineteenth, and Chapter 123's two references to the paper having been on the mole read **the Monday**. **The two handoff lines in `state/open-threads.md` are corrected with them.** The chapter decides the date; the planning files already agreed with the chapter.
2. **Chapter 122's bag figure contradicted the wage it recorded in the same breath.** The read-out at about six on Tuesday the twentieth said **Six marks in a bag** in the same paragraph-chain that records the four-mark wage of the twentieth already paid into the fourth envelope and the column at nine lines and thirty-six marks. The correct figure at that hour is **TWO** — 8 − 2 for the pot − 4 for the wage — which is what Chapter 123 and this file's money chain both carried. **It is Two marks in a bag.** The published chain 8, 6, 2, 9, 5, 8, 4 was never wrong; the chapter was reading 6 at the wrong hour.
3. **Chapter 124's money paragraph contradicted itself and the bag it opens with.** It opened **Nine marks in a bag** (which is 2 + 7 and only works on seven), itemised the job correctly as nine out of sixteen leaving the yard **seven**, and then said the pot of air bought on the Friday had left the job **four marks and not seven**. **The pot was out of the bag before the job and cannot reduce a job two days later.** **The paragraph now says the yard's is seven and makes the comparison true instead of false: seven is three more than the four the yard costed the same day of work to on the mole's steps on the thirty-first of May, when the pot and about a hundred fathoms of rope were both charged to the job, because the pot had gone out of the bag on the Friday and the ring, the shackle, the swivel and the four feet of warp all came on the ring, so that nothing else came off the day.** **The four written up on the steps on the thirty-first of May stands and is now checkable against a real day, which is better than the false clause it replaced.** The false sentence had propagated to `state/current.md` L40 and L96, this file's money-chain table, and `state/chapter-summaries.md` L602; **all four are corrected.** The Batch 0002 occurrence of the same figure is **NOT touched**: on the tenth of June the pot and the hemp both went out of the bag before the job, which is a different situation and is arithmetically sound where Chapter 124's was not.
4. **Chapter 130 gave the batch's headline money figure twice and the two disagreed.** L67 said **ten marks** in twenty-one days, which is 7 + 3 and is what every state file carries; L71 said **eleven**. **L71 is ten.**
5. **META LANGUAGE WAS IN THE PROSE IN SEVEN PLACES, AND TWO OF THEM HAD PROPAGATED.** Removed: Chapter 128's *opened by anybody **in this batch*** (now *by anybody in this yard*); Chapter 129's two *not paid **in this batch*** (now *in three weeks*); Chapter 130's *not paid **in this batch*** and *nothing in this batch* (now *in three weeks*); Chapter 130's first-column entry of the fifth of July, which put ***in this volume*** into a page of the yard's own book (**this season** — the writ's own term); and Chapter 124's *it is the sentence **this movement** turns on* (**this week**). **The two propagated copies — `state/current.md` L102 and `state/open-threads.md` L746, both quoting the fifth of July's fourth item as though it were a book entry — are corrected.**
6. **THE MAN WHO HOLDS THE BAR-OF-GRAVEL FIGURE WAS TWO DIFFERENT MEN INSIDE CHAPTER 127, AND THREE ACROSS THE STATE FILES.** Chapter 127's dictated answer gave it correctly to *a man of forty-eight of this harbor … on the *Grey Widow's* own account* — **Harl Dace** — but two narrative lines gave it to *a man in a boat in this harbor **with a bad wheel of his own*** and to *a man with a bad wheel and a slate*. That is **the unnamed man of fifty-one** in the same boat, whom the locks bar from being interviewed, hired or put on a lead, and **whose bad wheel is not the same object as Harl Dace's boat or as Coll Ferrow's, and the two bad wheels in this harbor are two men and may never be merged.** **Both chapter lines are corrected to the man the answer gives it to: the master of a boat lying in this harbor, not of this party, not of any association, with a slate of his own and a name on the sheet.** **This file's § 4 had the figure with Dace, kept the fifty-one separate in a second bullet, and then garbled the two into one line reading *The man of fifty-four whose bad wheel is in the boat, Harl Dace, above* — which contradicted Dace's stated age of forty-eight, merged the two bad wheels, and glued the cutter's officer onto the same sentence. That line is rewritten: the officer is now a fourth and separate man, is not Dace, is not the fifty-one and is not Ferrow, and the figure on the bar is Dace's.** **`state/current.md` L62 attached the *printed sheet is wrong about one thing* thread to the fifty-one-year-old and is rewritten to name Dace, forty-eight, with the two bad wheels held apart.** `state/open-threads.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` carried the same thread and are corrected too.
7. **Chapter 129 contradicted Chapter 130 and the state files on when the cistern last went dry.** The wharfinger on Tuesday the fourth said it had not gone dry since the **twenty-second** of June and that the office had **six** walks on its page; Chapter 130, the state files and the queued prompt all say the **nineteenth**, and the third of July was already the seventh walk. **Both are corrected to the nineteenth and to seven walks.**
8. **FIGURES THAT DID NOT SURVIVE ARITHMETIC.** Chapter 124's *the slip had been on the wall for **seventy-eight days*** — the slip went up Wednesday the third of May and Chapter 124 is the twenty-third, which is **fifty-one days**; its *free to read for **ten weeks*** is the same slip and is now **seven weeks**. The seventy-eight is corrected at source and in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. Chapter 129's *four hundred feet of one of them's own warp … for **a hundred and forty-two days*** — the sixth of February to the fourth of July is **a hundred and forty-eight**, and Chapter 125's hundred and thirty-nine at the twenty-fifth is right and the two now agree — is corrected at source and in this file's two places. Chapter 129's *the last four marks of anything this yard has had for **a fortnight*** is false: the fortnight to the fourth of July brought in ten marks, on the twenty-fourth and the first. **It now says the wage came out of the ten marks that have come into this yard since the twenty-fourth of June, and that the four it came out of have been in the bag since Friday.** A fourth figure in the same class, not on the reviewer's list and corrected on the same principle: **the cistern is said to have held *for three weeks* on the fourth and sixth of July, and it had held for a fortnight and a day and a fortnight and three. It now says a fortnight in both chapters**, and the *time in three weeks* ordinals throughout the batch — which the review found strained past the end of June — are reduced to bare ordinals, **the fourth, the fifth, the sixth, the seventh and the eighth time**, which are true on every day they are spoken.
9. **Chapter 123 wiped the slate wall on a Wednesday.** The wall is wiped every Monday at eight whether there is a sitting or not, Chapter 122 has it down on Monday the nineteenth, and Chapter 123 had it wiped "at eight that morning" on Wednesday the twenty-first, which wiped it twice in three days. **Chapter 123 now says the wall was wiped bare on Monday morning at eight and nothing has been put up on it since.**
10. **Chapter 125's forward look got two dates wrong.** *A man who went up the coast on **Sunday the twenty-first*** — he went on **Tuesday the twentieth** (Ch123's "yesterday afternoon", Chapter 124 and this file all say the twentieth), and the twenty-first of June is a Wednesday. *On **Friday the third of July** there is a day at the second seam on a hirer's own account* — that day is **Saturday the first** (Ch127 and Ch128) and the third is a Monday. **Both are corrected**, and the forward beat now points at days the batch actually pays.
11. **THIRD-COLUMN COUNTS DID NOT RECONCILE.** The column's five entries are the twenty-eighth of May, the eighth of June, the eighteenth of June, the twenty-sixth of June and the fourth of July, every one of them after the boy went off the stone on the fifteenth of May and every one of them under a head of column written on the twenty-first. **Chapter 123's "four entries under that line since" is Three**, which is what the clerk could see on the twenty-first. **Chapter 125's "the fourth time since the second of May and the third time since the boy went off the stone" is the fourth time on both counts, and its "the three figures it has got" is the four figures.** This file's § 3 said *five third-column entries of the batch* and then **listed six dates, the first of which, the twenty-second of May, is neither an entry nor a real date**; that bullet is rewritten with the five real entries and the note that three of them are in this batch. `state/open-threads.md` carried the same miscount and is corrected.
12. **Chapter 130 asserted a use of the hand lead's line the chapters do not support.** It listed the tenth of June, the twenty-fourth, the first of July **and the third of July** — the third is the cistern, the crown and the handbell, and nothing is cast — **and omitted the second of June, the day the four casts that Chapter 128 measures the first-of-July set against were actually made with that line (Chapter 114).** **The list is now the second of June, the tenth, the twenty-fourth and the first of July.** This file's *Labor* line said *used four times in the batch*, which inherited the gap: **it is four times since the second of June and three times in this batch, and nothing was cast with it on the third of July.** `state/current.md` L76 is corrected with it.
13. **Chapter 128 L111 read *no boat of this office of its own*.** It is **this office's own**, as in every other chapter.
14. **"Ashtonide" was a misspelling of the canonical "Ashtide" in six places across four chapters,** including twice in narration and an unedited self-correction artifact in Ollett's own mouth on the first page of Chapter 124. **All six are corrected and the artifact is gone.**
15. **The queued prompt for Chapters 131–140 stated a cistern practice the written batch breaks.** `workspace/volume-03/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` L119 said *the yard does not walk a Thursday and a Monday in the same week*, and the batch walks the twenty-sixth and twenty-ninth of June and the third and sixth of July; it also said *charged eight times in three weeks*, which is wrong twice, because five of the eight are in this batch and 30 May → 6 July is five weeks. **The prompt now says there is no such rule, names the one reason the batch does give for skipping a rota day — the gate takes four hours with three men and one of the three unpaid for five pay periods, from Chapter 121 — and tells a Movement 4 chapter that skips a rota day to give that reason or a better one on the page.** The same span and count are corrected in this file's § 1, § 5 and § 6 item 7, and in `state/open-threads.md`. **The prompt is this phase's own handoff and was repaired rather than left to mislead the next ten chapters.**
16. **`state/phase-ledger.json` IS A HUNDRED AND THIRTY CHAPTERS OUT OF DATE AND IT WAS NOT EDITED, BECAUSE IT IS THE CONTROLLER'S FILE.** It reads `"currentPhase": "phase-000-bootstrap"`, `"status": "running"`, and holds two phases, the second of them `volume-01-batch-0001`, so a selector reading it as it stands would rewrite ten completed chapters. **This is the one open gate item on the Batch 0003 review and it is FLAGGED, not fixed: a writer or a repair phase may not edit the ledger, and no prompt, volume file or batch file was created, deleted or renamed to compensate for it.** `state/current.md` L30 now says a hundred and forty chapters rather than a hundred and ten.

### Prose and craft items, applied

- **ALL TEN CHAPTERS CLOSED ON THE SAME SET-PIECE.** The read-out at the head of the dock, followed by a 200–400-word inventory in the same order, ran in Ch121–130 without exception. **The ritual stands — it is the yard's own practice and it is load-bearing, and it still runs once in all ten chapters — but five chapters no longer close on its full same-order inventory.** **Chapter 129** had the read-out staged **twice**, at about seven and about six, with overlapping content; the seven o'clock staging is gone and the third-column entry now stands on its own. **Chapter 126's read-out repeated Chapter 125's ninth-seam-and-list sentence almost word for word**; it is cut to the money and the debts and the chapter now ends on the letter coming up the coast. **Chapter 123's read-out repeated Chapter 122's almost item for item, two days apart**, and the thirty marks refused on the fourteenth are cut out of it because the woman who refused them is owed an undiminished figure in Ch122 and again in Ch124 and a third telling in a ledger is not a cost, it is a habit; **the chapter's list is now short and it ends on the chart.** **Chapter 128's read-out ended on a 200-word inventory of the lead plug** and is closed, and the chapter now ends on the shed being locked and on two forward beats — Monday's bare wall and the count of four going into the first column on the eleventh. **Chapter 130's read-out was seventeen paragraphs, the longest in the batch, and it now runs to six**, so the volume batch's last chapter ends on the two forward paragraphs it was written to end on. Chapters 121, 122, 123, 124, 125 and 127 keep the read-out and keep their existing forward beats.
- **Chapter 129's hook was under-owed.** The count of four going into the first column in the shipwright's hand was a bare date, and it was the only forward beat in the batch that the next chapter has to pay. **It now names the day, says why that day — a wage day and a market day, the only day in the next week a man can ask her a question and get it read back — and the state files say so and say that Chapter 133 owes it.**
- **The reviewer's other craft notes are confirmed and not changed:** the pressure types do alternate well, the three one-scene chapters are the strongest writing in the batch, the prose is clean and concrete and free of ornament, sentence rhythm varies, and there are no lore dumps.

### 8. Proposed canon changes

**None applied by this repair. One canon fact was corrected at source and is now binding: *the man who holds the figure on the bar of gravel is Harl Dace, forty-eight, master of the *Grey Widow*, and he has a slate and no bad wheel of his own in this harbor*, and the unnamed man of fifty-one with the bad wheel is a different man who is not to be interviewed, hired or put on a lead.** Nothing else in the batch's canon moved: the turn stands, the three reserved beats stand, the channel is the writ, Captain Holt's posting is still open, the one naming of the condition is still unspent and still owed to Movement 4, the end of the volume is untouched, and **no new antagonist, institution or final enemy was introduced.**
