## Batch 0004 as written (Chapters 131–140), Volume 03 — binding on Chapter 141 onward

**Movement 4, what the glass is joined to. THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT. A PHYSICAL JOIN FOUND BY LABOR, IN WEATHER, WITH A LEAD AND A SLATE; THE FOURTH RETURN; A CONSERVATOR WHO PROVES SIX THINGS AND CANNOT GIVE A SEVENTH; A SEAM READ FOR THE FIRST TIME AND A PUBLIC STATEMENT THAT IS WRONG IN THREE PARTS OUT OF FOUR AND IS DISPROVED BY FOULING; THE ONE NAMING OF THE CONDITION SPENT, IN CHAPTER 137; NINE PLACES OUT OF FOUR BOOKS IN A PRESS; THE YARD'S OWN RETURN NAILED UP ON A MARKET WALL; A SENTENCE SAID BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED IT THAT ENDS A CONSERVATOR'S TIME ON THE CREW; A REEVE WHO SPENDS AGAIN AND A THIRD LAY THAT LOSES FOUR DAYS; NOTHING IN A BAG; AND A BOAT WITH PEOPLE IN HER OFF THE OUTER MARKS THAT THIS PARTY IS NOT GOING TO LOOK AT.** The chapters are the authority on what happened. Everything in this section is taken from Chapters 131–140 and from nothing else, and **the whole batch is 30,890 words** (Ch131 2,721 · Ch132 3,211 · Ch133 2,402 · Ch134 3,506 · Ch135 2,636 · Ch136 3,335 · Ch137 3,079 · Ch138 3,581 · Ch139 2,310 · Ch140 3,854). **Every chapter is above the 2,200 floor; six are inside the 2,200–3,200 guideline and five are past it — 140 by 654, 138 by 376, 134 by 300, 136 by 147 and 132 by 10 — and all five ran because a cost could not be dropped, 132's ten being the cost of a required arithmetic repair that struck the pot of air out of that day's account and had to write the reason on the page.**

### 1. The calendar the batch actually ran on

**`outline/volume-03.md` lock L1's grid, unchanged, and the chapters ran on it without an exception.** 5 July a **Wednesday**, 6 a Thursday, 7 a **Friday**, 8 a **Saturday**, 9 a Sunday, 10 a **Monday**, 11 a **Tuesday** and a wage week, 12 a **Wednesday**, 13 a **Thursday**, 14 a **Friday**, 15 a **Saturday**, 16 a Sunday, 17 a **Monday**, 18 a **Tuesday** and a wage week, 19 a **Wednesday**, 20 a **Thursday**, 21 a **Friday**, 22 a **Saturday**, 23 a Sunday, 24 a **Monday**, 25 a **Tuesday** and a wage week, 26 a **Wednesday**. **The Mondays in this batch are the tenth, the seventeenth and the twenty-fourth. The Thursdays are the thirteenth and the twentieth. The wage Tuesdays are the eleventh, the eighteenth and the twenty-fifth. NO WEEKDAY WAS CORRECTED AND NO CHAPTER STATES A YEAR.**

**The ten chapters carry their own dates and no day is in a chapter twice, and no man is paid for two days in one chapter:** Ch131 the 7th and 8th; Ch132 the 9th and 10th; Ch133 the 11th and 12th; Ch134 the 13th and 14th; Ch135 the 15th and 16th; Ch136 the 17th and 18th; Ch137 the 19th and 20th; Ch138 the 21st and 22nd; Ch139 the 23rd and 24th; Ch140 the 25th and 26th. **Dated days referred to inside the batch from another chapter's day are forward references only and are not days narrated twice:** Tuesday the eleventh (the next wage, in Ch132, 134, 135, 138, 139, 140), Monday the seventeenth (a day booked by Edric Pringle, in Ch133, 135, 136), Tuesday the eighteenth (a day named ahead, in Ch133, 135, 136, 137), Thursday the twentieth (the third reading day, in Ch136), Tuesday the twenty-fifth (the next wage, in Ch133, 137, 138, 139), Sunday the sixteenth (the last read-out of Mara Venn's, in Ch138), **and Saturday the twenty-second (the day the sheet goes up, named exactly and not as *Saturday week*, in Ch133 and Ch138).**

**The top of the flood at the second seam's own face, as the chapters now stand, moves FORWARD through the batch and is binding:** about eleven in the morning on the eighth (Ch131, the hand lead's line and the snag), **half past twelve on the fifteenth** (Ch135, Mara Venn in the boat at the crevice), **twenty past one on the seventeenth** (Ch136, where the tide is not good at that face until that hour and the boat comes off at eleven, two hours and a quarter before it), **ten past two on the nineteenth** (Ch137, the third and fourth readings), **and half past two on the twentieth** (Ch137, the fifth and sixth readings, and the hour the yard enters against its own account for not walking the cistern gate). **THE HOURS AS FIRST WRITTEN RAN BACKWARDS — HALF PAST TWELVE, THEN HALF PAST THREE, THEN HALF PAST TWO, THEN HALF PAST TWO — AND THAT ORDER IS CORRECTED AT SOURCE IN CHAPTERS 136 AND 137. The tide at that face is not good again after the nineteenth until the middle of the next day, and there are six readings and one top of a flood a day, so the three reading days are the eighteenth, the nineteenth and the twentieth and the third is the twentieth.**

**The hearing room's slate wall.** It is wiped every Monday at eight whether there is a sitting or not, and it came down bare the first time on **Monday the eighth of May**, the morning after Ors Kest put the last figure on it. **It comes down on Monday the tenth, Monday the seventeenth and Monday the twenty-fourth of July, and on all three it comes down bare, nothing is put up in its place, there is no sitting, and the clerk keeps his own page and does not mention it.** That is **ten Mondays on the tenth, eleven on the seventeenth and twelve on the twenty-fourth**, on the count the earlier chapters establish. **THE COUNTER WAS RESTARTED IN CHAPTER 122 AT *THREE* AND CARRIED FORWARD BY CHAPTERS 125 AND 129, AND THAT RESTART WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED AT SOURCE IN ALL THREE, because the run from the eighth of May is unbroken: the eighth, fifteenth, twenty-second and twenty-ninth of May are one, two, three and four, the fifth and twelfth of June are five and six, the nineteenth and twenty-sixth of June are seven and eight, the third, tenth, seventeenth and twenty-fourth of July are nine, ten, eleven and twelve. Chapter 129 is retitled *Nine Mondays, and a Wage*. The only other figure in this run is the clerk's own in Chapter 112, who counts the Mondays of May and says five on the twenty-ninth, and he is counting from the first and is not wrong.**

**THE HEARING ROOM IS USED NOT AT ALL IN THIS BATCH. The market wall is used by this party ONCE, on SATURDAY THE TWENTY-SECOND OF JULY, in daylight, in a market, on no Monday and on no hearing-room day, and the sheet that goes up on it is the yard's own fourth return.** **THE PARTY'S OWN TWO LINES THAT HAD THE MATTER GOING UP ON A MONDAY ARE CORRECTED: Nessa Pike's objection and Calder Marr's answer both go onto the paper on the same Saturday morning, on the same nail, and Chapter 138 and Chapter 139 now say so.** **The one Monday left in the batch is another crew's, and it is spoken and not narrated: Perry Ollett says in front of the wall that he will be standing there on Monday the twenty-seventh with a better sheet, and no chapter of this batch shows him do it, and it is not this party's use of that wall.** No two institutional days in this batch are the same day, and there is only one institutional day this party stages.

**The harbor authority's cistern rota falls on a Monday and a Thursday.** The rota days inside the window are **Monday the tenth, Thursday the thirteenth, Monday the seventeenth, Thursday the twentieth and Monday the twenty-fourth, and that is all of them; the next is Thursday the twenty-seventh and the batch ends on the twenty-sixth.** **THE YARD CHARGED IT ON THE TENTH, THE THIRTEENTH, THE TWENTIETH AND THE TWENTY-FOURTH, AND DID NOT WALK THE SEVENTEENTH, AND THE ONE REASON IT GIVES IS ON THE PAGE, IN THE YARD'S OWN HAND, ON THE MONDAY MORNING, and it is not the two-walks-in-a-week rule: *that the gate takes four hours with three men, and that on Monday the seventeenth instant this party was on the second seam's own face from seven in the morning until about two on a woman's own account, and that the party could not walk a four-hour gate and do a day's work at the same time with the same three men, and that the party has not walked that gate and has entered that it has not walked it.*** **THERE IS NO SUCH RULE AS A TWO-WALKS RULE AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH STATES ONE.** The twentieth is walked from ten until about two **and the entry gives the hour of the flood that day against the yard's own account**, the top of the flood at that face being at about half past two. That is **the ninth, tenth, eleventh and twelfth walks in this batch and twelve on that office's own page at no figure since the end of May**,and the cistern has not gone dry since the nineteenth of June. **THE CISTERN THREAD IS NOT REOPENED. A wharfinger said the word *town* out loud in a market on the sixth of July and has not taken it back, and this batch refers to that once, in a man's mouth, on the twenty-sixth, and does not use it for anything and does not fix the harbor's poverty for anything.**

### 2. The money chain, mark by mark

**NOTHING in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, and nothing in a tin, on Wednesday the twenty-sixth of July — against FOUR marks in the same bag on Thursday the sixth. No office in this harbor has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May and none is going to.**

| Date | In | Out | Bag | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thu 6 Jul | — | — | **4** | 31 |
| Fri 7 – Sat 8 Jul | — | **2**, a pot of air bought of this harbor authority at this yard's own two-mark rate, filled at that office's compressor and carried on that office's own hose, and it is the whole of the air in this harbor. **Saturday: a hand lead's line snags at about four fathoms in the crevice at the foot of a hull lying against the second seam's own face and a piece of metal comes up. Nothing is paid for and nothing is charged to anybody** | **2** | 31 |
| Sun 9 Jul | — | nothing moves. **The piece of metal goes on a cloth on a bench and is not worked on. The two of them go up on the mole's crown at four in the afternoon and stand on it, and find out that the whole of this month's business is nine feet under their boots** | **2** | 31 |
| Mon 10 – Tue 11 Jul | **4**, a day at the second seam on a hirer's own account at this yard's own rate: gross thirteen, the eighth two, the share two, a hired hand three, the victuals one and rope one, all three of the last paid at the mole by the man who hired the day, that being **nine** out of thirteen and the yard's being four. **THE POT OF AIR IS NOT CHARGED TO THIS DAY AND IS NOT IN THIS FIGURE, because it went out of the bag on Friday the seventh and was spent in the water on the Saturday; Chapter 132 and Chapter 136 now both say so on the page.** **Both men in the water are hired and neither is of this party. A lump about the size of two fists and a chip of green glass about the length of a thumbnail come up on the ring and in the tallow of the lead, and that is a finding of worked glass, and the day of the finding is not counted and a return falls due on Friday the fourteenth** | **6** | 31 |

Three lines go to a printer in Veyr in the ordinary post at no cost. Edric Pringle books a day for the seventeenth and is owed eight shillings and tenpence and says so** | **2** | 31 |

**THE FULL CHAIN IN THE BAG: 4, 2, 6, 2, 7, 1, 4, 0.** The note is **thirty-one throughout and is not re-written, and the three counts of its weeks — the creditor's twenty-two, the yard's thirteen, and eleven as the last figure ever written in that page — do not move and are not moved by anything a yard does with a pencil.** The tin is empty and has been since the twenty-eighth of April.

**THE WHOLE OF THE MONEY THAT HAS COME INTO THIS YARD SINCE THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF JUNE IS TWENTY-TWO MARKS, AND THE EIGHT MARKS THAT WERE IN THE BAG ON THE FIFTEENTH OF JUNE WENT OUT IN JUNE AS THREE WEEKS OF A WAGE AND A POT OF AIR, AND THIS MONTH FOUR WEEKS OF A WAGE AND TWO POTS OF AIR WENT OUT, AND THERE IS NOTHING IN THE BAG, AND THAT FIGURE IS ON THE PAGE IN THE YARD'S OWN HAND AND IS PUT IN THE FIRST COLUMN WITH A REASON AGAINST IT, and the reason is that in April this yard was living on a creditor's page and in July it is living on a wage, and a wage is not a page, a wage is a man's four marks for six days' work he has not been told about and that has not been carried to him, and there is no man in this harbor who can be asked about it because he is on a herring boat.**

**The wage column, and it is the spine of the batch: eleven lines and forty-four marks at the end of Batch 0003, and FOURTEEN LINES AND FIFTY-SIX MARKS at the end of this one, the fifth of March the oldest line in it and staying where it is.** The three weeks of the eleventh, eighteenth and twenty-fifth of July are four marks apiece. **Each is paid in Calder Marr's own left hand because there is nobody else in that shed who writes; each goes into an envelope on the bench with the week written on the outside of it in the same hand; the envelopes are in the bench chest and not among the five rolled in oilcloth; there are nine of them now, one a week since the fourth of July; and none of the nine has been anywhere, and the reason is entered, and the reason is that a wage is not a reason to go to a door.**

**Debts, all standing, all unpaid, none reduced, and none paid in this batch.**

- **A compressor's gland**, at the creditor's own figure of five marks, fitted to the yard's own pump since Monday the nineteenth of May, **is still on the page at thirty-one and is not paid and the page has not been re-written, and the yard says out loud in four of the ten chapters that the gland has made no difference whatever because the thing after the gland is the hose, and that there is not one in this harbor longer than about nine fathoms.** Two pots of air were bought this month at two marks apiece and they are the whole of the air in this harbor.
- **Eight marks and a day of a boat to the harbor's bellwright, paid on the twenty-ninth of April, and eight marks and a day of a boat owing to the same man since the twenty-third of May** — entered as owing and not as paid, unpaid throughout the batch, not argued away, **NOT PAID IN THIS BATCH**, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed.
- **Forty marks to Bevin Rook** stand whole, unpaid, not reduced, not asked to be reduced, and no mark of it moved. A wage of four a week goes on running into an envelope and the boy is not on the stone. **The three nots of the eighth of June are recited in this batch and none of them is broken: nobody writes to him, nobody goes to see him, and nobody asks anybody who has seen him what he said. HE IS NOT IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH.**
- **Forty marks to Mara Venn**, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, not reachable by the writ, and **not one mark of it paid out of anything that has come into this yard since the eighth of May.** **On Sunday the sixteenth she made the shipwright read the line about it twice at the head of a dock and put the figure on it herself: nine months, and she asked for it to be said out loud in nine people's hearing, and said in a market on Saturday the twenty-second that he is a man who has run out of money and a man who has not and that she is not taking either half back.** She is not an assistant, is not asked to be useful, and is not going to be.
- **Eight shillings and tenpence to each of two women — Sella Brigg and Edric Pringle — undiminished and unpaid and not paid in this batch**, and **in no sentence together in this yard's book, which a woman of the second lay checked on Thursday the twenty-third and came up the hill to say was right.** About four hundred feet of Sella Brigg's own warp is on the bottom of this harbor, **a hundred and fifty-five days at the tenth of July and a hundred and seventy-one at the twenty-sixth, and a crew of Ashtide takes it on FRIDAY the twenty-eighth of July for twenty-six marks and a day — the weekday is corrected in place in Batch 0005's pass, because Chapters 138, 139 and 140 all establish that the twenty-sixth of July is a Wednesday, and the chapters are right and this line was wrong — and this yard told her to go to him and is not going in the way of it, and on the seventh of July she said out loud in a market that the yard's own sheet is not on that wall any more and there is a man from Ashtide over the top of it with four marks at the head of it.** **Edric Pringle hired a day at this yard's own rate on her own account and paid twelve of seventeen at the mole, and the two debts are never in one sentence with a warp in it and the two women are never merged.**
- **Thirty marks** offered on Wednesday the fourteenth of June and refused, and nothing in this batch changes it.
- **Nineteen winters of dues** on Elias Voke, disputed and unpaid, **now standing in a naval file since Thursday the twenty-second of June and in four lines of a letter in that man's own coat**, and **in this batch that lettering appears as the reason the fourth question the man in a city asked cannot be asked in Morrow Quay, and as the reason there is no disputant to seat.**
- **Twenty marks of unsold iron and four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in three pieces with a bell-metal gun-port among them**, in the same heap behind the shed, unsold, unweighed, unpriced, not offered to anybody, **not looked at in this batch**, and the man who would know bell metal at a glance is of another boat and is not asked and is not in a room.
- **A hand that will last a week costs four marks a week**, entered in the first column on Tuesday the eleventh in the shipwright's own hand, **and it is a wage and is not a fee and is not a boat's share and is in no column in this book, and nobody in this harbor is hiring at that figure to read a slate back, and the last one who was was twenty-two and is on a herring boat.**

### 3. Every document and instrument created in this batch

- **THE FOURTH RETURN, made Friday the fourteenth of July, ON THE DAY, out of a finding of Monday the tenth.** One sheet in the harbor authority's own paper in Calder Marr's own left hand, unshortened, with the place, the hour, the keeper and the limits against the head of it, and a copy for the harbor authority's ledger in the clerk's own hand, and the clerk's own page with the hour it was written and not the hour it was done. It went up the coast at about eight in a sealed jacket. **THE COUNTING RULE IS ON THE PAGE AND SPOKEN: the day of the finding is not counted and the return fell due on the fourth day after it, and the party entered the date on the Monday evening and entered that it was not going to try to move it and entered that nobody in that yard was to tell anybody in a market that a date is not a date.** Contents: the place (the second seam's own face, landward of the third seam's mark, ground no party claims and ground the first clause of the notice does not reach); the hour; the keeper; the limits, being the holder's own seventeen minutes with the six read out; **the first limb of the clause catches the chip by itself and the party has not needed the second limb and does not know that it would**; the lump, about the size of two fists, not stone, not oakum, not lime, not pitch, with a skin a quarter of an inch thick; the chip, green through the body and cloudy, thicker at one end by about a sixteenth, sea-worn to a frosted face, with a scalloped edge of about three in about half an inch, all the same hand, none running out of a point; **the disavowal line in full** (not the lump of the sixth of May, not the shard of the tenth of June, not the worked panel given back on the seventh of April, not a plate, not the ferry plate, not the plate at the fifth seam, not the lead plug, not a mark on a stone, not the compartment, whose finding stands in three steps and is not restated, not the run under this town, not the coast road, not a bar of gravel, not a cairn of ballast stones, not a stem on the Nine Foot, **and not a mark of nine joined lines, and the party is not going to write one, and if nine of anything is a row of nine then a man can count a row of nine and this party has counted one and is not going to count it twice**), and that neither object is in any office's book and neither carries a seal and neither has a number anywhere, **and that nobody is to write any two of those things together in any book in this harbor, and that this party has entered that instruction against itself and not against anybody else**; and the second column, four lines: the material of the lump, the material of the piece of metal, **what the packing is in and who put it there, which is a line of its own with the reason that no man on a mole can say whose hand cut anything and that this party declines to give a name and declines to give a trade, and that anybody who comes to this sheet in four years is going to want a name and is not going to get one from this party**, what is in the chip, which goes nowhere at all, and the man who could not tell which of his two hands was on the line on the eighth of July.
- **THE FINDING OF MONDAY THE TENTH OF JULY, and the dated lines that there is no fifth.** **THERE ARE FOUR RETURNS AND THERE IS NO FIFTH. Dated lines in the yard's own book for the seventeenth, eighteenth, nineteenth, twentieth and twenty-fourth of July say that no finding of worked glass was made and nothing was raised and no return goes up on any of those days, and that line is a fact about that book and is not in any office's book and is not sent to anybody.**
- **THE COUNT OF FOUR, in the first column, in the shipwright's own hand, on Tuesday the eleventh of July**, with the four dates and the four weathers, and **no name against it**, and her reason for the absence of a name, entered: a page in this book that says what a man of this yard can and cannot hear will be read by somebody in Veyr who is looking for exactly that, and she is not going to hand him the key by writing a name on a line about a bell. **This is the forward beat Chapters 129 and 130 owed to Chapter 133 and it is paid.**
- **THE NAMED LINE OF THE TWENTIETH OF JULY, in the holder's own left hand, in the second column, and it is the one naming of the condition Volume 03 is allowed.** See § 4.
- **THE FINDING OF MARA VENN OF THE FIFTEENTH AND SIXTEENTH OF JULY, entered in the yard's own book UNSIGNED AT HER OWN REQUEST, with the reason on the page in her own hand**, being that a finding of no office carries no seal and cannot be produced on demand by anybody, and a signature would make it a document, and this is not a document, it is what a woman with a lens and a pair of calipers of her own could see in four days. **She will not be asked to sign it and the answer is no.**
- **HER OBJECTION, entered by Mara Venn in her own hand, unsigned, on the twenty-second of July, in a market, in about forty people's hearing, and going up on the market wall on the same Saturday the twenty-second, on the same nail as the return**: that the party should publish the finding and should not publish the books, that she thinks the party is wrong to do both, that she has said so in a market and is not going to say it again in a shed, and that she is not going to be asked to sign it. **And the answer on the wall beside it, in fifteen words, in the holder's mouth and the shipwright's hand: both, and I will not be able to tell you which was right in October.**
- **THE SHEET THE YARD NAILED UP ON THE MARKET WALL ON SATURDAY THE TWENTY-SECOND OF JULY: its own fourth return, over the rival's printed sheet, with no scale and no price on it and nowhere on it to write a letter to.** **And the party's own hand's argument for it, said out loud on the mole's steps in about nine people: that the only thing that makes a wall worth reading is somebody else being able to be asked a question about it, and that a man in Veyr with a press who has already written once is the only person in nine hundred miles who is going to be obliged to look at a paper of this yard's.** **THE SHEET CANNOT BE UNNAILED AND WAS NOT GOING TO BE, AND THE RIVAL SAID SO OUT LOUD AND SAID HE WILL BE STANDING IN FRONT OF IT ON MONDAY WITH A BETTER SHEET AND IS NOT GOING TO PRETEND HE WOULD NOT BE.**
- **THE PAGE OF THE FIFTH OF JULY, the four things the party will not permit and the one thing it has decided, STANDS AND IS NOT ALTERED AND IS NOT REPEATED, and its third item — the channel, the writ, a man in a coat of any office arriving as a stranger with a paper about the second season, to be read, answered on the day it comes, and not treated as a friend or an enemy — is recited in Chapter 140 in one sentence and is the reason the party does not send anybody to look at a boat off the outer marks.** Its fourth item — no posting, appointment, commission, transport or instruction out of any city has put anybody past the harbor line, the outer marks and the bar of four hundred yards off them stand where the writ says they are, and the party is not going past them this season and has not tried and is not going to — **is what the party puts in the market in Chapter 140, word for word, when a man of the third lay gives it a boat's name.**
- **THE THREE LINES TO J. HESKETH OF VEYR, written on Wednesday the twelfth of July and sent in the ordinary post at no cost**, telling him that a return goes up this coast on Friday, that it states a fact and not a conclusion, that it is not for sale, that there is no price on it and it is not a sheet, and that if he prints it he is to print what it says and not what it is about. **He is told in advance and cannot be stopped and will print it, and the party knows that and has entered that it knows it.**
- **THE ENTRY OF THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF JULY IN THE HARBOR AUTHORITY'S OWN BOOK, in the officer's own hand and at his own cost**: that the footing of the mole from the third seam to the twelfth is to be examined, at the cost of that office, on the second of August, and that the moorings of the third lay are to be closed for the four days of it, and that the repair account of that office has been empty since the middle of February and the money is not coming, and that the entry is made nevertheless. **It is not a claim window, not an acknowledgement, not a ninth of them, not a work permit, not a charter and not a renewal, and it puts no person, a boat or a lead of this yard inside the first clause of the notice, and the party entered all of that in the first column on the same afternoon.**
- **THE TIDE-GANG'S STEP BOOK, THE WIER-BOOK, THE POOR-BOOK AND THE BUILDER'S DAY-BOOK OF THE PORT OF ASHTIDE, read in the market office press on Friday the twenty-first of July, and the nine places read out of them and laid out on the shed floor and entered in the first column in the holder's own left hand and read back by the shipwright with nobody else in the shed.** **No book was taken out of the press, nothing was copied, no fee was charged and nobody asked anybody's permission, and the party has entered that it had never been in that room in five months and that the reason is that it spent five months putting paper on walls.**
- **THE LINE OF WHAT THE PARTY DECLINES TO WRITE, in the second column of the twenty-first of July, in full: that the party will not join the two halves of its own finding, and that the account is about the sea, and that the finding is about the glass, and that neither of them is a reason to believe the other, and that a man in a city who puts them in one sentence is doing the thing this party did on the third of May, which is naming a road, and this party is not going to do it twice.**

**There is no ninth acknowledgement in this batch, no fifth name on the writ, no renewal, and no return made other than the fourth. The only days of this batch on which a person of this party was in the water are the eighth, the seventeenth, the eighteenth, the nineteenth and the twentieth, all of them at the second seam's own face landward of the third seam's mark, all of them with the six read out in order at the boat before anybody was wet, all of them with the tender being the shipwright, who has never been in the water in her life, and all of them with the clerk not present and the absence entered rather than filled in.**

### 4. The bodies and the crew

- **Calder Marr**, 32, line diver with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice and not used since the eighth of December, and the lift at the end of a childhood call gone and not restorable. **He goes in the water on the eighth, the seventeenth, the eighteenth, the nineteenth and the twentieth of July, inside limits of nineteen, seventeen and seventeen minutes that he set himself at the boat and that nobody raised, with a seated named weight and an independent line and a cutoff that is hers and the word is *Enough*. HE ENTERS NOTHING. HE TAKES ONE STAGE AND IT IS THE FAULTREADER STAGE, AND IT IS TAKEN LATE, BADLY AND AT A PRICE, AND IT IS NOT NAMED AS A STAGE BY ANYBODY AND IS NOT A RANK AND PUTS NO CREW BEHIND A MAN.**
  - **WHAT HE GAINS, on his second call down on Monday the seventeenth, with his hand flat on the cut where the two ends of the joint meet: he perceives that the two cuts in that joint are of different ages, and that the difference is not a difference of days or of years but the difference between a cut made once and a cut made on top of a cut that was there first.** It comes into his hand the way a change of angle comes into a hand, with no picture and no sound and nothing arriving before its source. **That is the whole of the perception. It identifies difference and not truth: it tells him there were mendings under mendings and it tells him nothing whatever about when, in what order, or by whose hand.**
  - **AND WHAT HE DOES WITH IT IS THE FAULT.** Sitting at four fathoms with his hand on the wood, **he made the old mending into a fact — not the mending, the age of it, the number of mendings and the hand that made them — and he supplied all three, and the hand on the wood told him none of them.**
  - **THE WRONG, on Tuesday the eighteenth at about half past three in the afternoon, at the head of this dock, in a market, in about eleven people's hearing, and he said it in the open air to nobody in particular:** that the six joints are a chain, that they run the length of this mole, that the spacing is the same at every one of them, that they were made once, in one time, by one set of hands, from the north end down, and that whatever they are for it is not to keep two boats off the bottom in a blow. **It is wrong in three parts out of four and the shipwright has entered that in the second column.**
  - **AND IT IS DISPROVED BY LABOR, NOT BY A SEAM AND NOT BY AN ARGUMENT.** Fouling goes on in order. The first two readings, taken on the eighteenth at the top of the flood with a bar laid flat and a scraper and a rule and the same hand, are **a quarter of an inch and a quarter of an inch, and they are the two at the root end of the crevice, which is the north end, and he had said the newest was the first.** The third and fourth, taken on the nineteenth, are **three inches and a quarter each, and they are sixty-eight and a hundred and thirty-seven feet south of the second of the thin ones, and the second is the inner of the two.** The fifth, taken on the twentieth, is **nine inches, and it is the fifth of the six and the second from the seaward end, and the packing in it is a different color to a depth of about a quarter of an inch with the same color under it.** The sixth, at the seaward end, is three inches and a quarter. **So the two north joints are the newest, the seaward end is not the first, the order he gave in a market is backwards, and a quarter and three inches and a quarter is not a morning but at least three different days in three different seasons, and the party cannot say how many more and is not going to arrange three thicknesses into a figure.**
  - **WHAT IT COST, in money:** the day of Monday the seventeenth was charged whole at seventeen gross with twelve of it gone at the mole, **and the boat came off that face at eleven, two hours and a quarter before the flood, because the man in the water could not say whether the basket had gone up four times or three, and he said three and the shipwright said four, twice. The crevice was half cleared and there is one top of the flood a day and there were two more floods.**
  - **WHAT IT COST IN PEOPLE, on the page, three faces, none of them forgiven:** **Tam Slee of the *Grey Widow*, the fourth name on the writ, asked in person on the first of May, not of this party, not of any association, not hired and not paid, who was in the water on the eighteenth, the nineteenth and the twentieth on his own account, and who said in a boat on the Tuesday that he went down because he was told to and is not doing it because of Tuesday, and asked that the difference be kept as a difference and not put in with the rest of it, and who was awake on a net loft floor on the twentieth and said he was not going to be told a second time that he went down on a party's word because he has not got anywhere else to be on a Friday; Edric Pringle of the second lay, who hired the day on the seventeenth and who came up the hill on the Wednesday evening and said the cause out loud — the tide was there at seven and the tide was there at two, the boat came off the face at eleven because the man in the water lost the count, and he is of the yard — and who would not be told it was the tide, and who said she was told a thing about the job and not about her money, and who is owed eight shillings and tenpence and did not ask; and Nessa Pike, who wrote the wrong down in her own hand in the second column with a reason against the line and read it back, and who did not stop him, and gave the reason she did not stop him, which is that a man saying his own trade in the open air is not doing wrong, that he has been right in public four times this month, and that a shipwright who contradicts a man in a market is a person who has decided that a man's mouth is a thing to be managed.**
  - **THE AFTERTASTE, once, in the second column, on the twentieth: that on the eighteenth, nineteenth and twentieth, in the water and in a boat, in company, it was a bad day on all three, and that on the evening of the twentieth, in the shed, with the shipwright reading this column, ordinary talk in the room was going on as more than one talk at once, and he gave an answer to a question that had been put to him a quarter of a second after the answer that answered it, and the shipwright was in the room and heard both and could not say which of the two he meant, and has said so and is not going to put it any other way.**
  - **THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 03 IS ALLOWED WAS SPENT IN CHAPTER 137 AND NOWHERE ELSE.** On the evening of Thursday the twentieth of July, at about a quarter past nine, in the shed, with a man of the *Grey Widow* awake on a net loft floor and nobody pretending he was asleep, **he said the word *a tide-sickness* out loud for the first time in seven months, and it went into the second column of the yard's own book in his own left hand with a name in it, and there is a name in that line and there is no name in any of the four above it.** He said he got the word from and that he produced the word himself, out loud, in the *Grey Widow's* cockpit on the thirteenth of May, that it is not a cause, that no cause is written, and that he is not going to be asked to take it out and will not fight anybody who wants it out. **He broke his own line of the second of June, which says there is no name in it and there is not going to be one this year, and the reason against the line is on the page and is the best sentence he has said this volume: a man who can lose a quarter of a second out of an ordinary conversation in a shed is a man whose account of a seam under four fathoms of water is a thing that can be asked about, and the party has decided it can be asked about in this book, on this page, on this date, by anybody who comes up this hill, and the only difference between that and a man with a seal is that it can be argued with. THE COUNT OF THE FOUR ABOVE IT IS STILL FOUR AND THEY HAVE NO NAME IN THEM AND HAVE NOT BEEN STRUCK OUT AND A REQUEST TO STRIKE A LINE OUT OF A COLUMN IS REFUSED. NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 03 AFTER CHAPTER 137 MAY PUT THE WORD IN AGAINST HIM, AND THE FIFTH MOVEMENT HAS ONE NAMING LEFT IN THE WHOLE BOOK AND IT IS NOT THIS ONE.**
- **Nessa Pike**, 27. **She is right in this batch six times and nobody thanks her for any of them.** She writes the count of four in the first column in her own hand and refuses both names. She prices a hand at eight marks a week and says the reader thing once in a sentence that means the same thing and says it is in the first column. She wins the argument about the word *joint* with a better rule than the holder's. She does not stop a man from saying a false thing in a market and enters the reason. She reads a woman's sentence back without improving on it and reads a word about her partner's ear back without improving on that either. **And she makes the market-office door into a standing arrangement on Monday the twenty-fourth and enters that the party did not arrange it and would not dare to.**
- **Bevin Rook**, 22, **off the stone since Monday the fifteenth of May and not replaced. HE IS IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH.** He is not written to, not visited, and nobody is asked who has seen him what he said, and the three nots stand and are recited in Chapters 133, 134, 137 and 140. Forty marks stand whole; a wage runs into an envelope; nine envelopes are on a bench and none of them is anywhere. **THE BATCH DOES NOT RESOLVE IT, DOES NOT REPAIR IT, DOES NOT PAY IT OFF, AND MAY NOT.**
- **Corvin Sarn**, 50, the harbor's own bellwright, paid, teaching nobody, not of this party, not of the Free Keel. **HE IS NOT IN THIS BATCH AND HE IS NOT ASKED. HE IS NOT IN A SHED, NOT IN A BOAT, NOT IN A MARKET, NOT ON A MOLE AND NOT IN A ROOM. NO DIGIT OF WHAT HE SAID ON THE SECOND OF JULY IS PRINTED IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND NO FIGURE OF IT GOES INTO ANY COLUMN AND THE CHEST WAS NOT OPENED AND WAS NOT BROUGHT DOWN THE HILL.** **The undertaking of the second of July stands and is recited in the disavowal line of the return and in the yard's own book on the twenty-first of July: the number is written nowhere and is not going to be, the yard has undertaken not to ask about it again, and that undertaking binds this party and not the office and not the market and not the boy.** He is still owed eight marks and a day of a boat since the twenty-third of May and it is not paid and it is not argued.
- **Mara Venn**, 30, of no office, a trade and not an office. **She has been in this harbor since about the thirtieth of June, staying above the chandler's, and nobody in this yard knew and nobody asked and she said so at the top of the mole's steps before anybody spoke.** She priced herself at six marks and two days out loud before she did anything and set the conditions of her own finding in words she chose. **She proved six things and could not give a seventh, and the seventh was who.** She entered her finding unsigned with the reason on the page, and her objection to the party's publication unsigned with the reason on the page, and both are on the market wall. **She refused to be useful again, in her own words, before anybody asked her, on Sunday the twenty-third, and that ends her time on the crew. SHE DID NOT BREAK WITH THE REGISTRY AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH FUSES THE TWO THINGS; HER BREAK WITH THE REGISTRY IS VOLUME 4 AND IS A DIFFERENT EVENT. SHE DID NOT LEAVE THIS HARBOR AND IS IN CHAPTER 140. She took her own brass calipers off the bench and the reason is on the page.** She refused the evidence when it was handed to her and said it was worth nothing to her and gave the reason, and the disagreement is on the page whole and unrepaired and is not settled by anything anybody handed to anybody. She is not an assistant, was never one, is not asked to be useful, is not to be paid, is not thanked, and the word *useful* is not to be written against her in this yard's book. Her forty marks are unpaid and going on being unpaid and she is the one who put the figure of nine months on the page herself.
- **Edric Pringle**, 54, of the *Anna Beth*, second lay, of no office. **She hires a day at this yard's own rate on her own account on Monday the seventeenth — gross seventeen, the yard's five — and pays the victuals and two hired hands at the mole, and she says the cause out loud before the figure, and she checks this yard's book on Thursday the twenty-third for the two-debts rule and comes up and says it is right, and she comes up on the Wednesday evening and says the tide was there at seven and at two and the boat came off the face because the man in the water lost the count.** She is owed eight shillings and tenpence, undiminished, unpaid, not paid in this batch, **and no warp figure is given for her anywhere in this batch and the two debts are never in one sentence.**
- **Sella Brigg**, of the third lay. **On Friday the seventh she is on the boards with a figure written on the back of a slate in chalk and she is not shy about it: twenty-six marks and a day and the twenty-eighth, because that is when a man with a compressor can get his boat out past the north wall. She has not told him about the eighth of March because that is between her and her own rope. And she says the other thing: yours is not on this wall any more and there is a man from Ashtide over the top of it with four marks at the head of it — and she says it is not to be cruel, and she has read the yard's own sheet twice this week.** She is owed eight shillings and tenpence undiminished, is owed about four hundred feet of her own warp on the bottom of this harbor, **and nothing is raised and nothing is sold in this batch.**
- **Perry Ollett**, 39, master of the *Sallow Reach*, a crew of Ashtide, six men, a compressor of her own, forty fathoms of her own hose, **not a villain and not turned into one.** He has taken a woman's warp for twenty-six marks and a day and the yard told her to go to him. He stands in front of the yard's own fourth return on a market wall on the twenty-second and reads it three times and says it is the only paper on that wall anybody in a city is obliged to look at, **and says he will be standing there on Monday with a better sheet and is not going to pretend he would not be, and the yard says: then stand there, that is the arrangement and it has been the arrangement since the ninth of November.** He is not given an office, a ship, a uniform or a title, explains nothing, threatens nobody, and does not use the word the yard refused.
- **Elias Voke**, 51, of the *Anna Ray*, nineteen winters. **He is not in a room in this batch and does not speak in it.** His nineteen winters are in this batch's four returns and in what the clerk said on the steps of the fourteenth of July, and they are the reason there is no disputant to seat and the only reason that anybody cannot take the thing out of that mole, and the yard says out loud that in four years that will be a reeve's price and not a party's.
- **Ors Kest**, 44. **HE SPENDS AUTHORITY AGAIN IN THIS MOVEMENT AND THE PRICE LANDS ON SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT THE YARD.** On Wednesday the twenty-sixth of July he enters, in his own book and at his own cost, that the mole's footing from the third to the twelfth seam is to be examined on the second of August and that the third lay's moorings are closed for the four days of it, and **the repair account of his office has been empty since the middle of February and the money is not coming and the entry is made nevertheless, and the reason is on the face of it in his own hand: an office whose instruments nobody contests can enter an instrument with nothing behind it, and that is worse than no instrument, and this office has an end of the first of September on the ninth seam's mooring and has had nothing else entered in five months that a person in this harbor can use.** The price is four days of a lay in the best of the season, and a man of the third lay said the figure out loud in a market in about forty people, and Mara Venn said what a lay's moorings are in August at no figure. **NEITHER MAN GETS WHAT HE WANTS. The reeve does not learn one thing about the plate and says thirteen words on the mole's steps: it is not and I will not tell you what it is about. The holder does not get a route and does not get a road and does not get a name for what is under his own mole.** He is not a friend and is not a hero and the volume does not pretend he was right about everything.
- **Tam Slee**, of the *Grey Widow*, the fourth name on the writ of the first of May, asked in person and content. **He is in the water on the eighteenth, the nineteenth and the twentieth on his own account, not hired, not paid, not of this party and not of any association, and he is one of the three faces the wrong lands on.**
- **Harl Dace**, 48, of the *Grey Widow*, **is not in this batch and is not asked.** The figure on the bar of gravel is his and the man in Veyr's question about it stands unanswered and the reason it stands is in the answer of the thirtieth of June.
- **The man of fifty-one with a bad wheel** is unnamed on the page, is not in this batch, is not in the water, is not hired, is not put on a lead, and is not merged with Coll Ferrow, whose bad wheel is the *Kestrel's Sister's* and is that boat's. **The two bad wheels are two men and were not in one sentence in this batch.**
- **Ewan Tristram**, the wharfinger of the second lay, **is not used. His thread closed inside Volume 03 and is referred to once, in a man's mouth on the twenty-sixth, and is not used to reopen anything and the harbor's poverty is not fixed for anything.**
- **J. Hesketh**, 44, a printer of Veyr. **He is told in advance, in three lines, in the ordinary post, at no cost, that a return is coming and that it is not for sale and that he is to print what it says and not what it is about. He is not a villain, is of no office, holds no seal, and is a man doing his trade who cannot be stopped.**
- **Alden Marr** is at the far bench in Chapter 133 on the Tuesday and the Wednesday and in Chapter 140 on the Tuesday, **is not asked for anything on any of the three days, asks nothing, and neither brother goes over to him on any of them.** No fragment, no report talk, no second story, no confession. The single first-column line that says so is in both chapters and can be checked by either brother on any day of a week by asking the other one.
- **The harbor authority's clerk** comes up the hill on Friday the fourteenth with a sealed jacket and does not go to the shed, and says for the seventh time that he cannot enter what a thumbnail of glass is, and writes on his own page that a party with no boy in it has put four returns up this coast in ten weeks and every one has been on the day except the first. **He then says, on the steps, unasked, in one go and without stopping anywhere in it, that the only reason nobody can dispute this finding is a reeve who sold a herring skipper's winter for a chart in a room in June. He is not an antagonist and did not become one.**
- **A boatwright of the second lay**, forty-one years in the harbor, comes down to the shed on the nineteenth because somebody he never met asked him at the top of the mole's steps, and gives the nine fastenings and the spacing with his own rule and says the spacing is not a spacing anybody on this coast works to, and that a man who says a hand's breadth is telling you about two men with two hands, and that men who lay out by hand are men working in a hurry or where there is no batten, **and says he has not said a word about where it came from and does not know who, and goes home to caulk a boat on Tuesday.** He is named in the book and the entry is in the first column because a man said it in a shed and has given his name and will not deny it. **He is an ordinary working man and is not a device and knows nothing and is not asked to know anything.**
- **A man of the third lay** says the number of the four days out loud in a market, and **a second man of the third lay says the name of a boat out loud in a street at half past six on the twenty-sixth, and neither of the two is a villain, neither is given an office, a ship, a uniform or a title, and neither is asked anything further.** **Captain Veyra Holt** is not in this batch, did not come up this hill, is not going to, and no face-to-face meeting occurs. **Magister Ilya Serr** does not appear in this batch. The first echo's witness is not in this batch.

### 5. The dated positions at the end of Chapter 140

- **The writ.** A provisional writ granted Monday the first of May, running to the first of March next, **not the Second Bell license, not a charter, renewing nothing, ground not widened and not to be widened.** The four names are unchanged, no fifth has been added, and the fourth is still a hired diver of another boat asked in person. **THE CHANNEL BY WHICH A DISGRACED OFFICER REACHES A MORROW QUAY REEVE IS THE WRIT AND WAS RULED ON THE PAGE IN CHAPTER 130 ON WEDNESDAY THE FIFTH OF JULY, AND CHAPTER 140 RECITES IT IN NINE WORDS AND APPLIES IT, and that is the whole of the channel and no second channel has been invented.**
- **The reporting clause. FOUR RETURNS HAVE NOW BEEN MADE UNDER IT AND THERE IS NO FIFTH: the fourth of May, two days late; the tenth of May, on the day; the seventeenth of June, on the day; and the fourteenth of July, ON THE DAY, out of a finding of Monday the tenth.** The counting rule is on the page three times in this batch and was spoken out loud in the entry. **The clause was not widened, narrowed, softened or argued by anybody. The yard's position that the first limb catches the chip by itself is on the page and is not improved on.**
- **The notice of the thirty-first of October.** Four clauses, not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered, and the harbor authority has no power to lift it. **Clause two is on the page as a live reason throughout and is argued by nobody: the two objects of nobody's are still on the mole's crown in the open air, about nine feet apart, under two tarpaulins, and one of the tarpaulins has had a corner off it since the seventeenth of June and has not been lashed back because lashing it back takes three men and a morning.** **There are eight acknowledgements in the harbor authority's ledger, both of this year's are spent, THIS BATCH ENTERED NONE, ASKED FOR NONE, AND NO PERSON, A BOAT OR A LEAD OF THIS YARD WENT INSIDE THE FIRST CLAUSE ON ANY DAY OF IT, and the ground of every day of work this month is entered in the first column so that a man in a city can check it against a notice, and the notice does not reach it.** The third, fourth, fifth and sixth joints are landward of the third seam's mark and the yard has said so out loud four times this month and is not going to stop.
- **The eighth-clause plan for the fifth seam** is refused and is in a record with a date on it. **IT IS NOT REOPENED IN THIS BATCH AND IT IS NOT REACHED.** It is referred to **twice**: in a hearing room in June by a man who had been told what a thing costs, and in **thirteen words on the mole's steps on the twenty-sixth of July, when the holder asked whether the entry of that morning was about the plate and the reeve said that it is not and that he will not tell him what it is about.** Nobody in this harbor has said a word about the plate since the seventeenth of May, which is seventy days, and the yard has entered that it does not know what the twenty-sixth's entry is about and has not asked. **The mechanism remains a change of politics in an office and not a better argument from a shed, and it has now produced a second thing in that office's book: four days of the third lay, closed, for an examination with no money behind it.** The plate is still in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched.
- **The bell.** Eight and a half feet off the south end, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, no tongue on the frame and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was, a new rope bought on the eleventh of January. **THE GOING OUT OF THE SECOND OF MAY IS DONE AND IS SPENT AND IS NOT REPEATED AND NO SECOND EIGHT MARKS IS PAID AND NO BOAT GOES OFF THE SOUTH END IN THIS BATCH.** The standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed and is recited in four of the ten chapters. **ITS NOTATION IS IN A NAVAL FILE AND IS NOT ON A WALL IN THIS HARBOR AND CANNOT BE TAKEN DOWN.** Eight marks and a day of a boat stands owing since the twenty-third of May, unpaid, unargued, **not paid in this batch.**
- **The lead plug.** In a chest in a shed with a lock the yard owns, with the boy's writing on the lid. **ITS NUMBER WAS SAID OUT LOUD ONCE, IN A ROOM, ON SUNDAY THE SECOND OF JULY, AND IS NOT WRITTEN IN ANY BOOK IN THIS HARBOR AND IS NOT GOING TO BE, AND THE YARD HAS UNDERTAKEN IN ITS OWN BOOK NOT TO ASK ABOUT IT AGAIN, AND THAT UNDERTAKING BINDS THE YARD AND NOT THE OFFICE AND NOT THE MARKET AND NOT THE BOY, WHO WAS NOT ASKED AND IS NOT TO BE. THE CHEST WAS NOT OPENED IN THIS BATCH AND WAS NOT BROUGHT DOWN THE HILL AND NO DIGIT IS PRINTED IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH.** It appears in this batch only in the disavowal line of the return, where it is named and refused in the same breath.
- **The glass storm.** Not used. The two written occurrences stand, the two second-column lines stand in two hands on two pages that are not on top of each other, **and no cause is named for it in this batch or in any volume and nothing in either line knows anything.**
- **The coast road.** Not resolved, not explained, not named a network by anybody in this batch including the rival, **and not connected to the join and not connected to anything under this town, and the yard says so out loud in the second column of the twenty-first of July and in the first column of the fifth of July.** Its figures are printed in a market in this harbor and going to press in a city. **The pilot out of a storm belt — Rowan Trewin, named in Chapter 115 of Batch 0002 and in no chapter of this batch — is still coming in the autumn for nothing with no instrument in this harbor that can call him and none that can stop him. THE YARD HAS NOT ENTERED THAT IT IS WAITING FOR HIM, AND Chapter 140 SAYS ONLY THAT HE IS STILL COMING AND THAT NO INSTRUMENT HERE CAN CALL HIM OR STOP HIM, and the yard did not go and look this month.**
- **The run under this town.** Not explained, not named, not pursued. **The join is in two hulls lying against a mole's own face, it is not in the mole's footing stone, it is landward of the third seam's mark, and the yard has refused on the page to relate it to the run, the plate, the ferry plate or the compartment, and has instructed that nobody is to write any two of them together.**
- **Custody.** Three items, three numbers, digits not quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request. **No paper states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material and no clerk answered it in this batch.**
- **The concealment.** Hidden. The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. **Nobody in this batch asked the boy and nobody asked the conservator, and the boy is not in the yard and is not to be written to.**
- **The delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, two of them with no call in them, **and neither figure is advanced in this batch by arithmetic or by a measurement. The mole head's handbell went on the third of July and nothing was entered. The delay is a hand's width of a second and has not moved and was not taken and was not written down.** No occasion is entered in this batch. The answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.
- **Labor.** Two pots of air bought at this yard's own two-mark rate, no delivery hose, the longest thing of hose in the harbor about nine fathoms, a gland fitted and unpaid and of no use; a hand lead's line made in the net loft on the second of May and used five times this month; **the yard's own boat in the water at the north end and out on the flood and back three days running and a day of the yard's own time at no figure**; a cistern charged four times in this batch and twelve times on that office's page at no figure, holding since the nineteenth of June, with one rota day not walked and the reason on the page; the ninth seam out with an end of the first of September; **the mole's footing entered for examination on the second of August with the third lay closed four days and no money behind it**; the north berth out; the third lay full; the tide gang four men short; the fire party's hose dead until the autumn; the mole-head light not on; the repair account empty; no boat of this office's own since the evening of the twenty-eighth of April; the *Kittiwake* on the hard; the hard at Ashtide; nineteen winters; the *Sea Wren* at the pile; **eleven copies, a list of eleven on a wall, a printed sheet over it, and now the yard's own fourth return over that, and a man from Ashtide who has had four of the eleven answered him and two of those have stopped writing, and this yard did not ask which four**; Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found, one line in the book, and this yard has not gone.

### 6. What this batch may not be used for

1. **It may not be used to name the condition again.** **THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 03 IS ALLOWED WAS SPENT IN CHAPTER 137, ON THE TWENTIETH OF JULY, IN THE SECOND COLUMN, IN THE HOLDER'S OWN LEFT HAND, WITH THE WORD *A TIDE-SICKNESS* AND WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE. NO LATER CHAPTER IN THIS VOLUME MAY PUT THE WORD IN AGAINST HIM.** The four lines above it are still nameless, are not to be struck out, and a request to strike one out is refused.
2. **It may not be used to ask the bellwright again, in a shed or in a room.** **HE WAS NOT ASKED IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND NO CHAPTER MAY PRINT A DIGIT OF WHAT HE SAID AND NO CHAPTER MAY PUT WHAT HE SAID IN THE THIRD COLUMN AND THE CHEST WAS NOT OPENED.** The undertaking of the second of July binds the yard for the rest of the volume.
3. **It may not be used to enter a ninth acknowledgement, to lift, narrow, re-serve or alter the notice, to argue clause two, to reopen the eighth-clause plan, to reach the plate at the fifth seam, to put a person, boat or lead of this yard inside the first clause, to widen the writ's ground, to add a fifth name, or to manufacture a second season.**
4. **It may not be used to make a fifth return, or to move the counting rule, or to put a return on a ground that a notice closes.** There are four returns and their dates are fixed and the dated lines for the seventeenth, eighteenth, nineteenth, twentieth and twenty-fourth of July stand.
5. **It may not be used to name a cause for the glass storm, to let it speak, to name the Nine Harbors, the Quietening or the Crown Seal, to explain the nine-line mark, to identify the marking on the shard, to name the compartment or to state that the sealed case was in standing space number four, to quote a custody number's digits, to put a senior examiner in a room, to name an office of returning memory, to have Captain Holt in a room or on a mole, to ask Sera Mott, to introduce a new antagonist, institution or final enemy, or to give Torren Skell an office, a ship, a uniform or a title.**
6. **It may not be used to resolve the coast road, to name it a network, to connect it to anything under this town, to connect the join to anything under this town, to name the nine final routes, or to say who edits the charts.**
7. **It may not be used to resolve, repair or pay off Bevin Rook's June, to write to him, to go and see him, to ask anybody who has seen him what he said, to reduce the forty marks, to stop the wage, or to treat the wage as a claim on his June.**
8. **It may not be used to pay Mara Venn's forty marks, to pay either woman's eight shillings and tenpence, to raise or sell the warp, to weigh, price or offer the bell-metal gun-port, to sell one mark's worth of the heap behind the shed, to re-write the chandler's note, to move any of its three counts of weeks, or to move the count of eleven copies.**
9. **It may not be used to make Perry Ollett a villain, to give him an office or a following, to make him the coast office or the circuit office, or to have him and the man of Veyr meet. It may not be used to have the coast office fixed, softened, defeated or forgiven, and it may not be used to have the harbor's poverty fixed for anything: the cistern holds, the light is not on, the north berth is out, the third lay is full, the hard at Ashtide is unanswered, and a third lay has just lost four days of the best of its season, and that is worse than it was on the sixth of July and is not going to be made better by anything.**
10. **It may not be used to stage a second dive of Calder Marr's own outside the six conditions, to take any power stage other than the Faultreader, or to have a seam settle an argument, prove a motive, convict anybody, or tell Calder what happened.** **THE SEAM TOLD HIM THERE WERE MENDINGS UNDER MENDINGS AND TOLD HIM NOTHING ABOUT WHEN, IN WHAT ORDER, OR BY WHOSE HAND, AND HE SUPPLIED ALL THREE, AND HE WAS WRONG IN THREE PARTS OUT OF FOUR IN A MARKET IN FRONT OF ELEVEN PEOPLE, AND THE VOLUME DOES NOT LET HIM OFF.**
11. **It may not be used to name Captain Holt's posting after her defeat.** **THE POSTING IS NOT DECIDED AND IS EXPLICITLY STILL OPEN, AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS VOLUME MAY NAME A POSTING BEFORE THE DEFEAT THAT FOLLOWS IT.** What Chapters 130 and 140 foreclose on the page, in the yard's own hand, is what a posting may not be. **A Movement 5 writer must decide the posting on the page with a date, must not make it a command in this harbor, an inspecting commission over a harbor authority, or anything that puts a person, a boat or a lead inside the closure of the notice, and may not invent a second channel, because the channel is the writ and was ruled on the fourteenth of June and restated on the fifth of July and applied on the twenty-sixth of July.**
12. **It may not be used to put the party past the outer marks, or to send anybody to look at the *Hearthwake*, or to ask the man of the third lay one question about her.** She is named in a market, she is entered in the first column, and the party is not going.

### 7. The costs this batch closes with

1. **A public error said in a market in eleven people's hearing and disproved by fouling in two days, and the three people it landed on, none of them thanked and none of them forgiven: a hired diver of another boat who was not paid and is not to be, a woman of the second lay who paid twelve marks at the mole for half a day and is owed eight shillings and tenpence, and the woman of this yard who is the reason there is a reader and who had to read the wrong down and then read a word about her partner's ear down after it.**
2. **A word in the yard's own book, in the holder's own hand, in the second column, with a name in it, and the four lines above it still nameless, and the second column now says that ordinary talk in a room goes on as more than one talk at once.**
3. **A day bought for seventeen and half of it done, and the half that was not done is on the page in the hirer own words, and the figure she was given in advance was a figure about the job and not about her money.**
4. **Nothing in a bag on Wednesday the twenty-sixth of July, and the note at thirty-one, and the tin empty, and the column at fourteen lines and fifty-six marks, and nine envelopes on a bench and not one of them anywhere, and no office in this harbor that has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May.**
5. **Six joints four fathoms under a mole, nine fastenings in about seven feet of each of them, three thicknesses of fouling that will not be arranged into a figure, and a chip of worked glass put into a packing on purpose by somebody who expected somebody to come, and the finding is public and cannot be un-printed and a printer in a city has been told in advance.**
6. **A conservator of forty marks standing who is not on the crew any more, who took her own calipers off the bench, who is owed nine months and put the figure on the page herself, and who is to be found on a Friday morning in a room open to anybody that this party did not arrange and would not dare to arrange.**
7. **A third lay closed for four days in the best of the season by an office with an empty repair account, and the price named out loud in a market by the man who pays it.**
8. **A man who offered in June to come up this coast in the autumn and look at five miles of that coast for nothing, who is still coming, and that there is no instrument in this harbor that can call him.**
9. **The name of a boat with people in her said out loud in a market in this harbor, off the outer marks, by a man of the third lay, and this party wrote it down where anybody can check it and did not go.**
10. **A condition on the page five times in five months in a form a man cannot check, four of them nameless, and the fifth carrying a word, and the count of four in the first column in another person's hand with no name against it.**

### 8. Proposed canon changes

**None applied. Three flagged rather than applied, and two author-level decisions taken on the page.**

1. **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THERE ARE FOUR RETURNS, NOT THREE, AND THE FOURTH IS THE FOURTEENTH OF JULY.** **`outline/volume-03.md` lock L5 enumerates the returns of Volume 03 as three and says "there are three, and only three, and their dates are fixed," and `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0003* item 1 and `state/open-threads.md` both say there is no fourth. THE PROMPT THAT AUTHORISED THIS BATCH QUALIFIED THAT IN ITS OWN FIRST LINE AND IT IS THE QUALIFIER THE CHAPTERS OBEY: *there is no fourth and a chapter of this batch may not produce one out of a day that produced nothing.* THIS BATCH PRODUCED SOMETHING ON MONDAY THE TENTH OF JULY — a finding of worked glass, whole or in part, on ground no party claims, caught by the first limb of the clause by itself — AND THE CLAUSE'S OWN WORDS REQUIRE IT. **THE FOURTH RETURN IS MADE ON FRIDAY THE FOURTEENTH OF JULY, ON THE DAY, AND IS ON THE MARKET WALL FROM THE TWENTY-SECOND, AND THE FOURTH DAY AFTER THE TENTH IS THE FOURTEENTH. NO FIFTH RETURN EXISTS AND DATED LINES STAND FOR THE SEVENTEENTH, EIGHTEENTH, NINETEENTH, TWENTIETH AND TWENTY-FOURTH OF JULY.** This is recorded rather than argued, and a Volume 03 audit should rule whether lock L5's enumeration is superseded by the chapters or amended.
2. **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THE JOIN'S PHYSICAL FACTS.** Six joints in one crevice at about four fathoms on the second seam's own face, landward of the third seam's mark; a scarph of two ends cut to each other, hollowed long to a many-sided hollow; **nine fastenings in about seven feet at a hand's breadth apart, three chisel blows on each head, the third a different depth from the first two, the same three blows on all nine**; a packing that is not stone, not oakum, not lime and not pitch, with a skin a quarter of an inch thick; the packing pressed in from the seaward side over fouling already three inches there; fouling on the seaward face of a quarter of an inch, a quarter of an inch, three inches and a quarter, three inches and a quarter, nine inches and three inches and a quarter, in that order from the root end to the seaward end; **a chip of worked glass in the packing, the same material as the chip out of the tallow of a lead, put there on purpose; and six joints standing in a straight line three hundred and fifty-one feet long.** **THE NINE FASTENINGS ARE NOT A MARK OF NINE JOINED LINES AND THE RETURN SAYS SO AND THE PARTY SAYS IT WILL NOT COUNT THE ROW TWICE, AND THE NINE COMMUNITIES ARE NOT THE NINE HARBORS AND ARE NOT CALLED ANYTHING.**
3. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the nine places.** Six of the nine are named on the page and are on this coast and stand over a joint: **the six places in six entries of a builder's day-book of the port of Ashtide, being forty feet, and seventy-three feet, and one hundred and forty-one feet, and two hundred and ten feet, and two hundred and seventy-nine feet, and three hundred and forty-eight feet, each at the second seam, the builder's name cut into a stone at the mole head.** Three are named and have no joint: **this harbor's own weir, in a weir-book, in a fisher's own hand, with the third joint a foot and four inches below its sill stone and the sill repaired four years after a storm in the same book; a village not on this coast and not on any chart anybody in this harbor has seen, in a poor-book, with twenty-two children put out of it and nine of them named; and a sand and shell lay of this coast that is not on any chart in this harbor, in a tide-gang's step book, in a column after the figures for the foot of this mole's face stop.** **The party has entered the three and has entered that it does not know why the three are there and is not going to guess and is not going to look for them this season. A later volume may place them; this volume may not, and nothing in this batch connects the nine to anything under this town or to a road or to a name.**
4. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — what a chip of worked glass carries.** The chapter says the glass was **put there on purpose by somebody who expected somebody to come**, and it says three times and in three voices that **nobody in this harbor can read what is in it and the trade that can costs six marks and two days and none of the three of them has six marks.** **No reading of the glass occurs, no echo is entered, and no chapter of this batch certifies anything a thing carries. If a later volume reads one, the six marks and two days are on the page and the yard's inability to pay is on the page.**
5. **STILL OPEN, EXPLICITLY, AND NOT DECIDED IN THIS BATCH — CAPTAIN HOLT'S POSTING AFTER HER DEFEAT.** Her defeat is in Movement 5 and has not happened. **WHAT CHAPTERS 130 AND 140 FORECLOSE ON THE PAGE, IN THE YARD'S OWN HAND, IS WHAT A POSTING MAY NOT BE, and the party puts the same sentence in a market in Chapter 140 when a man gives it a boat's name.** A Movement 5 writer must decide the posting on the page with a date, and **the channel is the writ and was ruled on the fourteenth of June, restated on the fifth of July, and applied on the twenty-sixth of July, and no second channel may be invented.**
6. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — *Hearthwake.*** The name of the vessel of Movement 5's climax is said out loud in a market in this harbor on the twenty-sixth of July by a man of the third lay, as a working boat with people in her lying off the outer marks since before the ninth of July, which the tide gang has walked past daily. **She is named and not looked at, no person of this party goes, nobody is sent, and the man who said the name is not asked one question about her. The entry is in the first column. No chapter of this batch puts a person, a boat or a lead of this yard past the outer marks or within four hundred yards of the bar.**
