## Batch 0004 review repairs — the disposition of every finding

A reviewer pass over Chapters 131–140, the four state files and the queued `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` returned **FIX**, with **fourteen required repairs** and a list of prose and craft items. **The repairs were applied in place. No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut, no cost was dropped, no planned plot was altered and no lock was softened.** The batch is **30,890 words where it was 30,635.** The reviewer's own log is `logs/batch-0004.review.log`.

**The reviewer recorded as holding, and those hold after the repairs:** the four debts the batch was given are paid; the join is found by labor in weather with a lead and a slate; the conservator proves six things and refuses the seventh; the Faultreader stage is taken with no ceremony, is named by nobody, and tells him nothing about when or by whose hand; the one naming of the condition is spent once in Chapter 137; Mara Venn's sentence is said before she is asked; the reeve spends again and the price lands on the third lay; the bellwright is not asked and no digit is printed; no new antagonist, no interface, no second person, no harem, no custody object, no fifth return, no acknowledgement entered, nobody past the outer marks; the ending is earned forward pull with no manufactured interruption; the ten pressure types alternate; the midpoint is a working day and not a room; the wrong, the public record and Mara's sentence fall on three separate days.

**THE REVIEW COULD NOT RUN `wc -w` IN ITS PASS AND RECORDED THE WORD COUNTS AS UNVERIFIED. THEY ARE NOW MEASURED: 30,890 across the ten chapters, every one above the 2,200 floor, five inside the 2,200–3,200 guideline and five past it — 140 by 654, 138 by 376, 134 by 300, 136 by 147 and 132 by 10.**

### The fourteen required repairs

1. **THE MONDAY THE TENTH'S DAY ACCOUNT DID NOT ADD UP, AND THE ERROR HAD PROPAGATED INTO THE STATE FILES.** `chapter-0132.md:39` and `:107` charged **the office's pot of air** to a day it had already been paid for on Friday the seventh, and then closed the day as **eleven out of thirteen and the yard's being four**, which is eleven plus four out of thirteen. With the pot struck out the day closes correctly — 13 − 2 − 2 − 3 − 1 − 1 = 4 — and the closing line is **nine out of thirteen and the yard's being four**, and the pot is now named as not being on that day, with the reason, in both places. **The bag on the Monday evening is SIX, not four**, which is what the published chain `4, 2, 6, 2, 7, 1, 4, 0` in § 2 and in the queued prompt already required and what the state file's own bag column carried while its breakdown contradicted it. **The false "nothing has come into it since the first of July", printed on the same line as four marks arriving that afternoon, is gone.** `state/continuity.md` § 2's table row and `state/chapter-summaries.md` carried the broken breakdown and are corrected with the reason on the page.
2. **A THIRD FALSE "LAST MONEY IN" DATE.** `chapter-0136.md:27` said the five marks of Monday the seventeenth were the first since **the third of July**. The last money in before them came in on **Monday the tenth**. **It is the tenth of July**, and the batch now carries one date.
3. **`chapter-0134.md:77` HAD THREE BROKEN FIGURES IN ONE SENTENCE.** "…is thirteen marks, **of which** sixteen have gone out as four weeks of a wage and six as two pots of air" is ungrammatical against a larger figure, the total as of the fourteenth is **fourteen**, and the outgoings are **three weeks of a wage and one pot of air**. **It now reads fourteen marks in and fourteen marks out, as three weeks of a wage and one pot of air**, which agrees with the bag at two and with Chapter 140's twenty-two for the whole period.
4. **THE SIX JOINT POSITIONS CONTRADICTED THE SPACINGS, AND THE STRING COULD NOT CONTAIN THE LAST JOINT.** The builder's day-book read on the twenty-first gives the six places as **40, 73, 141, 210, 279, 348 feet**, spacings of 33, 68, 69, 69, 69. `chapter-0136.md:71` had the second "sixty-eight feet south of the first" where the day-book puts it **thirty-three**, and that is corrected. `chapter-0137.md:17` measured the two thick readings from the two thin ones; they are **sixty-eight and a hundred and thirty-seven feet south of the second**, which is the inner of the two thin ones, and that is what it now says. **The string is now three hundred and fifty-one feet, not three hundred and forty-one, because a three-hundred-and-forty-one-foot span cannot contain a joint the builder's own book puts at three hundred and forty-eight.** The figure is corrected in Chapters 138, 139, `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md` and this file.
5. **THE ONSET DATE CONTRADICTED CHAPTER 88.** `chapter-0137.md:117` said the holder has had it since **the thirteenth** of October. `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0088.md:59` has him say in his own mouth that it has been since **the fourteenth**, and the fourteenth is the date this manuscript uses for the public sheet, the custody events and the ledger. **The onset is the fourteenth of October and is now the fourteenth in all four places that state it: Chapter 88, `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0111.md`, `chapter-0121.md` and `chapter-0137.md`. Chapters 111 and 121 are corrected at source and the two chapters they sit between already had it right.**

   **THE THIRTEENTH OF OCTOBER IS NOT STALE AND THE OUTLINE IS NOT AMENDED. THE REVIEW READ `outline/volume-03.md:37` AS THE ONSET FIGURE AND IT IS NOT: that line, and `outline/volume-02.md:35` and `state/current.md:628` beside it, are about **the entry** — the first anchored dive, on the thirteenth, eleven minutes forty inside — and that is a different fact from the day after it. The manuscript carries three distinct things on that date and they must never be fused: **THE THIRTEENTH IS THE DIVE, in Chapter 38 and in both outlines; THE FOURTEENTH IS THE ONSET, in Chapter 88 and now in Chapters 111, 121 and 137; AND THE THIRTEENTH IS ALSO THE DATE THE WORD *ENOUGH* ENTERED THIS YARD'S SIX CONDITIONS, said in a hearing room in Chapter 45 and again in Chapter 56 of Volume 02, which is a third thing again and is a trade practice and not a symptom.** The outline was left exactly as it stands and the first attempt at this repair, which changed it, was reverted.
6. **"THE SECOND LONGEST THIS COLUMN HAS RUN" WAS FALSE.** The column's latenesses are about ten hours, about an hour, about eight hours, about two hours, **about a day and a half**, about two hours and about three hours. Three hours is not second. **The ranking claim is cut and the sentence now says the party is not going to make a practice of it, which is the part that was always true.**
7. **`chapter-0139.md:39` MISDATED THE QUOTATION IT REFERS BACK TO.** Her retraction-reference said **on Tuesday**; that sentence is hers in the market on **Saturday the twenty-second**, and the Tuesday market four days earlier is Calder's *chain* statement and not hers. **It is Saturday, at the head of the dock, and it is load-bearing because it is the retraction.**
8. **"FIVE THICKNESSES" IS NOT WHAT THE CHAPTERS SAY.** The six readings are a quarter of an inch, a quarter, three and a quarter, three and a quarter, nine inches, and three and a quarter — **three distinct thicknesses** — and `chapter-0137.md:27` says "at least three different days in three different seasons". **The second column on the page and every state file now say three**, and this file's § 4, which already had them right, is the reference.
9. **"NINE DAYS IN THIS HARBOR" WAS WRONG IN THE TWO FILES A MOVEMENT 5 WRITER READS FIRST.** She arrived "since about the thirtieth of June" and first appears on the fifteenth of July — **about a fortnight**. `state/current.md` (two places) and `state/open-threads.md` are corrected; `state/continuity.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` already had it right.
10. **A DUPLICATED PASSAGE SPANNED TWO CONSECUTIVE CHAPTERS.** Chapters 131 and 132 both staged the two of them going up on the mole's crown at about four in the afternoon, standing on it for ten minutes in the wind, and both printed the tarpaulins, the fender, the forty feet of mooring rope and the nine-feet-under-their-boots line near-verbatim. **The Saturday visit in Chapter 131 is cut and replaced with a different errand on the same afternoon — Calder Marr walks down to a smith's door with the piece in his coat, does not go in, and comes back up the hill, which is what the first-column line about not asking a smith this week now has behind it.** The Sunday visit in Chapter 132 is the only crown visit in the batch and is stamped at half past three on the Sunday.
11. **THE SLATE-WALL "N MONDAYS" COUNTER WAS RESTARTED AND BATCH 0004 CONTINUED THE BROKEN COUNT.** The run from the eighth of May is unbroken: the eighth, fifteenth, twenty-second and twenty-ninth of May are one to four, the fifth and twelfth of June are five and six, **the nineteenth and twenty-sixth of June are seven and eight, and the third, tenth, seventeenth and twenty-fourth of July are nine, ten, eleven and twelve.** Chapter 122 restarted at *three* and Chapters 125 and 129 carried it. **All three are corrected at source**, Chapters 132, 136 and 139 now read **ten, eleven and twelve**, **Chapter 129 is retitled *Nine Mondays, and a Wage***, and § 1 above no longer ratifies the wrong series. The only other figure in this run is the clerk's own in Chapter 112, who counts the Mondays of May and says five on the twenty-ninth, and he is counting from the first.
12. **CHAPTER 131 STAGED A DIVE OF THE HOLDER'S OWN AND DID NOT READ THE SIX CONDITIONS ALOUD.** The prompt allowed one only if the six were read in order with the tender, and every other working day in the batch does it. **The six are now read out in order at the boat before anybody is wet**, with the limit at nineteen set by the diver and not raisable by the holder, the cutoff hers and the word *Enough*, and the stated reason entered with the clerk's absence on the page. The framing sentence is the batch's own and not a copy of the other days', because Chapter 136 defends the repetition and a repair should not be the thing that breaks it. **The state file's claim that all five of the party's days had the six read out is now true of all five.**
13. **THE HOUR OF THE TOP OF THE FLOOD RAN BACKWARDS.** It was half past twelve on the fifteenth, half past three on the seventeenth, half past two on the nineteenth and half past two on the twentieth — later, then two days earlier, then unmoved. **It is now about eleven on the eighth, half past twelve on the fifteenth, twenty past one on the seventeenth, ten past two on the nineteenth and half past two on the twentieth**, and it moves forward. Two consequences follow and both are on the page: the boat coming off the face at eleven on the seventeenth is now **two hours and a quarter before the flood** and not four and a half, and **there are six more floods before the seaward fouling can be read with a rule**, which is what six readings at one a day means. § 1 above now carries the five hours and they are binding.
14. **THE MARKET WALL WAS USED ON A MONDAY BY THIS PARTY, TWICE, AGAINST THE PROMPT.** The sheet went up on **Saturday the twenty-second**, which is correct; but Nessa Pike's line was said to have gone up **on the paper on Monday and not on Saturday**, and the yard's answer was said to go **on the wall on Monday**. **Both are corrected: the objection and the answer go onto the paper on the same Saturday morning, on the same nail, as the return.** The one Monday left in the batch is **Perry Ollett's**, he says in front of the wall that he will be standing there on the twenty-seventh with a better sheet, no chapter shows him do it, **and it is not this party's use of that wall.** § 1 above now says so in terms instead of claiming a flat "on no Monday" that the chapters did not bear out.

### The prose and craft items, applied

15. **A stray leading quotation mark** in `chapter-0132.md` at the shipwright's *That is a place* is gone.
16. **A doubled *and and*** in `chapter-0139.md:39` is gone, in the same sentence as the corrected date.
17. **The same Wednesday-afternoon market was forty people and then twenty.** `chapter-0140.md` no longer gives a second head-count: the woman of no office says the thing **none of the others in that market** could have said, and the forty standing at the man of the third lay's speech are untouched.
18. **"Nothing came in or out of that yard on Wednesday the nineteenth" was contradicted twice inside its own chapter** by a boatwright coming down to the shed and Edric Pringle coming up the hill. **It is now "Not a mark came in or out of that yard"**, which is what the chapter shows and what the money chain requires.
19. **The nine-inch joint was "in the middle".** It is the fifth of the six and the second from the seaward end, and Chapters 137, `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` all say so now.
20. **THE *IN ABOUT N WORDS* FORMULA WAS APPLIED TO ANSWERS OF FOUR DIFFERENT LENGTHS.** The device is the house's and is used accurately elsewhere, so it was not removed; it was made true. **The reeve's answer on the mole's steps is thirteen words and every file now says thirteen.** Calder Marr's answer in the market is fifteen. **The clerk's statement on the steps of the fourteenth of July is long and is now "in one go and without stopping anywhere in it"**, which is the figure Chapter 87 already uses for the same kind of sentence. The state files' claims that the plate was referred to twice *in nine words* are corrected, and the second one is the thirteen-word answer.
21. **THE WEIR WAS PUT UNDER "THE ELEVEN FEET OF STONE"**, which is the fifth-seam plate's locked figure, and a later writer would have fused a fisher's grandfather's weir with a thing nobody in this harbor has said a word about since the seventeenth of May. **The weir is under four feet of stone** and the third joint is still a foot and four inches below its own sill stone, which is a different measurement of a different object on the same face and is stated as such.
22. **THE WORD WAS SAID TO HAVE BEEN GIVEN TO HIM BY A MAN OF FIFTY.** It was not: Chapter 88 has him produce it himself, in the *Grey Widow's* cockpit, on the thirteenth of May, and Chapter 137 now says so, and drops the "a man of fifty … since he was thirty-one", which was also ambiguous against a book that already carries a man of fifty-one and two men of fifty-four. The second-column line records that the word was said out loud once before, on that day, in that boat, and that nobody has been asked anything about it.
23. **A PACKING WAS TO BE ANSWERED BY A GLASS BLOWER.** It is a **caulker**, which is the trade that makes packing and which is already on this coast in Chapter 137.
24. **Edric Pringle said she was not going to be paid "the four marks".** She is the hirer, she paid twelve of seventeen at the mole, and **five came up the hill**, so it is five.
25. **REPEATED STAGING.** The read-out at the head of the dock was framed identically in Chapters 134 and 140 — *in the wind, with the market gone in and about N people in the street and the mole head's light not on behind them* — and **Chapter 140 is reframed**. **The read-back refrain ran five times in identical words**, which the review called a tic rather than a fault, and **three of the five are now varied**: Chapter 131 keeps the full form because it is the first, Chapter 134 says the two of them were alone in the room, Chapter 140 says the two of them were the only two who heard it, and Chapter 137's is cut because a third person is awake in the room and the sentence was denying it.
26. **THE CLOSING READ-OUT HAD HARDENED INTO A FORMAT.** The device is the yard's own practice and it stays in all ten chapters. **The two chapters that were long for this reason are broken up**: Chapter 134's fifteen-sentence inventory paragraph of 1,563 characters is now three paragraphs, and Chapter 140's two paragraphs of 2,073 and 2,439 characters are now four and four, the largest of them 828 characters, with the same content in the same order and nothing dropped. **No cost was dropped from either.** The two longest chapters are 3,576 and 3,854, and Chapter 140 is the batch's close and carries the whole labor spine.
27. **`state/continuity.md` asserted an entry about Rowan Trewin that is on no page.** He is named in Chapter 115 of Batch 0002 and in no chapter of this batch, and Chapter 140 carries only *a man of fifty-four out of a storm belt … who is still coming*. **The claim that the yard has entered that it is waiting for him is struck** and the entry now says only what the page says. `state/open-threads.md` is corrected with it.
28. **The market wall was promised for "Saturday week"** from Wednesday the twelfth, which is the eighteenth or the twenty-fifth. **It is Saturday the twenty-second**, named exactly, in both places in Chapter 133 and in the chapter summary.
29. **The word counts were unverified.** They are measured and recorded above and in `state/current.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md`.

### One figure the review did not raise, and is corrected with the tide

**`chapter-0136.md:51` closed the seventeenth with "there are two more floods."** There are six: six joints, one top of a flood a day, two readings a day, and the three reading days are the eighteenth, the nineteenth and the twentieth. **It is now "there are six more floods before the fouling on that seaward face can be read with a rule,"** which is the mechanism the whole of Chapter 137 rests on and which the tide repair in item 13 made impossible to leave.

### The queued prompt was checked and one clause in it was corrected

**`workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` WAS READ AGAINST EVERY FIGURE THIS REPAIR TOUCHED, AND IT IS SOUND ON ALL OF THEM.** It carries the correct chain **4, 2, 6, 2, 7, 1, 4, 0**; the day-book's six places of **forty, seventy-three, one hundred and forty-one, two hundred and ten, two hundred and seventy-nine and three hundred and forty-eight feet**, which item 4 does not alter; the Saturday the twenty-second as the day the market wall was used; the fourteen lines and fifty-six marks and the nine envelopes; and the six cistern walks' history. **ONE CLAUSE WAS STALE AND IS CORRECTED: it said the plate had been referred to twice *in nine words each time*, and the second of the two is the thirteen-word answer on the mole's steps.** That one clause now says thirteen and points at item 20 above. **Nothing else in it was touched, and the prompt is this phase's own handoff and was corrected in the one place the review's own finding invalidated, on the Batch 0003 precedent recorded at item 15 of that batch's repairs — a queued prompt is not left to hand the next ten chapters a figure this repair has just ruled on.** It was not created, deleted, renamed or re-scoped, and no second phase was queued.

### Still open, and not this phase's to fix

**`state/phase-ledger.json` IS A HUNDRED AND FORTY CHAPTERS OUT OF DATE AND IT WAS NOT EDITED, BECAUSE IT IS THE CONTROLLER'S FILE.** It reads `"currentPhase": "phase-000-bootstrap"`, `"status": "running"`, with `volume-01-batch-0001` at `planned`, after a hundred and forty finished chapters. `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s selector claims the lowest incomplete batch, so a controller reading it as it stands would rewrite ten completed chapters. **THIS IS THE ONE OPEN GATE ITEM AND IT IS FLAGGED, NOT FIXED, and no prompt, volume file, batch file or marker was created, deleted or renamed to compensate for it.** `state/current.md` records the same flag.

**`outline/volume-03.md` lock L5 STILL ENUMERATES THREE RETURNS AND SAYS "THERE ARE THREE, AND ONLY THREE".** The batch produced a fourth on the fourteenth, correctly, because the tenth produced a finding of worked glass on ground no party claims. **It REMAINS FLAGGED FOR AUDIT AT § 8 ITEM 1 AND IS LEFT FLAGGED. It is not amended by this repair, because a repair phase records what the chapters decided and does not rewrite a lock.**

### Proposed canon changes

**None applied by this repair. No canon fact was created, removed or softened.** The four returns and their dates stand and lock L5's enumeration of three is still flagged at § 8 item 1 for a Volume 03 audit. The join's physical facts stand with the string corrected to three hundred and fifty-one feet and the spacings made to agree with the builder's day-book. **The onset of the condition is the fourteenth of October and is now stated that way in every chapter that states it — Chapter 88, and Chapters 111, 121 and 137 — while the dive itself is the thirteenth and both outlines and `state/current.md` still say the thirteenth, and no lock was amended to make them agree.** What the chip of worked glass carries is still unread. The coast road is still unresolved. The three places with no joint are still entered and still not guessed at. Captain Holt's posting is still open and the channel is still the writ. The *Hearthwake* is still named and still not looked at. Bevin Rook is still owed forty marks and is still in no chapter. **No new antagonist, institution or final enemy was introduced, and the planned ending is untouched.**

## Batch 0005 as written (Chapters 141–150), Volume 03 — binding on Chapter 151 onward and on the Volume 03 close

**VOLUME 03 MOVEMENT 5 IS WRITTEN AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX AND ITS RESOLUTION.** Twenty-nine thousand six hundred and eighty-eight words across ten chapters, Thursday the twenty-seventh of July to Tuesday the fifteenth of August 2026. **No card file existed for this batch; `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0005.md` is not in this repository, none was invented, and the shape was worked out of `outline/volume-03.md` and the four state files Batch 0004 had just written.**

### 1. The calendar the batch actually ran on

**The book's grid, per lock L1, and no weekday was corrected to an almanac.** 26 July a **Wednesday** (the end of Chapter 140) · 27 **Thursday** · 28 **Friday** · 29 **Saturday** · 30 **Sunday** · 31 **Monday** · 1 August a **Tuesday** and a wage week · 2 **Wednesday** · 3 **Thursday** · 4 **Friday** · 5 **Saturday** · 6 **Sunday** · 7 **Monday** · 8 **Tuesday** and a wage week · 9 **Wednesday** · 10 **Thursday** · 11 **Friday** · 12 **Saturday** · 13 **Sunday** · 14 **Monday** · 15 **Tuesday** and a wage week. **The distribution is the prompt's own suggested one, unshifted: 141 the 27th and 28th; 142 the 29th and 30th; 143 the 31st and 1 August; 144 the 2nd and 3rd; 145 the 4th and 5th; 146 the 6th and 7th; 147 the 8th and 9th; 148 the 10th and 11th; 149 the 12th and 13th; 150 the 14th and 15th. No day is in a chapter twice and no man is paid for two days in one chapter.**

**NO WEEKDAY HAD TO BE CORRECTED IN THE CHAPTERS, AND TWO STATE-FILE FIGURES WERE WRONG AGAINST THEM.** `state/open-threads.md` and the Batch 0005 prompt both say that a crew of Ashtide takes Sella Brigg's warp on **Tuesday the twenty-eighth of July**. **THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF JULY IS A FRIDAY ON THIS BOOK'S GRID, WHICH CHAPTERS 138, 139 and 140 all establish, AND CHAPTER 131 HAS HER SAY IN HER OWN MOUTH THAT HE WILL NOT DO IT ON A TUESDAY TIDE. THE CHAPTERS ARE RIGHT AND THE STATE FILES ARE WRONG. THE WARP IS TAKEN ON FRIDAY THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF JULY IN CHAPTER 141.** The prompt's own suggested distribution was followed without a shift.

**THE HEARING ROOM'S SLATE WALL CAME DOWN BARE ON MONDAY THE THIRTY-FIRST OF JULY, MONDAY THE SEVENTH OF AUGUST AND MONDAY THE FOURTEENTH OF AUGUST — THE THIRTEENTH, FOURTEENTH AND FIFTEENTH MONDAYS OF THAT RUN, continuing 10, 11 and 12 at the end of Chapter 140. All three are in Chapters 143, 146 and 150, and no sitting was held on any of them.**

**THE MARKET WALL WAS USED ONCE, BY THIS PARTY, ON TUESDAY THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST.** The fourteenth is a **Monday** and the wall is not used on a Monday. **THE HEARING ROOM WAS USED ONCE, ON SATURDAY THE TWELFTH OF AUGUST**, three days before the market wall, and never on the same day. **THE *VIGIL'S* OWN PAPERS ARE ON THAT WALL TWICE — Friday the twenty-eighth of July and Friday the fourth of August — AND NEITHER IS THIS PARTY'S USE OF IT, AND CHAPTER 145 SAYS SO IN TERMS, ON THE PRECEDENT OF THE BATCH 0004 REVIEW AT ITEM 14.**

**THE CISTERN ROTA DAYS INSIDE THE WINDOW ARE THE TWENTY-SEVENTH AND THIRTY-FIRST OF JULY AND THE THIRD, SEVENTH, TENTH AND FOURTEENTH OF AUGUST — SIX DAYS, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS NOW ACCOUNTED FOR. WALKED BY THIS PARTY: THE THIRD OF AUGUST, ON THE ROTA, AT NO FIGURE, THE FOURTEENTH TIME, THE GATE TAKING THREE MEN FROM TEN UNTIL ABOUT TWO (Ch 144). WALKED BY STRANGERS, AT NO FIGURE, AND NOT PAID FOR: THE THIRTY-FIRST OF JULY, THE THIRTEENTH TIME, TWO MEN OF THE SECOND LAY AND A MAN OF THE THIRD LAY (Ch 143); AND THE SEVENTH OF AUGUST, THE FIFTEENTH TIME, TWO MEN OF THE THIRD LAY AND A WOMAN OF THE SECOND LAY (Ch 146). NOT WALKED, WITH THE REASON ON THE PAGE IN THE YARD'S OWN HAND, THREE TIMES: THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF JULY, the party being on the second seam's face from seven until half past two and the gate taking four hours with three men against two and a half (Ch 141); THE TENTH OF AUGUST, both persons of the party being at the top of this mole's steps from ten in the morning with forty people on the crown and a sheet (Ch 150, and that is the only account of the tenth of August anywhere in the fifty chapters); and THE FOURTEENTH OF AUGUST, both persons being in the market office press from nine until about two with the same sheet (Ch 150). **THIS PARTY HAS WALKED THAT GATE THIRTEEN TIMES SINCE THE END OF MAY, WHICH IS THE FIGURE CHAPTER 150 NOW ENTERS, AND FIFTEEN WALKS STAND ON THAT OFFICE'S OWN PAGE AT NO FIGURE, AND THE CISTERN HAS HELD SINCE THE NINETEENTH OF JUNE, AND THE THREAD IS CLOSED AND WAS NOT USED AS A PRESSURE.**

### 2. The money chain, mark by mark

**NOTHING in a bag on Wednesday the twenty-sixth of July. NOTHING in a bag on Tuesday the fifteenth of August, thirty-one on the chandler's note, nothing in a tin, and three marks owing to a man of Ashtide.**

| Day | Day account | Bag at close |
|---|---|---|
| Wed 26 Jul | (end of Batch 0004) | **0** |
| Thu 27 Jul | Second seam, hirer's own account, nobody in the water. Gross 15, eighth 2, share 2, hired 3, second hired 3, victuals 1, rope 1 = 12 of 15. Yard's **3**. | **3** |
| Fri 28 Jul | — | 3 |
| Sat 29 – Sun 30 Jul | — | 3 |
| Mon 31 Jul | Second seam, hirer's own account. Gross 13, eighth 2, share 2, hired 3, victuals 1, rope 1 = 9 of 13. Yard's **4**. | **7** |
| Tue 1 Aug | **WAGE 4 out into an envelope.** Column 15 lines / 60 marks. Ten envelopes. | **3** |
| Wed 2 Aug | The dump, hirer's own account — ground the writ names. Gross 13, eighth 2, share 2, hired 3, victuals 1, rope 1 = 9 of 13. Yard's **4**. | **7** |
| Thu 3 – Fri 7 Aug | **Mon 7 Aug:** second seam, hirer's own account, nobody in the water. Gross 13, eighth 2, share 2, hired 3, victuals 1, rope 1 = 9 of 13. Yard's **4**. | **11** |
| **Tue 8 Aug** | **WAGE 4 out into an envelope — column 16 lines / 64 marks, eleven envelopes. TWO POTS OF THE OFFICE'S AIR at this yard's own two-mark rate, 4 out. The *Sallow Reach*, 6 marks named out loud, 3 paid at the mole, 3 NOT paid.** | **0** |
| Wed 9 – Mon 14 Aug | — | 0 |
| **Tue 15 Aug** | **THE WAGE IS NOT PAID AND THE ENVELOPE IS NOT MADE. Four marks short.** Column stays 16 lines / 64 marks. | **0** |

**THE CHAIN IS 0, 3, 3, 7, 3, 7, 7, 11, 0, 0 across the days money moves, and it is checkable in six chapters without leaving the prose. NO MONEY CAME IN FROM ANY OFFICE IN THIS BATCH AND NONE WENT OUT TO ANY PERSON EXCEPT THREE WAGES, TWO POTS OF AIR AND A DAY OF ANOTHER CREW'S BOAT. The whole of the money that has come into this yard since the twenty-second of July is fourteen marks and the whole of what went out is eighteen.**

**THE CHANDLER'S NOTE. NOT RE-WRITTEN, AND SAID OUT LOUD, IN THE YARD'S OWN HAND, AT THE HEAD OF THE DOCK, IN NINE PEOPLE'S HEARING, ON TUESDAY THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST — the prompt's second alternative, taken: the note is not re-written and is going to stay un-re-written; its three counts of weeks stand at twenty-two, thirteen and eleven as last written on the fourth of January and none of them has moved and none of them is moved by anything a yard does with a pencil; the refusal of the thirty-first of March stands unreversed; and re-writing a note with nothing in the bag is not a payment, which this yard said in a market on the third of May and says again on the fifteenth of August. Five marks of compressor's gland stand on it unpaid and useless. The tin is empty and has been since the twenty-eighth of April.**

**THE WAGE COLUMN: fifteen lines and sixty marks on Tuesday the first of August, sixteen lines and sixty-four marks on Tuesday the eighth, AND IT STOPS THERE.** The fifth of March is the oldest line and is still the oldest. **Eleven envelopes on a bench in the chest and not one of them has been anywhere in twelve weeks, and the reason is the reason it has been since the second of May: a wage is not a reason to go to a door. FOUR WAGE WEEKS OF THE LAST FIVE MONTHS WERE NOT PAID, AND THE MOST RECENT OF THEM IS THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST AND IS IN THE FIRST COLUMN WITH THE FIGURE AGAINST IT.**

### 3. Every document and instrument created in this batch

1. **A notice of the Crown, nailed on the market wall of Morrow Quay on Friday the twenty-eighth of July**, four paragraphs, signed *Veyra Holt, Master, commanding*, claiming the *Hearthwake* as Crown material as one of a formation described in a schedule held elsewhere, requiring any person who boards, works upon, takes from or assists to report in writing to the harbor authority within four days, and stating that no assistance will be rendered. **Nailed over this yard's own fourth return.** It has four days and the reporting clause's hand on it.
2. **A second notice of the Crown on the same nails on Friday the fourth of August**, five lines longer, superseding and withdrawing the first, adding *the said vessel and every part of her contents being thereby Crown material*, and *an order given to them in the month of June and not obeyed*. The two-hand fact, the wet fold, the ink stamp off by a sixteenth of an inch, and the clause that reads like a man writing to himself, are in Mara Venn's six things.
3. **A first-column entry of Monday the second of August**: the *Hearthwake* grounded by the head at about half past two, seven foot of list, the grapnel at two hundred yards, the hour, and the fact that the party made no report and knows what a reader of a page in five years will make of it.
4. **A first-column entry of Wednesday the second of August**: the *Vigil* at anchor two and a half miles off, and the refusal to keep a watch.
5. **A first-column entry of Tuesday the first of August: the boy.** The thing Bevin Rook was kept for, two hundred days, the fourth of May and the eleventh of May, the fifteenth of May, and that he cannot do it and the party is not going to ask him.
6. **A first-column entry of Monday the seventh of August at nine in the evening: the decision, its cost, and the broken sentence**, entered before the day and not after, in four paragraphs.
7. **A second-column entry of Wednesday the ninth of August**, the bare fact, with a reason, and **without the word**.
8. **A written limit on the testimony**, in four people's own words, signed by four, on the mole's steps on Thursday the tenth.
9. **The page**, written out in the market office press on Friday the eleventh and carried up the coast in a bundle: the place of every copy of it, the nine places with a date against each, the two columns with a reason against every line of the second, the limit, the five bells, and the breaking of the sentence. **Forty copies at no price. One copy in a city going to press.**
10. **A first-column entry of Tuesday the fifteenth of August: the wage not paid.**
11. **A third-column entry of Tuesday the fifteenth of August**, at about an hour's lateness, not read back.
12. **A first-column entry of Sunday the thirteenth of August: the question asked in a market, and the refusal to answer it, with the reason against it**, and Elias Voke's sentence, which the man made Calder Marr read back twice.

**NO RETURN WAS MADE IN THIS BATCH AND THERE IS NO FIFTH.** Dated lines stand in the yard's own book for the twenty-seventh, twenty-eighth, twenty-ninth, thirtieth and thirty-first of July and the first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, seventh, tenth and eleventh of August, and for the eighth and ninth of August in a different form: the party was past the outer marks on its own account, raised nothing, told no office and will tell no office. **A basket half out of a hatch in a boat is not a raising, and the reporting clause of the first of May does not reach ground beyond the outer marks, and the yard has entered that it raised nothing and is not going to let anybody call a thing a raising that came up out of a hatch in a boat and went back down into a boat.**

### 4. The dated positions at the end of Chapter 150

- **The writ.** A provisional writ granted Monday the first of May, running to the first of March next, **not the Second Bell license, not a charter, renewing nothing, ground not widened and not to be widened: the mole from the first seam to the twelfth, the dump, the north wall, the first and second seams, all ground landward of the third seam, private ground within the line. The four names are four and no fifth has been added. THE WRIT IS BOUNDED BY THE OUTER MARKS, AND THE BATCH'S CENTRAL FACT IS THAT EVERYTHING THE CLAUSE TOUCHES IS ON THE WRIT AND THEREFORE EVERYTHING THE CLAUSE TOUCHES IS INSIDE THE OUTER MARKS.**
- **THE CHANNEL IS THE WRIT AND IT WAS RULED ON THE PAGE ON WEDNESDAY THE FIFTH OF JULY AND IT WAS APPLIED TWICE IN THIS BATCH: on Saturday the twelfth of August, in the hearing room, to a man in a coat with a paper about the second season, who was answered on the day it came and was not treated as a friend or as an enemy, and the yard entered that he was let up the stairs BECAUSE of that standing arrangement. No second channel was invented and none may be.**
- **The notice of the thirty-first of October.** Four clauses, not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered. **This batch entered no acknowledgement, asked for none, and put no person, boat or lead of this yard inside the first clause on any day. There are eight in that ledger, both of this year's are spent, and there is no ninth, and the party has entered that it did not ask for one and is not going to, twice, in Chapters 143 and 149.**
- **The eighth-clause plan for the fifth seam** is refused, is in a record with a date on it, **was not reopened, was not reached, and is not referred to in any chapter of this batch. The plate is still in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched.**
- **The bell.** Eight and a half feet off the south end, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was.** **THE GOING OUT OF THE SECOND OF MAY IS DONE AND IS SPENT AND WAS NOT REPEATED, NO SECOND EIGHT MARKS WAS PAID, NO BOAT WENT OFF THE SOUTH END IN THIS BATCH, AND THE STANDING SENTENCE SAID IN A STREET ON THE THIRD OF MAY STANDS UNREVERSED AND IS THE WHOLE OF THE FINAL CHAPTER'S LAST IMAGE: in about four years a bell is going to be out in a boat at eight marks and a day of a boat then as it is now, and the whole of the craft is the going out, and the four of them were told on the third of May so that in four years there is no argument about it.** Eight marks and a day of a boat stands owing since the twenty-third of May, unpaid, unargued, **not paid, not argued and not asked about in this batch.**
- **THE LEAD PLUG. IN A CHEST IN A SHED WITH A LOCK THE YARD OWNS. THE BELLWRIGHT WAS NOT ASKED AGAIN AND IS IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AS A PERSON — NOT IN A SHED, NOT IN A BOAT, NOT IN A MARKET, NOT ON A MOLE AND NOT IN A ROOM. NO DIGIT OF WHAT HE SAID ON THE SECOND OF JULY IS PRINTED IN ANY CHAPTER OF IT. THE CHEST WAS NOT OPENED AND WAS NOT BROUGHT DOWN THE HILL, AND IT IS NAMED ONCE, IN CHAPTER 150, IN THE READ-OUT, AS A THING THAT WAS NOT OPENED. This is not a virtue and is not put forward as one: a yard that has undertaken not to ask does not ask, and the work did not need him.** The undertaking of the second of July binds the yard for the rest of the volume.
- **THE CONDITION. THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 03 IS ALLOWED WAS SPENT IN CHAPTER 137, ON THE TWENTIETH OF JULY, IN THE SECOND COLUMN, IN THE HOLDER'S OWN LEFT HAND, WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE, AND IT IS SPENT AND IT IS NOT AVAILABLE IN CHAPTER 141 OR IN ANY CHAPTER AFTER IT. The bare fact is carried TWICE in this batch, both times in the second column, both times with a reason against the line, and neither carries a name, and the four lines above the named one are still nameless and are not struck out and a request to strike a line out of a column is refused.** The count of four in the first column, in a shipwright's hand, with no name against it, stands.
- **The glass storm.** Not used. Two written occurrences stand and no cause is named for it in this batch or in any volume and nothing in either line knows anything.
- **The coast road.** Not resolved, not explained, **not called a network by anybody in this batch, and not connected to the join or to anything under this town.** Its figures are printed in a market and going to press in a city. **The man of fifty-four out of a storm belt is still coming in the autumn for nothing, and no instrument in this harbor can call him and none can stop him, and the yard did not go and look and has entered that it did not.**
- **The run under this town.** Not explained, not named, not pursued, and not referred to in any chapter of this batch.
- **Custody.** Three items, three numbers, digits not quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request, and **no paper in this batch states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material and no clerk answered it.**
- **The concealment.** Hidden. The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. **Nobody in this batch asked the boy and nobody asked the conservator, and the boy is in no chapter of it and is not written to.**
- **The delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions and two of them with no call in them, **and NEITHER FIGURE IS ADVANCED IN THIS BATCH BY ARITHMETIC OR BY A MEASUREMENT. NO OCCASION WAS ENTERED. The delay is a hand's width of a second and it has not moved and was not taken and was not written down, AND NOBODY IN THIS YARD HAS SAID WHY AND NOBODY IN THIS HARBOR HAS EVER SAID WHY AND THIS PARTY IS NOT GOING TO BE THE FIRST.** The answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.
- **The two objects of nobody's are still living in the open air on the mole's crown above the third seam's mark**, about nine feet apart, under two tarpaulins, one of them with a corner off it since the seventeenth of June and not lashed back, and **NEITHER OBJECT HAS MOVED IN NINETY DAYS, AND THE MARKING ON ONE OF THEM IS NOT IDENTIFIED AND IS NOT GOING TO BE AND HAS SETTLED NOTHING.**
- **Six joints four fathoms under that same stone**, nine fastenings in about seven feet of each, three thicknesses of fouling, a chip of worked glass put into a packing on purpose, and a line of six places three hundred and fifty-one feet long **which this party has declined to call a chain, in a market, in eleven people's hearing, in the words of a man who was wrong in three parts out of four of the same sentence and has not taken it back.**
- **Labor.** No air in this harbor except six pots bought this season at this yard's own two-mark rate, **two of which were in a boat past the outer marks on the eighth of August.** No delivery hose; the longest thing of hose about nine fathoms; a gland fitted since the nineteenth of May, unpaid at five marks, and useless because the thing after the gland is the hose. **A hand lead's line used on the twenty-seventh, the thirty-first and the seventh of August and NOT used past the outer marks, because a hand lead's line is not what anybody wanted past the outer marks.** One herring berth short. The ninth seam out of service with an end of it entered for the first of September, seventeen days from the end of the batch. The third lay full and shut four days in the first week of August. The tide gang four men short, and a man of it who gave nine days of his own watching. The fire party's hose dead until the autumn. The mole-head light not on. No boat of the harbor authority since the evening of the twenty-eighth of April, and nothing in that office's book that will put one past the outer marks. The *Kittiwake* on the hard with no cradle. The hard at Ashtide unanswered since the ninth of November. Nineteen winters. The *Sea Wren* at the pile, **and this party has not thanked him and has not been asked to.** Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found, one line, and this yard has not gone. Twenty marks of unsold iron and four hundred and forty pounds of old iron with a bell-metal gun-port in it, unweighed, unpriced, not looked at. Four of the eleven copies not reached and not asked.

### 5. The bodies, the crew, and the people

- **Calder Marr**, thirty-two. He goes in the water **four times in this batch and the last three are on the eighth of August, seventeen minutes each, limit his own, the cutoff hers, the word *Enough*, the six read aloud in order at the boat, and the reason entered in the yard's own book with the clerk's absence and the absence of any instrument entered rather than filled in.** He reads **one seam** — mendings under mendings in the *Hearthwake's* garboard, in six feet of water in the dark, on the first call down — **and THIS TIME HE DOES NOT SUPPLY THE REST, and the reason is on the page: a man who has been wrong in public in a market once about the word *chain* does not get to be the man who supplies the rest of a sentence about a boat with people in her.** He loses about a second and a half and it is the cost of the volume. **He is not repaired and the volume does not let him off.** No stage was taken in this batch and none may be.
- **Nessa Pike**, twenty-seven. The tender. **She never says the word, and the reason is entered, and the reason is that a man of thirty-two going to a boy of seventeen was not going to be stopped by a woman in a boat in about nineteen minutes, and if it had been twenty-five she would have pulled it.** She did not say the reader thing a fourth time and the reason is on the page. **She is the person holding the pencil in the last line of Chapter 150, and she knows she is, and she has said on the record that if she is ever the one holding it, the thing she told him not to write down on the seventh of August is the thing to check.**
- **Bevin Rook**, twenty-two. **HE IS IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AS A PERSON. He was not written to, not visited, and nobody asked anybody who has seen him what he said, and the three nots went in on the eighth of June and are not being taken out.** The first column says, in the yard's own hand and with a date, that he cannot do the thing he was kept for. **His forty marks stand whole, unpaid, not reduced, not asked to be reduced, and were NOT paid, repaired, resolved or reduced in this batch. The head of the third column carries two times in his hand and a request to scrape them out is refused.** The final chapter's pencil is not his.
- **Mara Venn**, thirty. **NOT ON THE CREW, NOT ASKED FOR ANYTHING BY THIS PARTY, AND PRESENT IN TWO CHAPTERS AS A PERSON WHO USES A ROOM.** On Friday the fourth a woman of the second lay asks her a question in the market office and she answers at nothing **once**, says so in front of nine people, says the yard did not ask and would not have got a different figure, and says the not-saying costs her and not the boat. On Friday the eleventh she is at the long table and gives six things about the bells and the seventh thing, which is the boundary of her trade. **Her forty marks are unpaid, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, and were not paid in this batch. She did not break with the Registry, which is Volume 4 and a different event, and no chapter of this batch fuses the two. She did not leave the harbor.**
- **Ors Kest**, 44. **He spends authority a third time, on Sunday the sixth of August, in his own book, at his own cost, and the price lands on the third lay again and on his own office's standing in a market. HE SAYS ON THE MOLE'S STEPS THAT THERE IS NOTHING IN HIS BOOK THAT WILL PUT A BOAT OF HIS PAST THE OUTER MARKS AND NOTHING IN THE HOLDER'S EITHER, AND THAT THIS IS NOT A FAILURE OF EITHER BOOK, IT IS THE DESIGN. HE IS NOT A FRIEND, HE IS NOT A HERO, THE VOLUME DOES NOT PRETEND HE WAS RIGHT ABOUT EVERYTHING, AND HE HAS NO SECOND SEASON IN HIS KEEPING AND A SPENT LEDGER.** The plate is not mentioned, not asked about, and not reached.
- **Perry Ollett**, 39, of the *Sallow Reach*, a crew of Ashtide, six men, a compressor of her own, forty fathoms of her own hose. **HE IS NOT A VILLAIN, NOT A RESCUER, NOT AN OFFICE AND NOT A DEVICE. HE NAMES SIX MARKS IN A DOORWAY ON A SUNDAY AND IS PAID THREE AT THE MOLE AND THREE IS A DEBT, AND HE SAYS OUT LOUD THAT HE IS NOT DOING IT BECAUSE HE IS A GOOD MAN, THAT HE WILL SAY THE FIGURE HIMSELF IF ANYBODY GETS IT WRONG, AND THAT IF A BOY GOES OFF HER FOREDECK HE IS GOING IN ON A LINE OFF HIS OWN BOW AND WILL REFUSE THE YARD'S LINE, BECAUSE A BOAT OF HIS ALONGSIDE A BOAT OF THEIRS WITH A POT IN IT IS A THING THAT HAS GONE WRONG IN OTHER WATERS.** One of his two men said a word in his own language when the case went down and the other said nothing, and that is the whole of what another crew saw of the choice.
- **Harl Dace** is not in this batch and is not asked. **The man of fifty-one with a bad wheel** is not in this batch, not interviewed, not on a lead, not hired. **Noll Tarner** is not in this batch by name and is not thanked. **Torren Skell** is not used. **Ewan Tristram** is not used. **Rowan Trewin** is not in this batch by name. Ambrose Sell, Magister Ilya Serr and Halven Rook are not in this batch.**
- **THE CREW AND PEOPLE OF THE *HEARTHWAKE*. NESSA VALE, thirty-eight, of the Sill, who said the name on the mole's steps on the tenth and would not say it twice; AWN PROUDE, her brother-in-law; TOM KITTOW; and EWAN KITTOW, seventeen, who went into the water on the eighth and was gone after by a man of this yard, and who had put a line round his own waist before he went over the rail. THEY ARE THE PEOPLE THE VOLUME IS ABOUT AND THEY ARE NOT CARGO AND THEY ARE NOT A DEVICE AND THEY ARE NOT A RESCUE SERVICE. THEY HAVE NO BOAT, NO WAGES AND ABOUT NINE MARKS BETWEEN THEM, THEY ASKED THIS YARD FOR NOTHING, AND THE YARD HAS ENTERED THAT IT WOULD HAVE LIKED TO AND HAD NOTHING.**
- **Alden Marr**, 61. **At the far bench on Thursday the twenty-seventh, on Monday the thirty-first and Tuesday the first, and on Monday the seventh. Not asked for anything on any of the four days, asks nothing, and Calder Marr does not go over to him on any of them, and the yard has entered that he did not and that neither brother has said why and neither brother is going to.** No fragment, no report talk, no second story, no confession.
- **The harbor authority's clerk** is in the hearing room on the twelfth of August and keeps his own page and says for the seventh time this year what he cannot enter, and the thing he cannot enter this time is a person of no office having five bells at five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards. **He is not an antagonist and did not become one.**
- **A man of fifty-one who is better on a lead than anybody in that yard** is not in this batch and is not a reason that can be entered in a book. **A man of the tide gang, Tam Rundle, thirty.** **A woman of the second lay, Nell Ardery, sixty-odd, whose mother was on the Sill.** **A man of a boat of the second lay, sixty, forty-one years on this water, who asked the volume's question.** **A man in a coat, Amos Trewhella, of an office in a city whose name the clerk wrote down and this yard has not repeated, who is not a friend and is not an enemy and is not going to be either.**
- **Captain Veyra Holt** is not in a room, is not on a mole, and no face-to-face meeting occurs. **She is described from a distance and from a paper.**

### 6. What this batch may not be used for

1. **It may not be used to name the condition again.** It is spent in Chapter 137 and this batch carries the bare fact twice with a reason and without a name. **A later volume may not put the word in against him.**
2. **It may not be used to ask the bellwright again, in a shed or in a room, and may not print a digit, and may not open the chest.** He is in no chapter of this batch as a person.
3. **It may not be used to enter a ninth acknowledgement, to lift, narrow, re-serve or alter the notice, to reopen the eighth-clause plan, to reach the plate at the fifth seam, to put a person, boat or lead of this yard inside the first clause, to widen the writ's ground, to add a fifth name, or to manufacture a second season.**
4. **It may not be used to make a fifth return, or to move the counting rule, or to report the work of the eighth and ninth of August to any office.** The clause does not reach past the outer marks and the party has entered that it told no office and will tell no office.
5. **It may not be used to name a cause for the glass storm, to let it speak, to name the Nine Harbors, the Quietening or the Crown Seal, to explain the nine-line mark, to identify the marking on the shard, to name the compartment or to state that the sealed case was in standing space number four, to quote a custody number's digits, to put a senior examiner in a room, to name an office of returning memory, to have Captain Holt in a room or on a mole, to ask Sera Mott, to introduce a new antagonist, institution or final enemy, or to give Torren Skell an office, a ship, a uniform or a title.**
6. **It may not be used to resolve the coast road, to name it a network, to connect it to anything under this town, to connect the join to anything under this town, or to name the nine final routes.**
7. **It may not be used to pay Bevin Rook's forty marks, to write to him, to go and see him, to ask anybody who has seen him what he said, to reduce the figure, to stop the wage, to hire a boy to read a position back, or to treat the wage as a claim on his June.**
8. **It may not be used to pay Mara Venn's forty marks, to pay either woman's eight shillings and tenpence, to re-write the chandler's note, to move any of its three counts of weeks, to move the count of eleven copies, to weigh, price or offer the bell-metal gun-port, or to sell one mark's worth of the heap behind the shed.**
9. **It may not be used to make Perry Ollett a villain, an office, a rescuer or a device; to make the coast office, the man in a city, the man in the coat or the reeve a villain; to make the harbor's poverty better. A cistern holds, a mole's footing is going to be looked at, the third lay has lost four days and now more, the light is not on, the north berth is out, the hard at Ashtide is unanswered, nineteen winters are in a file, and a third lay of a working harbor is poorer on the fifteenth of August than it was on the sixth of July.**
10. **It may not be used to stage a dive of the holder's own outside the six conditions, to take any power stage, or to have a seam settle an argument, prove a motive, convict anybody, or tell him what happened.**
11. **It may not be used to name a posting before the defeat that follows it. THE POSTING IS NOW DECIDED ON THE PAGE WITH ITS DATES AND THE CHANNEL IS RULED AND APPLIED TWICE.**
12. **It may not be used to answer the volume's question.** The question *who changed the rule* was put in a market in this harbor on Sunday the thirteenth of August in about forty people's hearing, was not answered, and the not-answering is in the first column in the yard's own hand with the reason against it. **The Nine Harbors are not named as a compact anywhere and the word *Harbors* is not on the page of the fifteenth of August anywhere.**

### 7. The costs this batch closes with

1. **THE BELL-ARCHIVE. A case of five bells under the after hatch of the *Hearthwake*, about five and a half fathoms down inside the four hundred yards, off a mole nobody is allowed to go past on a writ, on a vessel claimed whole by an office in a city. It was half out of the water on a line round a man's forearm when a boy of seventeen went off a foredeck into seven fathoms, and HE LET GO OF THE LINE, and the case went down straight and the grating lid was the last of it. IT IS THE ONLY LOCAL COPY OF A COMMUNITY'S ACCOUNT OF ITSELF. THE COMMUNITY IS FOUR PEOPLE WHO ARE ALIVE AND WHO HAVE NO BOAT. THE PERSON IT BELONGS TO IS NOT MISSING AND CANNOT BE TOLD IN A MARKET, AND WAS NOT: the page of the fifteenth of August says where the bells are and does not pretend that anybody is coming for them.**
2. **THE CHOICE, MADE WITH HIS HANDS, AND THE TWO THINGS BOTH TRUE.** The act proves the crew's reliability — four people of a community of this coast are alive because a yard with no money and no instrument went past its own outer marks on a Tuesday morning — **and it destroys the thing they were going to prove it with, and both of those are true and neither of them cancels the other, and that sentence is said three times in this batch and by three different people: FIRST BY NESSA VALE OF THE SILL, in the open air on a mole's steps on the tenth, in the middle of eleven minutes of testimony, a quarter of an hour before the holder says it in the same scene — where he says it again and then yields the first saying to her in front of forty people; NEXT BY THE HOLDER, in that same scene, refusing to go round the side of a limit four people wrote; AND LAST BY ELIAS VOKE IN A MARKET ON THE THIRTEENTH, WHO SAID IT ONCE AND WOULD NOT SAY IT TWICE. **THE YARD'S OWN BOOK HOLDS IT THREE TIMES — OUT OF A ROOM IN APRIL, OUT OF A MARKET IN JULY, AND OUT OF THE MARKET ON THE THIRTEENTH — AND THE HOLDER HAS ENTERED THAT HE DID NOT WRITE DOWN THE ONE SAID ON THE MOLE ON THE TENTH.**
3. **THE SENTENCE. THE YARD BROKE A SENTENCE IT PUBLISHED IN A MARKET ON THE FIFTH OF JULY AND REPEATED IN A MARKET ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH, AND THE BREAKING WAS ENTERED IN THE FIRST COLUMN IN ADVANCE, ON THE SEVENTH, IN THE HOLDER'S OWN LEFT HAND, WITH THE COST UNDER IT. THE COST IS THE SENTENCE ITSELF, WHICH CANNOT BE SAID AGAIN, and the reason is on the page: a yard that has said a thing in a market and has not done it is a yard whose saying is worth nothing in any figure that yard puts on any wall in any year after this one.**
4. **A WAGE OF FOUR MARKS NOT PAID ON TUESDAY THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST, and the envelope not made, and four marks short, and the money that would have paid it spent on a pot of air and on a day of another crew's boat. The wage column stops at sixteen lines and sixty-four marks and the fifth of March is still the oldest.**
5. **THREE MARKS OWING TO PERRY OLLETT OF THE *SALLOW REACH* FOR THE EIGHTH OF AUGUST, a debt to a man of another crew, with a date on it, which is not in any column of this book and is not going to be paid this month.**
6. **A BOY OF TWENTY-TWO WHO CANNOT DO THE THING HE WAS KEPT FOR, SAID IN THE YARD'S OWN HAND ON THE FIRST OF AUGUST WITH A DATE ON IT, AND THE THREE NOTS STANDING, AND THE HEAD OF THE THIRD COLUMN KEEPING HIS HAND.**
7. **THE MAP NOBODY ELSE KNEW ABOUT. The yard's advantage was a thing nobody else had and it is gone and it is gone twice over: because the party published the fourth return and the nine places on the twenty-second of July, and because on the fifteenth of August the nine places went up on a market wall under a naval paper with the place of every copy of it on it, in about sixty boats, and one copy of it is in a city going to press.**
8. **THE QUESTION, ASKED IN A MARKET AND NOT ANSWERED, AND THE REFUSAL ON THE PAGE.**
9. **A THIRD LAY THAT LOST FOUR DAYS OF THE BEST OF ITS SEASON IN THE FIRST WEEK OF AUGUST to an examination with no money behind it, and a reeve with nothing left to put in his book, and a third lay that is not this party and is not going to be asked to pay anything and is not going to be thanked and is not going to be used.**
10. **THE FINAL IMAGE, IN THE YARD'S OWN HAND: one herring berth short, a mooring out of service with an end of it seventeen days off, nineteen winters unpaid, a mole-head light that is not on, a fire party's hose that will not be rolled until the autumn, a page on a market wall under a naval paper, a person going out in a boat in about four years because the whole of the craft is the going out, a boy who is not on the stone, a pencil in somebody else's hand, and a delay of a hand's width of a second that nobody has said why.**

### 8. Proposed canon changes

**None applied. Four flagged rather than applied, and six author-level decisions taken on the page.**

1. **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THE BELL-ARCHIVE AND ITS LOSS.** **A working vessel of one of the nine communities, the *Hearthwake*, of about forty tons, of a community called the Sill, this harbor's own weir, whose sill stone lies under four feet of stone at the south end of this mole and a foot and four inches above the third of the six joints.** Four people in her since the second of June, pumping since about the twenty-first of July, a list of four foot by the twenty-ninth, grounded by the head on the second of August by a grapnel from the *Vigil's* own tender, and hollow to about the fourth of August. **A case of five bells under her after hatch, not a set, a line cut into the lip of each that is not a letter in any hand on this coast, the largest about a foot across the mouth; the oldest about a hundred and forty years by the sound of it; the other four being the ones the community could not leave behind; the rest lost in a fire in a year nobody in this batch names.** The bells went back into the water out of Calder Marr's hands on the eighth of August at about twenty past three and are at the bottom. **THE ARCHIVE IS LOST, IRREVERSIBLY, AND NOBODY ELSE'S FINDING IS SUBSTITUTED FOR IT.**
2. **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — CAPTAIN HOLT'S DEFEAT AND POSTING, WITH DATES, VIA THE WRIT.** **THE DEFEAT IS NOT A COURT AND NOT A BATTLE. The *Vigil* enforced a Crown claim on a working vessel of a community of this coast by nailing a paper about it on the market wall of a working harbor, in daylight, in a place where a return made under a party's own writ was on the wall under it; the enforcement was emptied in the open by a claim that is not sustainable in the form it was brought, once a Crown notice has been publicly enforced over a boat with living people in her and publicly emptied; and the officer was relieved of the vessel and of the command of this coast. THE POSTING, DECIDED IN A PAPER READ IN A HEARING ROOM ON SATURDAY THE TWELFTH OF AUGUST, DATED, DESCRIBED FROM A DISTANCE, AND ENTERED IN THE YARD'S OWN BOOK WITH ITS DATES: the *Vigil* is not to be employed upon this coast; Captain Veyra Holt is relieved of that vessel and of the command of this coast FROM THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST, and is posted FROM THE FIRST OF SEPTEMBER to the making up of the coast's wreck lists, in a city, at a table, with every vessel reported, claimed, entered and closed upon this coast for the year and the returns of the officers employed upon it. She does not come to this harbor and has not been in it. The posting is not a command in this harbor, is not an inspection of or over the harbor authority, is not a commission, and puts no person, no boat and no lead past the outer marks or past the imperial line, and the yard checked that against its own entry of the fifth of July and the check holds. THE SECOND SEASON OF THE WRIT IS NOT GRANTED, NOT REFUSED AND NOT DEALT WITH BY THAT PAPER, and the writ runs to the first of March next. The channel is the writ and no second channel exists.**
3. **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — HOW THE OUTER MARKS WERE HANDLED.** **The party broke its own published sentence, and the breaking, its date, its hour and its whole cost were entered in the first column in advance on Monday the seventh of August at nine in the evening, and the party went on its own account with no instrument of any office, inside the four-hundred-yard line and about three miles inside the imperial line, and told no office and will tell no office.** The choice was forced by the structure and not by a loophole: the *Vigil's* undertaking of the twenty-first of June means no dispute can happen inside the harbor line, and the party said in a market on the twenty-sixth of July that it was not going past the outer marks, and the boat with the people in her was off the outer marks. **THE COST ON THE PAGE IS THE SENTENCE, WHICH CANNOT BE SAID AGAIN, AND A WAGE NOT PAID, AND THREE MARKS OWING TO A MAN OF ANOTHER CREW.**
4. **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THE CHANDLER'S NOTE IS NOT RE-WRITTEN AND IT IS SAID OUT LOUD.** See § 2. The three counts of weeks do not move and the refusal of the thirty-first of March stands unreversed.
5. **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THE NINE PLACES AS THE FINAL PAGE CARRIES THEM, AND *UNDER A NAVAL PAPER*.** **The page of the fifteenth of August carries the nine as this yard is able to state them and calls the thing nothing: six by a builder's own figures at this harbor's own second seam, with a day against each and no year because the book gives none in any hand the yard can read; and three as the books give them, being this harbor's own weir called the Sill, a village not on this coast and not on any chart anybody here has seen, and a sand and shell lay of this coast not on any chart in this harbor. THE LIST IS NOT A COMPACT, THE NINE ARE NOT THE NINE HARBORS, AND THE WORD *HARBORS* IS NOT ON THE PAGE ANYWHERE. AND *UNDER A NAVAL PAPER* IS SETTLED IN THE PROSE: the *Vigil's* notice of the fourth of August was nailed on the market wall on the outside of whatever was there, so when the yard's own sheet went up on Tuesday the fifteenth of August on the same nails, on the outside of whatever was there, it went up UNDER the naval paper, in the sense that the naval paper is above it, and the arrangement has been the arrangement since the ninth of November, and the shipwright says so out loud in a market and refuses to pretend that a sheet under a naval paper is a sheet on its own.** The yard's own fourth return of the twenty-second of July has been under four papers since the twenty-eighth of July and is still on that wall.
6. **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THE LIMIT ON THE TESTIMONY, AND WHO BEARS IT.** **A bell-archive is not a thing that is read out; it is a thing that is struck, in a hall, in front of the people it is about, and there is nobody in this town it is about. Four people of the Sill wrote that themselves, in their own words, on a mole's steps on the tenth of August, in about forty people's hearing, and signed it, and it is a real limit and not a formality: they will not say it in a shed, they will not say it into a book that goes out of this harbor, they will not say it to a man with a seal, they will not say one word about who took their vessel off them, and they will not give their names to any office in any city. SOMEBODY BEARS IT: the four bear it, because they have given a local account of a local practice and got nothing for it and are owed a thing they cannot have; and the holder bears it, because he asked for it to be lifted on the tenth, could not lift it, did not ask twice, and has entered the asking on the page with a date.**
7. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the two "everything in her" clauses on the *Vigil's* notices.** The notice of the fourth of August adds *the said vessel and every part of her contents being thereby Crown material* to the first notice's *any part of her*, and Mara Venn says in the market office that it is the only line in either paper that reads like a man writing to himself and that she does not know why a man writes a longer word in a longer notice, and has entered that she does not know. **No person is identified, no informer is implied, no office is named, and nothing is resolved. A later volume may account for it; this volume may not, and this batch does not.**
8. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the practical cause of the *Hearthwake's* loss.** The party has no instrument, no money and no trade on this coast that reaches five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards, and no office in this harbor will send anybody, and the reeve has said so out loud in his own book and on a mole's steps. **Whether any later volume supplies a way, and at what price, and to whom it pays, is not decided here and was not decided in the chapters.**
9. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the date of the *Vigil's* relief and the date of the posting are the fifteenth and the first of September, and the second season is undecided.** A Volume 03 audit and the Volume 04 planning phase should rule whether the relief lands before or after the first of September, because the yard's own sentence about what a posting may not be is checked on the page and the check holds as written.
10. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the attribution of the volume's closing sentence.** `outline/volume-03.md` says the volume's argument is "a sentence a fisher said in Volume 02." **No fisher in Volume 02 is recorded as having said it: the sentence is Calder Marr's in a room in Chapter 97, and the same shape is Ors Kest's in a room in Chapter 58, and neither man is a fisher.** Chapter 149 therefore gives it to **Elias Voke**, a herring skipper of fifty-one, in a market in this harbor on the thirteenth of August, in his own mouth, on his own nineteen winters, and has him refuse to say it twice — which is what the outline asks the volume for, and is recorded here so that no later phase goes looking for a Chapter in Volume 02 that does not say it.
11. **STILL OPEN AND STILL UNDECIDED, EXPLICITLY — the answer inside the delayed note, the ninth-line mark, the marking on the shard, the three places with no joint, the coast road, the first echo's witness, the run under this town, the compartment, the custody items, the lead plug's number, and the plate at the fifth seam.** Nothing in this batch touched any of them and no chapter of it may be used to touch them.
