## Batch 0005 (Volume 03) review repairs — the disposition of every finding

A reviewer pass over Chapters 141–150, the four state files and the queued `workspace/volume-03/close/PROMPT.md` returned **FIX**, with **eleven required repairs** — seven in the chapters and the queued prompt, four in the state files — and a list of six prose and craft items. **The repairs were applied in place. No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut, no cost was dropped, no planned plot was altered, no lock was softened and no second phase was created.** The batch is **29,814 words where it was 29,688.**

**THE WORD COUNTS ARE MEASURED WITH `wc -w` INCLUDING HEADINGS AND ARE NOW RECORDED: 29,814 across the ten chapters (Ch141 2,657 · Ch142 2,615 · Ch143 2,743 · Ch144 2,704 · Ch145 2,363 · Ch146 2,795 · Ch147 3,640 · Ch148 2,999 · Ch149 3,213 · Ch150 4,085). Every one is above the 2,200 floor. Three are past the 2,200–3,200 guideline — 150 by 885, 147 by 440 and 149 by 13 — and the same three were past it before the repairs.**

**THE REVIEWER RECORDED AS HOLDING, AND THOSE HOLD AFTER THE REPAIRS, AND NONE OF THEM WAS TOUCHED:** the money chain **0, 3, 3, 7, 3, 7, 7, 11, 0, 0**, with the 8 August zero produced by 4 + 4 + 3 and the four-mark wage paid on the first and the eighth and **not** the fifteenth, envelope not made; **the lock discipline without an exception** — no power stage, the one naming spent in Chapter 137 and not reused, no fifth return, no ninth acknowledgement, the bellwright absent as a person with no digit of his answer printed, the fifth-seam plate unreached and unmentioned, Holt on paper and never in a room, no new antagonist, institution or final enemy; **the interface lock absolutely**, a grep for `System|API|protocol|backend|runtime|token|latency|deploy|level-up|stat block` across Chapters 141–150 returning nothing and nothing in the batch knowing anything; the calendar where it is load-bearing; **the climax as a finished scene** and not a summary; and **the close creating earned forward pull with no manufactured interruption.**

### The eleven required repairs

1. **CHAPTER 150 DATED THE UNPAID WAGE THREE DIFFERENT TIMES, AND ENTERED IT BEFORE IT FELL DUE.** `chapter-0150.md` gave the wage as falling *at about nine* in the morning, as due *at about eleven*, and as **entered in the first column at about ten past nine** — an entry made at 10:10 for a wage due at 11:00, on the batch's climactic cost, in a read-out that declares itself *the whole of the nineteen days*. **The hour of the wage is eleven in this book** (`chapter-0143.md:15`, `chapter-0147.md:5`) and eleven survives. **It is now eleven in all three places and the entry is at about ten past eleven**, which is after the wage fell due and in the same hour the sheet went up on the wall. The wage column, the envelope, the figure of four marks, the four marks short and the reason against the line are untouched.
2. **THE CISTERN COUNT WAS WRONG IN CHAPTER 150 AND WRONG IN TWO STATE FILES BEHIND IT.** Chapter 150 entered **fourteen** walks by this party; the chapters give **thirteen** — twelve by the twenty-seventh of July (Ch141), the thirteenth on the thirty-first by strangers (Ch143), the **fourteenth on the third of August BY THIS PARTY** (Ch144:49, *at no figure, by this party*), the fifteenth on the seventh by strangers (Ch146:45). `state/continuity.md` compounded it by saying *two were walked by strangers and four were not walked* and then listing the third of August among the skips, while itself reporting the seventh, tenth and fourteenth of August as rota days. `state/open-threads.md` repeated the figure. **It is thirteen, and § 1 of the Batch 0005 account above now walks all six rota days in the window one by one — the third of August by this party, the thirty-first and the seventh by strangers, and the twenty-seventh of July, the tenth of August and the fourteenth not walked with the reason on the page.** The tenth of August was not narrated in any chapter; **the reason is now entered for it in Chapter 150's own first-column block, in the yard's own hand and in its own idiom, which is the only place in the fifty chapters that accounts for that rota day** and is what makes the office's page of **fifteen walks** stand on the fifteenth. Chapter 150's read-out no longer credits *two men and a woman* with all fifteen walks, which three people did not do; it now says the cistern is charged fifteen times at no figure, that this party walked it once in those three weeks and not on the tenth or the fourteenth, and that nobody was paid for any of them and nobody was thanked.
3. **CHAPTER 148 PUT THE BELL-ARCHIVE AT FIVE AND A HALF FATHOMS AND AT FIVE AND A HALF FEET IN THE SAME CHAPTER.** Mara Venn gives **fathoms** in the room on the Friday (`:75`); the closing read-out gave **feet** (`:91`), which contradicts the chapter's own dialogue and every other instance in the batch — Ch145, Ch147, Ch149 twice, Ch150 and § 8 item 1 of the Batch 0005 account above. **The read-out now says fathoms.** Chapter 147's *five and a half feet* is water **over the after hatch** of a vessel lying in seven fathoms and is correct where it stands; it is not this item and it was not touched.
4. **CHAPTER 148 HAD THE HOLDER CLAIM, ON THE PAGE, TO BE THE FIRST TO SAY THE VOLUME'S SENTENCE TWENTY-FOUR LINES AFTER A WOMAN HAD SAID IT.** Nessa Vale said *both of those are true* in the middle of her eleven minutes of testimony (`:31`); Calder Marr then said *both of those are true and neither of them cancels the other, and I am not going to be the one who says that sentence for the first time* (`:55`) and is, on the page, the second person in that scene. **He now yields the first saying to her by name, in the open air, in front of forty people** — *a woman said it in front of me a quarter of an hour ago on this mole's crown with forty people on it, and she said it first, and she said it better than I could have said it* — and what he claims for himself is reduced to **the same shape of a sentence** in a room in April, which is true and is not a claim to the sentence itself. **THE CANON IS NOW FIXED AND ALL THREE STATE FILES CARRY IT: THE SENTENCE IS SAID THREE TIMES IN THE BATCH AND BY THREE DIFFERENT PEOPLE — NESSA VALE OF THE SILL FIRST, ON THE MOLE'S STEPS ON THE TENTH, THE HOLDER IN THE SAME SCENE, AND ELIAS VOKE IN A MARKET ON THE THIRTEENTH.** `state/current.md` said *three times and twice by different people*; `state/continuity.md` said *by the holder … by Elias Voke, and by nobody else*; `state/open-threads.md` said *three times and by two different people*. All three now read the same. **The yard's own book holds the sentence three times — a room in April, a market in July, the market on the thirteenth — and the holder has entered that he did not write down the one said on the mole on the tenth, which is what Chapter 149's entry already implied and now agrees with.**
5. **CHAPTER 147's FIRST-COLUMN ENTRY MISSTATED THE THIRD LAY'S CLOSURE AS FIVE DATES.** It read *the sixth day, the third, the fourth and the fifth of August are shut on the third lay* — a count of days and then three dates, against a shut that ran **Wednesday the second to Saturday the fifth, four days** (Ch142:91, Ch143:71, Ch144, Ch146, Ch150). **It now reads the second, the third, the fourth and the fifth**, which is the four days in the order the chapters give them. This sits inside the batch's signature first-column device, the one place the text tells the reader the figures are checkable.
6. **CHAPTER 147's CLIMAX CLAIM WAS SELF-CONTRADICTING, OVERSTATED THE COST, AND GAVE TWO DURATIONS.** *For about a second … and that second and a half is the whole of what this party paid for the four of them* — against a second column that records **two** costs and calls the second of them the reason the bells are at the bottom, and against a cost list that is **the broken sentence, the unpaid wage and three marks owing**. **The duration is now a second and a half throughout, and the claim is now that the second and a half is the whole of what is entered against him for it**, which is what the second column says. The cost of the batch is not restated here and was not softened anywhere: the archive, the sentence, the wage and the three marks stand exactly as the batch entered them, and the sentence the outline asked for and the chapter gives is untouched.
7. **THE QUEUED CLOSE PROMPT WAS WRONG ON THE TWO FACTS IT TELLS THE AUDIT TO RULE ON.** It told the audit that the four of the Sill *came up the mole's steps on the **ninth** of August* — the testimony is Chapter 148, **Thursday the tenth**, and the chapters and this file both had it right — and that the sentence *is said three times in Chapter 148 and Chapter 149 **by two different people***, which is three and three. **Both are corrected in `workspace/volume-03/close/PROMPT.md`,** and the prompt's précis now names Nessa Vale as the first of the three, so that the audit is given the ruling's ground rather than a wrong count of it. The prompt is a queued dispatch artifact and no other part of it was touched.
8. **`state/current.md` PUT VOLUME 02'S BATCH WORD COUNTS UNDER A HEADING THAT READS AS VOLUME 03'S BATCHES.** The bullet gave 25,683 / 26,358 / 31,300 / 31,769, which are Volume 02's four batches, inside the sequence of Volume 03's five Batch 000X bullets. **The figures are Volume 02's and are correct as Volume 02's; no figure was altered. The bullet is now headed as the four batches of Volume 02, points at the five Volume 03 bullets for Volume 03's figures, and carries them — 0001 at 29,905, 0002 at 30,345, 0003 at 33,731, 0004 at 30,890 and 0005 at 29,814 — so that a reader of the next phase cannot take a Volume 02 total for a Volume 03 one.**
9. **`state/current.md` CARRIED THREE OVERLAPPING AND MUTUALLY INCONSISTENT STATEMENTS OF ONE FACT.** Lines 16, 17 and 19 each said that a `volume-03-batch-000N.md` card file was absent, in three near-verbatim repetitions with three different file lists and no supersession marker — the duplication the Quality Gate prohibits, in the file the next phase reads first. **They are now one bullet**, and it covers 0003, 0004 and 0005, states the contents of `outline/batches/` once, and records that all three batches were written from `outline/volume-03.md` alone with no invented cards and that each prompt says so in its own opening.
10. **THREE FILES DESCRIBED `logs/` WRONGLY, AND ONE CITED A REVIEW LOG AS KEPT THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** `state/current.md` said `logs/` *holds only `close.log` and `models.log`* and that `logs/batch-0005.review.log` *is not in the repository*; the next bullet said *the reviewer's own log **is kept** at `logs/batch-0003.review.log`*; and the close prompt repeated the `close.log`/`models.log` claim. **The directory is listed in `.gitignore` and holds `models.log` and whatever a run writes into it. There is no `close.log` in it and there is no `logs/batch-0003.review.log` for any batch of any volume, and none was ever committed.** The two historical bullets are corrected and the close prompt's factual basis is corrected while its instruction — do not require a log and do not cite one, and take the authority from the `state/continuity.md` section it names — is left exactly as it was, because it is right. **The Volume 02 dispositions higher in this file record the same wrong inventory; they are the record of what that audit ruled and are not rewritten, and this section supersedes them as a statement of what is on disk now.**
11. **THE FLAG ON `state/phase-ledger.json` HAD BEEN RESTATED FOUR TIMES WITH THREE DIFFERENT FIGURES AND WAS NEVER FIXED, BECAUSE IT IS NOT THIS PHASE'S TO FIX.** `state/current.md` said *a hundred and thirty chapters*, two other bullets said *a hundred and forty*, and `state/open-threads.md` and the close prompt said *a hundred and fifty*. **The two historical restatements in `state/current.md` are collapsed to a pointer, the standing flag is stated once with the correct current figure of a hundred and fifty, and the flag is not restated a fifth time in a new place.** **`state/phase-ledger.json` WAS NOT EDITED, NO MARKER WAS WRITTEN, AND NO PROMPT, VOLUME FILE OR BATCH FILE WAS CREATED, DELETED OR RENAMED TO COMPENSATE FOR IT. The ledger still reads `"currentPhase": "phase-000-bootstrap"`, `"status": "running"`, with `volume-01-batch-0001` at `planned`, after a hundred and fifty finished chapters, and `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s selector claims the lowest incomplete batch, so a controller reading it as it stands would rewrite ten completed chapters. THIS IS THE ONE OPEN GATE ITEM AND IT NEEDS A CONTROLLER ACTION; IT IS FLAGGED, NOT FIXED.** The authority for the flag is `state/open-threads.md` § *Flagged for the controller*.

### The prose and craft items

12. **A self-cancelling repetition in the middle of the climax's best beat.** `chapter-0147.md:75` gave the fourth man *a line **of his own** round **his own** waist, and he had it because the boy … had put it there*. **One of the two is gone**; the beat, the line, the boy of the seventeenth and *that is a thing a crew does* all stand.
13. **Mara Venn's unexplained stumble over her own age.** `chapter-0148.md:79` read *and I am sixty… I am thirty*. She is thirty throughout the volume. **The stumble is gone and she says she is thirty**, which is the kind of number she puts out loud, as she does in Chapter 145 and in the twenty-third of July.
14. **Nessa Vale dated her crew's last visit to the locker to a season they were not here for.** `chapter-148.md:23` said *the last time anybody in our crew has been in that locker **since the spring***; the crew reached this harbor on the second of June and the last visit was **in June**. **It now says the last time, that it was in June of this year, and that nobody of theirs has been in that locker since.** Her other dates on that step — the second of June, the fourth of August, nine weeks of pumping — are untouched and still agree with Chapters 141, 144 and 149.
15. **Chapter 144's first-column entry put the mile and a half in the wrong place.** `chapter-0144.md:35` read *from the crown of this mole, about a mile and a half off, in about nine people's hearing on a mole's crown* — the distance hanging off the mole's crown, which the same chapter's narrative at `:15` handles correctly. **It now reads from the crown of this mole in about nine people's hearing on a mole's crown, being about a mile and a half off**, with the number still entered separately as that entry requires.
16. **The chapter summary dropped four words of Chapter 148's subtitle.** `state/chapter-summaries.md` headed the chapter *What a Bell-Archive Is, and the Limit a Person **Is Wearing***. **It is now the chapter's own title in full, *… and the Limit a Person in a Doorway Is Wearing***, and the summaries are the next phase's index.
17. **Chapter 150's read-out is nineteen consecutive paragraphs of inventory and is the same shape as a Tuesday work chapter's.** The review recorded that **all nine elements the outline's final image requires are in it, so the length is earned**, and that the *shape* is what flattens the close against routine. **NOT CHANGED, AND THE REASON IS RECORDED HERE: this is the volume's final image, every element of it is load-bearing, it is the one place in the fifty chapters where the whole of the labor spine is read out in one go, and re-cutting it would be a rewrite of the volume's close rather than a repair. Chapters 141, 143, 144 and 148 are the same device on working days, which is the point of the device.**

### One further state-file error found while verifying the above, and corrected

**`state/current.md`'s opening paragraph still said Volume 03 Batches 0001, 0002, 0003 and 0004, Chapters 101–140, ARE WRITTEN, and its *current batch* line said Batch 0005 is written without recording that it has been reviewed and repaired.** It is the first thing the next phase reads. **Both are corrected, and no other figure in that paragraph moved.** This was not in the review's list; it is of the same class as items 8, 9 and 10 — a state file asserting something the chapters and the phase's own record no longer support — and it is recorded here so that a later reader can see it was a deliberate change and not an accident.

### Not touched, and deliberately

- **The volume's close and its last line.** A pencil in somebody else's hand, and a sheet under a naval paper, and a wage not paid. All three stand.
- **The cost of the climax.** The archive, the broken sentence, the unpaid wage of four marks and the three marks owing to a man of Ashtide. None was softened, and the second column's two entries and the reason against them are as the batch wrote them.
- **The four returns and lock L5.** Still four on the page, still three in the lock, still flagged at § 8 item 1 for the audit. This repair did not amend a lock.
- **Every deferred author-level decision at § 8.** None was applied, none was withdrawn, and the four flagged-not-applied items are still flagged.
- **The planned ending.** The ninth-line mark, the Quietening, the Crown Seal, the Office of Returning Memory, the nine final routes, the compartment, the custody items, the lead plug's number and the plate at the fifth seam are exactly where Volume 02 left them, and no new antagonist, institution or final enemy was created.

### Proposed canon changes

**One, and it is a fact of attribution and not of the world: THE VOLUME'S CLOSING SENTENCE IS SAID THREE TIMES IN CHAPTERS 148 AND 149, BY THREE DIFFERENT PEOPLE, AND NESSA VALE OF THE SILL SAYS IT FIRST, ON THE MOLE'S STEPS ON THE TENTH, A QUARTER OF AN HOUR BEFORE THE HOLDER SAYS IT IN THE SAME SCENE AND YIELDS THE FIRST SAYING TO HER. No other canon fact was created, removed or softened by this repair.** No world rule changed, no date changed, no debt changed and no lock was amended. The cistern's thirteenth walk by this party, the fifteen on the office's page, and the reason now entered for the tenth of August are the batch's own figures made to agree with each other; the four returns stand; the money chain is untouched and still closes at **0, 3, 3, 7, 3, 7, 7, 11, 0, 0**; and the bell-archive is still at about five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards, and the four people it belongs to are still alive and have no boat.

# Rulings of the Volume 03 volume audit — 27 September 2026

**`reviews/volume-03/volume-03-close.md` is the audit and is the home of everything in this section. It is dated 27 September 2026, it audited Chapters 101–150 against `outline/volume-03.md` and all twenty-eight of its locks, and it is the third volume audit in this series and the authority on every ruling Volume 03 deferred. `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md` and `reviews/volume-02/volume-02-close.md` are untouched and remain the authority on what they ruled.**

**METHOD: Chapters 131–150 read in full. Chapters 101–130 read through the five *Batch 000X as written* sections above and the five *Volume 03, Batch 000X* sections of `state/chapter-summaries.md`, with Chapters 88, 91, 99, 121, 123, 127, 129, 133, 134, 136, 137 and 138 read in full, and with every load-bearing fact in dispute re-derived by search against the chapter rather than taken from a state file.**

## 1. The fourteen rulings, each dated 27 September 2026

1. **LOCK L5 IS AMENDED. THE CLAUSE STANDS. THE ENUMERATION IS SUPERSEDED BY THE CHAPTERS. THERE ARE FOUR RETURNS IN VOLUME 03 AND THERE WAS NO FIFTH, AND THE FOURTH IS CANON.** Return 1, Thursday the fourth of May 2026, two days late, out of the raising of Tuesday the twenty-eighth of April. Return 2, Wednesday the tenth of May, on the day, out of the look of Saturday the sixth. Return 3, Saturday the seventeenth of June, on the day, out of a foot and a half of worked glass raised on the tenth of June that raised nothing. Return 4, **Friday the fourteenth of July, on the day, out of a finding of Monday the tenth.** **THE DAY OF A FINDING IS NOT COUNTED AND THE RETURN FALLS DUE ON THE FOURTH DAY AFTER IT, AND MONDAY THE TENTH PLUS THE ELEVENTH, TWELFTH, THIRTEENTH AND FOURTEENTH IS FRIDAY THE FOURTEENTH.** All four were caught by the first limb by itself; **the party has not needed the second limb and does not know that it would, and the yard is allowed to be wrong on the page about which limb caught the lump in April and is not allowed to be quiet about which.** **NOTHING IS RECALLED, NOTHING IS CORRECTED, NOTHING IS UN-SAID, NOTHING IS RE-SENT, AND NO OFFICE IS WRITTEN TO ABOUT THE ENUMERATION.** The clerk's own page has said four in his own hand since the fourteenth of July. **A volume's internal bookkeeping is not a record and a lock is not a document.** The amended form is at `outline/volume-04.md` lock L5 and governs every later volume.
2. **The condition's onset is the FOURTEENTH of October 2025.** Every chapter of Volume 03 that states it states the fourteenth, and **the phrase *thirteenth of October* is in no chapter of the volume.** **THREE THINGS AND THREE DATES, AND THEY MAY NEVER BE FUSED: the dive was the night of the THIRTEENTH; the onset is the FOURTEENTH; the order and the seizure were the FOURTEENTH at half past six in the morning.** A dive is not the thing an onset is measured from, and `outline/volume-03.md` lock L2 and this file both carry the thirteenth for the dive, which is correct and is not a statement about the onset.
3. **The join's physical facts, re-derived against the chapters and not against a state file.** Six joints in a crevice at about four fathoms on the second seam's own face, landward of the third seam's mark, ground no party claims and ground the first clause of the notice does not reach; **in a straight line, with a string and two pins coming to three hundred and fifty-one feet from the north end of the first to the south end of the last.** Fouling, with the same bar laid flat, the same scraper, the same rule, the same state of tide and the same hand: **a quarter of an inch, a quarter of an inch, three inches and a quarter, three inches and a quarter, nine inches, three inches and a quarter**, from the root end of the crevice to the seaward end; the third is sixty-eight feet south of the second and the fourth sixty-nine south of the third; the fifth is the second from the seaward end and its packing is a different colour to a depth of about a quarter of an inch and the same colour under that. **Nine fastenings in about seven feet at a hand's breadth apart, three blows a head and the same three blows on all nine, and a thumb and a bit by the boatwright's own rule, and men who lay out by hand are men working in a hurry or a place where there is not a batten. THERE ARE THREE THICKNESSES OF FOULING AND THAT IS THREE, AND THE PARTY DOES NOT KNOW HOW MANY MORE AND IS NOT GOING TO ARRANGE THEM INTO A FIGURE. THE NINE FASTENINGS ARE NOT A MARK OF NINE JOINED LINES AND THE NINE PLACES ARE NOT THE NINE HARBORS, AND THE PARTY HAS SAID SO IN ITS OWN HAND, TWICE, AND MARA VENN HAS SAID IT A THIRD TIME.**
4. **The nine places as the page carries them, and the three with no joint.** Six in a builder's day-book of the port of Ashtide, in six entries, in a builder's hand and not a clerk's, at the second seam: **forty feet, seventy-three feet, one hundred and forty-one feet, two hundred and ten feet, two hundred and seventy-nine feet, three hundred and forty-eight feet**, a day against each and **no year in any hand this yard can read**. Three in books with no joint under them: **this harbor's own weir called the Sill, a village not on this coast and not on any chart anybody in this harbor has seen, and a sand and shell lay of this coast not on any chart in this harbor**, and the third of the six places above lies **a foot and four inches below the sill stone of the Sill**. **THE THREE ARE OWED A PLACEMENT IN A LATER VOLUME AS A FACT AND NOT AS AN IDENTIFICATION, THEY ARE NOT THE NINE-LINE MARK AND MAY NOT BE FUSED WITH IT, AND THE PARTY DOES NOT KNOW WHY THE THREE ARE THERE AND IS NOT GOING TO GUESS.**
5. **What a chip of worked glass carries is STILL OPEN BY DESIGN and stays unread.** The second column says *what is in the chip, which goes nowhere at all, with the reason that this party cannot read what a thing carries and is not going to try and is not going to pay six marks and two days to have it read and has not got six marks.* **A LATER VOLUME THAT READS IT MUST HAVE SOMEBODY PAID SIX MARKS AND TWO DAYS, AND THE PARTY MUST NOT HAVE PAID THEM, AND THE NOT-PAYING MUST BE ON THE PAGE.** The ground is landward of the third seam's mark and the full Second Bell license is not granted and is not approached, **so the water is reachable today and only today by this party on its own account.**
6. **The figure on the bar of gravel.** The yard's is **two and three-quarters on the flood and two and a quarter on the ebb, made once on the second of May with a hand lead's line and a slate. Rowan Trewin's is two and a half on the same tide and two and none the less on the ebb, made four times in nineteen years, and his argument is a figure and not a feud: a figure a man made once is a figure anybody can check and a figure a man made four times is a figure only one man can check.** **BOTH ARE IN A PRESS IN A CITY IN THE SAME MONTH AND NEITHER IS GOING TO BE WITHDRAWN AND THIS VOLUME DOES NOT DECIDE WHICH IS RIGHT. FIVE MILES OF THAT COAST WAS NOT LOOKED AT AND ELEVEN MILES OF WATER WAS RUN TO LOOK AT TWO STONES AND A BAR, AND THE YARD HAS SAID SO ON A PAGE AND CANNOT UN-SAY IT. THE MAN WHO IS WRONG ABOUT ONE THING THIS YARD CANNOT DO ONE SINGLE THING ABOUT IS THE YARD ITSELF.**
7. **The glass storm's two written occurrences stand in two hands on two pages that are not on top of each other, and no cause is named in any volume.** It may be used again. **It may never become a voice that tells anybody anything and it may never be the reason a person does what they do**, and the delay on the call in the water is not given and the yard does not intend to invent one.
8. **The delayed note: eleven logged occasions and two of them with no call in them, neither count advanced in any of the fifty chapters by arithmetic or by a measurement, no occasion entered, the figure last taken by the boy himself on Tuesday the second of May and not taken since and not taken on the eighth of August.** The delay is **a hand's width of a second**, it has not moved, **and nobody in this yard has said why and nobody in this harbor has ever said why and this party is not going to be the first.** The answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint. **THE FOUR SECOND-COLUMN LINES ABOVE THE ONE NAMED LINE ARE STILL FOUR, ARE STILL NAMELESS, AND ARE NOT STRUCK OUT; THE COUNT OF FOUR IS IN THE FIRST COLUMN IN A SHIP'S HAND WITH NO NAME AGAINST IT; AND THE ONE NAMING, SPENT IN CHAPTER 137 ON THE TWENTIETH OF JULY, IS NOT AVAILABLE AGAIN.**
9. **The coast road, its four figures, the pilot's two figures, and the Kettle.** The cairn on the Old Custom House at eleven feet by pole at half-tide; the stem on the Nine Foot called the Accountant at nine fathoms at the middle cast of four; the bar at two and three-quarters on the flood and two and a quarter on the ebb; **and the Kettle, which dries three days out of a month and which was not looked at and is not looked at in Volume 03 either.** **THE YARD REFUSED A LICENCE TO ONE IN NINE SENTENCES ON THE BACK OF A PAPER ON THE FOURTEENTH OF JUNE, THE THIRTY MARKS WOULD HAVE PAID FOR ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED FEET OF A WOMAN'S OWN WARP ON THE BOTTOM OF THIS HARBOR, THE ROAD IS PUBLISHED AND CANNOT BE UN-PUBLISHED, AND IT IS NOT RESOLVED IN VOLUME 04 EITHER. THE WORD *NETWORK* WAS USED ONCE BY A RIVAL, REFUSED IN PUBLIC, AND IS IN NO MOUTH IN ANY OF THE FIFTY CHAPTERS.**
10. **The custody items: three items, three numbers, digits never quoted, none returns, none is asked for, and the word *custody* is not in any of the fifty chapters at all.** The municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request. **NO PAPER IN THIS HARBOR OR IN THE CIRCUIT OFFICE'S COPYING ROOM STATES WHAT A MUNICIPAL CLAIM DOES TO A PRESUMED CROWN MATERIAL, IT WAS NOT ANSWERED IN VOLUME 03, AND IT MAY NOT BE ANSWERED BY A CLERK IN VOLUME 04 OR ANY OTHER. THAT SENTENCE IS A LIVE INSTRUMENT.**
11. **The ninth seam's end of the FIRST OF SEPTEMBER 2026, entered in the reeve's own hand on Monday the third of July, and it falls seventeen days after the volume closed and is Volume 04's first movement and not this volume's resolution. The first of September 2026 is a FRIDAY on this book's grid.** **And the finding, which is a fact about an office and not about a villain: AN OFFICE WHOSE INSTRUMENTS NOBODY CONTESTS HAS NOW ENTERED THREE THINGS IN ITS OWN BOOK, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM COST SOMEBODY WHO WAS NOT IN THE ROOM** — the examination of the mole's footing, the closure of the third lay's moorings for four days, and two acknowledgements whose price is a herring skipper's nineteen winters and a lay's warping. **The reeve's own argument against himself is on the page and it is that an instrument entered and not contested is worse than no instrument, because it looks like a decision and there is no money behind it.**
12. **THE ATTRIBUTION OF THE VOLUME'S CLOSING SENTENCE. NO FISHER IN VOLUME 02 IS RECORDED AS HAVING SAID IT. THE SENTENCE IS CALDER MARR'S IN A HEARING ROOM IN VOLUME 02, CHAPTER 97, AND THE SAME SHAPE IS ORS KEST'S IN A ROOM IN VOLUME 02, CHAPTER 58, AND NEITHER MAN IS A FISHER.** **THE OUTLINE'S CLAUSE IS DISCHARGED AND NOT VARED.** Volume 03 gave the sentence to three people in three mouths — **NESSA VALE OF THE SILL FIRST, on the mole's steps on the tenth, a quarter of an hour before the holder said it in the same scene and yielded the first saying to her in front of forty people, and ELIAS VOKE LAST, a herring skipper, in a market on the thirteenth, who said it once and would not say it twice and made the holder read the last sentence back twice** — and **the yard's own book holds it three times, out of a room in April, out of a market in July and out of the market on the thirteenth, and the holder has entered that he did not write down the one said on the mole on the tenth. THE SENTENCE'S ORIGIN IS SETTLED AT VOLUME 02, CHAPTER 97, IN A HEARING ROOM, AND NO LATER PHASE MAY GO LOOKING FOR A CHAPTER IN VOLUME 02 THAT SAYS IT.**
13. **THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF JULY 2026 IS A FRIDAY.** Two state files and one prompt called it a Tuesday. **The chapters are right and the state files are corrected in place: 26 July is a Wednesday, 27 July a Thursday, 28 July a Friday, and the warp is taken on the Friday in Chapter 141, on the page, in two places.** The rule is the rule of the two previous audits: the chapters decide a date and the planning files are corrected to them.
14. **The four items Batch 0005 flagged and did not apply are disposed of.** (a) **The two *everything in her* clauses on the* Vigil's *notices are OPEN BY DESIGN and are not Volume 04's either** — a conservator of no office entered that she does not know why a man writes a longer word in a longer notice, and **no person is identified, no informer is implied, no office is named, and nothing is resolved.** (b) **The five bells: a later volume MAY supply a way and the price must fall on somebody who is not the yard and must be entered; and the harbor authority may not supply one, no office in Veyr may supply one, and no person with a seal may say that anybody may go** — a boat of another crew with a compressor and forty fathoms of his own hose already exists in this harbor and has already been paid three marks out of six. (c) **The relief lands on the fifteenth of August and the posting begins on the first of September, and the fortnight between them is the only thing about the posting that is not decided, and a Volume 04 chapter may use a fortnight in a city as a fortnight in a city and may not use it to put a woman in a room in Morrow Quay.** (d) The attribution, at item 12.

## 2. The four forward rulings on the bellwright and the lead plug, made at `outline/volume-04.md` locks L8 and L9

- **RULED 1: HE IS STILL OWED IT IN VOLUME 04, AND AT WHOSE COST, AND THE COST MAY NOT BE ARRANGED BY A VOLUME 04 WRITER.** Eight marks and a day of a boat has stood owing since the twenty-third of May 2026, unpaid and unargued. **A Volume 04 chapter may not pay it out of the yard's own book, may not have this yard buy a second going out inside four years, and may not have the yard pay it as a wage, a fee or a gift. THE DEFAULT IS THAT IT STANDS, AND A VOLUME THAT MOVES IT MUST MOVE IT BY PUTTING IT ON THE PAGE AS A THING SOMEBODY PAID AND SOMEBODY ELSE DID NOT.**
- **RULED 2: THE ONE NAMING IS SPENT FOREVER.** The yard's undertaking of the second of July is not a device to be picked up when a chapter is short of a fact, and **the chest is not a mystery-box and the number is not a reveal and no volume may open the chest for the sake of opening it.**
- **RULED 3: THE PLUG IS NOT A STORY OBJECT AND MAY NOT BE USED AS ONE.** It is not a custody object, not one of the three items, and not connected to the ferry plate, the Oren mark, the coast road, the worked panel, the compartment, the run under this town, the join at the second seam or the five bells, **and the party has entered that instruction against itself and not against anybody else, and that instruction is now binding on every later volume.**
- **AND THE CHART.** The bell's ground-gear notation of the twenty-fifth of December went out of this harbor on the chart on the twenty-second of June into a vessel's keeping and **there is no second copy, because the notation is on the chart and the chart is the paper. NO LATER VOLUME MAY PUT THE FOUR-HUNDRED-YARD BAR BACK IN THIS OFFICE'S HANDS, MAY HAVE A CLERK SAY WHAT THE CHART SAYS, OR MAY HAVE THE YARD PRODUCE THE CHART.**

## 3. The eleven mechanical repairs, listed individually with the lock each breaks and the fix

**Four weekdays, lock L1.** **Ch 106**, in the first column: *for the week of* **Monday the ninth to Sunday the fifteenth** of May inclusive → *Tuesday the ninth to Monday the fifteenth*; the ninth of May is a Tuesday and the fifteenth a Monday, and the sentence's own reason is only coherent if the week's own Tuesday was the ninth. **Ch 115**, in the opening paragraph: *The cistern was charged on the* **Thursday the fourth** of June → *Sunday the fourth*, with **and it is not a rota day** added in the yard's idiom, because the same chapter's seventh line says the boat came in on the Sunday the fourth of June. **Ch 119**, in the read-out: *and not on the* **Thursday the nineteenth** → *and not on the rota day, the nineteenth*, with *on the rota* moved off the fourth of June, which was not a rota day, and on to the twenty-ninth of May and the twelfth of June, which were. **Ch 143**, in the first column: *on* **Tuesday the fourth** of May *and on* **Monday the eleventh** of May → *Thursday* both, which is what the grid gives and what makes the two read-backs a weekly pattern in the four days before the boy went off the stone on Monday the fifteenth; the dates stand and the act described — reading a position into a slate for a man in a boat — is a different act from reading the yard's own money columns back at the head of a dock, and nothing else in the fifty chapters contradicts it.

**Six wrong letters, house spelling.** **Ch 115** *harbourmaster* → *harbormaster*. **Ch 120** ×4 and **Ch 130** ×1, *licence* → *license*. **The book writes *harbor* one thousand six hundred and sixty-eight times across three volumes and *harbour* once, and *license* thirteen times and *licence* five. A search of all fifty chapters for the whole British-spelling class now returns nothing.**

**Two duplicated paragraphs, against the `AGENTS.md` Quality Gate.** **Ch 134's** sixty-word description of a return's jacket, byte-identical to Ch 121's, is now *What was in the jacket, and this is the second jacket of this volume and the form is the same form, and the yard is not going to describe a form twice*, with the remaining facts identical and none added and none removed. **Ch 141's** twenty-two-word cistern line, byte-identical to Ch 136's, is now *And the sluice behind the engine house was not walked out to on that Thursday, and the reason is on the page, and it is a working reason and it is in this yard's own hand.* **In each pair the first instance is left exactly as it is and stands as the device. A byte-comparison of every paragraph of twelve words or more across the fifty chapters now returns ZERO duplicates.**

**Thirty-six exact repeated sentences of twelve words or more remain, and every one is the volume's own device, and none is repaired:** eighteen are lines of the two-column read-out, which goes in the same words every time because the words are the practice; four are the six conditions read at the boat, whose repetition lock L25 requires and whose reason is on the page; two are the two Crown notices quoted verbatim; and twelve are stock sentences of the house about a town that does not change, which is the volume's subject.

# Volume 04 planning-phase review repairs — the disposition of every finding

**A reviewer pass over `outline/volume-04.md`, `outline/batches/volume-04-batch-0001.md` and the queued `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, read against the fifty chapters of Volume 03, `reviews/volume-03/volume-03-close.md` and the four state files, returned FIX with twenty-nine items in the created files. All twenty-nine are disposed of below and in the three files themselves. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED — THERE IS NO VOLUME 04 PROSE — NO CARD WAS CUT, NO MOVEMENT BOUNDARY WAS MOVED, NO COST WAS DROPPED, NO LOCK WAS SOFTENED AND NO PLANNED PLOT WAS ALTERED. The batch is still ten cards, the midpoint is still Chapter 155 and the climax is still Chapter 160. This section is the authority for the repairs and is the disposition the next batch inherits.**

## 1. The calendar, which was the largest single fault

**NINE OF THE TEN CARDS HAD BEEN DATED ON THE REAL 2026 CALENDAR INSTEAD OF THIS BOOK'S, AND ONLY CHAPTER 157 WAS RIGHT.** The book's grid is fixed by the chapters: **3 May 2026 is a Wednesday** (`reviews/volume-03/volume-03-close.md` § 3.1), and every weekday in this series is the real 2026 weekday plus three, which is why 1 May is a Monday, 14 August a Monday, 15 August a Tuesday and 1 September a Friday. **Corrected: the Mondays in August 2026 are the 7th, the 14th, the 21st and the 28th, and the September Mondays are the 4th, 11th, 18th and 25th, and the wage Tuesdays are the 1st, 8th, 15th, 22nd and 29th of August and the 5th, 12th, 19th and 26th of September.** Every weekday in both planning files and in the prompt has been re-derived from that anchor and re-checked.

**THE CARD SEQUENCE IS NOW, AND IT IS CONTIGUOUS FROM THE SIXTEENTH OF AUGUST TO THE SECOND OF SEPTEMBER:** **151 Wednesday 16 – Thursday 17 August · 152 Saturday 19 – Sunday 20 August · 153 Monday 21 – Tuesday 22 August · 154 Wednesday 23 – Thursday 24 August · 155 Friday 25 – Saturday 26 August · 156 Sunday 27 – Monday 28 August · 157 Friday 1 – Saturday 2 September · 158 Friday 8 September · 159 Saturday 9 September · 160 Sunday 10 September, with the sitting named for a day in the week after.** The two gaps are deliberate and are now on the page: the market office is open to anybody on a Tuesday and a Friday morning, the next such morning after Chapter 157 is **Friday the eighth**, and the middle of the week carries the slate wall and nothing else.

**Three summary lines that contradicted their own day-by-day lists are corrected.** `outline/volume-04.md` lock L1 said *24 and 31 August are Mondays* and that *the earlier Mondays in the volume are the 17th, 24th and 31st of August*; both were impossible and both are gone. L1 also carried two phrasings that contradicted each other inside one lock — *the correction is withdrawn and the grid stands* and *from the grid as corrected* — and the operative rule is now a single plain sentence: **the chapters decide a date and every planning file is corrected to them, and nothing in an audit is of authority over a weekday in either direction.**

**THE SLATE WALL'S COUNTER NO LONGER RESTARTS.** The audit recorded 1 May 2026 as the **zeroth** and 14 August as the **fifteenth** and warned that a Volume 04 writer would otherwise add one. `outline/volume-04.md` L1 had it restarting at a *Monday the seventeenth of August*, which is a **Thursday**. **The counter now runs: the first of May the zeroth, 8 May the first, 14 August the fifteenth, 21 August the sixteenth and the first Monday this volume reaches, 28 August the seventeenth, 4 September the eighteenth, 11 September the nineteenth, 18 September the twentieth, 25 September the twenty-first**, and no Volume 04 chapter may count a wipe of a Monday in Volume 03 as its own. Two card pulls inherited the old figure and both are corrected: Chapter 151's pull now names **Monday the fourteenth of August as the fifteenth** and Monday the twenty-first as the first one the volume reaches, and Chapter 160's pull now names **Monday the eleventh of September as the fourth one this volume reaches and the nineteenth since the first of May.**

## 2. The money, which did not close and now does

**Chapter 153 paid three marks of a six-mark debt that does not exist, and the chain then broke between Chapters 153 and 155.** The debt is **three** marks (L2; `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0147.md:15`; the audit's costs table). It is now **not paid at all in this batch**, the yard enters in the first column with the figure and the date why, and the reason is the card's own: the bag held one mark after the wage was in it and a man is not paid a shilling of it and told it is a payment. **The same sentence no longer says *in front of the man who hired the day* — the day is hired by a woman of the third lay and the only man connected to the debt is named separately.** The wage column is now stated as a figure and not as arithmetic: **seventeen lines and sixty-eight marks.** The batch's whole money is now written out in one place in hard rule 3 and in the prompt, and it closes: **nothing at the opening · Chapter 153: five in, four out, ONE MARK · Chapter 154: nothing · Chapter 155: a shilling at the press, NINETEEN PENCE · Chapter 156: five in, FIVE MARKS AND NINETEEN PENCE · the wage of Tuesday the twenty-ninth paid out of that, and the column at EIGHTEEN LINES AND SEVENTY-TWO MARKS · Chapters 157–160: nothing, and a mark and nineteenpence at the end.**

**The wage of Tuesday the twenty-ninth of August falls between Chapters 156 and 157 and is now entered as a figure in the yard's own book and read out in Chapter 157, and no chapter is set on that day.** It is paid rather than unpaid because a wage falls on a Tuesday and Chapter 156 brought five marks in, and the column's two movements in the batch are both checkable. **This is the one figure in the batch that no chapter enacts, and it is marked as such in both files so that no writer pays it twice or omits it.**

**The creditor is one man and not two. *A man of Ashtide* and *Perry Ollett of the* Sallow Reach *are the same debt, and L2 now says so in those words, clears him to be named once — in Chapter 153 — and forbids naming him again, asking him a question, taking a statement from him or sending the yard up this hill to see him.** The starting condition in the batch file and the opening of the volume outline now carry the name once instead of two forms of it.

## 3. Three beats that belonged to later movements

**CHAPTER 159 WAS PAYING THE VOLUME'S DECLARED MIDPOINT TWENTY CHAPTERS EARLY, AND THE SAME FILE FORBADE IT.** Hard rule 18 and the prompt both said *no hearing is held at which the book is read out whole*, and the outline reserves that to Movement 4; Chapter 159 was titled *The Book Is Produced Whole* and had the yard *produce everything, unshortened, inside about a quarter of an hour* with a man in a coat reading a party's whole record in the same time. **Chapter 159 is re-cast as production with no reading: the book goes in whole, the copy goes into a file, not one line is read aloud, the man in a coat says he will read it in a city, and the yard cannot stop him and enters that it does not know what he will be done with it. The chapter's argument is the not-reading** — there is a line in the book the holder would have read if the room had been a shed, he does not read it, and the yard cannot yet tell whether the not-reading was a rule or a fear. **Two structural notes are now in `outline/volume-04.md` § *Midpoint reversal* and hard rules 15 and 18 so that this cannot recur: no chapter of Movements 1, 2 or 3 may produce a copy of a record in a room, read a line of the crew's book aloud in a room, or put a seam on a document, and a head-of-dock read-out of the yard's own sheet in a market is not the reserved event.**

**CHAPTER 160 WAS SPENDING MOVEMENT 3'S TURN AND THE VOLUME'S NEW-VOLUME QUESTION TWENTY CHAPTERS EARLY**, delivering the Registry's position in writing, the phrase *risk management*, and the party's not-understanding. **The Registry's position, the phrase and the document are out of Chapter 160 entirely.** What the notice gives the yard instead is a date, a room, a clerk's name, and a fourth paragraph that is a list of what the party is asked to produce — one item of which is a record of any finding made on or about the eighth of August — **and the yard notices that the form has been drafted by somebody who has read a market wall of this harbor and enters that it does not know who.** The phrase and the document are now explicitly reserved: **the phrase *risk management* is not on any page of this volume before Chapter 171, and a clerk may say that a record is not in an office's possession and may not say why.** Those words are now in the volume outline at Movement 3's turn, in batch hard rule 18, and in the prompt's *What this batch must not do*.

**CHAPTER 155 WAS PRE-DELIVERING MOVEMENT 5'S CENTRAL DOCUMENT IN MOVEMENT 1.** It had the yard putting on a wall its own copy of a record it was not permitted to copy — a copy of the provisional public copy record of the seized shard, which is the volume's climax object and the thing the party has to *force the release of*. **The card is re-cast, not cut, and it is retitled *The Sheet Under the Two That Came Down*.** The man in a coat's room still establishes that a provisional public copy record exists, that the yard may ask for a copy of the copy at its own cost, and that the copy is not the object — **and the request is entered, the copy is not made, is not released and is not printed, and the sheet that goes up in about forty people's hearing carries a fact, a limit, the place of every copy of itself, and the line that this yard asked an office in a city for a copy of a record and has not been given one.** What the party publishes for the first time in three volumes is therefore a refusal it received rather than a fact it established, and the release stays in Movement 5.

## 4. The closure, the third lay, and the ninth acknowledgement

**BATCH HARD RULE 5 FORBIDDEN WHAT CHAPTER 156 REQUIRED.** The rule said no person, boat or lead of this yard goes inside the first clause **on any day of this batch**, with no exception, while Chapter 156 puts three of them inside it on the mole's toe with the ninth on the page; the prompt already carried the exception and the batch file had not been updated to match. **The batch file now carries the same exception as the prompt: no person, boat or lead inside the first clause on any day of this batch EXCEPT the day in Chapter 156, which is inside it with the ninth on the page.** The garbled tail of that rule — *and that is not a remembered rule* — is gone.

**CHAPTER 156 WAS PAYING THE NINTH WITH THE THIRD LAY'S FOUR DAYS, WHICH `outline/volume-04.md` FORBIDS TWICE** (line 120: *it may not be the third lay again*; line 212: *it is not the third lay's four days again*), and Volume 03's mole's-footing examination has already cost that lay four days of the best of its season. **The cost has moved.** The day inside the closure is now a day on the mole's toe for a **woman of the second lay** who has gear of her own under the toe off the third seam and no other ground for it, who books the Monday and pays twelve of seventeen at the mole the way she always has — **so the day's money is not the yard's** — **and the price falls on a man of the first lay who had the mole's toe booked for the same Monday for a day of his own and is out of a day and a day's work because this yard asked for the ninth.** The yard enters his name and the figure, does not pay it, is not going to thank him, is not going to send for him, and is not going to say sorry in a market, and the reason is on the page: a notice does not say what a man loses, and a party that has published itself is not a party that gets to be sorry in a street and mean it. The card's stated reason for needing the ninth is also corrected: it is no longer *a third lay will not book a Monday or a Tuesday*, which Chapter 153 contradicts, and the reeve is made to say the other half of his own price out loud before anybody goes in the water — that this office has now been asked twice and paid twice and cannot pay a third time.

## 5. Continuity, dates and figures inside the cards

- **Chapter 151 miscounted nothing but contradicted itself about the wall**, saying that nothing went up on the sixteenth, seventeenth or eighteenth while being set on the nineteenth and twentieth. It is set on **the sixteenth and seventeenth**, and the wall is bare from the morning of the sixteenth and nothing goes up on it until the twenty-fifth. **Its ending type no longer says *a loss with no agent* while the same card names a boat, a man in a coat, a barge from the naval pile and a witness with the hour: the agent exists and is owned, and the ending type is a loss with an agent the yard will not name in a room.**
- **Chapter 153 miscounted the envelopes** — *two of them were never made*. Canon is **one**: eleven envelopes on a bench, the first of August made, the eighth made, the fifteenth not made and no envelope written (`chapters/volume-03/chapter-0147.md:7`, `chapter-0148.md:89`, `chapter-0150.md:49,53`; `state/current.md:110`). **The card now says one, names the three weeks, and states that the count of eleven and the number not made are two different figures and neither is the other.**
- **Chapter 153's comparison day was wrong and is corrected**: the figure written on the mole's steps in front of everybody is the five of this day beside **the seven the same day of work brought on the twenty-fourth of June**, which is what `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0136.md:25` does. The *day of the seventh of July* it carried has no board figure behind it and is gone.
- **Chapter 154 contradicted itself about the market wall** — *not used on either day, the sheet goes up in the next chapter* in one line and *the yard does the thing he asks and does it on a wall* in another. The first line now says the sheet goes up in the next chapter, **on the Friday.**
- **Chapter 155 erased the yard's own fourth return in one sentence**, saying the new sheet goes up on nails where *there is nothing on those nails now*. **The fourth return of the twenty-second of July is on that wall, it is under the new sheet, and it is still readable from the side of it in the right wind** — the card's own continuity fact and Chapter 151's already said so, and the action now agrees with them.
- **Chapter 155 re-published a list of copies in a market one day after the pilot had objected to exactly that**, and the card owed the reader the reason. **The yard now says out loud that it heard the objection and does not agree with it, enters in the first column that a man who keeps a channel by hand wants the place of every copy said in a room, that the only room this party can be got into in the next three weeks is a room somebody else has arranged, and that the yard has chosen a market, knows what it is doing, and is not going to pretend to a man of fifty-four that it did not choose it** — and the sheet carries that on its own face.
- **Chapter 155's press-keeper was *not of any office*** while the press is the market office's, unlocked on a Tuesday and a Friday morning while the counting runs (`chapters/volume-03/chapter-0138.md:5`, `chapter-0150.md:9`). **The press is now the market office's own press at the back of its counting floor under a stone slab, the woman who opens it belongs to that room and her mother opened it before her, the shilling is the standing arrangement a man of Ashtide used for the* Sallow Reach's *sheet, and no chapter may call the keeper a man who is not of any office.**
- **Chapter 158 welded two sixes** — six things and no seventh, and six marks and two days. **The echo is now marked as deliberate and the card says the six marks and two days is not one of the six things and is not to be set beside them as though it were**, with the shard unread and the price unpaid, as the audit requires.
- **Chapter 159's book was *fifty-eight weeks of somebody else's weather*.** The book runs from the fourteenth of October 2025 and the ninth of September 2026 is **fifty-one weeks** after it, and it is the yard's own book: **fifty-one weeks of the yard's own weather.**
- **The clerk's tally was undated and is dated.** Canon is Chapter 140's read-out on Tuesday the twenty-fifth of July: *the clerk said on the steps that he cannot enter what a thumbnail of glass is and has said so seven times this year.* **Chapter 152 is therefore the eighth, said in a doorway on Saturday the nineteenth of August, and Chapter 159 is the ninth** — and the card names the seventh as the one on the mole's steps on the twenty-fifth of July, which is on the page, instead of inventing a weekday the chapters do not give. The counting device is now checkable and dated in both files.
- **The ten day-schedule is now printed as a table in the prompt**, so that a writer cannot read a weekday out of a card that has been repaired.

## 6. The people, and the one who is not named

**FINDING 19 — *the volume's antagonist has no face in the room* — is answered in the direction the chapters allow and not the one a reader might expect.** The man in a coat **stays unnamed**, and the reason is on the page: **Chapter 149 of Volume 03 has the holder tell him he does not know him and is not going to find out this month**, and a name given now would be a name given to a man nobody in this harbor has been introduced to, in a volume whose own rule is that a name is spoken by a crew that needs him. **A Volume 04 chapter may not name him, may not have anybody here learn his name, and may not have him take a coat off.** That is now written into lock L15, into batch hard rule 19, and into the prompt's rule 14, so that the absence reads as a decision on the page rather than as a gap.

**Against that, the clerk is no longer referred to as an office.** **Ambrose Sell is named wherever a clerk of the circuit office appears** — Chapters 152, 159 and 160 — and hard rule 19 lists the faces this batch actually has: Ors Kest, Ambrose Sell, Rowan Trewin, Mara Venn, the unnamed woman of the second lay, the unnamed woman of the third lay, and a man of the third lay who saw a boat at the wall at about seven in the morning and gave his name and the hour. **A volume with no antagonist's face is not a volume with no faces.**

## 7. The handoff, the flag, and what was not touched

- **`outline/volume-04.md` § *Handoff to Chapter 161* was stale against its own cards** — it ended the batch with a page under a naval paper nobody has covered and *three marks owing*, both of which Chapters 151 and 153 had already spent. **It is rewritten to the batch's actual end state**, and it is the first thing the next batch reads.
- **The divergence from `outline/series.md:95,196` is now FLAGGED rather than silent.** The series outline plans that Calder gives Mara the original anchor and that they become intimate in Volume 4; `outline/volume-04.md` refuses both, in its resolution and in its planned relationship change, in the words *neither is repaired by either of them giving the other anything*. **Volume 03's audit set the precedent that a better on-the-page argument outranks the outline, the ending is untouched, and `outline/ending.md` is not amended by it — but the divergence is now written on the face of the volume outline**, with a date, so that no later phase and no reader of the series outline takes it for a quiet drop.
- **`state/phase-ledger.json` was NOT edited and no marker was written.** It is the controller's file and the flag stands where it stood. **What has changed and is recorded at `state/open-threads.md` § *Flagged for the controller* is the exposure count**: `workspace/` now holds **twenty-two** `PROMPT.md` files, and the assurance in `state/current.md` covers only what the Volume 03 close created, which is not the same claim. A controller reading the ledger as `PHASE_SYSTEM.md:186` describes would still select Volume 01 Batch 0001 and rewrite ten finished chapters, and no action is available to a writer, an audit or a repair phase.
- **`workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/PROMPT.md:43` still says *twenty-seven marks in a bag* where the figure is thirteen.** It is a past phase's dispatch artifact, the reviewer put it outside this phase, and it is behind the reader list that prompt puts first, so **it is left and recorded, not fixed.**
- **NOT EDITED: `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md`, the six bible files, `outline/volume-01.md`, `outline/volume-02.md`, `outline/volume-03.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, `scripts/`, `.github/workflows/`, `.opencode/agent/`, `state/phase-ledger.json`. NOT CREATED: no Volume 04 batch-0002 prompt and no later prompt. Exactly one phase remains queued, and it is the one that was queued.**
