## Batch 0001 as written (Chapters 151–160), Volume 04 — binding on Chapter 161 onward and on the Volume 04 close

**VOLUME 04 MOVEMENT 1 IS WRITTEN.** Thirty-one thousand and nineteen words across ten chapters, Wednesday the sixteenth of August to Sunday the tenth of September. **The batch midpoint (Ch 155) and the batch climax (Ch 160) are both paid. The volume's central pressure is on the page. No card was cut, no movement boundary was moved, no cost was dropped, and no lock was softened.** The cards in `outline/batches/volume-04-batch-0001.md` were followed as the schedule, and where a card's own phrase conflicted with the book's calendar the chapters decide it and the correction is recorded below.

### 1. The calendar the batch actually ran on

**The book's grid, per lock L1, and no weekday was corrected to an almanac.** 16 August a **Wednesday** · 17 **Thursday** · 19 **Saturday** · 20 **Sunday** · 21 **Monday** · 22 **Tuesday** and a wage week · 23 **Wednesday** · 24 **Thursday** · 25 **Friday** · 26 **Saturday** · 27 **Sunday** · 28 **Monday** · 29 **Tuesday** and a wage week · 1 September a **Friday** · 2 **Saturday** · 8 **Friday** · 9 **Saturday** · 10 **Sunday**, **with the sitting appointed by the notice of the tenth for a day in the week following.** The distribution is the prompt's own and was followed without a shift: 151 the 16th and 17th; 152 the 19th and 20th; 153 the 21st and 22nd; 154 the 23rd and 24th; 155 the 25th and 26th; 156 the 27th and 28th; 157 the 1st and 2nd of September; 158 the 8th; 159 the 9th; 160 the 10th. **No day is in a chapter twice and no man is paid for two days in one chapter.**

**ONE CORRECTION MADE IN THE CHAPTERS AGAINST A CARD'S OWN PHRASE, AND THE CHAPTERS ARE RIGHT.** Chapter 160's card pulled forward to *a wage of four due on Tuesday the fifteenth*. **On this book's grid the fifteenth of September is a Friday, and there is no weekday rule this phase may break. THE NEXT WAGE TUESDAY AFTER CHAPTER 160 IS TUESDAY THE NINETEENTH OF SEPTEMBER, and that is what Chapter 160 says, and it is nine days off.** The wage Tuesdays in September are the 5th, the 12th, the 19th and the 26th. The card is not the authority on a weekday; lock L1 and the chapters are.

**THE HEARING ROOM'S SLATE WALL CAME DOWN BARE FOUR TIMES IN THIS BATCH, ALL BARE, ALL WITH THE CLERK KEEPING HIS OWN PAGE AND SAYING NOTHING, AND NO SITTING WAS HELD IN THAT ROOM ON ANY OF THEM:** Monday the twenty-first of August (**sixteen** Mondays since the first of May, Ch 153) · Monday the twenty-eighth (**seventeeth**, Ch 156) · Monday the fourth of September (**eighteenth**, Ch 157's forward pull and Ch 159's opening) · and Monday the eleventh of September (**nineteenth**, named forward in Ch 160). **The fourteenth of August, the fifteenth, is carried retrospectively in Chapter 151 and is not counted as this batch's own. The counter did not restart and the fourteenth of August is not a wipe of this batch.** Chapter 151's clerk says the wall *has come down bare fifteen times, the last of them on Monday, and the next one is on Monday morning and will make sixteen*, which is the sixteenth being the twenty-first.

**THE MARKET WALL IS PUBLISHED ON TWICE AND THE HEARING ROOM IS USED ONCE, AND THE TWO ARE NEVER THE SAME DAY, VERIFIED TWICE.** The market wall carries a new sheet on **Friday the twenty-fifth of August at about eleven in the morning** and is found bare on the morning of **Wednesday the sixteenth**; the hearing room over the tide-gang store is used on **Saturday the ninth of September** and on no other day of the batch. **The market office is a third room and it is used on Friday the twenty-fifth (the press, the presskeeper and the man in a coat) and on Friday the eighth of September (Chapter 158, from the steps outside it). The market office is not the hearing room and the market wall is not either, and no two of the three rooms are the same day in this batch.**

### 2. The money chain, mark by mark, and where it closes

**NOTHING in a bag on the morning of Wednesday the sixteenth of August, against A MARK AND NINETEEN PENCE in it on the evening of Sunday the tenth of September.**

| Day | Day account | Bag at close |
|---|---|---|
| Wed 16 Aug | Nothing in. A morning of a man's time and a night's looking at a wall. | **0** |
| Thu 17 – Sat 19 Aug | Nothing in. A form read in a doorway. | 0 |
| **Sun 20 Aug** | **SEVEN PENCE of postage out of an empty bag**, on a letter in the ordinary post to a circuit office in a city asking for a copy of a record. | **0** |
| **Mon 21 Aug** | Second seam's own face, hirer's own account, nobody in the water. Gross 17, eighth 2, share 2, victuals 2, a hired hand 3, a second hired hand 3 = 12 of 17. Yard's **5**. | **5** |
| **Tue 22 Aug** | **WAGE 4 out into an envelope. COLUMN 17 LINES / 68 MARKS.** The three marks to a man of Ashtide NOT paid and entered unpaid with the reason. | **1** |
| Wed 23 – Thu 24 Aug | Nothing in. A man's figure on a mole's steps. | 1 |
| **Fri 25 Aug** | **A SHILLING at the press of the market office for forty copies.** The third shilling this book has moved that was not the yard's income. | **19p** |
| Sat 26 Aug | Nothing in. Forty copies out. | 19p |
| Sun 27 Aug | Nothing in. A reeve's last entry named in the open air. | 19p |
| **Mon 28 Aug** | The mole's toe, INSIDE THE FIRST CLAUSE, on a woman's own account, with the ninth acknowledgement. Gross 17, same board, same figure. Yard's **5**. | **5m 19p** |
| **Tue 29 Aug** | **WAGE 4 out into an envelope. COLUMN 18 LINES / 72 MARKS. NOT A SCENE AND NO CHAPTER IS SET ON IT** — it is a line in the yard's own book and it is read out as a figure in Chapter 157. | **1m 19p** |
| Fri 1 – Sun 10 Sept | Nothing in. A mooring, a door, a table, a notice. | **1m 19p** |

**THE CHAIN IS 0, 0, 0, 5, 1, 1, 19p, 5m 19p, 1m 19p ACROSS THE DAYS MONEY MOVES, and it is checkable in six chapters without leaving the prose. No office in this harbor has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May and none is going to. The whole of the money that has come into this yard since the fifteenth of August is ten marks and the whole of what went out is nine marks and seven pence.**

**THE WAGE COLUMN: seventeen lines and sixty-eight marks on Tuesday the twenty-second of August, eighteen lines and seventy-two marks on Tuesday the twenty-ninth, and the fifth of March the oldest throughout.** **ELEVEN ENVELOPES ON A BENCH AND ONE OF THEM WAS NEVER MADE** — the week of Tuesday the fifteenth of August — and the count of eleven and the number not made of one are entered in the second column together, in Nessa Pike's hand, with the reason that **an envelope that is not made is not a wage and the column is not the wage**, because a piece of paper with a week on it is a thing the yard does so as to know at the end of a year which weeks it did, and it is not a receipt, and nobody in this harbor has ever received one. **The forty marks to a boy of twenty-two are not a receipt and are not reduced by anything on that line.**

**THE CHANDLER'S NOTE. NOT RE-WRITTEN.** Thirty-one on it, its three counts of weeks standing at twenty-two and thirteen and eleven as last written on the fourth of January and none of them moved and none of them moved by anything a yard does with a pencil, five marks of compressor's gland on it unpaid and of no use whatever because the thing after the gland is the hose, and the tin empty since the twenty-eighth of April. Carried in the read-out material of Chapters 155, 157, 159 and 160.

**THREE MARKS OWING TO PERRY OLLETT OF THE *SALLOW REACH*, A CREW OF ASHTIDE, FOR A DAY OF WORK ON THE EIGHTH OF AUGUST, WITH A DATE ON IT, IN THE FIRST COLUMN, IN NO COLUMN OF THE BOOK, AND NOT A SHILLING OF IT PAID.** **HE IS NAMED ONCE IN THIS VOLUME, IN CHAPTER 153, IN THE FIRST-COLUMN ENTRY, AND IN NO OTHER CHAPTER OF THE BATCH.** Nobody asked him a question, took a statement from him, sent him a letter, or sent the yard up this hill to see him, and all four of those are entered with the reason, which is that the first time this yard put a man's figure in a book and then went and asked him about it, it got a page with a limit on it and a man saying *I have thought about it for five days*, and this yard is not going to do that twice in a year to a man it owes three marks to. **The reason it is not paid is in the first column: the figure in the bag was one mark after the wage of that Tuesday had gone into the envelope, a debt is a debt, a man is not paid a shilling of it and told it is a payment, and the yard will pay him the rest of it in one figure on a day when it has that figure and not in three instalments of one.**

### 3. Every document and instrument created or discovered in this batch

1. **A notice served on the party at the top of this mole's steps on Sunday the tenth of September, four paragraphs, in about nine people's hearing, in daylight, by a clerk of the circuit office, with a clerk's name at the foot of it.** It appoints a sitting for **a day in the week following the tenth** at the hearing room over the tide-gang store at ten in the morning; it gives the harbor authority power to seat one of two disputants and take such finding as is given without prejudice to any claim, **and it says on its own face that that construction is the construction of the second limb of a reporting clause and is applied by the notice to this party and that the party did not write it and cannot un-write it**; it says nothing in it touches, grants, refuses or deals with the second season of the writ of the first of May; and its fourth paragraph is a list of what the party is to produce, **one item of which is any sheet or paper bearing the party's own figures, made by the party and put up on a public wall in this harbor on or about the twenty-fifth of August instant, with a copy of it, and another of which is any record of any finding made on or about the eighth of August instant.** **The notice contains no position of any office, no document, and no phrase belonging to any office, and it does not answer the question asked in a market on Sunday the thirteenth of August.**
2. **A form of four lines brought up the hill on Saturday the nineteenth of August and read out in a yard and not in a shed.** A formal inquiry opened into certain matters touching the working of a salvage concern at Morrow Quay between the first of May and the fifteenth of August instant; the parties named are four and are those whose names are on the writ of the first of May; the party is required to produce its crew's book and the returns made under a reporting clause **at a sitting to be appointed**; and nothing in it touches, grants, refuses or deals with the second season of the said writ. **The yard took the room and the truth about the day and signed nothing on the step.**
3. **A paper read in the hearing room on Saturday the ninth of September** — a production and not an appointment, requiring the crew's book whole and a copy of it, the writ, the four returns, an answer to the clause's own question, and stating that a sitting to be appointed will follow and will be served on the party and not left with anybody.
4. **A letter in the ordinary post, posted Sunday the twentieth of August at about seven in the evening, with seven pence of postage on it out of a bag that held nothing, asking a circuit office in a city for a copy of a record: the record of this yard's own fourth return of the fourteenth of July.** A copy of that return went into the harbor authority's ledger on that Friday morning in a clerk's own hand and a third copy went into a file in a city on the same morning. **The request is dated on the envelope as well as in the letter, because a thing that has not been answered in a month is going to be a thing somebody says was never asked.** THE REQUEST HAS BEEN ENTERED. THE COPY IS NOT MADE AND IS NOT RELEASED AND NO DATE ATTACHES TO EITHER.
5. **A sheet on the market wall of Morrow Quay, Friday the twenty-fifth of August at about eleven in the morning, forty copies of it, a shilling for the forty.** It carries, in its own words: the place of every copy of itself; six figures from a builder's own day-book of the port of Ashtide with a day against each and no year; the two figures for the bar of gravel side by side with the sentence about them, the sentence that a road nobody else can use is not a road and a road everybody can use is not this yard's, and the plain statement that this yard has not looked at five miles of that coast; the fact that a case of five bells is at about five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards and is not on the sheet and is not for sale and went back into the water out of a man's hands on the eighth of August; **what this yard asked for on the twentieth and has not been given, the date, and that a copy of a record is not a thing anybody in this harbor can check; and why the sheet is on a wall and not in a room.** **It is on the same nails, on the outside of whatever is there, and what is there is this yard's own fourth return of the twenty-second of July, which is still on those nails and is still the only paper on that wall any office in any city is obliged to look at, and a man can read it if he stands at the side of it in the right wind.** A sheet that goes up goes up on the outside of whatever is there and that has been the arrangement since the ninth of November.
6. **A copy of this yard's whole crew's book, made with a pen in a hearing room on Saturday the ninth of September in about two minutes and put into a file.** **The first time in four volumes that a copy of this yard's own record has left this harbor. Not one line of it was read aloud in that room.**
7. **A ninth acknowledgement in the harbor authority's ledger**, entered on Monday the twenty-eighth of August in the reeve's own hand, at his own cost, on a page with the work done on the mole's toe inside the first clause of the notice of the thirty-first of October, the hour in and the hour out against it. **The tenth may not be entered.**
8. **Twenty-two first-column entries and four second-column entries** in the yard's own book and in the ship's hand, and one first-column entry carried forward as the reason a party does not put a name on a thing it cannot check, being the decision of the fifth of July.
9. **A third-column entry of the eighth of August of Volume 03 still stands as the last entry, and the third column was NOT filled on the tenth of September of this batch, which is the second day since the second of May that it has not been filled on a day.**

**NO RETURN WAS MADE IN THIS BATCH AND THERE IS NO FIFTH.** Dated lines stand for the days of the batch on which no finding of worked glass was made and nothing was raised, and the standing line for the eighth and ninth of August of Volume 03 is unchanged: the party was past the outer marks on its own account, raised nothing, told no office and will tell no office.

### 4. The dated positions at the end of Chapter 160

- **The writ.** Granted Monday the first of May, running to midnight on the first of March next, **not the Second Bell license and not leading to one, not a charter, renewing nothing, ground not widened and not to be widened, four names and no fifth.** **Its second season is a named absence and BOTH the form of the nineteenth and the notice of the tenth say on their face that they do not touch, grant, refuse or deal with it, and the yard has entered both of those halves: a paper that does not mention a thing is not a decision about the thing.**
- **The notice of the thirty-first of October.** Four clauses, not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered, and **the harbor authority has no power to lift it and would not if it had.** Clause two is the live reason the two objects of nobody's are living in the open air on the mole's crown, **about nine feet apart, under two tarpaulins, one with a corner off it since the seventeenth of June and not lashed back, and neither object has moved: ninety-one days on the twenty-third and twenty-eighth of August, and not moved in this batch.** The man in about five fathoms in April is not the reason the notice is on the page, and the yard has not said that it was not worth the paper it is on and has not said that it was, and has entered both nots. **The two objects of nobody's were in view on the mole's steps on the twenty-third, the twenty-seventh and the tenth of September and were not touched on any of those days.**
- **The acknowledgements: eight, and this batch entered the ninth, and the tenth may not be entered.** **There are eight in that ledger and both of this year's were spent before this batch and one of them was asked for by this party and one was not, and the reeve named that in the open air before he granted the ninth, and the cost of the ninth fell on a man of the first lay, Corby Vance, who had the mole's toe booked for the same Monday and is out of a day and a day's work, and the yard entered his name and the figure and did not pay it, is not going to thank him, is not going to send for him, and is not going to say sorry in a market.**
- **The eighth-clause plan for the fifth seam** is refused, is in a record with a date, **was not reopened, was not reached, and the plate was not measured, not touched and not asked about in any chapter of this batch.** The mechanism remains a change of politics in an office. **The plate note closes in this month with a document and not with a word: there is a page gone out of a file in an office, and this party has not been told whose hand took it, and the absence of the page and the absence of the asking are both entered and both dated.**
- **The bell.** Eight and a half feet off the south end, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, no tongue on the frame and two parts of a chain in a lead plug. **THE GOING OUT OF THE SECOND OF MAY IS DONE AND IS SPENT AND WAS NOT REPEATED, NO SECOND EIGHT MARKS WAS PAID, NO BOAT WENT OFF THE SOUTH END IN THIS BATCH, AND THE STANDING SENTENCE SAID IN A STREET ON THE THIRD OF MAY STANDS UNREVERSED AND IS CARRIED IN THE CLOSE OF CHAPTER 160.** Eight marks and a day of a boat stands owing since the twenty-third of May, unpaid, unargued, and **the bellwright was not asked and is in no chapter of this batch as a person.**
- **THE LEAD PLUG. IN A CHEST IN A SHED WITH A LOCK THE YARD OWNS. NOT ASKED ABOUT, NOT PRINTED, NOT OPENED, AND NOT BROUGHT DOWN THE HILL.** The undertaking of the second of July binds the yard and not the office and not the market and not the boy.
- **THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN THIS BATCH, and the batch's hard rule permitted at most one naming and only if a chapter earned it, and no chapter earned it.** The bare fact is carried twice in the second column, both times with a reason against the line and **without a name**: the not-reading of the crew's book in a hearing room on the ninth, and the not-knowing about reading the second column out in a room on the tenth. The four lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four, are still nameless, and are not struck out. The count of four stands in the first column in a ship's hand with no name against it.
- **The glass storm.** Not used. Two written occurrences stand and no cause is named for it in this batch or in any volume and nothing in either line knows anything.
- **The coast road.** Not resolved, not explained, **not called anything by anybody in this batch.** The two figures for the bar of gravel are both on the market wall side by side for the first time, in nineteen words, and neither is withdrawn and this volume does not decide which is right. **The yard entered on the page that it has not looked at five miles of that coast and has not been asked to by anybody and is not going to in the season, and the reason is a working reason and is that a yard of two and a half people that has spent a fortnight being asked for a book by an office does not have three days for five miles of a coast it published in June.** The refused license of nine sentences stands and the road is published and cannot be un-published.
- **The run under this town.** Not explained, not named, not pursued, and not referred to in any chapter of this batch.
- **Custody.** Three items, three numbers, **digits never quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request, and the word custody does not occur in any of the ten chapters of this batch**, which is the lock satisfied by absence and is a fact about the chapters and not a debt.
- **The concealment.** Hidden. The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in the reeve's chart notation. **Nobody in this batch asked the boy and nobody asked the conservator, and the boy is in no chapter of it as a person and is not written to.**
- **The delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions and two of them with no call in them, **and NEITHER FIGURE WAS ADVANCED IN THIS BATCH BY ARITHMETIC OR BY A MEASUREMENT. NO OCCASION WAS ENTERED AND NO MEASUREMENT WAS TAKEN.** The delay is a hand's width of a second and it has not moved and was not taken and was not written down, and nobody in this yard has said why and nobody in this harbor has ever said why and this party is not going to be the first.
- **Labor.** No air bought in this batch and no hose and no gland paid. One herring berth out. The north berth out. The tide gang four men short. The mole-head light not on, because a light costs oil and the repair account of the office that owns it has been empty since the middle of February and is not going to be filled this year. The fire party's hose dead until the spring. The cistern charged on a rota on a Monday and a Thursday, walked thirteen times by this party since the end of May at no figure, not walked since the third of August, holding since the nineteenth of June. The hard at Ashtide unanswered since the ninth of November. Nineteen winters, in a naval file and in a list in a city. The *Sea Wren* at the pile, and this party has not thanked him and has not been asked to. **THE THIRD LAY WAS NOT USED AGAIN AND LOST NOTHING IN THIS BATCH. A MAN OF THE FIRST LAY LOST A DAY.**
- **The ninth seam's mooring was out of service from the first of May with an end of it entered on the third of July, being the first of September, and IT IS BACK IN SERVICE.** It was found sound at about twenty past nine on the morning of Friday the first of September at about four feet on the flood by a tide-gang man and a man of the third lay with a pole and a lead line and a rule, and the reason it is in service is that those two men walked out to it on their own time on four days in August because the entry said the end of it was the first of September and they read the entry. **Nobody in the harbor authority ordered it, the office's repair account was not filled, the office did not pay either man, and this yard did not pay either man and did not ask.** It is not a repair. It is *looked at*, in the words of the man of the third lay, and the yard entered the whole of it against its own interest.

### 5. The people, and the one who is not named

- **Calder Marr**, thirty-two. **He goes in no water in this batch.** He is on a mole's steps four times, on a market wall once, in a market office once and on the steps outside it once, in a hearing room once and in a shed most nights. **He reads no seam in this batch, on a document or on anything else, and the word *faultreader* is on no page of it.** He wrote down, on the eighth of September, a sentence he was told on the seventh of August not to write down, and he has not told her he wrote it, and the not-telling is entered.
- **Nessa Pike**, twenty-seven. The tender, the pump, the fall, the board and the slate, and the standing shutdown. **She won the argument about the second column in nine words on Sunday the twentieth: *a column is not a defense of anything and was never one*.** She counted the envelopes and made him count them once. She said out loud in a market on the twenty-fifth that the yard has been holding a record and not keeping a book for five months without knowing it. She did not thank the reeve and neither did he, and neither said why. **She is the person holding the pencil.**
- **Mara Venn**, thirty. **NOT ON THE CREW, NOT ASKED FOR ANYTHING BY THIS PARTY, AND PRESENT ONCE, ON FRIDAY THE EIGHTH OF SEPTEMBER, IN A ROOM SHE WENT INTO HERSELF.** She is at the long table with a book of her own, a pencil and her own brass calipers, and a man in a coat at the end of the table asks her about five bells in a case, and she answers the room and not the four people it is about. **She gives six things and no seventh, and the seventh is the boundary of her trade: six marks and two days, the same on every coast, which does not come down, which she has not got, and which this yard has not offered her and has not been asked to and has entered that it is going to say no if it ever is.** **THE TWO SIXES ARE THE SAME FIGURE PUT DOWN TWICE AND THE ECHO IS DELIBERATE, and she says so herself and asks for somebody in the room to be annoyed about it.** **The shard of worked glass nobody in this harbor can read stays unread. Her forty marks stand unpaid, first after food, since the fifteenth of October.** **She did not break with the Registry in this batch, which is Volume 4 and a different event, and no chapter of this batch fuses the two, and she is not of this crew at the end of it and did not join it.**
- **Ors Kest**, forty-four. **HE SPENT THE LAST ENTRY IN HIS LEDGER, ON HIS OWN MOTION AND AT HIS OWN COST, AND HE NAMED THE PRICE IN THE OPEN AIR BEFORE HE GRANTED IT, WHICH IS WHAT HE HAS DONE EVERY TIME.** He named the man of the first lay who pays. He said the office has been asked twice by this party and has paid twice and cannot pay a third time and is not going to be asked a third time. **He said the plate is not what this is about, in the same thirteen words he used in July, and he would not say what it is about, and the yard did not ask a second question.** The holder did not thank him and said why in nine words. **He is not a friend and the volume does not pretend he was right about everything.**
- **Ambrose Sell**, clerk of the circuit office. **NAMED WHEREVER A CLERK OF THE CIRCUIT OFFICE APPEARS, in Chapters 152, 159 and 160.** He is the mouth of a clause and not an antagonist, and he says so in his own words, and he has said a thing like that nine times this year and has kept all nine in a book of his own: the seventh on the mole's steps on the twenty-fifth of July, the eighth in a doorway on this hill on the nineteenth of August, the ninth in the hearing room on the ninth of September, and in each case he said what a box holds. **He served the notice of the tenth on the mole's steps, read the first paragraph off the paper in his hand and the other three from memory, and then said that he had said it from the paper, and the yard has written down that he did not and that he said he did.**
- **The man in a coat** is **NOT NAMED IN THIS BATCH AND MAY NOT BE NAMED IN THIS VOLUME.** Chapter 149 of Volume 03 has the holder tell him he does not know him and is not going to find out this month. He is in the market office on the twenty-fifth of August and in a room he entered himself on the eighth of September, and he is a channel and not a face, and no chapter of this batch gave him a coat off, an office, a name or an opinion about this yard. He **did** say one thing in a doorway on the nineteenth of August that was not the mouth of a clause, in his own words and off his own page.
- **Rowan Trewin**, fifty-four or a little under, nineteen years, two other men, a boat of about forty feet with a leaded keel and no harbor on her anywhere. **He came back late, as he said he would, and he came for the five miles and not for the bar.** He said the thing this yard had published and not said out loud, that a page carrying the place of every copy of itself is a page that tells a man where the copies are; that a wall worth reading is one somebody can be brought up in front of; that he has been in nine of the boats and has asked for a copy in none of them and is not going to; and that no authority in this harbor can bring him to stand in front of this party's wall, and he would not want to. **He asked for his two and a half to be put beside the yard's two and three-quarters on a wall and not in a column and not in a book, because a book is the yard's and a wall is the yard's and a press is a man's. The yard did it, in nineteen words, in front of him, and did not argue it, and is not going to guess which figure is right.** He is not thanked, has not asked to be, and said so in advance. **He will be back in the spring for nothing and no instrument in this harbor can call him and none can stop him.**
- **Danno Fisk**, of the third lay, about thirty-eight, thirty-one years in this harbor, named in this batch for the first time. He saw a boat at the market wall at about seven in the morning on the sixteenth with a man in a coat standing on her boards doing nothing, and a second man in the bottom of her whose face he did not see, and a barge out of the naval pile two hours later, and three sheets put into her in a bundle with the third one under the other two so it went in wet. **He gave his name and the hour and gave a quarter of an hour instead of a minute because he had no watch on his person, and he was not asked for a face and did not give one.** He is the man of the third lay who read the end of it on the ninth seam's mooring off this yard's wall on the third of July, told two other men in a market, and walked out to that mooring four times in August. **He used this yard's sheet and said so and said it is not a favor and that he is not going to be thanked for it and would be unpleasant about it if anybody tried.**
- **Tam Rundle**, thirty, of the tide gang, which is four men short. He walked out to the ninth seam's mooring on four days in August on his own time, gave the date off the third of July as the whole reason he was there, and was paid nothing by anybody.
- **Corby Vance**, of the first lay, named in this batch for the first time. **He had the mole's toe of this mole booked for Monday the twenty-eighth of August for a day of his own at a figure of his own and is out of a day and a day's work because this yard asked for the ninth acknowledgement.** The yard entered his name and the figure and has not paid it, is not going to thank him, is not going to send for him, and is not going to say sorry in a market.
- **Alden Marr**, sixty-one. **At the far bench on six days of this batch and asked for nothing on any of them, asking nothing, and Calder Marr does not go over to him on any of them, and the yard has entered that he did not and that neither brother has said why and neither brother is going to.** No fragment, no report talk, no second story, no confession.
- **The woman of the third lay** who books the days of the twenty-first and the twenty-eighth and pays twelve of seventeen at the mole; the woman of the second lay with gear of her own under the mole's toe off the third seam who hires the twenty-eighth; the woman of the second lay of about sixty with a basket who asked Mara Venn a question in a market office and who was in the market on the twenty-fifth; the presskeeper of the market office, nineteen years in that room, whose mother shifted the slab before her, who charges a shilling for a sheet a man owns; the clerk of this harbor authority, who is not a man of any consequence and keeps his own page. **A volume with no antagonist's face in it is not a volume with no faces, and all of these are ordinary and none of them is a device and none of them knows anything.**
- **Captain Veyra Holt** does not come to this harbor, is not in a room, is not on a mole, and appears in no chapter of this batch as a person. **Captain Veyra Holt is a name, a signature and a date and a posting, and from the first of September she is at a table in a city making up the coast's wreck lists, which means this yard's four returns are in her hands and nobody in Morrow Quay will ever be able to check what she writes with them.**
- **Bevin Rook is in no chapter of this batch as a person.** The three nots stand. The head of the third column keeps his hand. The forty marks stand whole and are not paid, resolved, repaired or reduced. **No chapter of this batch hires a boy to read a position back and calls that the same thing, and the yard has entered that it is not going to.**

### 6. What this batch may not be used for

1. **It may not be used to name the condition.** The batch named it nowhere. The bare fact is on the page twice, with a reason, and without a name. **A later volume may not put the word in against him either.**
2. **It may not be used to ask the bellwright, to print a digit, or to open the chest.** He is in no chapter of this batch as a person, no digit of his answer is printed, the chest was not opened and was not brought down the hill.
3. **It may not be used to enter a tenth acknowledgement, to lift, narrow, re-serve or alter the notice of the thirty-first, to reopen the eighth-clause plan of the seventeenth of January, to reach the plate at the fifth seam, to widen the writ's ground, to add a fifth name, to manufacture a second season, to name Perry Ollett again, to ask him anything, or to put a person, boat or lead of this yard inside the first clause of that notice on any day other than the twenty-eighth of August.**
4. **It may not be used to make a fifth return, to move the counting rule, or to report the work of the eighth and ninth of August to any office.**
5. **It may not be used to name the Nine Harbors as a compact, the Quietening, the Crown Seal, the Office of Returning Memory, Halven Rook in a room, Orison, or any of the nine final routes; to explain the nine-line mark; to identify the marking on the shard; to name the compartment; to quote a custody number's digits; to put a senior examiner in a room; to ask Sera Mott; to introduce a new antagonist, institution or final enemy; or to name the man in a coat.**
6. **It may not be used to obtain, make, release, print, quote, summarize or describe the provisional public copy record, which exists and has not been given; nor to put the phrase *risk management* on a page, which is not on any page of this volume before Chapter 171; nor to produce or describe a document in which any office accepts a removal; nor to have any person in this batch state what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material.**
7. **It may not be used to read a line of the crew's book aloud in a room, to put a seam on a document, or to begin the volume's climax.** The book was produced whole on the ninth and not one line of it was read out of it. **THE READING OF THAT BOOK IN A ROOM IS THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT AND IS IN CHAPTERS 181 AND LATER.**
8. **It may not be used to take a power stage, or to have a seam settle an argument, prove a motive, convict anybody, or tell Calder what happened.**
9. **It may not be used to pay Bevin Rook's forty marks, to write to him, to go to see him, to ask anybody who has seen him what he said, to reduce the figure, or to hire a boy to read a position back; to pay Mara Venn's forty marks, to pay her six marks and two days, or to reduce either of them; to pay either woman's eight shillings and tenpence; to pay the bellwright; to re-write the chandler's note or move any of its three counts; to pay a shilling of the three marks owing; or to make the harbor's poverty better for anything.**
10. **It may not be used to answer the market question of the thirteenth of August, to name a compact, to resolve the coast road, to call it anything, to look at the Kettle, or to produce the reeve's chart.**
11. **It may not be used to fuse the lead plug with anything, to fuse the nine fastenings with the nine-line mark, to convert the nine places into the Nine Harbors, or to ask why the three places with no joint are there.**

### 7. The costs this batch closes with

1. **THE PARTY'S OWN CLAUSE, TURNED ROUND.** The second limb of a reporting clause, written by this yard in April, agreed to in good faith, printed on a market wall in July in forty copies in about sixty boats, applied to this party by a notice served on a mole's steps on Sunday the tenth of September, **and the party did not write the notice and cannot un-write it and is not going to try.** The shipwright's sentence about it is on the page in a shed at ten o'clock at night: *that is the same box we built in April and it has been turned round so that we are the thing on the table and not the person reading the table.*
2. **A COPY OF THE YARD'S WHOLE RECORD IN A FILE IN A CITY, IN ANOTHER MAN'S HAND**, and a man in a coat who said out loud at the table that he would read it in a city, and a yard that cannot stop him and does not know what he will be done with it and has entered that it does not know.
3. **THE READING OF THAT BOOK IS COMING AND THE YARD KNOWS IT AND CANNOT SAY WHAT IT WILL SAY.** Three weeks. A line in the second column saying the holder does not know whether he is going to read the second column out in a room, with a reason against it and no name in it. **The yard has entered that it is not going to spend the three weeks reading anything, and that a man who tries to find out what he will say in a room by reading is going to find out what he wished he would have said.**
4. **THREE SHEETS OFF A MARKET WALL IN ONE MORNING AND INTO A BOAT, AND THIS YARD'S OWN SHEET OF THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST WENT WITH THEM**, and a further four sheets gone inside nine hours of the same two days, and about sixty boats and a city press, and **neither of those is coming back.**
5. **A DAY OF A MAN OF THE FIRST LAY**, out of a Monday, bought with a reeve's last entry, and the yard has not paid it, is not going to thank him, is not going to send for him, and is not going to say sorry in a market, and the reason is that a notice does not say what a man loses and a party that has published itself is not a party that gets to be sorry in a street and mean it.
6. **THE STANDING SENTENCE HE COULD NOT USE.** *A finding is not a conclusion.* It has been in the first column in his own hand since the second of May and it is the best sentence in the book, and it will not go in, because it was written for a return and this is not a return — **and the yard noticed on Sunday the tenth that it has been standing in front of a clause for five months without noticing that the clause was about glass.**
7. **A WAGE OF FOUR MARKS PAID ON THE TWENTY-SECOND AND FOUR ON THE TWENTY-NINTH**, the column moving twice for the first time since the eighth of August, and eleven envelopes on a bench of which one was never made.
8. **THREE MARKS OWING TO A MAN OF ASHTIDE, NOT PAID, ENTERED UNPAID WITH THE REASON AGAINST THE LINE AND THE FIGURE AND THE DATE ON IT.**
9. **THE NINTH SEAM'S MOORING BACK IN SERVICE, FIXED BY TWO MEN WHO READ A DATE OFF A WALL, AND THE YARD ENTERED THE WHOLE OF IT AGAINST ITS OWN INTEREST** and does not want to be thanked for it and has not been.
10. **A PUBLICATION, AND AN ERROR MADE IN PUBLIC BY THE MAN WHO PUBLISHED IT**: the sixth of six builder's figures said wrong on the second reading in about forty people's hearing, corrected off the sheet in his hand without going back to the top, in about a second and a half, entered against himself in the second column with a reason and without a name of any kind in it.
11. **A MAN WHO STOOD ON THE STEPS OUTSIDE A ROOM FOR EIGHTY MINUTES AND DID NOT GO IN**, and did not ask afterwards what was said, and is not going to be the reason any of it is asked, and has entered both, and the four people of the Sill are not asked anything because a limit they wrote and signed says it is not to be said to a man with a seal.
12. **A REFUSAL PUBLISHED.** For the first time in three volumes this yard has put on a public wall something it did not get: a request with a date on it and a refusal with no date on it. **A request is a thing a party can publish. An answer is not, and the sheet says so in its own words: THIS YARD IS NOT PRINTING AN ANSWER IT DOES NOT HAVE.**
13. **A WALL THAT TELLS A MAN WHERE THE COPIES ARE**, published deliberately in about forty people's hearing by a party that was told on Wednesday by the one man in this harbor it cannot go around that he wanted it said in a room, and the party said on the sheet's own face that it chose the market and is not going to explain itself out of that.
14. **THE THIRD COLUMN NOT FILLED ON THE TENTH OF SEPTEMBER**, the second day since the second of May it has not been filled, and nobody appointed, and a column nobody fills is a column somebody else fills.
15. **A HARBOR POORER ON THE TENTH OF SEPTEMBER THAN IT WAS ON THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST**, by one day of a man's work and by nothing that anybody did for it.

### 8. Proposed canon changes

**None applied. Six author-level decisions taken on the page, and two recorded as a divergence from a card rather than from an outline.**

1. **AUTHOR-LEVEL, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THE INQUIRY'S NOTICE, ITS FOUR PARAGRAPHS, ITS DATE AND ITS ROOM.** A sitting appointed for a day in the week following the tenth of September, at the hearing room over the tide-gang store on the west basin, at ten in the morning. The second paragraph gives the harbor authority power to seat one of two disputants and take such finding as is given without prejudice to any claim, **and states on its own face that that construction is the second limb of a reporting clause applied by the notice to this party.** The third paragraph says nothing in it touches, grants, refuses or deals with the second season of the writ. The fourth is a list of six things to produce, one of which is any sheet or paper bearing the party's own figures put up on a public wall on or about the twenty-fifth of August, with a copy. Served in daylight in about nine people's hearing at the top of the mole's steps, by Ambrose Sell, clerk. **The notice contains no position of any office, no document, and no phrase belonging to any office.**
2. **AUTHOR-LEVEL, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — A COPY OF THE CREW'S WHOLE BOOK IS IN A FILE IN A CITY.** Fifty-one weeks of the yard's own weather and the yard's own shame, made with a pen in about two minutes on Saturday the ninth of September, put into a file unopened, and a man in a coat who said at the table that he is not going to open it in that room and is going to read it in a city. **The reading of it in a room is the volume's midpoint and is not in this batch.**
3. **AUTHOR-LEVEL, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THE SIXTH BUILDER'S FIGURE, SAID WRONG TWICE.** Two hundred and seventy-seven feet, said in a market in about forty people's hearing on the twenty-fifth of August, for a figure that is two hundred and seventy-nine, corrected off the sheet in the holder's own hand without going back to the top, and entered in the second column against himself with the reason that the sixth figure is a figure a man in this harbor cannot check that afternoon and there was nobody in front of that wall who could have stopped him. **This is the first entry in the second column in five months that is a mistake of this yard's own rather than a limit of a man in a boat.**
4. **AUTHOR-LEVEL, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THE SENTENCE OF THE SEVENTH OF AUGUST IS NOW WRITTEN DOWN.** *Whatever was in that boat went back into it and this party got nothing out of the water on the eighth of August and there is nothing in this harbor that has come of it but four people alive.* Entered in the second column on Friday the eighth of September with a reason against the line and no name in it, six weeks after she told him not to write it down, **and he has not told her that he wrote it, and the not-telling is on the page.**
5. **AUTHOR-LEVEL, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THE NINTH ACKNOWLEDGEMENT AND ITS PRICE.** Entered on Monday the twenty-eighth of August in the reeve's own hand at his own cost on a day of work inside the first clause of the notice, for a woman of the second lay with gear of her own under the mole's toe, at twelve of seventeen paid at the mole, the yard's own five out of the same board, the hour in and the hour out entered. **The price fell on a man of the first lay, Corby Vance, who had the toe booked for the same Monday, and he is out of a day and a day's work, and the yard entered his name and the figure and has not paid it, is not going to thank him, is not going to send for him, and is not going to say sorry in a market. THE TENTH MAY NOT BE ENTERED AND THE REEVE HAS SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE CANNOT PAY A THIRD TIME.**
6. **AUTHOR-LEVEL, TAKEN ON THE PAGE AND BINDING — THE NINTH SEAM'S MOORING IS IN SERVICE AGAIN, AND IT WAS TWO MEN READING A DATE OFF A WALL.** Found sound at about twenty past nine on the morning of Friday the first of September at about four feet on the flood, with a pole and a lead line and a rule, by Tam Rundle of the tide gang and Danno Fisk of the third lay, who had walked out to it on their own time on the sixth, fourteenth, twenty-first and twenty-eighth of August because an end of it entered in the harbor authority's book on Monday the third of July said the first of September. **It is not a repair. It is *looked at*. Nobody in the harbor authority ordered it, the repair account was not filled, nobody was paid, and this yard entered the whole of it against its own interest and is not going to be thanked for it and has not been.**
7. **DIVERGENCE FROM A CARD, RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY OBEYED.** `outline/batches/volume-04-batch-0001.md`'s Chapter 160 next-chapter pull says *a wage of four is due on Tuesday the fifteenth*. **On this book's grid the fifteenth of September is a Friday and there is no wage Tuesday in that week. The next wage Tuesday is the nineteenth, and Chapter 160 says the nineteenth. The chapters decide a date and the card is corrected to them**, and the card file was not edited.
8. **DIVERGENCE FROM A CARD, RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY OBEYED.** Chapter 151's card says the market wall is used on the Wednesday and not on the Thursday, while its own continuity facts describe the yard's fourth return as still on the wall. **The chapter therefore brings the counting of the bare nails forward to about five o'clock on the Wednesday evening, so that the market wall is touched once and not twice, and the Thursday is a shed.**
9. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the fourth paragraph of the notice.** The demand for *any sheet or paper bearing this party's own figures, made by the party and put up on a public wall in this harbor on or about the twenty-fifth day of August instant* is the item that tells the yard that whoever drafted the notice has read a market wall of this harbor. **The yard does not know who, has entered that it does not know, and is not going to guess.** A later volume may notice it again; this volume may not answer it.
10. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the clerk reading the notice partly from memory and then saying he read it from the paper.** Ambrose Sell read the first paragraph off the paper in his hand in the open air and the other three from his own memory, and then said that he had said it from the paper, and the yard has written down that he did not and that he said he did, and is not going to improve on either. **No Volume 04 chapter may improve on it.**
11. **STILL OPEN AND STILL UNDECIDED, EXPLICITLY —** the answer inside the delayed note; the nine-line mark, which closes in Volume 5; the three places with no joint, owed a placement in a later volume as a fact and not as an identification; the coast road and the Kettle; the custody items and the live question no paper in this harbor answers; the lead plug's number; the plate at the fifth seam and the page gone out of a file; the run under this town; the compartment; what the shard carries; the marking on the mole's crown; the first echo's witness; Neave Tarrant; and the question about who changed a rule. **Nothing in this batch touched any of them and no chapter of it may be used to touch them.**
